If the internal group is not tied to the frame, the charge vanishes at the bifurcation surface for a regular connection with vanishing momentum map, and the term is absent. The first reading assigns the coefficient \(-2\kappa_X\) of \(W\) to \(\kappa_X\operatorname{tr}\Omega^2\) and the second assigns \(0\), against \(-4\kappa_X\) on the \(\delta\rho=0\) modes.

For the diagonal family \(\Theta^{12}=r^{-\epsilon c_a}\), \(\Theta^{34}=r^{-\epsilon c_b}\), \(\epsilon=n-1\), one finds exactly \(H=c_a\mathcal J_{12}+c_b\mathcal J_{34}\), with the Riemann deltas \(R_{1212}=-2\pi\epsilon c_a\), \(R_{a3a3}=R_{a4a4}=-\pi\epsilon c_b\) (symbolic computation). The cone angle of the normal plane fixes \(c_a=1\); \(c_b\neq0\) makes the metric induced on \(\Sigma\) singular at order \(\epsilon\) (read off the delta structure; not separately checked). With all deltas inserted in the cross term of the weighted \(a_2\) (an extension of the cross-term rule beyond the cone, not derived), the gap of the preceding paragraph becomes \(-N_{\mathrm{tot}}c_b/15+2\kappa_X(1+c_b)\) about a self-dual \(\bar\Theta\) and the same expression about an anti-self-dual one with \(\Theta^{34}=-r^{-\epsilon c_b}\), for which \(H=c_a\mathcal J_{12}-c_b\mathcal J_{34}\), and equals \(2\kappa_X\) at \(c_b=0\). The \(\kappa_X\) part vanishes only for a holonomy of chirality opposite to that of \(\bar\Theta\), while the \(N_{\mathrm{tot}}\) part then becomes nonzero, so no content-independent profile removes the gap in this family.

On the 17 sampled points with totally geodesic \(\Sigma\) (exact rational arithmetic; not proved), \(\tau_\Omega=R_\Sigma-2W+\bigl(a_1^{v}-\tfrac12X'\bigr)\), with \(a_1^{v}\) the \(a_1\) density of the vector sector. The coefficient of \(R_\Sigma\) in the basis \((W,R_\Sigma,R,e,X')\) of the fit, in the completed coefficient, is therefore \(\kappa_X-N_{\mathrm{tot}}/30\), the value of the Levi-Civita cross-term rule, and the remainder is a combination of the \(\Lambda^2\)-order densities \(a_1\) and \(X'\) in that basis; no statement is made about their topological character beyond it. These statements are for the diagonal family, the modes and replica stated, and the hypotheses of the preceding paragraphs; they do not derive the holonomy selected by the matrix model, the replica, or a value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). Problem~B stays open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\paragraph{Edge and contact terms of the vector and connection sector at the Weyl coefficient (conditional; not closure).}
Keep the setting of the preceding paragraph: the weighted \(a_2\) density, the modes with \(\delta\rho=0\) about a constant self-dual or anti-self-dual \(\bar\Theta\), the completed replica, a totally geodesic \(\Sigma\), cross terms at first order in \(1-n\), and the completed gap \(2\kappa_X\) in the coefficient of \(W\), read in the basis \((W,R_\Sigma,R,e,X')\). On the twelve modes \(W\) and \(R_\Sigma\) are independent (rank 2), with \(R_{ii}=W-R_\Sigma\) and \(R_{ijij}=2W-R_\Sigma\).

\emph{Endomorphism contacts.} To first order in the amplitude about a constant \(\bar\Theta\) the endomorphisms \(E_V\) of the vector and \(E_f\) of the Dirac operator equal \(e\,\mathbb I\): the antisymmetric part is a divergence of \(\Theta^{\rho\sigma}\partial_\rho\Theta^{\mu\nu}\), which vanishes at first order about constant \(\bar\Theta\), and the symmetric traceless part is quadratic (exact arithmetic, cubic jets of both chiralities, two amplitudes). Since \(e\) vanishes for \(\delta\rho=0\), every cross term of \(a_2\) with a delta of an endomorphism, uniform or confined to the normal block, vanishes at this order for every content: a contact of Kabat type has no coefficient of \(W\) in this presentation. For the Hodge operator of the metric \(G\) (\(E=-\mathrm{Ric}\), \(\Omega=\mathrm{Riem}\)), the same cross-term rule on the same modes gives \(6/5\) and \(-31/15\) for the Maxwell field with its ghosts, equal to its \(12\kappa_C\) in the coefficient of \(W\), of which the endomorphism contact carries \(3\) (the value is \(-9/5\) without it). The matrix-derived vector operator is not the Hodge operator: on these modes \(\mathrm{Riem}^2=2C^2\) and \(\mathrm{Ric}^2=C^2/2\), and \(v-4s\) is \(-C^2/3\) against \(C^2/12\). That mechanism is therefore not available for it.

\emph{Edge modes.} In the classes treated in~\cite{DonnellyWall2016}, products of a two-dimensional base with a bifurcate horizon and a compact fibre, and \(S^1\times H^3\), the edge sector of the Maxwell field is a scalar of wrong-sign statistics on the entangling surface, and its logarithmic density is the two-dimensional trace anomaly of the Laplacian of \(\Sigma\), \(-R_\Sigma/24\pi\); that paper does not treat the dependence on the Weyl tensor, and leaves that on the extrinsic geometry open. In the basis above \(-R_\Sigma\) has coefficient \(0\) of \(W\). A weight \(w\) built from \(\operatorname{Pf}\Theta\) and \(\operatorname{tr}\Theta^2\) in \(-w^{-1}\operatorname{div}(w\nabla)\) adds nothing at first order, because the Hessians of both vanish on the modes. A potential \(E_\Sigma=\alpha R_{ii}+\beta R_{ijij}\) would change the density to \(-(R_\Sigma+6E_\Sigma)\) and would need \(\alpha+2\beta=\kappa_X/3\) for the coefficient \(-2\kappa_X\) of \(W\). This depends on \(n_D\) through the Dirac weight, whereas the vector carries one edge scalar, if it carries the edge sector of a Maxwell field, and the Dirac fields none. No edge term independent of \(n_D\) equals \(2\kappa_X\) for all \(n_D\).

\emph{Holonomy contact.} For the diagonal profile with \(\lvert\Theta^{34}\rvert=r^{-\epsilon c_b}\) the holonomy is \(H=c_a\mathcal J_{12}+\chi c_b\mathcal J_{34}\), with \(\chi=+1\) about a self-dual and \(\chi=-1\) about an anti-self-dual \(\bar\Theta\) (symbolic computation), and the coefficient of \(W\) in \(\operatorname{tr}(\mathcal J_{34}\Omega_{12})\) is \(2\chi\). The gap, in the extended cross-term rule of the preceding paragraph, is therefore the same for both chiralities, \(N_{\mathrm{tot}}(3c_a-2c_b-3)/30+2\kappa_X(2-c_a+c_b)\). With \(s=c_a-c_b\), the contraction of the first-order Weyl tensor with the Weyl tensor of the deltas is \(2sW\), and the Euler combination \(\mathrm{Riem}\cdot\mathrm{Riem}'-4\,\mathrm{Ric}\cdot\mathrm{Ric}'\) is \(2c_aR_\Sigma-2c_bW\), which contains \(W\) for \(c_b\neq0\). If that \(W\) part is dropped as topological, the gap is \((s-1)N_{\mathrm{tot}}/10-2(s-2)\kappa_X\): the metric part requires \(s=1\) and the connection part \(s=2\). The profile with \(\delta\rho=0\), \(c_b=-c_a\), has \(s=2\) and removes the \(\kappa_X\) part, leaving \(N_{\mathrm{tot}}/15\) (\(N_{\mathrm{tot}}/10\) after dropping the Euler part); its induced metric on \(\Sigma\) is singular at first order and the scalar of its Riemann delta, \(2c_a+4c_b\), has the sign opposite to that of the cone.

No edge, endomorphism-contact or holonomy term of this family therefore supplies \(-2\kappa_X W\) for all contents. The required term is proportional to the index-weighted sum that multiplies \(\operatorname{tr}\Omega^2\), the same for every fibre representation; of the candidates examined only a holonomy generator beyond \(\mathcal J_{12}\) attached to \(\Sigma\) has this property, and none of the profiles of the diagonal family provides it without a singular metric on \(\Sigma\). The gap is also relative to the comparator \(12\kappa_C^T\) assigned to \(\operatorname{tr}\Omega^2\) by the metric-universal rule; whether the entropy of this non-metric term is that value or the Noether value \(-2\kappa_X\) of the preceding paragraph, which equals the replica, is not decided here.

These statements are for the Poisson-limit operators, one loop, \(\alpha=1\), a totally geodesic \(\Sigma\), cross terms at first order in \(1-n\) on the \(n<1\) branch, the modes and replica of the preceding paragraphs, the cross-term rule extended to \(c_b\neq0\) (not derived), and an edge sector of the abelian type of the cited paper. They do not treat non-diagonal profiles, the non-delta singular first-order parts, the exact heat trace of the cone with holonomy, the symplectic gauge group, or the mode with \(\delta\rho\neq0\), and give no value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). Problem~B stays open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\paragraph{Comparator for the entropy of the bundle curvature and the holonomy dictionary (conditional; not closure).}
Keep the setting of the preceding paragraph: the modes with \(\delta\rho=0\) about a constant self-dual or anti-self-dual \(\bar\Theta\), a totally geodesic \(\Sigma\), cross terms at first order in \(1-n\), and the weights \(\kappa_X=1-\tfrac12n_D\) (vector \(1\), each Dirac field \(-\tfrac12\)). Write \(\mathcal J=\mathcal J_{12}\) for the generator of the rotation of the normal plane, \(\alpha_v\mathcal J\) for the holonomy around \(\Sigma\) of the vector bundle connection and \(\alpha_f\mathcal J\) for that of each Dirac fibre (\(\mathcal J\to\tfrac12\gamma^{12}\)).

\emph{The comparator is an extension.} The functionals \(\tfrac1{12}\operatorname{tr}\Omega^2\) and \(-\tfrac13C^2\) have the same value on every sampled mode (exact arithmetic, both chiralities), but their polarisations differ on pairs of modes with different wave vectors and between a mode and a generic closed second jet (exact arithmetic). The entropy at first order in the amplitude is the cross term of the quadratic form with the direction of the replica, which lies outside the modes, so the quadratic form on the modes does not fix it. The value \(12\kappa_C^T\) is the entropy of the metric functional with the same value on every sampled mode; it is not shown to be the entropy of \(\operatorname{tr}\Omega^2\). Under the identification of the matrix index with the tangent index through the coframe \(e=\bar\omega\), \(\Omega^v=R\oplus N\) on the modes, with \(R\) the Levi-Civita curvature, \(\langle N,R\rangle=0\), \(\langle N,N\rangle=\langle R,R\rangle\) and \(\operatorname{tr}(\mathcal J N_{12})=0\); the holonomy generator pairs with \(R\) only (without that identification \(\langle\Omega^v,R\rangle=0\), while the values \(\langle\Omega^v,\Omega^v\rangle=2\langle R,R\rangle\) and \(\operatorname{tr}(\mathcal J\Omega_{12})\) are unchanged).

\emph{The composite constraint.} For \(b_\sigma=G_{\sigma\tau}\Theta^{\rho\tau}\partial_\rho\Theta\) the transformation law of a one-form valued in the adjoint holds under the rotation acting on the chart and on the matrix indices together, and fails under a rotation of the chart alone, of the matrix indices alone, under a non-orthogonal diagonal linear map and under a non-linear change of chart (exact check at a point, Euclidean section). The flow generated by the rotation of the normal plane therefore acts on the fibre through \(\mathcal J\), and no generator \(\mathcal J_{34}\) enters the pairing, since the corresponding rotation does not vanish on \(\Sigma\). Introducing a multiplier \(\lambda\) for \(\omega-F[\Theta]\) fixes \(\lambda\) in terms of \(D(\partial L/\partial\Omega)\) and reproduces the Euler--Lagrange expression of the composite Lagrangian (a check of the field equations in a one-dimensional toy, not of a charge). A first law of the form of the Lorentz--diffeomorphism Noether charge~\cite{JacobsonMohd2015} can be formed only with this tie. If the connection is treated as an auxiliary independent field on which the Lagrangian depends through its curvature, it gives the density \(\kappa\,\operatorname{tr}(H\Omega_{12})\) with \(H=\mathcal J\); the charge of the constraint term is not computed. A covariance under general diffeomorphisms of the chart is not available for \(b\).

\emph{Dictionary.} Let \(E(\alpha_v,\alpha_f)\) be the coefficient of \(W\) in the cross term \(\sum_i\kappa_i\operatorname{tr}(H_i\Omega_{12})\) with the holonomy \(H=\alpha_v\mathcal J\) on the vector bundle and \(H=\alpha_f\mathcal J\) on each Dirac fibre. It is \(N_{\mathrm{tot}}/10-2\alpha_v+n_D\alpha_f\), because \(\operatorname{tr}(\mathcal J\Omega_{12})\) has coefficient \(-2\) of \(W\). The Noether value of the preceding paragraph is \(E(1,1)\). The periodic, fixed-state and completed replicas have the coefficients of \(W\) of \(E\) at \((\alpha_v,\alpha_f)=(0,0)\), \((0,1)\), \((1,1)\) for all \((N_s,n_D)\) (exact arithmetic on six modes of each chirality), so their agreement with \(E\) at their own holonomy is an identity. The comparator \(12\kappa_C^T\) equals \(E(2,2)\); this is a statement about the coefficient of \(W\) and not about a holonomy of the bundle. Relative to \(E(1,1)\) the residuals of the periodic, fixed-state and completed replicas are \(2-n_D\), \(2\), \(0\). For the periodic and completed replicas this agreement is the identity between two cross terms of the same \(a_2\) with the same deltas. The Dirac entry is a check: the index weight \(-\tfrac12\) of the heat coefficient and the spin term of the Landau--Aharonov--Bohm spectrum agree. The residual \(2\) of the fixed-state replica is the vector holonomy that integer-\(n\) gluing omits, a contact of the type \(S_{\mathrm{BH}}=S_{\mathrm{ent}}-Q\), by analogy with the Noether-charge term for non-minimally coupled scalars of~\cite{FrolovFursaev1997}.

These statements are for the modes, replica and hypotheses of the preceding paragraphs, the Euclidean section and the coefficient of \(W\) only. They do not derive the holonomy selected by the matrix state, the continuation in \(n\) of the spin-complete replica (not analytic at \(n=1\) in the ledger above), the charge of the constraint term of the composite connection, the Euler coefficient, the logarithmic order off these modes, a first law at order \(\Lambda_{\mathrm{UV}}^2\) (where \(\rho^2X\) has no bundle curvature and the gap of the normalisation paragraph is not addressed), or a value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). Problem~B stays open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\paragraph{Order \(\Lambda^2\): contacts, vector holonomy and the Noether coefficient of \(\rho^2X\) against the gap of the normalisation paragraph (conditional; not closure).}
Keep the setting of the paragraph on the normalisation of the tensor kinetic term and of the paragraphs on the logarithmic coefficient and its contact terms: Poisson limit, Euclidean section, \(\alpha=1\), one loop, the \(\LUV^2\) term, a totally geodesic \(\Sigma\), cross terms at first order in \(1-n\), the family \(\Theta^{12}=1/f(x^1,x^2)\), \(\Theta^{34}=1\), \(f=\lvert z\rvert^{n-1}\), and \(\sigma=+1\) for \(n<1\), \(\sigma=-1\) for \(n>1\). Write the entropy of a sector as \(S=(\LUV^2A_G/8\pi)\,c\), so that a flat-cone scalar component has \(c=\tfrac16\) and \(1/G=c\,\LUV^2/2\pi\). Then \(c_A=N_{\mathrm{tot}}/6\) (the antiperiodic Dirac gluing adds \(n_D\)) and \(c_T=N_{\mathrm{tot}}/6-4\kappa_X\), the values of the paragraphs on the normalisation and on fermionic content. Before any contact, \(c_A-c_T=4-n_D\) for the fixed-state replica and \(4-2n_D\) for the periodic one; at \(n_D=0\) this is \(1/G_A-1/G_T=2\Lambda_{\mathrm{UV},v}^2/\pi\), nonzero and independent of \(N_s\).

\emph{Contacts.} The coefficient \(a_1=\operatorname{tr}(R/6-E)\) is linear in the endomorphism, so its cross term with the deltas is read off from the delta content of the family. The uniform \(E_\delta\) of the contact paragraph lies on every scalar, connection, ghost and Dirac component and shifts \(c\) by \(-N_{\mathrm{tot}}/4\); it carries the factor \(N_s\), and with the scalars at \(\Lambda_{\mathrm{UV},s}\) and the connection with its ghosts at \(\Lambda_{\mathrm{UV},v}\) the residue is \(\tfrac72\Lambda_{\mathrm{UV},v}^2-\tfrac14N_s\Lambda_{\mathrm{UV},s}^2\) at \(n_D=0\), which cannot follow a gap that does not depend on \(N_s\). The holonomy \(\alpha_v\mathcal J\) of the vector bundle around \(\Sigma\) twists the two components with \(\mathcal J=\pm1\) by \(a=\alpha_v\lvert1-n\rvert\). The constant term of the heat trace of one component on the cone of angle \(2\pi n\) with phase \(e^{2\pi\mathrm ia}\) is \(C(n,a)=B_2(a)/(2n)-n/12\), \(B_2(a)=a^2-a+\tfrac16\), \(0\le a\le1\); it gives \((1/12)(1/n-n)\) at \(a=0\) and the Aharonov--Bohm value \(-a(1-a)/2\) at \(n=1\), and agrees to \(10^{-12}\) with a numerical sum over the Bessel orders \(\lvert m+a\rvert/n\) at twelve pairs \((n,a)\). Its term linear in \(a\) shifts \(c\) of the vector by \(-\alpha_v\sigma\): \(-1\) for \(\alpha_v=1\) on \(n<1\), the value that takes the vector with its ghosts from \(\tfrac13\) to \(-\tfrac23\); the cone angle of the family fixes \(\alpha_v=1\). For a Dirac field with the less singular extension the constant is \(a^2+\tfrac16\) per block and the symmetric twists \(a_k=k/n\) sum to zero, so a common holonomy shift has no term of first order: the Dirac holonomy contact is zero at this order. At \(n_D=0\) and \(\alpha_v=1\) the residue \(c-c_T\) is
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lc@{}}
\toprule
replica and contacts & \(c-c_T\)\\
\midrule
fixed-state, no delta, no holonomy & \(4\)\\
fixed-state and vector holonomy, \(n<1\) / \(n>1\) & \(3\) / \(5\)\\
uniform \(E_\delta\) only & \(\tfrac72-\tfrac14N_s\)\\
\(E_\delta\) and holonomy, \(n<1\) / \(n>1\) & \(\tfrac52-\tfrac14N_s\) / \(\tfrac92-\tfrac14N_s\)\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
with the single cutoff of the normalisation paragraph. The first two rows do not vanish for any \(N_s\); the last two vanish at \(N_s=14\), \(10\), \(18\) only, a choice of content. For \(n_D>0\) the first two rows become \(4-n_D\) and \(4-n_D-\alpha_v\sigma\), which vanish at \(n_D=4\) and \(n_D=3\) (\(n<1\)) for every \(N_s\), again a choice of content, and equal \(2\) and \(1\) at \(n_D=2\). A holonomy with \(\alpha_v\sigma=4\) would close the bosonic gap; it needs a second circulation of modulus \(3\) in \(\Theta^{34}\), which makes the metric induced on \(\Sigma\) singular, and it is not used. The statement of the normalisation paragraph that \(\rho^2X\) contributes no entropy refers to the fixed-state replica, with \(\rho\) regular, or equivalently to the presentation \(r=0\) below; a Wald-type coefficient of \(\rho^2X\) is presentation dependent.

\emph{Dragging, regularity, covariance.} Take \(e^A{}_\mu=\eta^{AB}\omega_{\mu B}\) as in the paragraph on the torsion invariant. Let \(\xi\) be the rotation (Lorentzian boost) of the normal plane with generator \(\mathcal J\). The family, for any \(f(r)\), and every constant \(\bar\Theta\) with \([\mathcal J,\bar\Theta]=0\) (the span of \(e_{12}\) and \(e_{34}\), two of the six components) are invariant under the flow that rotates the chart and the matrix index together, and not under the rotation of the chart alone: for \(\bar\Theta=a(e_{12}+e_{34})\) the Lie derivative along the chart rotation is \(\pm a\,\mathrm{diag}(1,1,0,0)\), the sign following the orientation of \(\mathcal J\) (exact, both signatures). The background \(n=1\) is regular; for \(n\ne1\), \(\Theta^{12}=1-(n-1)\ln r+O((n-1)^2)\) and \(X=2(n-1)^2r^{-2n}\) are singular on \(\Sigma\); this singular-\(\rho\) family is the second reading of the paragraph on the geometry that carries the cone. The Noether-charge construction of~\cite{IyerWald1994} needs a diffeomorphism covariant Lagrangian of the dynamical fields, stationary fields with \(\mathcal L_\xi\phi=0\), and regularity on the bifurcation surface; the term \(Y(\phi,\mathcal L_\xi\phi)\) by which the charge is ambiguous is linear in \(\mathcal L_\xi\phi\) and drops out for stationary fields, which is the step that makes the entropy independent of the ambiguities~\cite{JacobsonKangMyers1994}. Here \(\mathcal L_\xi e^A{}_\nu=e^A{}_\sigma\partial_\nu\xi^\sigma\) on \(\Sigma\), so \(e_{A\mu}\mathcal L_\xi e^A{}_\nu=\nabla_\nu\xi_\mu\ne0\): the frame is dragged only up to a rotation of the matrix index, a global symmetry of the induced density. This is the form of the obstruction for the unit vector of Einstein-aether theory, where the derivation fails and no entropy has been identified~\cite{Foster2006}; here a compensating rotation exists but is rigid. The contraction of the matrix index is with the fixed \(\eta\) of the action, not with a spacetime metric.

\emph{Noether coefficient.} In the coframe variables the density \(\tfrac{N_{\mathrm{tot}}}6\rho R[g_\rho]+\kappa_X\rho^2X\), with \(X=T_{\lambda\mu\nu}T^{\lambda\mu\nu}\), is a diffeomorphism covariant first-order functional of \(e^A{}_\mu\) (through \(\det e\) and \(\mathrm de\)). For every such functional \(J^\mu=\partial_\nu Q^{\mu\nu}-E^\mu{}_Ae^A{}_\sigma\xi^\sigma\) with \(Q^{\mu\nu}=(\partial L/\partial\partial_\mu e^A{}_\nu)\,e^A{}_\sigma\xi^\sigma\) (checked at three random fields to \(10^{-30}\)), which vanishes where \(\xi=0\): the torsion invariants carry no Noether charge on \(\Sigma\), and the Noether coefficient is the coefficient of \(R\) in the presentation chosen, where \(R\) denotes \(R[G]\) with \(\sqrt G=\rho^2\); the presentations differ from \(\rho R[g_\rho]\) by terms in \(V^2\) and total derivatives, which are kept in the remainder. It is not unique, because \(R=-\mathcal T-2\nabla_\mu V^\mu\) with \(\mathcal T=\tfrac14T_{\lambda\mu\nu}T^{\lambda\mu\nu}+\tfrac12T_{\lambda\mu\nu}T^{\mu\lambda\nu}-V_\mu V^\mu\) and \(V_\mu=\partial_\mu\ln\rho\) (exact at three random two-jets, with the identity of the paragraph on the torsion invariant). With the vector, axial and tensor parts \(V,S,q\) of the torsion, \(X=\tfrac23V^2+S^2+q^2\) and \(\mathcal T=-\tfrac23V^2-\tfrac14S^2+\tfrac12q^2\). Eliminating \(q^2\), \(V^2\) or \(S^2\) gives the coefficient \(r=-2\), \(1\), \(4\) of \(R\) in \(X\), and the variables \((G,\rho,b)\) give \(r=0\); any real \(r\) is obtained by adding \(r(R+\mathcal T+2\nabla_\mu V^\mu)=0\). The Noether coefficient is \(c_W(r)=N_{\mathrm{tot}}/6+\kappa_Xr\), so \(c_W-c_T=\kappa_X(r+4)\), zero for every content only at \(r=-4\). On the sampled modes with \(\delta\rho=0\) of both chiralities, \(S^2:q^2:X=\tfrac13:\tfrac23:1\) and \(\langle R\rangle=-\tfrac14\langle X\rangle\); the presentation \(r=-4\) is the one whose torsion remainder, \(\tfrac{10}3V^2+2S^2-q^2\), vanishes on every such mode and not on pairs of modes with different wave vectors. The value \(r=-2\) of the paragraph on the torsion invariant leaves \(c_W-c_T=2\kappa_X\), the part of the on-mode value of \(X\) that its remainder \(2V^2+\tfrac32S^2\) carries. Among \(aT_{\lambda\mu\nu}T^{\lambda\mu\nu}+bT_{\lambda\mu\nu}T^{\mu\lambda\nu}+cV^2\) the antisymmetric part of the frame equation vanishes identically only for \((a,b,c)\propto(\tfrac14,\tfrac12,-1)\) (numerical, \(d=3\), residual \(10^{-29}\)), so for \(\kappa_X\ne0\) the density is not invariant under local rotations of the frame, and the class of \(X\) modulo that direction is canonical.

\emph{Cross term with the connection of the cone.} On the family the connection of the vector bundle is \(b=(1-n)\mathcal J\,\mathrm d\theta\), with holonomy \(2\pi(1-n)\mathcal J\) and no delta. The cross term of \(\rho^2X\) with a regular first-order connection \(\beta\) is \((n-1)\int\Omega_{12}\ln(L/r)\,\mathrm d^2x\) up to normalisation (exact for polynomial \(\beta\)): the weight is the Green function of the normal plane and not \(\delta_\Sigma\), so it is a bulk term of the kind removed by the equations of motion in the construction of~\cite{LewkowyczMaldacena2013}, not an area term. The charge of the constraint term of the composite connection is not needed for this dependence, which is algebraic in \(b\), but it is not computed.

None of the contacts above (E-type, \(-\alpha_v\sigma\) with \(\alpha_v=1\), zero for Dirac) supplies the required \(-4\kappa_X\) of \(c\): the available vector contact has modulus \(1\) and the \(E_\delta\) contact depends on \(N_s\). The Noether coefficient is not obstructed but undetermined: the density fixes no value of \(r\), the value \(r=-4\) would match and is selected only by the match, and the value \(r=-2\) of the paragraph on the torsion invariant leaves \(2\kappa_X\). In the coframe variables it stays undetermined until the frame is dragged by a flow whose internal part is a local symmetry, that is until the question of local frame rotations (Problem~D) is settled; in the variables \((G,\rho,\vartheta)\) of the paragraph on Moser variables the Noether coefficient takes two values related by one numerical identity, and covariance does not select one. These statements are for the Poisson-limit operators, one loop, \(\alpha=1\), the \(\LUV^2\) term, the family and the first-order additive cross-term rule on the \(n<1\) and \(n>1\) branches; they do not derive the replica, the holonomy or the state selected by the matrix model, the first law with the rigid compensating rotation, the Dirac extension, the exact spectrum of the singular cone with holonomy, or a value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). Problem~B stays open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\paragraph{Order \(\LUV^2\): local frame rotations, the Goldstone-extremised density and the Noether coefficient of \(\rho^2X\) (conditional; not closure).}
Keep the setting of the preceding paragraph and of the paragraph on the torsion invariant: Poisson limit, the \(\LUV^2\) term, \(e^A{}_\mu=\eta^{AB}\omega_{\mu B}\) with \(\omega=\Theta^{-1}\) closed, \(V_\mu=G^{\lambda\nu}T_{\lambda\mu\nu}\), equal to \(\partial_\mu\ln\rho\) on the Poisson class, \(\mathcal T=\tfrac14T_{\lambda\mu\nu}T^{\lambda\mu\nu}+\tfrac12T_{\lambda\mu\nu}T^{\mu\lambda\nu}-V_\mu V^\mu\), the density \(\tfrac{N_{\mathrm{tot}}}6\rho R[g_\rho]+\kappa_X\rho^2X\), and the weights \(\kappa_X=1-n_D/2\).

\emph{Classification.} Under \(\delta e^A=\lambda^A{}_B(x)e^B\), \(\lambda_{AB}=-\lambda_{BA}\), the metric \(G\) and \(\rho\), with \(\rho^2=\lvert\det e\rvert\) for a general coframe, are unchanged and \(\sqrt{\lvert G\rvert}\,(a_1T_{\lambda\mu\nu}T^{\lambda\mu\nu}+a_2T_{\lambda\mu\nu}T^{\mu\lambda\nu}+a_3V_\mu V^\mu+a_4V^\mu\partial_\mu\ln\rho+a_5\lvert\partial\ln\rho\rvert^2)\) of an arbitrary coframe changes by \(\sqrt{\lvert G\rvert}\,P^{\mu|\lambda\nu}\partial_\mu\lambda_{\lambda\nu}\), with \(P\) linear in \(T\) (checked against a finite variation). The change is a total derivative for every coframe only on the span of \(\mathcal T\) and \(\lvert\partial\ln\rho\rvert^2\), that is \((a_1,a_2,a_3,a_4)\propto(\tfrac14,\tfrac12,-1,0)\) with \(a_5\) free: the null space of the divergence of \(\sqrt{\lvert G\rvert}P\) at random two-jets has dimension two and is this span, for \(d=3,4\) and both signatures, and at quadratic order about a constant frame the invariance conditions have the exact rational solution \(a_1=-a_3/4\), \(a_2=-a_3/2\). Both members are functions of \(G\) up to total derivatives. The exact algebraic identity \(X=2\mathcal T+\tfrac32S_{\lambda\mu\nu}S^{\lambda\mu\nu}+2V_\mu V^\mu\) of the irreducible torsion parts then shows that, with \(V_\mu V^\mu\) replaced by its class value \(\lvert\partial\ln\rho\rvert^2\), the non-invariance of \(\kappa_X\rho^2X\) is the axial term \(\tfrac32\kappa_X\rho^2S^2\) (the difference \(2(V_\mu V^\mu-\lvert\partial\ln\rho\rvert^2)\) vanishes on the class but is not invariant); on generic Poisson-class two-jets its rotation variation is nonzero, and no addition of multiples of \(V_\mu-\partial_\mu\ln\rho\), which vanishes on the class, reaches the invariant span. The frame sector of the induced density is therefore not invariant under local rotations of the \(\Theta\)-built frame unless \(\kappa_X=0\), that is \(n_D=2\) in the weights of the paragraph on fermionic content.

\emph{Dressed density.} Let the Stueckelberg field \(\Lambda\) of Equation~\eqref{eq:dressed-omega} act on the coframe, \(e\to\Lambda e\), with the density \(\kappa_X\rho^2X[\Lambda e]\); this is an input of Assumption~\ref{ass:local-lorentz} and is not derived. The density is then invariant under \(e\to\Lambda'e\), \(\Lambda\to\Lambda\Lambda'^{-1}\) of the pair, and \(\Lambda\) is treated as a field fixed by its own stationarity at the \(\Theta\)-built frame \(e\). In the unitary gauge \(\Lambda=\mathbb I\) with \(e\) that frame the density is the undressed one and \(r\) stays undetermined, as in the preceding paragraph. About constant \(\bar\Theta\), for plane-wave perturbations of the Poisson class (random constant \(\bar\Theta\) and wave vectors, both signatures), the stationary point has \(S=0\), leaves \(\mathcal T\) and \(\lvert\partial\ln\rho\rvert^2\) unchanged, and replaces \(\rho^2X\) by \(2\mathcal T+2V_\mu V^\mu\): one half of the on-class value on the two modes with \(\delta\rho=0\) (ratio \(0.5\) to \(10^{-12}\)). The reason is that \(S\) is annihilated by the wedge with the wave vector while the rotations shift it by the full image of that wedge. The Hessian over the twelve real rotation amplitudes is positive definite for \(\eta=\delta\) and indefinite, with six positive and six negative eigenvalues among twelve real parameters, in the Lorentzian cases tested. On the family \(\Theta^{12}=1/f\), \(\Theta^{34}=1\) the axial torsion vanishes pointwise, \(X=2\lvert\partial\ln\rho\rvert^2_G\), and the rotation equation holds identically (\(n=0.8,1.2\)), so the family lies on the stationary set without extremisation.

\emph{Noether coefficient.} At the stationary points above, where \(S=0\) and \(V_\mu=\partial_\mu\ln\rho\), the density depends on \(e\) only through \(G\) and \(\rho\), and \(\mathcal T\simeq-R\) up to a total derivative, so \(\rho^2X\to-2R+2V_\mu V^\mu\): the joint flow whose internal part is a local symmetry selects \(r=-2\), and \(c_W=N_{\mathrm{tot}}/6-2\kappa_X\) equals the tensor coefficient \(c_T^{\mathrm{ext}}=N_{\mathrm{tot}}/6-2\kappa_X\) of the dressed form. The value \(r=-4\) is the on-class tensor coefficient with \(S^2\) retained, for which the compensating rotation is not a symmetry and the construction of the preceding paragraph is not available; any value of \(r\) other than \(-2\) corresponds to a different choice of the non-invariant remainder, a choice that the symmetry does not make. Hence \(c_A-c_T^{\mathrm{ext}}=2\kappa_X\) for the fixed-state replica at \(n_D=0\), and \(c_A-c_T^{\mathrm{ext}}=2\) for the antiperiodic Dirac gluing at every \(N_s\) and \(n_D\), smaller by \(2\kappa_X\) than the value \(4-n_D\) before the dressing, that is half of it at \(n_D=0\) and unchanged at \(n_D=2\); with the vector holonomy contact \(-\alpha_v\sigma\), \(\alpha_v=1\), the residue is \(2-\alpha_v\sigma\), that is \(1\) on \(n<1\) and \(3\) on \(n>1\). A contact of modulus \(2\) would close it; no such contact is derived here.

These statements are for the Poisson-limit density, the \(\LUV^2\) term and the weights above, quadratic order about constant \(\bar\Theta\) for the plane-wave class, the stationary set of the rotation parameters (tree level), and the one family named; they do not derive the Stueckelberg field or the local rotation symmetry from the matrix model, the independent diagonal connection \(B\) or a covariant bundle connection for the fluctuation operator, the stationary set beyond the cone family, the loop-level treatment of \(\Lambda\), the entropy of the rigid compensating rotation, the replica or the holonomy selected by the matrix state, or a value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). Problems~B and~D remain open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\paragraph{Moser variables: the covariant form of the connection sector on the stationary set and an identity between curvature and gradient invariants (conditional; not closure).}
Keep the setting of the two preceding paragraphs: four matrix coordinates, the Poisson limit, \(\omega=\Theta^{-1}\) closed, \(e^A{}_\mu=\eta^{AB}\omega_{\mu B}\), \(\rho=\lvert\operatorname{Pf}\omega\rvert\), \(G^{\mu\nu}=\eta_{\alpha\beta}\Theta^{\alpha\mu}\Theta^{\beta\nu}\), \(V_\mu=\partial_\mu\ln\rho\), the vector operator \(\mathcal O_v\) in the background gauge with \(\alpha=1\), and the tree-level equation \(\mathfrak J^\nu=\Box_Gx^\nu-G^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\ln\rho=\rho^{-1}\partial_\mu(\rho G^{\mu\nu})=0\). Write \(s_\eta=+1\) for \(\eta=\delta\) and \(s_\eta=-1\) for \(\eta=\operatorname{diag}(-1,1,1,1)\).

\emph{The composite connection.} Let \(w_{c|ab}\) be the components along \(e_c\) of the Levi-Civita spin connection of \(e\), and \(B_{c|ab}=-T_{c|ab}\) the composite connection of \(\mathcal O_v\) in the frame components of the torsion. Then \(B_{c|ab}=w_{a|cb}-w_{b|ca}\) for every coframe: the form index and one frame index of the spin connection are exchanged (this is \(\mathrm de^A=-w^A{}_B\wedge e^B\); checked against \(w\) computed from the metric at random jets, residual \(10^{-15}\), both signatures). Under \(e\to(1+\epsilon\lambda)e\) with \(\lambda_{AB}=-\lambda_{BA}\) the inhomogeneous part of \(w_{c|ab}\) is \(\partial_c\lambda_{ab}\) (up to a convention-dependent sign) and that of \(B_{c|ab}\) is the exchanged expression \(\partial_a\lambda_{cb}-\partial_b\lambda_{ca}\) with the same sign: \(B\) is not a connection for local rotations of the matrix index. A local similarity \(\mathcal O\mapsto S\mathcal OS^{-1}\), \(S(x)\) invertible, leaves the diagonal of the heat kernel, hence \(a_1\) pointwise, unchanged (checked, \(10^{-14}\)), so no Stueckelberg field acting on the index by such a similarity changes a local heat-kernel invariant of \(\mathcal O_v\). The dressing of the preceding paragraph replaces \(B\) by \(B[\Lambda e]\) and is not a similarity. About the sampled generic constant \(\bar\omega\) (twelve draws, both signatures) the only first-order rotation that keeps \(\eta_{AB}e^B{}_\mu\) antisymmetric, a necessary condition for \(e\) to come from a two-form, is \(\lambda=0\); the Goldstone-extremised rotation of the preceding paragraph therefore does not satisfy this condition at those backgrounds. This was not tested for (anti-)self-dual \(\bar\omega\).

\emph{Moser variables.} For embedding functions \(X\) of a fixed symplectic leaf, \(S[X]=\int\mathrm d^4y\,L(\omega_X)\) with \(L(\omega)=\rho\,\eta_{ac}\eta_{bd}\Theta^{ab}\Theta^{cd}\) and \(\omega_X\) the push-forward of the leaf form. A variation \(a^\mu\) of \(X\) changes \(\omega_X\) by \(\mathrm dA\), \(A_\nu=a^\mu\omega_{\mu\nu}\) to first order, and \(L\) is an algebraic function of the six components of \(\omega\). The second-order term of \(S\) is therefore half the Hessian of \(L\) along \(\mathrm dA\) plus a term proportional to \(\mathfrak J\). That Hessian equals \(2\mathsf M_G+b\,\mathsf E\) at six sampled rational points, in exact arithmetic and for both signatures (not proved for general \(\omega\)): \(\mathsf M_G\) is the quadratic form \(\rho\,G^{\mu\rho}G^{\nu\sigma}F_{\mu\nu}F_{\rho\sigma}\), \(\mathsf E\) the quadratic form \(2\,\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}F_{\mu\nu}F_{\rho\sigma}\) with \(\epsilon^{0123}=1\), and \(b=-\operatorname{sgn}(\operatorname{Pf}\Theta)\,\eta_{ac}\eta_{bd}\Theta^{ab}\Theta^{cd}/8\), so that the quadratic action is \(\rho\,G^{\mu\rho}G^{\nu\sigma}F_{\mu\nu}F_{\rho\sigma}+b\,\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}F_{\mu\nu}F_{\rho\sigma}\) up to the term proportional to \(\mathfrak J\). Apart from that term, the connection sector is a Maxwell field of the metric \(G\) with weight \(\rho\), coupled to the pseudoscalar \(\vartheta=\rho b\) through \(b\,\epsilon FF\); the variables \((G,\rho,\vartheta)\) are not the variables \((G,\rho,b)\) of the paragraph on the order \(\Lambda^2\), and the axial torsion is not the dual of \(\partial\vartheta\) (tested, residual \(0.3\)-\(1.0\)); the gauge function \(D_\mu a^\mu\) equals \(-\rho^{-1}\partial_\nu(\rho G^{\nu\lambda}A_\lambda)\). At sampled jets with \(\mathfrak J=0\) and \(\partial\mathfrak J=0\) (numerical: six draws, both signatures, first-order coefficients to \(10^{-15}\), both vanishing at zeroth order) the operator \(S\mathcal O_vS^{-1}\), \((Sa)_\nu=a^\mu\omega_{\mu\nu}\), has the same principal, first-order and zeroth-order coefficients as the Maxwell--axion operator in the \(\rho\)-weighted Lorenz gauge; along a one-parameter family leaving \(\mathfrak J=0\) the first- and zeroth-order differences grow linearly with the parameter. In these variables the field has no frame index: the bundle is the cotangent bundle with the Levi-Civita connection of \(G\), and \(\ln\rho\) and \(\vartheta\) enter as tensor couplings. This is covariance of the operator under changes of the coordinates \(y\) when \(G\), \(\rho\) and \(\vartheta\) are treated as tensor fields. These fields are built from \(\omega\) with the constant \(\eta\) contracted with the matrix index, which is the coordinate index, so it is not a symmetry of the matrix action under independent chart diffeomorphisms, and it is not a local Lorentz symmetry of the matrix index.

\emph{Heat-kernel coefficient.} For the Maxwell--axion operator the coefficient \(a_1\), in the normalisation in which \(\sqrt{\lvert G\rvert}\,a_1(\mathcal O_v)=\tfrac46\rho R[g_\rho]+\rho^2X\), is
\[
  a_1=-\tfrac13R_G+\Box_G\ln\rho-\lvert V\rvert_G^2+6s_\eta\,\lvert\partial\vartheta-\vartheta V\rvert_G^2
\]
(the Hodge operator with the dilaton term \(V\) and the axion term; the dilaton part is derived analytically, and the axion term is checked at generic jets, ratio \(1\) to six digits, both signatures). On fourteen random jets per signature with \(\mathfrak J=\partial\mathfrak J=0\) it equals \(a_1(\mathcal O_v)\) to \(3\times10^{-11}\), and \(\sqrt{\lvert G\rvert}\,a_1(\mathcal O_v)=\tfrac46\rho R[g_\rho]+\rho^2X\). With \(\rho R[g_\rho]=\rho^2(R_G+3\Box_G\ln\rho-\tfrac32\lvert V\rvert_G^2)\) this gives, on those jets,
\[
  X=-R_G-\Box_G\ln\rho+6s_\eta\,\lvert\partial\vartheta-\vartheta V\rvert_G^2 .
\]
On the same jets the six invariants satisfy one linear relation to numerical accuracy (rank five of six),
\[
  s_\eta\,\lvert\partial\vartheta-\vartheta V\rvert_G^2=-\tfrac12R_G+\lvert V\rvert_G^2-\tfrac32\Box_G\ln\rho
\]
(residual \(6\times10^{-12}\); numerical, not proved), so that on those jets also \(X=-4R_G+6\lvert V\rvert_G^2-10\Box_G\ln\rho\).

\emph{Consequence for the Noether coefficient.} With \(c_W=N_{\mathrm{tot}}/6+\kappa_Xr\) as in the paragraph on the order \(\Lambda^2\), the coefficient of \(R_G\) in \(X\) is \(r=-1\) when the gradient of \(\vartheta\) is kept as an independent invariant and \(r=-4\) when it is eliminated by the identity above. The value \(r=-4\) is the on-mode value of the normalisation paragraph; the value \(r=-2\) of the preceding paragraph, which rests on the stipulated dressing, corresponds to neither. For \(n_D=0\) the residue of the fixed-state replica is \(c_A-c_W=-r\), that is \(1\) or \(4\), to be compared with the vector holonomy contact of modulus \(1\) of the paragraph on the order \(\Lambda^2\). Covariance of the density therefore does not fix \(r\): it leaves two representations related by one identity of the stationary set, and the choice between them is the choice of which of \(G\), \(\rho\), \(\vartheta\) are held fixed under the variation of the first law; in the class all three are functions of \(\Theta\). On the two modes with \(\delta\rho=0\) the term in \(\lvert\partial\vartheta-\vartheta V\rvert^2\) and the part of \(a_1(\mathcal O_v)-a_1\) proportional to \(\mathfrak J\) are each mode dependent and not proportional to the tree-level kernel; their sum is, and gives the value \(\rho^2X\to1\) in the units of that paragraph.

These statements are for the Poisson limit, four matrix coordinates, \(\alpha=1\), one loop, the density \(a_1\), points with \(\mathfrak J=\partial\mathfrak J=0\) for the operator identity (numerical, at sampled jets), and the covariant formula for \(a_1\). The Hessian, operator and stationary-set identities are numerical at the sampled points stated. They do not cover the Dirac sector for \(n_D>0\), whose X-term enters with its own coefficient and whose Dirac operator \(\gamma^\mu D_\mu\), the Poisson limit of \(\Gamma^\mu[\hat X_\mu,\Psi]\), is a frame derivative without a spin-connection term and is not shown to have a covariant form; the terms proportional to \(\mathfrak J\) off the stationary set; the replica, the tip domain or the choice of independent fields in the first law; the gauge parameters other than \(\alpha=1\); more than four matrix coordinates; or a value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). They derive no local Lorentz symmetry. Problems~B and~D remain open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\paragraph{Covariant variables, the joint flow and the Noether charge of \(\rho^2X\) on the bifurcation surface (conditional; not closure).}
Keep the setting of the paragraph on Moser variables and of the paragraph on the order \(\Lambda^2\), with \(n_D=0\): four matrix coordinates, the Poisson limit, \(\omega=\Theta^{-1}\) closed, the density \(\tfrac{N_{\mathrm{tot}}}6\rho R[g_\rho]+\kappa_X\rho^2X\) with \(X=T_{\lambda\mu\nu}T^{\lambda\mu\nu}\), and \(c_W(r)=N_{\mathrm{tot}}/6+\kappa_Xr\). Let \(\xi\) be the rotation of the \((x^1,x^2)\) plane (the boost for \(\eta=\operatorname{diag}(-1,1,1,1)\), with \(x^1\) the time coordinate), \(\Sigma=\{x^1=x^2=0\}\), and \(B^a{}_b=\partial_b\xi^a\).

\emph{Covariant variables.} Let \(X^a\) be the matrix coordinates as functions on the leaf and \(\gamma_{\mu\nu}=\eta_{ab}\partial_\mu X^a\partial_\nu X^b\), so that \(\gamma=\eta\) in the matrix chart and the curvature \(R[\gamma]\) vanishes. The Poisson-limit operators are built from \(\{X^a,\cdot\}=\Theta^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu X^a\partial_\nu\) and from contractions of matrix indices with \(\eta\), so their local invariants are unchanged by the global translations and \(\eta\)-rotations of the \(X^a\); since a local diffeomorphism onto flat space is fixed by \(\gamma\) up to such an isometry, they are functionals of \((\gamma,\omega)\) covariant under changes of chart, with \(e^\alpha{}_\mu=\gamma^{\alpha\beta}\omega_{\mu\beta}\), \(G=\omega\gamma^{-1}\omega^T\), \(\rho=\lvert\operatorname{Pf}\omega\rvert/\sqrt{\lvert\det\gamma\rvert}\) and the torsion of \(e\) taken with the Levi-Civita connection of \(\gamma\). Densities carry the factor \(\sqrt{\lvert\gamma\rvert}\), which is \(1\) in the matrix chart, so that \(\sqrt G=\rho^2\sqrt{\lvert\gamma\rvert}\). The invariants \(X\), \(T_{\lambda\mu\nu}T^{\mu\lambda\nu}\), \(V_\mu V^\mu\), \(\mathcal T\), \(\nabla_\mu V^\mu\) and \(R_G\), computed in a random polynomial chart with \(\gamma\) the pull-back of \(\eta\), equal those of the matrix chart (exact arithmetic, one closed \(\omega\) and one chart per signature). The field \(\gamma\) is the contraction \(\eta\) of the matrix action written in another chart; it is not the metric \(G\) and adds no field. In this sense the dependence on \(\eta\) noted in the paragraph on Moser variables is tensorial.

\emph{Joint flow.} With both indices transformed, \(\mathcal L_\xi\omega=0\) holds for the family \(\Theta^{12}=1/f(r)\), \(\Theta^{34}=1\) and for constant \(\bar\omega\) in the span of \(e_{12}\) and \(e_{34}\), and fails for a generic constant \(\bar\omega\) (symbolic, both signatures); this is the invariance under the joint flow of the paragraph on the order \(\Lambda^2\). Then \(\mathcal L_\xi\gamma=0\), the fields \(e\), \(G\), \(\rho\) and \(\vartheta\) are Lie-dragged and regular, and \([e,B]=0\) on \(\Sigma\). The matrix coordinates are not Lie-dragged: \(\mathcal L_\xi X^a=B^a{}_bX^b\), the rigid rotation of the matrix index. In the variables \((\gamma,\omega)\) the hypothesis \(\mathcal L_\xi\phi=0\) of~\cite{IyerWald1994} holds; in the variables \((X^a,\omega)\) it fails by a global, not a local, transformation.

\emph{Charges on \(\Sigma\).} For a density depending on \((\gamma,\omega)\) and their first derivatives the Noether current contains at most first derivatives of \(\xi\), so its charge on \(\Sigma\), where \(\xi=0\), vanishes up to an exact form; this covers \(\rho^2X\), \(\rho^2\mathcal T\) and \(\rho^2V_\mu V^\mu\), and \(\rho^2\nabla_\mu V^\mu\) is a divergence. For a density \(\sqrt G\,\ell(R)\), with \(R\) the curvature of a torsion-free connection, the charge on \(\Sigma\) is \(2P_\alpha{}^{\beta\mu\nu}B^\alpha{}_\beta\) with \(P=\partial(\sqrt G\,\ell)/\partial R^\alpha{}_{\beta\mu\nu}\), whatever the connection, since \(\xi=0\) there. At random jets of a non-flat \(\gamma\) (exact arithmetic, three per signature)
\[
  R_G+\mathcal T+2\nabla_\mu V^\mu=R_W:=G^{\sigma\nu}(e^{-1})^\mu{}_\alpha\,R[\gamma]^\alpha{}_{\beta\mu\nu}\,e^\beta{}_\sigma ,
\]
the curvature decomposition of the Weitzenb\"ock connection of \(e\); for flat \(\gamma\) this is the identity \(R=-\mathcal T-2\nabla_\mu V^\mu\) of the paragraph on the order \(\Lambda^2\). With \([e,B]=0\) the charges of \(\sqrt G\,R_W\) and \(\sqrt G\,R_G\) on \(\Sigma\) coincide pointwise. Hence, for every real \(r\), the density \(L_r=L_0+r\kappa_X\sqrt G\,R_W\), with \(L_0\) the induced density in which \(X\) is the torsion invariant, equals \(L_0\) on the class, and its charge on \(\Sigma\) has the coefficient \(c_W(r)\). The density \(L_0\) gives \(r=0\), the coefficient \(c_A=N_{\mathrm{tot}}/6\) of the fixed-state replica, on which \(\rho^2X\) is bounded.

\emph{Compensation.} A term \(f^{abcd}R[\gamma]_{abcd}\) shifts the response \(T_\eta^{bd}\) of the density to \(\gamma\) at \(\gamma=\eta\) by \(E^{bd}=-2\partial_a\partial_cf^{abcd}\), which is identically conserved. For a Killing field \(\xi\) of \(\eta\), \(\partial_bQ^{ab}=2\xi_cE^{ac}\) with \(Q^{ab}=-2f^{abcd}\partial_c\xi_d-4\partial_df^{abcd}\xi_c\) (exact polynomial identity at sampled \(f\) with the symmetries of a curvature tensor, both signatures), and \(Q^{ab}=-2f^{abcd}\partial_c\xi_d\) on \(\Sigma\) is the charge of the added term there. By Stokes' theorem on the Cauchy surface of the wedge, the boost charge of the shift of \(T_\eta\) equals the shift of the charge on \(\Sigma\) minus the flux of \(Q^{ab}\) at spatial infinity. For variations of \(f\) of compact support the first-order change of the charge on \(\Sigma\) is therefore the boost charge of the change of \(T_\eta\), and the relation of~\cite{IyerWald1994} between the charge on \(\Sigma\) and the boost charge of \(T_\eta\), with its term at infinity, holds for every \(r\); for constant \(f\) the improvement vanishes and the charge on \(\Sigma\) is carried by that term. In the variables \((X^a,\omega)\) this freedom corresponds to the ambiguity \(\Theta\to\Theta+\mathrm dY\) of the symplectic potential, which leaves the charge on \(\Sigma\) unchanged only when \(\mathcal L_\xi\phi=0\)~\cite{JacobsonKangMyers1994}; here \(Y(\phi,\mathcal L_\xi X)\) with \(\mathcal L_\xi X^a=B^a{}_bX^b\) does not drop out.

\emph{Consequence.} Within these hypotheses the Iyer--Wald identity for the induced density does not select \(r\); the response \(T_\eta\) to \(\gamma\) is not identified here with \(T^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mu\nu}\) of Equation~\eqref{eq:first-law}. Each of the coefficients \(c_W(-1)\) and \(c_W(-4)\) of the paragraph on Moser variables, \(c_W(-2)\) of the paragraph on local frame rotations and \(c_W(0)=c_A\) is the charge on \(\Sigma\) of a density \(L_r\) equal to \(L_0\) on the class, and their differences are edge terms of the boost generator of \(T_\eta\), of the type of the superpotential of Equation~\eqref{eq:Top-FP}. For \(n_D=0\) the fixed-state replica has \(c_A-c_T=4\kappa_X\), so the requirement \(G_A=G_T\) of the paragraph on the normalisation holds if and only if the edge term that accompanies the replica contributes the coefficient of \(r=-4\), that is \(-4\kappa_X\) in units of \(c\) relative to \(L_0\). It is a property of the modular Hamiltonian of the state at \(\Sigma\), or of a local symmetry under which the compensating rotation would be a gauge transformation, and not of the induced density. The two readings of the paragraph on the comparator are related in the same way: a density of the bundle curvature, taken as a function of the curvature of the connection \(\Gamma[\gamma]-\tfrac12V\,\mathbb I+b\) with the fibre index identified with a tangent index by \(\partial X\), has the charge \(2(\partial L/\partial\Omega^\alpha{}_{\beta\mu\nu})B^\alpha{}_\beta\) on \(\Sigma\), the reading with \(H=\mathcal J\); taken as a function of \(\Omega-R[\gamma]\) it has none, the other reading; the two differ by a term of the form \(f^{abcd}R[\gamma]_{abcd}\). For the constraint \(b=F[\Theta]\), with \(F\) the covariantised composite built from the first jets of \((\gamma,\omega)\), a multiplier term depends on first derivatives of \((\gamma,\omega)\) only and has no charge on \(\Sigma\); this is the charge that the paragraphs on the comparator and on the order \(\Lambda^2\) list as not computed, and it is computed only in this setting.

These statements are for the Poisson limit, four matrix coordinates, the bosonic sectors with \(n_D=0\), the \(\LUV^2\) density, backgrounds Lie-dragged by the joint flow and regular on \(\Sigma\), and local charges; the identities are exact at the sampled jets and polynomials stated. They do not determine the edge term, the modular Hamiltonian, the replica or the Dirac sector, do not identify \(T_\eta\) with \(T^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mu\nu}\) of Equation~\eqref{eq:first-law}, use no metric other than the pull-back of the matrix contraction \(\eta\), and give no value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). Problems~B and~D remain open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\paragraph{Matrix gauge realisation of the compensating rotation, the superpotential of the stress and the edge term (conditional; not closure).}
Keep the setting of the preceding paragraph: four matrix coordinates, the Poisson limit, \(\omega=\Theta^{-1}\) closed, \(n_D=0\), the \(\LUV^2\) density, \(c_W(r)=N_{\mathrm{tot}}/6+\kappa_Xr\), \(\xi\) the rotation (boost) of the normal plane of \(\Sigma\), \(B^a{}_b=\partial_b\xi^a\), and \(\{f,g\}=\Theta^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu f\,\partial_\nu g\).

\emph{Gauge realisation.} If \(\mathcal L_\xi\omega=0\) on a star-shaped domain, then \(\iota_\xi\omega\) is closed and \(\iota_\xi\omega=\mathrm d\varepsilon\) with \(\varepsilon=0\) on \(\Sigma\); there \(\mathrm d\varepsilon=0\) and the Hessian of \(\varepsilon\) is \(B^{T}\omega\neq0\), and \(\{\varepsilon,f\}=\xi^\mu\partial_\mu f\) for every function \(f\). In the matrix chart \(\{\varepsilon,X^a\}=B^a{}_bX^b\): the rigid rotation of the preceding paragraph is the Poisson-limit matrix gauge transformation generated by \(\varepsilon\), for every background invariant under the joint flow and in both signatures (Poincar\'e lemma; exact checks for \(\omega=\bar\omega+\mathrm dA\) with \(A\) a random polynomial invariant one-form). For constant \(\bar\omega\) this is the Poisson limit of the quadratic generator of the constant stabiliser in Section~\ref{sec:status-D}. It follows from the fact that in the Poisson limit gauge transformations act on the \(X^a\) as diffeomorphisms preserving \(\omega\); it adds no symmetry and is an isotropy of the background, not a local rotation of the matrix index.

\emph{Gauge-covariant charge.} With the \(X^a\) as fields on the leaf and \(\omega\) fixed, \(\delta_\varepsilon X^a=\mathcal L_\zeta X^a\) with \(\zeta\) the Hamiltonian field of \(\varepsilon\), so the Noether current and charge of \(\delta_\varepsilon\) are those of the diffeomorphism \(\zeta\), with the same ambiguities, and here \(\zeta=\xi\). The combination of \(\xi\) with the compensating transformation \(-\varepsilon\), the analogue of the construction of~\cite{JacobsonMohd2015}, leaves every field invariant and its charge vanishes identically. The charge of \(\delta_\varepsilon\) alone equals that of \(\xi\): on \(\Sigma\) it depends on the second derivatives of \(\varepsilon\) and is nonzero for a curvature density, although \(\varepsilon\) and \(\mathrm d\varepsilon\) vanish there. In the variables \(\Theta(x)\) of the matrix chart, which these transformations leave invariant, the remaining freedom is that of a potential \(A\) of \(\omega=\bar\omega+\mathrm dA\); the compensating parameter \(\iota_\xi A+\varepsilon-\bar\varepsilon\), \(\mathrm d\bar\varepsilon=\iota_\xi\bar\omega\), vanishes on \(\Sigma\), and so does its charge when \(A\) enters only through \(\omega\), leaving the charge of the preceding paragraph. In both readings the term \(Y(\phi,\mathcal L_\xi X)=Y(\phi,\delta_\varepsilon X)\) is unchanged and \(r\) is not selected.

\emph{Superpotential of the stress.} On a flat background the identically conserved symmetric tensors linear in \(h\) with two derivatives are the combinations of the linearised Einstein tensor and \((\partial_\mu\partial_\nu-\eta_{\mu\nu}\Box)h^\alpha{}_\alpha\), and the divergence \(\partial^\rho\Sigma_{\rho\mu\nu}\) of every \(\Sigma\) linear in \(\partial h\), antisymmetric in \((\rho,\mu)\) and with symmetric divergence, is such a combination (exact classification, both signatures). Each is \(\partial^\rho\partial^\sigma U_{\mu\rho\nu\sigma}\) with \(U\) linear in \(h\) and with the symmetries of a curvature tensor. For a Killing field \(\xi\) the current \(\xi^\nu\partial^\rho\partial^\sigma U_{\mu\rho\nu\sigma}\) is the divergence of an antisymmetric tensor that reduces on \(\Sigma\) to \(\partial^\rho\xi^\nu U_{\mu\rho\nu\sigma}\); for the conformal Killing field of a diamond a bulk term in \(\partial\partial\xi\) remains (exact checks). On traceless \(T_{\mu\nu}\) the superpotential term of Equation~\eqref{eq:Top-FP} is therefore \(\lambda\,\mathcal E_{\mu\nu}[T]\): its edge contribution changes the coefficient of \(\mathcal E\) and is not independent of it. For \(T^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mu\nu}\) defined as the response of the induced quadratic action to \(G\) on the two modes with \(\delta\rho=0\), the Euler operator annihilates divergences and the coefficient is \(c_T=c_W(-4)\), so the edge term carried by \(T^{\mathrm{op}}_{\mu\nu}\) so defined is that of \(r=-4\), which restates the definition of \(r=-4\) by agreement on those modes. With the area coefficient \(c_A=c_W(0)\) of the fixed-state replica, the requirement \(G_A=G_T\) of the paragraph on the normalisation then needs the further term \(-4\kappa_X\) from the modular Hamiltonian of the state; the stress, so defined, does not supply it.

\emph{Edge terms of the fluctuation Lagrangians.} For a thermal state the modular Hamiltonian is the integrated Noether current of the quantum fields plus a term at the outer boundary; relative to the integral of the stress tensor it differs on \(\Sigma\) by the Noether charge of the matter Lagrangian, with the ambiguities of that charge set to zero, and by a contribution of the background dependence of the path-integral measure~\cite{Chen2022}. The tree-level fluctuation Lagrangians of the Poisson-limit paragraphs depend on the background through \((\gamma,\omega)\) and their first derivatives only, so their Noether charge on \(\Sigma\) vanishes, by the argument of the preceding paragraph, for fluctuations regular there. The measure contribution is defined there through a deformation localised near \(\Sigma\) that multiplies the opening angle by \(n\); about constant \(\bar\Theta\), with \(\Theta\) the only field, a localised first-order perturbation changes no opening angle, because the integral of \(R_G^{(1)}\) over the normal plane is \(-2\) times the flux of \(\nabla\ln\rho\) (paragraph on replica prescriptions), so that construction is not available in this class. In the Moser variables the linearised gauge transformation \(\delta a^\mu=\Theta^{\mu\rho}\partial_\rho\lambda\) of Remark~\ref{rem:ch3-trace-quadratic} acts as \(\delta A=-\mathrm d\lambda\) on \(A_\nu=a^\mu\omega_{\mu\nu}\), and on the stationary set the connection operator is the Maxwell--axion operator of the paragraph on Moser variables, whose curvature coupling is the Ricci term of the Hodge operator of \(G\), so the \(U(1)\) structure and the Ricci coupling on which the Kabat value \(-1\) of the vector contact rests are present there. If the state carries that edge sector, the edge-mode term of~\cite{DonnellyWall2016} for a Maxwell field, the area coefficient becomes \(c_A-1=c_W(-1)\) and \(3\kappa_X\) remains: the part that the stationary-set identity of the paragraph on Moser variables assigns to the gradient of \(\vartheta\) when that gradient is kept as an independent invariant, a first-order invariant with no charge on \(\Sigma\) in that presentation; in the presentation that eliminates it by the identity this part is carried by the charge of \(R_G\) with \(r=-4\), and the density does not select between the two.

These statements are for the Poisson limit, four matrix coordinates, \(n_D=0\), the \(\LUV^2\) density, backgrounds invariant under the joint flow and regular on \(\Sigma\), local charges, linear order about a flat background for the classification, and the cited construction as stated; the checks are exact at the sampled backgrounds, and the stationary-set identity used is numerical. They do not determine the edge term of the matrix state, its modular Hamiltonian or the replica, do not treat Moyal corrections, under which gauge transformations with non-quadratic generators are not diffeomorphisms, more than four matrix coordinates, the Dirac sector or the measure of the matrix integral, and derive no local Lorentz symmetry. Problems~B and~D remain open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\section{Problem C: conformal mode and Cassini}
\label{sec:status-C}

\paragraph{Ledger label.}
Status: OPEN. Priority: CRITICAL PATH. Completion:
PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL. A scalar-tensor result is a reduced-scope
outcome only when its operator, coupling and phenomenological test
are supplied; it does not by itself meet the matrix-derived criterion.

The radial singlet has the tree-level mass
\[
m_\sigma^2=\lambda v^2.
\]
This does not by itself determine the mass or matter coupling of a
scalar mode of the composite metric. In particular, the stated
Repair~I prescription is intended to build a vierbein from the Lorentz-valued
polar factor \(\Lambda\), evaluated at \(\sigma=0\), rather than from
the radial field.

\paragraph{Obstruction to the stated Repair I frame.}
Suppose \(\Lambda(x)\in\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\) and expand about the locked
configuration,
\[
\Lambda=\mathbb I+\Omega+O(\Omega^2),
\qquad
\Omega_{\mu\nu}=-\Omega_{\nu\mu}.
\]
Then \(\partial_\mu\Omega_{\alpha\beta}\) transforms in
\(V\otimes\mathfrak{so}(3,1)\), where \(V=(\tfrac12,\tfrac12)\)
is the Lorentz vector representation. After complexification,
\[
\bigl(V\otimes\mathfrak{so}(3,1)\bigr)_{\mathbb C}
=
\left(\tfrac32,\tfrac12\right)
\oplus\left(\tfrac12,\tfrac12\right)
\oplus\left(\tfrac12,\tfrac32\right)
\oplus\left(\tfrac12,\tfrac12\right).
\]
A vierbein fluctuation transforms in \(V\otimes V\), whose
complexified decomposition is
\[
(V\otimes V)_{\mathbb C}
=
(0,0)\oplus(1,0)\oplus(0,1)\oplus(1,1).
\]
These representations have no common irreducible summand. Hence
there is no nonzero Lorentz-equivariant linear map from
\(\partial_\mu\Omega_{\alpha\beta}\) to a vierbein fluctuation using
only the locked Lorentz-invariant tensors. Consistently, the displayed
formula
\[
e^A{}_\mu=\left\langle\partial_\mu\Lambda^A{}_I\right\rangle
\]
leaves \(I\) uncontracted and does not, as written, define a vierbein.

A simple proposed contraction with a fixed nonzero internal vector
\(n^I\) does not repair non-degeneracy. If
\[
e^A{}_\mu=\partial_\mu\Lambda^A{}_I\,n^I,
\]
then Lorentz orthogonality gives
\[
(\Lambda n)_A e^A{}_\mu
=
(\Lambda n)_A\partial_\mu(\Lambda n)^A
=
\frac12\partial_\mu\bigl((\Lambda n)^2\bigr)
=0.
\]
Every column of \(e^A{}_\mu\) therefore lies in the
three-dimensional subspace orthogonal to \(\Lambda n\), so
\(\operatorname{rank}(e)\leq 3\). This contraction cannot produce a
non-degenerate four-dimensional frame.

\paragraph{Status.}
Under the stated Repair~I ingredients, the linearized composite
vierbein required for the later metric-scalar analysis is not
constructed. This is an obstruction to that frame prescription, not
a proof that a completed theory has or lacks a physical scalar pole.
Repair~II, an independent coframe, or additional symmetry-breaking
background tensors would change the construction and require a
separate analysis. The radial mass \(m_\sigma^2=\lambda v^2\) remains
established, but it is not a metric-scalar mass. The scalar spectrum
and matter coupling relevant to Cassini are therefore still
undetermined. The Repair~II pure-trace relation \(\psi=8\sigma/v\)
with \(m_\sigma^2=\lambda v^2\) is a potential-only statement; the
induced Einstein--Hilbert term adds to the kinetic coefficient of
\(\sigma\), so it does not fix a physical pole mass, mixing or
Cassini coupling.

In the Repair~II pure-trace truncation, adding the induced
Einstein--Hilbert term to the microscopic kinetic term gives, in the
healthy-sign normalization,
\(Z=2N-3/(4\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}}v^{2})\)
(Equation~\ref{eq:Z-sigma}), conditional on the sign of
\eqref{eq:Smicro} as corrected in Chapter~3 and on the declared
curvature convention. For \(Z>0\), the truncated pole has
\(m^{2}=V''/Z\); for \(Z<0\) it is ghost-like and, for \(\lambda>0\),
tachyonic in this truncation, which therefore cannot support
Assumption~\ref{ass:gap-psi} there. This signals an instability of the
chosen background and truncation, not a statement about the full scalar
spectrum. At \(Z=0\) the quadratic kinetic
term degenerates and establishes no canonically normalized
finite-mass pole. This result also assumes \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}>0\),
which is not derived (Problem~F), and does not determine mixing, the
non-minimal coupling, the matter coupling, or a Cassini bound.
If matter is assumed to couple only through \(g=\Omega^2\eta\), the
unmixed truncated scalar exchanges, for \(Z>0\), a Yukawa potential of
strength \(\beta=M_{\mathrm P}^{2}/(Nv^{2}-3M_{\mathrm P}^{2})\)
relative to the nominal Einstein-sector Newtonian potential
(Chapter~17); this is conditional on the assumptions listed there and
is not a Cassini bound.

\emph{Off-shell trace--tensor mixing from the induced
Einstein--Hilbert term (conditional; not closure).}
Assume Repair~II, which is assumed rather than derived, with
\(g_{\mu\nu}=\eta_{\mu\nu}+h_{\mu\nu}\) and
\(h_{\mu\nu}=T_{\mu\nu}+\tfrac14\eta_{\mu\nu}\psi\). Also assume the
induced Lorentzian term is
\(S_{\mathrm{EH}}=(16\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}})^{-1}\int\sqrt{-g}\,R\),
with \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}>0\). For a general symmetric \(h_{\mu\nu}\),
define \(\mathcal E_{\mu\nu}[h]:=-2G^{(1)}_{\mu\nu}[h]\); on traceless
tensors this agrees with \(\mathcal E_{\mu\nu}\) of
Equation~\ref{eq:einstein-op}. In these conventions, up to boundary
terms,
\[
  S_{\mathrm{EH}}^{(2)}
  =\frac{1}{16\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}}}
   \int\mathrm{d}^4x\,\frac14 h^{\mu\nu}\mathcal E_{\mu\nu}[h].
\]
For \(s_{\mu\nu}=\tfrac14\eta_{\mu\nu}\psi\),
\[
  \mathcal E_{\mu\nu}[s]
  =\frac12\bigl(\partial_\mu\partial_\nu\psi
                -\eta_{\mu\nu}\Box\psi\bigr).
\]
Expanding the quadratic action in this field basis gives
\[
  S_{\mathrm{EH}}^{(2)}
  =\frac{1}{16\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}}}
   \int\mathrm{d}^4x\left[
     \frac14T^{\mu\nu}\mathcal E_{\mu\nu}[T]
    +\frac14T^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\partial_\nu\psi
    +\frac{3}{32}(\partial\psi)^2
   \right].
\]
Thus, using \(\psi=8\sigma/v\) at linear order, the cross term is
\[
  \frac{1}{16\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}}}
  \int\mathrm{d}^4x\,\frac{2}{v}
  T^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\partial_\nu\sigma .
\]
The tensor-only term here is normalized relative to the assumed
\(S_{\mathrm{EH}}\); Equation~\ref{eq:EH-lin} states a structure and
is not used to set this coefficient. The cross term is
basis-dependent: it is the usual conformal-mode mixing in the
gauge-invariant quadratic Einstein--Hilbert action and can be
rearranged by a field redefinition. It does not by itself establish an
observable effect or scalar-exchange strength. Although it vanishes if
transversality is imposed, transversality cannot be assumed when
forming the coupled off-shell Hessian. The displayed microscopic action
on the fixed scaffold does not specify the full coupled, gauge-fixed
tensor operator or the remaining scalar couplings.
Problems~B, C, D, F and~G remain open.

\section{Problem D: local Lorentz}
\label{sec:status-D}

\paragraph{Ledger label.}
Status: OPEN. Priority: CRITICAL PATH. Completion:
PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL. Reduced-scope result: D2, imposed local
Lorentz symmetry, supports only a scope-limited Presentation-B
model; D remains OPEN and the matrix-derived criterion is unmet.

The counting of generators under the locked vacuum is given in
Chapter~10. The condensate term supplies a quadratic Higgs-type
contribution in the broken-direction combination \(\Xi\); it does not
establish a physical pole mass without kinetic terms and canonical
normalization. The diagonal connection's dynamics and torsion remain
open. Torsion-free behavior follows only under the conditional
Palatini--Holst hypotheses of Proposition~
\ref{prop:B-torsion-free}; NAMCG has not been shown to induce those
hypotheses.

For a Laplace-type operator,
\(a_1=\operatorname{tr}(R(g)/6-E)\); the explicit universal
curvature term is the Levi-Civita scalar \(R(g)\). Because \(E\) is
unspecified, no conclusion about additional \(B\)-dependence of
\(a_1\) follows, and a Palatini \(R(B)\) term is not established. If
\(B\) enters the bundle connection of the actual operator, \(a_2\)
may contain a \(\operatorname{tr}F(B)^2\) term; whether it survives
in the weighted trace depends on the unspecified operator
representation and weights. Deriving the \(B\)-dependence of \(E\)
and the bundle connection remains open.

\paragraph{Missing step.}
Derive \(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\) from the matrix algebra alone, or state
that Presentation~A is not ontologically prior (the present text
does the latter), and derive the effective dynamics of the
independent diagonal connection or retain it as unresolved field
content. Generator counting alone does not settle its torsion or
propagating modes. The locked frame and its non-degeneracy must also be derived rather
than assumed (Assumptions~\ref{ass:frame-lock} and~\ref{ass:nondeg}).
Under D2, only explicitly scope-limited
Presentation-B claims are available; specifying the full operator
content does not close D or meet the matrix-derived criterion. The
permitted and unestablished claims, including the consequent
truncation for Problem~F, are listed in
Section~\ref{sec:ch17-bearing}.

\paragraph{Symmetries supplied by the stated Presentation~A
(matrix-derived criterion of Problem~D; not closure).}
The matrix-coordinate action~\eqref{eq:Smatrix} is invariant under
unitary conjugation
\(\hat X^\mu\mapsto U\hat X^\mu U^\dagger\), provided the matter
variables transform so that their term is invariant: each matrix
commutator transforms by conjugation and the trace is cyclic. This
is a matrix gauge transformation
\(\delta\hat X^\mu=i[\epsilon,\hat X^\mu]\); the stated action and
infrared dictionary do not identify it with a transformation of an
independent tangent frame.

As an action-level statement, the commutator term is covariant under
constant Lorentz transformations of the spacetime index \(\mu\),
because its indices are contracted with \(\eta_{\mu\nu}\). The cubic
term is covariant if \(C_{\mu\nu\lambda}\) transforms as a tensor;
for fixed \(C_{\mu\nu\lambda}\), it is a symmetry only when \(C\) is
invariant. The matter term must obey the corresponding transformation
law. Likewise, the chosen background \(\Theta^{\mu\nu}\) in
Equation~\eqref{eq:NC} is invariant only under its stabilizer if held
fixed; transforming \(\Theta\) along with the fields expresses
covariance, not a symmetry of that fixed background. None of these
constant transformations establishes a spacetime-dependent local
Lorentz gauge symmetry.

On the \(C_{\mu\nu\lambda}=0\), central constant-\(\Theta\) branch of
Remark~\ref{rem:ch3-trace-quadratic}, evaluate the matrix-gauge variation
on the background \(\bar X\). By the Jacobi identity,
\[
  \delta_\epsilon[\bar X^\mu,\bar X^\nu]
  =i[\epsilon,[\bar X^\mu,\bar X^\nu]]=0,
\]
since \([\bar X^\mu,\bar X^\nu]=i\Theta^{\mu\nu}\mathbb I\). In the
irreducible Moyal-symbol realization, the corresponding linearized
fluctuation transforms as
\(\delta a^\mu=\Theta^{\mu\rho}\partial_\rho\lambda\), leaving
\(\mathcal F_\Theta^{\mu\nu}\) invariant as shown in that remark. This
local matrix-gauge statement is distinct from the global constant
Lorentz covariance described in the preceding paragraph, which requires
transforming \(\Theta\) as a tensor and is a symmetry of a fixed
\(\Theta\) only for transformations that stabilize it. On this branch,
matrix conjugation alone supplies neither a transformation of an
independent tangent-frame index nor six independent local Lorentz
parameters. This is a limitation of the stated matrix-gauge action on
the central background, not a no-go for an enlarged matrix model with a
derived frame map and additional fields.

More specifically, on the central, nondegenerate constant-\(\Theta\)
Moyal branch, define
\(D'_A f:=\Lambda_A{}^B\star D_B f\), where
\(D_B=i[\bar X_B,\,\cdot\,]\) are the inner derivations, whose
principal symbols \(\Theta_B{}^\mu\partial_\mu\) are linearly
independent. The Leibniz defect is
\[
  D'_A(f\star g)-(D'_Af)\star g-f\star(D'_Ag)
  =[\Lambda_A{}^B,f]_\star\star D_Bg .
\]
Independence of the principal symbols implies that this defect
vanishes for all \(f,g\) exactly when every \(\Lambda_A{}^B\) is
central. In the irreducible nondegenerate constant-\(\Theta\)
representation, the centre consists of constants, i.e.\ multiples of
the identity. This conclusion is limited to that branch and this
left-multiplication action; it does not rule out a connection-twisted
or module-valued frame, or an enlarged matrix model. A matrix-derived
frame module with a connection that compensates this defect is a
conjectured route, not a result established here. Problems~B, C, D, F
and~G remain open.

For real, nondegenerate constant \(\Theta\), consider
\(\Lambda\in\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\) that preserves the fixed tensor,
\(\Lambda\Theta\Lambda^T=\Theta\). In a Lorentz frame with
\(\Theta^{01}=E\), \(\Theta^{23}=B\), \(EB\ne0\), and all other
independent components zero, its identity-component stabilizer is
\(\mathrm{SO}^+(1,1)\times\mathrm{SO}(2)\): a boost in the
\(01\)-plane and a rotation in the \(23\)-plane. Equivalently, in
the self-dual representation its stabilizer is
\(\mathrm{SO}(2,\mathbb C)\), of real dimension two. This is a
constant-background stabilizer, not a local Lorentz gauge symmetry.

The corresponding linear automorphisms of the Heisenberg algebra
are implementable by inner automorphisms of its irreducible Weyl
representation. Infinitesimally, write \(\Lambda=\mathbb I+A\);
preservation of \(\Theta\) requires
\[
  A\Theta+\Theta A^T=0.
\]
Since \(\Theta\) is invertible,
\[
  H_{\mu\nu}:=(\Theta^{-1})_{\mu\rho}A^\rho{}_\nu
\]
is symmetric. On the common invariant domain of the Weyl
generators, the symmetrically ordered quadratic generator
\[
  G=\frac14 H_{\mu\nu}
    \{\bar X^\mu,\bar X^\nu\}
\]
satisfies
\[
  \delta\bar X^\mu=i[G,\bar X^\mu]
  =A^\mu{}_\nu\bar X^\nu.
\]
The finite implementation is the metaplectic unitary, unique up
to phase by the Stone--von Neumann theorem. This implements only
the constant stabilizer: it supplies neither six arbitrary local
Lorentz parameters nor an independent tangent-frame index or spin
connection. Invariance of the full action also requires the
remaining fields and couplings to transform compatibly. This
conditional representation-theoretic result does not establish
Problem~D1. Problems~B, C, D, F and~G remain open.

A conditional classical-symbol construction is possible if the
background Poisson tensor varies. Let
\(\Theta^{\mu\nu}(x)\) be a real, nondegenerate Poisson tensor,
so its bracket
\[
  \{f,g\}_\Theta
  :=\Theta^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu f\,\partial_\nu g
\]
obeys the Jacobi identity. For local coordinate functions \(x_A\),
with the frame index \(A\) identified with the coordinate label,
define the Hamiltonian vector fields
\(V_A:=X_{x_A}\), where \(X_f(g):=\{f,g\}_\Theta\), and assume
they form an invertible frame. The Poisson Jacobi identity gives
\[
  [V_A,V_B]=X_{\Theta_{AB}},
  \qquad
  \Theta_{AB}:=\{x_A,x_B\}_\Theta .
\]
Define \(C^C{}_{AB}\) by
\([V_A,V_B]=C^C{}_{AB}V_C\). A provisional candidate inverse
metric is
\[
  g_{\mathrm{cand}}^{\mu\nu}
  :=\eta^{AB}V_A{}^\mu V_B{}^\nu .
\]
It is Lorentzian by construction when the frame is invertible, but
is not thereby identified with the emergent metric of
Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}.

Under a local frame change
\(V'_A=\Lambda_A{}^B(x)V_B\), with
\(\Lambda(x)\in\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\), the bracket, expanded in the
original frame \(V_F\), is
\[
  [V'_A,V'_B]
  =
  \left(
    \Lambda_A{}^D\Lambda_B{}^E C^F{}_{DE}
    +\Lambda_A{}^D V_D(\Lambda_B{}^F)
    -\Lambda_B{}^D V_D(\Lambda_A{}^F)
  \right)V_F .
\]
Accordingly, the structure functions of the new frame are the
displayed coefficients contracted with
\((\Lambda^{-1})_F{}^G\), since
\([V'_A,V'_B]=C'^G{}_{AB}V'_G\). For this provisional frame
and metric, the dual coframe \(\vartheta^A\) determines the
composite Levi-Civita spin connection through
\[
  \mathrm{d}\vartheta^A+\omega^A{}_B\wedge\vartheta^B=0,
  \qquad
  \omega_{AB}=-\omega_{BA}.
\]
It transforms inhomogeneously under the local frame change.

For constant \(\Theta\), the fields \(V_A{}^\mu\) are constant,
so \(C^C{}_{AB}=0\) and this composite connection and its
curvature vanish in that frame. A varying \(\Theta\) is therefore
needed for this construction to be nontrivial, although variation
alone does not guarantee nonzero curvature. This is a
classical Poisson-symbol construction: it does not address the
exact Moyal Leibniz defect for noncentral frame coefficients, derive
local frame rotations from the matrix action, or supply independent
connection dynamics. Problems~B, C, D, F and~G remain open.

For the hand-chosen Poisson background of
Remark~\ref{rem:ch3-varying-theta},
\[
  \Theta=a\,\partial_0\wedge\partial_1
         +b(y)\,\partial_2\wedge\partial_3,
  \qquad b(y)=b_0e^{\kappa y^2},
\]
the Hamiltonian frame \(V_A=X_{x_A}\), with
\(X_f(g)=\{f,g\}_\Theta\), is
\[
  (V_0,V_1,V_2,V_3)
  =(a\partial_1,-a\partial_0,b\partial_3,-b\partial_y).
\]
Its only nonzero frame bracket is
\[
  [V_2,V_3]=b'(y)V_2=2\kappa y\,b(y)\,V_2.
\]
The associated structure function is \(C^2{}_{23}=b'(y)\);
the composite connection contains the logarithmic derivative
\[
  \omega^2{}_3=\frac{b'(y)}{b(y)}\,\mathrm{d}x^3
  =2\kappa y\,\mathrm{d}x^3.
\]
With \(\eta_{AB}=\operatorname{diag}(-,+,+,+)\), the provisional
inverse metric constructed from this frame is
\[
  g_{\mathrm{cand}}^{\mu\nu}
  =\eta^{AB}V_A{}^\mu V_B{}^\nu
  =\operatorname{diag}(a^2,-a^2,b(y)^2,b(y)^2)
  =K_\Theta^{\mu\nu}.
\]
It therefore reproduces the tensor used in
Remark~\ref{rem:ch3-varying-theta}; with that remark's
normalization \(K_\Theta^{\mu\nu}=\ell_*^4\tilde g^{\mu\nu}\),
\(g_{\mathrm{cand}}=\ell_*^{-4}\tilde g\).

A dual coframe is
\[
  \vartheta^0=\frac{\mathrm{d}x^1}{a},\qquad
  \vartheta^1=-\frac{\mathrm{d}x^0}{a},\qquad
  \vartheta^2=\frac{\mathrm{d}x^3}{b(y)},\qquad
  \vartheta^3=-\frac{\mathrm{d}y}{b(y)}.
\]
Its torsion-free connection has
\(\omega^2{}_3=2\kappa y\,\mathrm{d}x^3\), and
\(\mathcal R^2{}_3=2\kappa\,\mathrm{d}y\wedge\mathrm{d}x^3\).
In the curvature convention of
Remark~\ref{rem:ch3-varying-theta}, this gives
\(R[g_{\mathrm{cand}}]=4\kappa b(y)^2\).
This identifies the candidate Poisson-frame metric with the
already chosen toy geometry, up to the stated constant
normalization; it is not an independent derivation of that
geometry.

The calculation is at classical Poisson-symbol order on a
hand-chosen background. It derives neither that background from
the matrix action nor local Lorentz gauge symmetry, a
noncommutative frame module, or independent connection dynamics.
Problems~B, C, D, F and~G remain open.

For the Poisson block above, restrict to the
\(C_{\mu\nu\lambda}=0\) branch of~\eqref{eq:Smatrix}, retain only
the quartic matrix term, and omit matter. The matrix equation is
\[
  [\hat X_\nu,[\hat X^\nu,\hat X^\mu]]=0,
\]
with indices contracted using \(\eta\). At leading
Poisson-symbol order, using
\([\hat f,\hat g]\sim i\{f,g\}_\Theta\), this requires
\[
  \eta_{\nu\lambda}\Theta^{\lambda\rho}
  \partial_\rho\Theta^{\nu\mu}=0.
\]
For \(\Theta^{01}=a\), \(\Theta^{23}=b(y)\), with \(y=x^2\),
the \(\mu=3\) component vanishes identically, while the
\(\mu=2\) component is \(b(y)b'(y)\). Nondegeneracy requires
\(b\ne0\), so this leading-order source-free equation forces
\(b'=0\) on each connected patch. Hence the hand-chosen curved
profile \(b=b_0e^{\kappa y^2}\), with \(\kappa\ne0\), is not a
source-free leading-order solution of this ansatz. Supporting that
profile would require a specified balancing contribution, such
as a cubic term, matter source, or corrections from a specified
deformation quantization. This calculation is at classical
Poisson-symbol order only; it does not define a beyond-leading-order
product for varying \(\Theta\), make a claim about the full matrix
model, or exclude other backgrounds. It is not a derivation or
closure of Problem~D1. Problems~B, C, D, F and~G remain open.

In particular, Presentation~A as specified contains no coframe
\(e^A{}_\mu\), independent tangent index \(A\), or spin connection
\(\omega_\mu{}^{AB}\). The infrared dictionary~\eqref{eq:dictionary}
is an assumption mapping a matrix commutator to a covariant derivative
with \(A_\mu\); it supplies neither a local Lorentz transformation
law for a derived frame nor the transformation and dynamics of
\(\omega_\mu{}^{AB}\). The boost obstruction recorded in Problem~F
concerns the displayed Presentation-B potential, not the
Presentation-A matrix action. These facts delimit what the stated
action and dictionary establish; they do not rule out an enlarged
matrix construction. Consistent with the preceding paragraph, the
matrix-derived criterion of Problem~D remains open: closure requires
an explicit matrix-to-frame map and a derivation of the local
transformation law, invariant action, and connection content from
that map. Problems~B, C, D, F and~G remain open.

\paragraph{Linear metric content of a real locked block (conditional; not closure).}
Restrict to a real \(4\times4\) locked block, \(e=\mathbb{I}+M\) with \(M\)
real, and \(g=e^{T}\eta e\) as in~\eqref{eq:g-from-e}. To first order
\(\delta g=M^{T}\eta+\eta M\). For real symmetric \(M=H\):
\(\delta g_{00}=-2H_{00}\), \(\delta g_{ij}=2H_{ij}\), \(\delta g_{0i}=0\).
For real antisymmetric \(M=K\): \(\delta g_{0i}=-2K_{0i}\),
\(\delta g_{ij}=0\). On this real slice the three directions \(K_{0i}\)
change the metric, and the quadratic potential of the preceding Hessian
paragraph is independent of \(K\). (For complex \(\Phi\) the Hermitian and
anti-Hermitian parts of that paragraph are not the real symmetric and
antisymmetric parts used here; the correspondence holds only on the real
slice.)

Internal transformations that preserve the positive-definite contraction
in~\eqref{eq:Smicro} are unitary; those that also map real frames to real
frames are orthogonal; those that further preserve \(\eta_{AB}\) commute with
\(\eta\). Their Lie algebra is \(\mathfrak{so}(3)\): no boost generator
survives (at the group level the intersection is
\(\mathrm{O}(1)\times\mathrm{O}(3)\)). This concerns the internal index only.
For the tangent index, \(\Phi\mapsto\Phi\Lambda\) sends
\(\mathrm{Tr}\,\Phi^\dagger\Phi\) to
\(\mathrm{Tr}\,\Lambda\Lambda^\dagger\Phi^\dagger\Phi\). This equals
\(\mathrm{Tr}\,\Phi^\dagger\Phi\) for arbitrary \(\Phi\) only if
\(\Lambda\Lambda^\dagger=\mathbb{I}\); a boost does not satisfy this and
generically changes the contraction, although it may leave it unchanged for
particular configurations. Hence the contractions displayed
in~\eqref{eq:Smicro} and~\eqref{eq:V} are not invariant under local boosts
as symmetries of the action. Imposing local Lorentz invariance in D2
(Assumption~\ref{ass:local-lorentz}) therefore requires a completed action,
