with the tangent connection and an invariant contraction, and not merely a
relabelling of~\eqref{eq:Smicro}. Whether such an action exists, and what its
spectrum is, is not examined here. Problem~D remains open.

\paragraph{Ghost-free versus boost-invariant contraction (conditional; not closure).}
On the real locked \(4\times4\) block write \(\Phi=\mathbb{I}+M\). Consider two
candidate quadratic kinetic contractions, both stated for the fixed scaffold
and neither derived from~\eqref{eq:Smicro}.
(i) The kinetic term in~\eqref{eq:Smicro}, with its positive-definite
matrix-space contraction, gives every component the same relative kinetic
sign, so there are no relative-sign differences; however, the displayed
contractions in~\eqref{eq:Smicro} and~\eqref{eq:V} are not invariant under
boosts of either index, as noted above.
(ii) A contraction with \(\eta\) on both indices, invariant under constant
independent \(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\times\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\) transformations,
gives component \(M^I{}_A\) the relative kinetic sign
\(\eta_{II}\eta_{AA}\). Fix the overall sign so that \(M^0{}_0\) and
\(M^i{}_j\) have the healthy sign; then the six components
\(M^0{}_i,M^i{}_0\) have the opposite sign. This invariance is global: for
local transformations covariant derivatives and the corresponding connections
are needed, and the candidate expression does not specify them. If the six
broken generators of Proposition~\ref{prop:goldstone-count} are gauged and eat
the six \(\eta\)-antisymmetric components, three of the opposite-sign
components (the \(0i\) part of the antisymmetric block) are absorbed; three
opposite-sign \(\eta\)-symmetric \(0i\) components remain. Whether these are
removed by a constraint, a further gauge condition, or the diffeomorphism
structure of Chapter~\ref{ch:lorentz-locking} is not established. This is a
sign count for the quadratic form about \(\Phi=\mathbb{I}\) only; it is not a
spectrum or unitarity result and is not identified with the tensor sector of
Chapter~\ref{ch:lorentz-decomposition}. Boost invariance and absence of
opposite-sign components are therefore not both available from the
contractions considered here. Problem~D remains open.

\paragraph{A boost-invariant completed action: quadratic test (conditional; not closure).}
This paragraph tests one candidate structural change, \emph{not} contained
in~\eqref{eq:Smicro}: replace the positive-definite contractions by
\(\eta_{IJ}\eta^{AB}\) contractions, so that the kinetic term is
\(-g^{(0)\mu\nu}\eta_{IJ}\eta^{AB}\,\mathcal{D}_\mu\Phi^I{}_A\,
\mathcal{D}_\nu\Phi^J{}_B\) with the covariant derivative~\eqref{eq:D-both},
and the potential is \(\tfrac{\lambda}{4}\mathrm{Tr}(Q-v^2\mathbb{I})^2\) with
\(Q=\eta^{-1}\Phi^{T}\eta\Phi\); both are invariant under constant
\(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\times\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\), with local invariance given by the
connections. It is introduced only to test whether the opposite-sign modes
found above can be removed. It would change the definition of the model, and
nothing here shows that it is derivable from Presentation~A.

About \(\Phi=v(\mathbb{I}+M)\) on the real locked \(4\times4\) block, with
\(\tilde M=\eta^{-1}M^{T}\eta\), the quadratic potential is
\(\lambda v^{4}\,\mathrm{K}(M_s)\) for the \(\eta\)-self-adjoint part
\(M_s=\tfrac12(M+\tilde M)\), where
\(\mathrm{K}(X)=\eta_{IJ}\eta^{AB}X^I{}_AX^J{}_B\). The ten self-adjoint
components carry the potential and the six anti-self-adjoint components are
flat, matching Proposition~\ref{prop:goldstone-count}. Because \(\mathrm{K}\)
is indefinite, this quadratic form is negative along the three \(0i\)
self-adjoint directions for \(\lambda>0\): \(\Phi=v\mathbb{I}\) is a stationary
point but a saddle of this candidate potential, not a stable vacuum, so what
follows is a quadratic diagnostic about a stationary background and not a
spectrum about a stable vacuum. The kinetic form has signature \((7,3)\) on the
self-adjoint block and \((3,3)\) on the anti-self-adjoint block; the two
blocks do not mix. At the background \(A_\mu=0\), the relative combination
\(\omega_\mu-iA_\mu\) in the convention of~\eqref{eq:D-both} (equivalently
\(\omega_\mu-A_\mu\) if \(A_\mu\) is redefined as a real Lie-algebra-valued
connection) enters algebraically and removes the anti-self-adjoint components
from the kinetic term, while the diagonal combination does not enter at
quadratic order. What remains is the self-adjoint block, with seven components
of one kinetic sign and three (the \(0i\) components) of the opposite sign;
since kinetic and potential forms coincide on this block, the
equation-of-motion mass squared is \(\lambda v^2\) in the normalisation of the
preceding Hessian paragraph for all ten. Since \(\delta g=\eta M+M^{T}\eta\),
this block is the linearised metric perturbation, and its quadratic action
contains only \((\partial h_{\mu\nu})^2\) and \(h_{\mu\nu}h^{\mu\nu}\): the
derivative index is contracted only with \(g^{(0)\mu\nu}\), so no
\(\partial_\mu h^{\mu\nu}\) structure appears, the mass form is nondegenerate
on all ten components, and the quadratic action has no linearised
diffeomorphism invariance. Hence, for this candidate at this order, boost
invariance is obtained but three opposite-sign modes remain, the potential is
indefinite, and the quadratic form is not of Fierz--Pauli type (hypothesis~5 of
Theorem~\ref{thm:mT}). Whether higher-order terms, a kinetic term for
\(\omega_\mu\), or an induced action change this is not examined. This is a
bounded no-go for the stated candidate at quadratic order about the flat
scaffold; it does not exclude other completed actions. Problems~D and~B remain
open.

\paragraph{Diffeomorphism symmetry of the displayed action (conditional; not closure).}
In~\eqref{eq:Smicro} the field \(\Phi^I{}_A\) carries no coordinate index: it
is a set of scalar fields on the scaffold, and a world index appears only
through the identification of the tangent index with the coordinate index
in~\eqref{eq:vev-is-frame}. With \(g^{(0)}_{\mu\nu}\) held fixed
(Assumption~\ref{ass:aux-metric}), a diffeomorphism of the scaffold coordinates
acts on the argument \(x\) of~\(\Phi\), and \(S_{\mathrm{micro}}\) is
invariant under those diffeomorphisms that preserve \(g^{(0)}\), such as its
isometries; this does not exclude other symmetries. If \(g^{(0)}_{\mu\nu}\) is
transformed as a tensor together with \(\Phi\), the functional
\(S_{\mathrm{micro}}[\Phi,g^{(0)}]\) is covariant in the spurionic sense; this
does not by itself make \(g^{(0)}\) a dynamical or compensating field, nor
establish a gauge symmetry of the fixed-background theory. The composite
metric \(g=e^{T}\eta e\) built from the identified frame is a tensor only if
the identification \(A\leftrightarrow\mu\) is itself made covariant, which
\eqref{eq:Smicro} does not provide. The statement of
Section~\ref{sec:ch10-diffs} that diffeomorphisms are the gauge symmetry of
the composite metric, and the diffeomorphism-invariance hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:conservation} and Assumption~\ref{ass:Sent-density}, are
therefore conditional on such an extension, not consequences of
\(S_{\mathrm{micro}}\) as displayed. This is a scope statement; it does not
exclude a covariant completion, and Problems~B, D and~G remain open.

\paragraph{What linearised diffeomorphism invariance fixes (conditional; not closure).}
Consider the most general parity-even, Lorentz-invariant quadratic action for
an unconstrained symmetric \(h_{\mu\nu}\) on a flat background with at most
two derivatives. Up to integration by parts and overall normalisation, in
momentum space it has four derivative terms with coefficients
\(a_1,\dots,a_4\), multiplying \(p^2h_{\mu\nu}h^{\mu\nu}\),
\(p^\mu h_{\mu\nu}p_\lambda h^{\lambda\nu}\), \(h\,p^\mu p^\nu h_{\mu\nu}\),
\(p^2h^2\), and two mass terms \(m_1h_{\mu\nu}h^{\mu\nu}+m_2h^2\). Requiring
invariance under \(\delta h_{\mu\nu}=p_\mu\xi_\nu+p_\nu\xi_\mu\) forces
\(a_2=-2a_1\), \(a_3=2a_1\), \(a_4=-a_1\) and \(m_1=m_2=0\): the massless
Fierz--Pauli form for the unconstrained field (a direct symbolic check, used
as a standard result and not as a new derivation). This classifies the
unconstrained symmetric-\(h\) action. It is not identified here with the
trace-constrained tensor block of~\eqref{eq:FP} used in hypothesis~5 of
Theorem~\ref{thm:mT}; relating the two requires a separate argument that is
not given. The displayed action~\eqref{eq:Smicro} is not in this class. In the
\(\eta\)-contracted candidate tested above, the block identified with
\(\delta g_{\mu\nu}\) has no \(\partial_\mu h^{\mu\nu}\) structure and a
nonzero mass term. For the displayed action, the real-slice symmetric block
\(H\) does not span the full \(\delta g_{\mu\nu}\), since \(H_{0i}\) leaves
the metric unchanged, and no diffeomorphism-invariant quadratic structure
appears in its Hessian. A covariant
completion of the action may therefore be a necessary structural ingredient
for a diffeomorphism-invariant quadratic action, but it is not sufficient for
Problem~B, which also requires entanglement stationarity, the area
contribution, and their relation to the emergent metric. Problems~B and~D
remain open.

\paragraph{Related covariant quantum-space constructions, and what they do
not supply (conditional; not closure).}
Steinacker and collaborators study the IKKT matrix model on covariant
quantum spaces in which the frame is read off from the adjoint action
\([X^a,\cdot\,]\) and averaged over an internal \(S^2\) fibre.  The Euclidean,
linearised analysis~\cite{Steinacker2016Gravity} is reported to give
linearised Einstein equations under conditions on the background, dimensional
reduction and an infrared cutoff; we have not checked these conditions.  A
Lorentzian space built on the minimal doubleton representation of
\(\mathfrak{so}(4,2)\), a quantised twistor space \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\) over a
\(k=-1\) FLRW spacetime, is described in~\cite{MantaSteinacker2025}, and an
induced gravitational action with an explicit effective Newton constant is
reported at one loop for such backgrounds with fuzzy extra dimensions
in~\cite{MantaSteinackerTran2024}.  The absence of ghosts for the Lorentzian
higher-spin theory is reported in~\cite{Steinacker2019NoGhost}, at the expense
of manifest Lorentz invariance.  We have not reproduced any of these results.
Taking them as stated, they are analogous to, not the same as, the
construction tested above: the frame comes from a chosen representation
rather than from the vacuum value of a field \(\Phi^I{}_A\); the ghost problem
is addressed by giving up manifest Lorentz invariance, which the locking
assumption of Chapter~\ref{ch:lorentz-locking} presupposes; and gravity is
induced at one loop rather than obtained from entanglement stationarity as in
Problem~B.  They therefore do not close Problems~D, B, F, C or~G.  They report
that a Lorentzian, ghost-free construction exists in a matrix-model setting;
whether an analogue exists for the \(\Phi\)-field action of
Chapter~\ref{ch:matrix-action} with Lorentz locking retained is not
established here.  Adopting an induced-gravity route in place of Problem~B
would be a structural change to the programme and is not proposed here.

\paragraph{The matrix-model no-ghost argument and the \(\eta\)-contracted
candidate (conditional; not closure).}
In~\cite{Steinacker2019NoGhost} the vector-type fluctuation spaces are
reported to carry an indefinite inner product of signature \((+++-)\) before
gauge fixing; the gauge-fixing condition is equivalent to orthogonality to
the pure-gauge modes, which reduces the space to signature \((++0)\), and the
quotient by the null gauge mode leaves two positive-norm modes.  The
negative-norm directions are thus removed by a gauge symmetry whose modes mix
the derivative index with the field index.  We have not reproduced this.

In the \(\eta\)-contracted candidate tested above, the tangent-index
connection enters algebraically and removes only the \(\eta\)-anti-self-adjoint
directions, so the three opposite-sign self-adjoint \(0i\) directions are not
gauge directions of it.  Shifts of the form
\(\delta h_{\mu\nu}=\partial_{(\mu}\xi_{\nu)}\) would be one route to
removing such modes; they are defined only through the identification of
tangent and coordinate indices of~\eqref{eq:vev-is-frame}, and invariance
under them is sufficient for, but not shown necessary for, ghost removal.  For
this candidate at quadratic order, with kinetic part \(a_1(\partial h)^2\) and
mass form \(\lambda v^{4}\mathrm{K}(M_s)\), the quadratic action does not
vanish on pure-gauge \(h=\partial\xi\) for generic momentum (checked
symbolically on a model quadratic form \(a_1k^2\,h\!\cdot\!h+m\,h\!\cdot\!h\);
it vanishes only on shell, \(a_1k^2+m=0\), which does not test invariance).
By the lemma above, invariance would require cross terms with the
Fierz--Pauli ratios and a massless symmetric block, whereas this candidate has
no such cross terms and the nonzero mass \(\lambda v^2\).  The
positive-definite kinetic form of the displayed action~\eqref{eq:Smicro} has
no negative-norm scalar-kinetic directions, but its tensor sector was not
analysed here, and its real-slice block does not span \(\delta g_{\mu\nu}\),
as noted above.  Two repairs of the candidate are visible and neither is
adopted: adding such cross terms and a potential flat along the shift
directions, or realising the shifts non-linearly with a compensating field.
Each introduces a new object and hypothesis not derived from the matrix
action.  This is a bounded statement about the quadratic form of this
candidate and does not exclude other completions.

\paragraph{A compensating field alone does not change the spectrum
(conditional; not closure).}
Restore the shifts above by a Stueckelberg replacement
\(h_{\mu\nu}\to h_{\mu\nu}+\partial_{(\mu}\xi_{\nu)}\) in the \(\eta\)-contracted
candidate, with a new field \(\xi_\mu\) and gauge transformations
\(\delta h=\partial\epsilon\), \(\delta\xi=-\epsilon\).  The gauge \(\xi=0\) is
reachable and in it the action equals the original, so the physical spectrum
is unchanged: the signature \((7,3)\) of the self-adjoint block, the three
opposite-sign \(0i\) directions and the mass \(\lambda v^2\) persist.  The
compensating field is a reparametrisation of this candidate; removing the
ghosts would need structure beyond it, such as a kinetic structure for
\(\xi\) and a constraint, which is not examined here.  This concerns the
quadratic form of this candidate and does not exclude other completions.
Problems~B and~D remain open.

\paragraph{Integrable frames and indefinite gauge kinetic terms
(conditional; not closure).}
Two further routes to a tangent-index shift symmetry for the
\(\eta\)-contracted candidate tested above are not supplied by the linearised
quadratic form.  Both statements are bounded to that candidate and to the
stated class of kinetic terms.

First, suppose the frame is integrable,
\(\Phi^I{}_\mu=\partial_\mu\phi^I\), where \(\Phi^I{}_\mu\) denotes
\(\Phi^I{}_A\) converted by the tangent--coordinate identification
of~\eqref{eq:vev-is-frame}.  The perturbation of its symmetric block is then
\(h_{\mu\nu}=\partial_{(\mu}\pi_{\nu)}\), and its antisymmetric block is a
local Lorentz rotation.  The linearised Riemann tensor of any pure-gauge
perturbation \(h_{\mu\nu}=\partial_{(\mu}\xi_{\nu)}\) on a flat background
vanishes; this is the standard gauge invariance of the linearised curvature.
The induced metric of an integrable frame is therefore flat, and invariance
under the shifts is automatic but carries no linearised gravity.  This does
not exclude other structures.  A shift symmetry that carries gravity must act
on a non-integrable \(\Phi\).  Such a \(\Phi\) would be expected to play the
role of a gauge field for the shifts, with torsion \(\mathcal{D}\Phi\) as its
field strength, and invariant kinetic terms would be expected to be built from
torsion and curvature rather than from the displayed contraction of
\(\partial\Phi\) with the scaffold.  This is a structural expectation
following from requiring the symmetry, not a derivation and not a property of
the displayed action.

Second, a quadratic kinetic term for connections of a non-compact tangent
group built from an invariant bilinear form is indefinite.  For
\(\mathfrak{so}(3,1)\) the Killing form has signature \((3,3)\), positive on
the three boosts and negative on the three rotations, and the same holds for
any nonzero real combination of the Killing form and its dual invariant form
(the signature was computed numerically in the adjoint basis).  This has the
same signature as the \((3,3)\) anti-self-adjoint kinetic block found above;
no identification of the two is claimed.  A Yang--Mills-type term built from
an invariant form for such a connection therefore carries wrong-sign modes,
and ghost removal would need other structure, such as first-order terms with
constraints, which this does not exclude.

What remains is to derive the required kinetic structure and constraint from
the matrix action without inserting them by hand.  A one-loop effective action
is reported in~\cite{MantaSteinackerTran2024} to induce a gravitational action
for a different matrix model; we have not checked its operator against that of
Chapter~\ref{ch:heat-kernel}, and it is not shown here to supply the symmetry
or the constraint.  We expect, but have not shown for the present operator,
that the field strength of the tangent-index connection enters the one-loop
action at four-derivative order through the \(a_2\) density of
Chapter~\ref{ch:heat-kernel} (the standard Seeley--DeWitt term
\(\propto\mathrm{tr}\,\Omega_{\mu\nu}\Omega^{\mu\nu}\)), and that a
two-derivative torsion-squared term would arise only if torsion appeared in
the endomorphism \(E\) of~\eqref{eq:O-eta}, which
Chapter~\ref{ch:heat-kernel} does not specify.  Problems~B, C, D, F and G
remain open.

\paragraph{Diagonal invariance and the self-adjoint traceless block
(conditional; not closure).}
Write the real locked block as \(\Phi=v(\mathbb{I}+M)\), with
mixed-index components \(M^I{}_A\), and let the unbroken diagonal
\(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\) of Proposition~\ref{prop:goldstone-count} act by
conjugation, \(\delta M=[\alpha,M]\) with \(\eta\alpha\) antisymmetric.
The sixteen components decompose as \(\mathbf 1\oplus\mathbf 6\oplus\mathbf 9\):
the trace, the six \(\eta\)-anti-self-adjoint components, and the nine
\(\eta\)-self-adjoint traceless components (six \(ij\) and three \(0i\)).
The nine components form the real irreducible representation \((1,1)\),
which has trivial commutant, so by Schur's lemma a quadratic form on them
that is invariant under the diagonal group is unique up to a scale.  The
form \(\mathrm{K}\) of the boost-invariant completed-action paragraph above,
restricted to this block, is such a form, with signature \((6,3)\); the
signature \((7,3)\) quoted there includes the trace direction.  Every
nonzero invariant form on the block is therefore \(c\,\mathrm{K}\), with
signature \((6,3)\) or \((3,6)\) according to the sign of \(c\).  A
numerical check (\texttt{numpy}, not a proof) found four independent
invariant quadratic forms on the sixteen components, whose restrictions to
the nine-component block span a one-dimensional space, consistent with
this count.

The statement assumes that the quadratic form is invariant under constant
diagonal \(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\) acting by conjugation.  The displayed
potential does not have this property (see the paragraph on the assumed
group \(G_{\mathrm{int}}\supseteq\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\) in Problem~F); the
condition is a requirement on completions of the action, not a property of
the displayed action.  Under it, for a potential Hessian or for a kinetic
form with the spacetime index contracted through \(g^{(0)\mu\nu}\) only, as
in~\eqref{eq:Smicro}, the form on the nine components is indefinite unless
it vanishes, so a nonzero Hessian is not positive there, and a nonzero
kinetic form has at least three wrong-sign directions (three or six,
depending on the sign of \(c\)).  This holds for every diagonal-invariant
form without derivative--tangent mixing, which is a class of constructions
and not all of them.  Terms that tie the derivative index to the tangent
index through the vacuum identification of Equation~\eqref{eq:vev-is-frame}
add invariants and are not analysed here, so a Fierz--Pauli-type structure
is not excluded.  The trace and the anti-self-adjoint block are not
constrained by this argument.  It adds no field, symmetry or constraint,
does not identify the nine components with the tensor sector of
Chapter~\ref{ch:lorentz-decomposition}, and leaves Problem~D open.

\paragraph{Tree-level terms plus an assumed induced Fierz--Pauli term:
quadratic test (conditional; not closure).}
This paragraph tests which tree-level coefficients are compatible with a
healthy spin-two sector if an induced Fierz--Pauli term is assumed.  On
the \(\eta\)-self-adjoint block, Equation~\eqref{eq:g-from-e} gives
\(\delta g_{\mu\nu}=2(\eta M)_{\mu\nu}\), which is symmetric; write
\(h_{\mu\nu}\) for a field proportional to \((\eta M)_{\mu\nu}\), the
normalisation being absorbed into the coefficients below, and identify
tangent and coordinate indices through Equation~\eqref{eq:vev-is-frame}.
Take the quadratic form at momentum \(k\) to be
\(Q=(tk^2+m)\,h_{\mu\nu}h^{\mu\nu}+(t'k^2+m')\,h^2+g\,Q_{\mathrm{FP}}\),
with \(k^2=\eta^{\mu\nu}k_\mu k_\nu\).  The first two terms are the most
general tree-level form invariant under the diagonal \(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\)
with the derivative index contracted through \(g^{(0)\mu\nu}\) only (the
trace has an independent coefficient), and \(Q_{\mathrm{FP}}\) is the
Fierz--Pauli structure with ratios \((1,-2,2,-1)\), indices raised with
\(\eta\).  The induced term is an assumed input in the form of a
linearised Einstein--Hilbert term with unspecified coefficient \(g\), in
the manner of Chapter~\ref{ch:heat-kernel}; its coefficient and sign,
which depend on Problem~F, are not computed here.  The Lagrangian is
taken as \(\mp Q\), with the sign chosen so that the spin-two modes are
healthy; all wrong-sign statements below are relative to the spin-two
modes.

A symbolic computation (\texttt{sympy}, a check and not a proof) confirms
that \(Q_{\mathrm{FP}}\) is invariant under \(\delta h=k\xi\) and gives,
at \(k=(\omega,0,0,q)\), a determinant of the ten-by-ten form with the
following structure.  If \(t=t'=m=m'=0\), the four components \(h_{0\mu}\)
are non-dynamical and the form is the massless Fierz--Pauli form.  If
\(t\neq0\), there are three poles at \(k^2=-m/t\), five spin-two poles at
\((g+t)k^2+m=0\), and two poles in the scalar sector, so the
\(h_{0\mu}\) components propagate.  For \(t'=m'=0\), \(g>0\) and
\(|t|\ll g\), the coefficient matrix of \(\omega^2\) has, at \(t=0\), one
direction of sign opposite to the spin-two modes (the trace) and four null
directions; for \(t>0\) the three \(h_{0i}\) directions have a sign opposite
to the spin-two modes, and for \(t<0\) the \(h_{00}\) direction does.  For
\(t=t'=0\) and \(m'\neq-m\) there is one additional scalar pole, whose
sign (ghost or tachyon) is not determined here; it is absent for
\(m'=-m\), the Fierz--Pauli mass form, leaving only the five spin-two poles
at \(gk^2+m=0\).  Diagonal invariance alone does not impose \(t=t'=0\) or
\(m'=-m\).  Within this class and these parameter ranges, therefore, a
tree-level derivative term on the symmetric block adds propagating
components that carry directions of sign opposite to the spin-two modes,
and a tree-level mass leaves no extra scalar pole only at the
Fierz--Pauli trace ratio.  These are conditions that such a completion
would place on the tree-level coefficients.  The displayed kinetic term of
Equation~\eqref{eq:Smicro} is not diagonal-invariant (see the preceding
paragraphs) and so is not a member of this class.  The vector and ghost
sectors, the sign and size of \(g\), the continuation and the one-loop
operator are not treated, so this does not show that an induced route is
ghost-free, does not identify the block with the tensor sector of
Chapter~\ref{ch:lorentz-decomposition}, and leaves Problems~B, C, D, F
and~G open.

\paragraph{Displayed action plus an assumed induced Fierz--Pauli term:
time-kinetic test (conditional; not closure).}
This paragraph keeps the displayed action as the tree-level term and
adds only the induced term assumed above; it introduces no new
tree-level structure.  On the \(\eta\)-self-adjoint block, with
\(h_{\mu\nu}\) proportional to \((\eta M)_{\mu\nu}\) as above, the
displayed kinetic term of Equation~\eqref{eq:Smicro} contracts the
components with the Euclidean form, \(\mathrm{Tr}\,M^{T}M=\sum_{\mu\nu}
h_{\mu\nu}^2\) for \(M=\eta h\), so take
\(Q=tk^2\sum_{\mu\nu}h_{\mu\nu}^2+g\,Q_{\mathrm{FP}}+Q_{\mathrm{mass}}\)
with \(t>0\), the same \(Q_{\mathrm{FP}}\) and the same sign convention
as above.  The displayed potential acts through the Hermitian part
\(H=(M+M^{T})/2\) of the Hessian paragraph in Problem~F; for \(M=\eta h\)
the \(0i\) entries of \(H\) cancel, so \(Q_{\mathrm{mass}}\propto
h_{00}^2+\sum_{ij}h_{ij}^2\) acts on seven components and the three
\(0i\) components are massless at this order.  A symbolic computation
(\texttt{sympy}, a check and not a proof) gives the
\(\omega^2\)-coefficients of the form at \(k=(\omega,0,0,0)\), which do not
depend on the mass, in the convention where healthy modes have negative
coefficient: \(-2t\) (once), \(-4t\) (three times), \(4g-2t\) (once, the
trace direction), \(-2(g+t)\) (twice) and \(-4(g+t)\) (three times).  All
ten are healthy exactly when \(t>0\) and \(-t<g<t/2\); for \(g>0\) only
\(g<t/2\) binds.  Without the mass term, the determinant at
\(k=(\omega,0,0,q)\) factors as
\(t^4(g+t)^2(q^2-\omega^2)^6\,
\bigl[(t-g)q^2-(t+g)\omega^2\bigr]^2\,
\bigl[t(t-g)(q^2-\omega^2)^2-2g^2(q^2+\omega^2)^2\bigr]\)
up to a constant.  The squared factor gives modes with
\(c^2=(t-g)/(t+g)\neq1\) for \(g\neq0\), and the final quartic factor is
also non-luminal for \(g\neq0\).  The dispersion is then
Lorentz-violating at order \(g/t\), in tension with the exact Lorentz
invariance that the locking construction of
Chapter~\ref{ch:lorentz-locking} is intended to deliver; this tension is
recorded, not resolved.

If the induced term is the linearised \((1/16\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}})\int
\sqrt{-g}\,R\), with \(\delta g_{\mu\nu}=2h_{\mu\nu}\) from
Equation~\eqref{eq:g-from-e} and the standard normalisation of the
linearised Einstein--Hilbert quadratic form (used, not re-derived), then
matching the coefficient of \(k^2h_{\mu\nu}h^{\mu\nu}\) gives
\(t=v^2\) from Equation~\eqref{eq:Smicro} and \(g=1/(16\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}})\).
For \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}>0\), the window above is then
\(1/G_{\mathrm{eff}}<8\pi v^2\), and \(1-c^2\approx 2g/t=1/(8\pi
G_{\mathrm{eff}}v^2)\) is small only if \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}v^2\gg1\).
Inserting the conditional parameterization of
Equation~\eqref{eq:Geff-structure}, the window reads
\(A\mathcal N_0\Lambda_{\mathrm{UV}}^2/v^2+B\ln(\Lambda_{\mathrm{UV}}/\mu)
+C\mu^2/v^2<8\pi\); if the logarithmic and \(\mu^2\) terms are
negligible, it reads \(A\mathcal N_0\Lambda_{\mathrm{UV}}^2<8\pi v^2\).
The coefficients \(A,B,C\), \(\mathcal N_0\), \(\mu\) and
\(\Lambda_{\mathrm{UV}}\) are undetermined (Theorem~\ref{thm:Geff} and
Problem~F), so this identifies the combination that Problem~F would have
to satisfy and does not decide whether it does.  The window has the same
structure as the condition \(Z>0\) with
\(Z=2N-3/(4\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}}v^2)\) of the Repair~II pure-trace
truncation above (a conformal-mode kinetic coefficient that turns
negative when \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}v^2\) is small), but the numerical factors
differ and no equivalence is claimed.  The result is for an assumed
linearised induced term; it is a statement about the time-kinetic matrix
and the dispersion at quadratic order, so it is not a spectrum or
unitarity result.  It does not treat the vector, ghost or \(\omega_\mu\)
sectors, does not identify the block with the tensor sector of
Chapter~\ref{ch:lorentz-decomposition}, and leaves Problems~B, C, D, F
and~G open.

\paragraph{Matrix gauge transformations and the frame index in the conjugate-coordinate class (conditional; bounded obstruction).}
Work in the Poisson limit on the leaf \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\) with \(T^a\), \(y^\mu\), \(m=M^{45}=y^4\), the sign \(s\), \(\ell^2=-y_ay^a\) and \(R=1\) of the paragraphs on the conjugate-coordinate class in Section~\ref{sec:status-F}, so that \(y\cdot y:=y_\mu y^\mu=-\ell^2-m^2\). With \(\varsigma=\pm1\) an orientation fixed by the realisation and \(\epsilon^{0123}=1\),
\[
M^{\mu\nu}=\frac{m\,(y^\mu T^\nu-y^\nu T^\mu)+\varsigma\ell\,\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\lambda}y_\rho T_\lambda}{y\cdot y},
\]
by hand at \(y^i=0\) and on the explicit realisation at random points (residual \(10^{-12}\)). A first-harmonic gauge parameter is \(\Lambda=\xi_\mu(y)T^\mu\), defined modulo \(\xi_\mu\to\xi_\mu+k\,y_\mu\); take \(\xi\cdot y=0\). The Lie--Poisson relations give exactly
\[
\{\Lambda,T^\nu\}=\lambda^\nu{}_\sigma T^\sigma-s\,m\,T^\sigma\partial^\nu\xi_\sigma,\qquad
\lambda^{\nu\sigma}=\frac{s}{y\cdot y}\bigl[m(\xi^\nu y^\sigma-y^\nu\xi^\sigma)+\varsigma\ell\,\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\xi_\mu y_\rho\bigr],
\]
\[
\{\Lambda,y^\mu\}=s\,m\,\xi^\mu-s\,T^\sigma\partial_\rho\xi_\sigma M^{\rho\mu}.
\]
The first term of the first equation is the algebraic part of the gauge variation, left uncomputed in the paragraph on the first-order response. It is a local rotation of the index of \(T\), by a bivector \(\lambda(\xi)\) of the three-dimensional space \(W_y\), with three independent functions. It is not independent of the displacement of the base point: \(y_\nu\lambda^{\nu\sigma}=-s\,m\,\xi^\sigma\), so the boost part of \(\lambda\) is what keeps \(T\) orthogonal to the displaced \(y\). At \(y^i=0\) one unit of \(\xi_i\) gives a boost \(m/y^0\) and a rotation \(\ell/y^0\). Also \(W_y\) is not a subalgebra of \(\mathfrak{so}(3,1)\) for \(m\neq0\): for \(aK(\xi)+bJ(\xi)\), with \(K,J\) the boosts and rotations about \(y\), the commutator is \((b^2-a^2)J(\xi\times\xi')+2abK(\xi\times\xi')\) up to a common sign, which stays in the span only for \(a=0\) (exact span of dimension six at \((y^0,y^4)=(5,3),(13,5),(5,4),(25,7)\)). For a plane wave, the derivative term exceeds the rotation by the factor \(\sinh\tau\cosh\tau\,|q|\ell\) with \(\sinh\tau=m/\ell\), so in the regime \(|q|\ell\gg1\) of the frozen symbol the transformation is the Maxwell shift of the paragraph on the tree-level form, and the rotation is of relative order \(1/(|q|\ell)\) there.

Fibre orders do not mix in this action. For \(\zeta(y)\) with \(\zeta\cdot y=\zeta\cdot\zeta=0\), the function \((\zeta\cdot T)^J\) is of fibre order \(J\), and \(\{f(\zeta\cdot T)^J,T^a\}\) is of fibre order \(J\): the terms produced are \((\zeta\cdot T)^{J-1}(\tilde\zeta\cdot T)\) with \(\tilde\zeta\cdot y=\tilde\zeta\cdot\zeta=0\), and their Laplacian on the fibre vanishes, using \(\zeta^\mu\partial\zeta_\mu=0\) for the term with a derivative of \(\zeta\) (numerical projection on the fibre for \(J\le4\) agrees). So at linear order about \(T^a\) only \(J=1\) acts on the frame amplitudes. Moreover no nonconstant gauge parameter fixes the base point: \(\{\Lambda,y^\mu\}=0\) implies \(\{\Lambda,M^{\mu\nu}\}=0\) by the Jacobi identity, hence \(\{\Lambda,T^\mu\}=0\) from \(T^\mu=-M^{\mu\sigma}y_\sigma/y^4\), and \(y^\mu,M^{\mu\nu},T^\mu\) with \(y^4\), a function of \(y^\mu\) on the sheet, separate the points of the sheet. An \(\mathrm{SO}(4,1)\)-equivariant base of positive dimension on which a copy of \(\mathfrak{so}(3,1)\subset\mathfrak{so}(4,1)\) acts vertically would be a map of the sheet onto \(\mathrm{SO}(4,1)/\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\), since that subalgebra is maximal; none exists, since the stabiliser \(U(2)\) of a point of the sheet has a compact torus of rank two and the compact subgroups of \(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\) have rank one. Within the Poisson limit, this single orbit, the base functions \(y^\mu\) and linear order about \(T^a\), the matrix gauge group therefore gives no six-parameter local rotation of the frame index. The hypothesis relaxed below is the representation.

\paragraph{First-order equivariant class: local \(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\) inside the unitary group (conditional; not closure).}
Keep Equation~\eqref{eq:Smatrix} with \(C_{\mu\nu\lambda}=0\) and, except where a fermion is named, without the matter term, and change only the Hermitian operators. Let \(\mathcal A\) be a commutative algebra of operators on the Hilbert space of Definition~\ref{def:matrix-coords}, with \([\hat X_a,\mathcal A]\subset\mathcal A\), let \(\mathcal S_{cd}\) be anti-Hermitian derivations of \(\mathcal A\) forming a free and proper unitary action of \(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\), and write \(\hat X_a=\mathrm i\mathbb D_a\), with
\[
[\mathcal S_{cd},\mathbb D_a]=\eta_{ad}\mathbb D_c-\eta_{ac}\mathbb D_d,\qquad
[\mathbb D_a,\mathbb D_b]=-\mathsf T_{ab}{}^c\mathbb D_c+\tfrac12\mathsf R_{ab}{}^{cd}\mathcal S_{cd}+\mathsf F_{ab},
\]
with coefficients in \(\mathcal A\). Take the spectrum \(Z\) of \(\mathcal A\) to be a smooth manifold, with \(\mathcal A\) acting as functions and the adjoint actions of \(\mathbb D_a\), \(\mathcal S_{cd}\) a basis of its vector fields at each point. This absolute parallelism makes \(Z\) a principal bundle over \(Z/\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\) carrying a frame and a connection, by the standard reconstruction of a Cartan connection. Given \(\mathcal A\), they are read off from the operators and the action contains no metric; \(Z\), its smooth structure and \(\mathcal A\) are data of the class, as the leaf and the base functions are of the conjugate-coordinate class. In coordinates, \(\mathbb D_a=u_a{}^be_b{}^\mu(\partial_\mu-u_f{}^g\omega_\mu{}^c{}_g\,\partial/\partial u_f{}^c)+\mathsf t_a-\mathrm iA_a\) with \(u\) a Lorentz matrix, \(\mathsf t_a=\tfrac12\mathsf T_{ac}{}^c\) from Hermiticity in the measure \(|\det e|^{-1}\mathrm d^4y\,\mathrm d\mathrm{Haar}(u)\) (the divergence of the first term in it is \(\mathsf T_{ac}{}^c\), by hand, and checked exactly for unipotent frames) and \(A_a\) a real order-zero field. Multiplication by \(|\det e|^{-1/2}\) carries this to the reference measure \(\mathrm d^4y\,\mathrm d\mathrm{Haar}(u)\), with the coefficients of the relations unchanged, so operators with different \(e\) act on one Hilbert space, whose inner product does not contain \(e\), as in the statement of~\cite{HanadaKawaiKimura2006b} that for any manifold the large-\(N\) matrices contain a set unitarily equivalent to these covariant derivatives, with the manifold not fixed; this selects none of them. This is the construction of~\cite{HanadaKawaiKimura2006}, there on \(\mathrm{Spin}(10)\) with the Euclidean form, read here with the Lorentzian contraction of the matrix index, as for the principal \(\mathrm{Spin}(D-1,1)\) bundle of~\cite{AsanoKawaiTsuchiya2012}; the boost generators are anti-Hermitian on this Hilbert space. The kinetic tensor \(K^{\mu\nu}=\eta^{ab}\mathbb D_ay^\mu\mathbb D_by^\nu=\eta^{cd}e_c{}^\mu e_d{}^\nu\) does not depend on the fibre, and its Lorentzian signature comes from \(\eta\), so Assumption~\ref{ass:lorentzian} is retained. The merged obstruction for matrices as base coordinates does not apply, since \(y^\mu\) is not among the \(\hat X_a\).

Local rotations are then matrix gauge transformations, as found in~\cite{HanadaKawaiKimura2006}. For \(\lambda^{cd}=u^c{}_fu^d{}_g\hat\lambda^{fg}(y)\) with six arbitrary functions \(\hat\lambda\), \([\tfrac12\lambda^{cd}\mathcal S_{cd},\mathbb D_a]=\lambda^c{}_a\mathbb D_c-\tfrac12(\mathbb D_a\lambda^{cd})\mathcal S_{cd}\), which rotates \(e\) and shifts \(\omega\) by \(\nabla\hat\lambda\); the finite form is the pull-back by a vertical automorphism of \(Z\), a unitary operator, so \((e,\omega)\) related by a local rotation are one gauge orbit. The coefficient \(\lambda^c{}_a\) lies in the commutative \(\mathcal A\), so the Leibniz defect of the central Moyal branch in the opening paragraphs of this section is absent. The fibre coordinate \(u\) has the index structure of the Stueckelberg field of Equation~\eqref{eq:dressed-omega}, here as an element of the operator algebra; this is an observation and not an identification with Presentation~B. Longitudinal diffeomorphisms are also present, as in the same reference, through \(\Lambda=-\tfrac{\mathrm i}2(\xi^\mu\partial_\mu+\partial_\mu\xi^\mu)\) in the reference measure. The field content of the class is \(e\) (16), \(\omega\) (24), \(A\) (4), with gauge parameters \(6+4+1\), and the background-gauge ghost operator on End of the Hilbert space is \([\hat X^a,[\hat X_a,\cdot\,]]\). On the \(\mathrm{Spin}(3,1)\) cover of \(Z\), which needs a spin structure, and for \(\Psi=\rho(u)\psi(y)\) with \(\gamma^au_a{}^b=\rho(u)\gamma^b\rho(u)^{-1}\), one has \(\gamma^a\mathbb D_a\Psi=\rho(u)\gamma^be_b{}^\mu(\partial_\mu+\tfrac14\omega_{\mu cd}\gamma^c\gamma^d)\psi\) at \(\mathsf t=A=0\), the Dirac operator with this connection, and an element of \(\mathcal A\) in any representation of \(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\) sees \(\nabla^a\nabla_a\). A constant \(c\)-number \(\Theta_{ab}\) as in Equation~\eqref{eq:NC} is also available on the flat bundle, through \(A_a\propto\Theta_{ab}(u^{-1}y)^b\), which leaves \(K\) unchanged and breaks the global diagonal Lorentz group to the stabiliser of \(\Theta\); curved backgrounds have operator-valued commutators, and the scale of Equation~\eqref{eq:LUV-def} is then not defined by them.

\emph{Matrix equation.} The trace of the action diverges on these operators, as stated in~\cite{HanadaKawaiKimura2006}. A heat-kernel regulated action is given for the Euclidean \(\mathrm{Spin}(n)\) form in~\cite{Hanada2006}, with leading curvature term \(\mathsf R_{abcd}\mathsf R^{abcd}\) and not the Einstein--Hilbert action; that regulator depends on the background and breaks the higher-spin gauge symmetries. No action is used here: read the field equation as stationarity against variations of finite rank. From the two relations alone, \([\mathbb D^a,[\mathbb D_a,\mathbb D_b]]\) equals
\[
\bigl(-\mathbb D^a\mathsf T_{ab}{}^e+\mathsf T_{ab}{}^c\mathsf T^a{}_c{}^e+\mathsf R_{ab}{}^{ae}\bigr)\mathbb D_e+\tfrac12\bigl(\mathbb D^a\mathsf R_{ab}{}^{de}-\mathsf T_{ab}{}^c\mathsf R^a{}_c{}^{de}\bigr)\mathcal S_{de}+\bigl(\mathbb D^a\mathsf F_{ab}-\mathsf T_{ab}{}^c\mathsf F^a{}_c\bigr),
\]
each part checked at random unipotent frames and connections unrelated to each other (exact on jets; the first two in dimensions three and four, the third in dimension three); the first two brackets are given in~\cite{FurutaHanadaKawaiKimura2007}, with \(\mathsf T\) of the opposite sign. Since \(\mathbb D_e\) and \(\mathcal S_{de}\) are independent, each bracket vanishes separately. For \(\mathsf T=0\) the first is the Ricci tensor, \(\mathsf R_{ab}{}^{ae}=-\mathrm{Ric}_b{}^e\), the second follows from it by the Bianchi identity, and the third is \(\nabla^a\mathsf F_{ab}=0\): in the torsion-free sector the matrix equation is \(\mathrm{Ric}=0\) with a free Maxwell field, the result of~\cite{HanadaKawaiKimura2006}. This was checked as an operator identity on the bundle in exact arithmetic for Ricci-flat pp-waves, Schwarzschild and Kasner, and for non-Ricci-flat pp-waves, \(S^2\times\mathbb R^2\) and de Sitter space, where \([\mathbb D^a,[\mathbb D_a,\mathbb D_b]]=-\mathrm{Ric}_b{}^e\mathbb D_e\) with the Ricci tensor computed separately. No bilinear term is needed for these solutions, and the equation holds with its second-order form, so the order count of the paragraph on the tree-level equations of the level-one amplitudes, whose hypotheses include the orbit, does not arise. With the bilinear term of the conjugate-coordinate class, \(\mathrm{Ric}_{ab}=-\mu^2\eta_{ab}\), the massive case of~\cite{HanadaKawaiKimura2006,FurutaHanadaKawaiKimura2007}, in which maximally symmetric spaces are solutions. The Lie algebra identity is common to the two classes: the left-invariant fields \(E_{AB}\) on \(g\in\mathrm{SO}(4,1)\), with \((E_{AB})^C{}_D=\delta^C_A\eta_{BD}-\delta^C_B\eta_{AD}\), obey under the Lie bracket the same \(\{T_a,\{T^a,T^b\}\}=-3T^b\) for \(T_a=E_{a4}\). With \(\hat X_a=\mathrm iE_{a4}\) and \(\mathcal S_{ab}=E_{ab}\) this is again \(\mu^2=-3\), now with base functions \(Y^A=g^A{}_4\), annihilated by \(\mathcal S_{ab}\), and \(\eta^{ab}g^A{}_ag^B{}_b=\eta^{AB}-Y^AY^B\): unit de Sitter space, with \(\mathrm{Ric}_{ab}=3\eta_{ab}\). How the minimal representation sits in the regular one is not examined. On a background where the bivector map of \(\mathsf R\) is invertible, the generators are themselves built from commutators, \(\mathcal S_{cd}\propto(\mathsf R^{-1})_{cd}{}^{ab}[\mathbb D_a,\mathbb D_b]\) at \(\mathsf T=\mathsf F=0\): rank six on the sampled points of Schwarzschild and Kasner, rank two on the Ricci-flat pp-wave and zero on flat space.

\emph{Spectrum about flat space (exact, linearised, without a norm).} With \(\mathbb D_a\to\mathbb D_a-h_a{}^c\mathbb D_c+\tfrac12\omega_a{}^{cd}\mathcal S_{cd}\), for \(q^2\neq0\) the kernel is the ten gauge directions (rank 30 of 40, at sampled \(q\)). At null \(q\) there are fourteen further solutions. Two have \(\mathsf T=0\) and are the transverse-traceless polarisations, simple zeros at \(q^2=0\). Twelve have torsion, one of them with totally antisymmetric torsion, which agrees in type with the propagating rank-three field noted in~\cite{HanadaKawaiKimura2006}, whose remark mentions no other torsion mode. In~\cite{FurutaHanadaKawaiKimura2007} the antisymmetric and trace parts of the torsion are read as the field strength of a \(B\)-field and the gradient of a dilaton, and the other components are found to propagate as well. Six of the twelve lie at the head of a chain of length two along two transversal lines through the null \(q\), with no longer chain: there the operator has a double zero at \(q^2=0\), with solutions linear in \(y\) times the wave. These six heads have \(\mathsf R=0\) and \(\mathsf T\neq0\). So the set \(\mathsf T=0\) solves the equations and carries only the two simple zeros, while the first-order class is not torsion-free as a set of solutions. No action is used here, so no sign of a norm follows here for these modes, and this is not a statement about an Ostrogradsky ghost in the sense of Section~\ref{sec:status-N}. The count with multiplicity, \(8+2\cdot6=20\), equals the number of physical components \(h_i{}^\mu\) and \(\omega_i{}^{cd}\), with \(i\) transverse, in~\cite{FurutaHanadaKawaiKimura2007}, where those with an odd number of time indices are expected, without an action, to have negative norm, and a mass or a constraint is suggested to remove them; ghost issues of this route are also noted in~\cite{Steinacker2019NoGhost}. The double zeros are not mentioned in~\cite{FurutaHanadaKawaiKimura2007}; the split is a short consequence of the block-triangular form of its linear equations, in which the connection sources the frame and not conversely.

\emph{Sources.} Fields of order zero do not source the Ricci tensor at tree level: for \(\hat X^i=\phi^i\otimes L^i\) with \(\phi^i\in\mathcal A\) and fixed Hermitian \(L^i\), the term \([\hat X^i,[\hat X_i,\hat X_b]]\) is of order zero, and \(\mathsf F_{ab}\) enters only the order-zero part, so the first bracket is unchanged and \(\mathrm{Ric}=0\) stands at \(\mathsf T=0\), and likewise for fermion bilinears with \(\Psi\) in \(\mathcal A\). This agrees with the statement of~\cite{HanadaKawaiKimura2006} that the classical equation has no energy-momentum term. It is not a general obstruction: at \(\mathsf T\neq0\) the first bracket makes the torsion a source of the Ricci tensor, and with further matrices that are first-order operators along a compact factor, the same identity gives \(\mathrm{Ric}^{(D)}=0\), whose reduction carries the stress of the Kaluza--Klein fields. For five matrices, \(\mathrm ds^2=\mathrm dU(\mathrm dV+c_0x^2\mathrm dU)+\mathrm dx^2+\mathrm dz^2+(\mathrm dw+f_0x\,\mathrm dU)^2\) solves the matrix equation exactly when \(c_0=-f_0^2/2\), that is \(\mathrm{Ric}^{(4)}_{ab}=\tfrac12F_{ac}F_b{}^c\) (exact). This fixes no \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\), which needs an action or a loop. In~\cite{HoKawaiSteinacker2025} the emergence of general relativity with matrices as momenta is described, with a reference to~\cite{HanadaKawaiKimura2006}, as not having been very successful so far, with no reason given in that sentence. That paper treats commutative backgrounds through bilocal fields, and says of expansions in differential operators, in an analysis without this fibre, that the time component of each tensor field has negative norm and that the action and the inner product are ultraviolet divergent; for noncommutative backgrounds it says that the classical action cannot be interpreted as the Einstein--Hilbert action and attributes that term to one loop. This is a caution on the route, and the result above does not answer it: it is at the level of the equation of motion, with sources, the regulated trace and the selection of the class untreated.

These statements are for Equation~\eqref{eq:Smatrix} with \(C_{\mu\nu\lambda}=0\), tree level, the stated representations and first-order operators, the identities being exact at the sampled points and in the two relations. Nothing here derives that the matrix integral selects this class or its algebra \(\mathcal A\), or the frame and its non-degeneracy, which are properties of the class and are not derived from dynamics, so Assumptions~\ref{ass:frame-lock}, \ref{ass:nondeg} and~\ref{ass:lorentzian} are not discharged; Assumption~\ref{ass:local-lorentz}, a statement about the action~\eqref{eq:Smicro}, is not derived, since this class is not shown to reproduce Presentation~B. Domains of the unbounded operators, the regulated trace, the norm and masses of the torsion modes, higher-order and non-equivariant fluctuations, the bilocal modes of End of the Hilbert space, loops, supersymmetry and a value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\) are not treated, and the frame of this class is not identified with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}. Problems~D, F and~N remain open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\paragraph{First-order equivariant class about flat space: the pairing of the action, commutant labels and the double zeros (conditional; not closure).}
Take the flat point of the preceding class, \(\hat X_a=\mathrm iu_a{}^b\partial_b\) on \(L^2(\mathbb R^4\times\mathrm{SO}(3,1))\) with the reference measure, \(C_{\mu\nu\lambda}=0\), no matter term, at quadratic order in fluctuations. With \(p_b\) the eigenvalue of \(-\mathrm i\partial_b\), \(\hat X_a\) is multiplication by \(-u_a{}^bp_b\), and \((p,u)\mapsto(\tilde p=up,u)\) has unit Jacobian, so \(\hat X_a\) is unitarily equivalent to \(-\tilde p_a\otimes1\) on \(L^2(\mathbb R^4)\otimes L^2(\mathrm{SO}(3,1))\). Uniformly distributed \(p_{(a)}\) of this kind appear for \(\mathrm{Spin}(n)\) in~\cite{Hanada2006}, which then applies the quenched reduced model. At this point the matrices have joint spectrum \(\mathbb R^4\) with uniform multiplicity: apart from that multiplicity they do not distinguish the class from a commuting background without a fibre, and the algebra \(\mathcal A\) remains data. For Hermitian Hilbert--Schmidt fluctuations \(A_a\), with kernels between points \(m=(p,u)\) and \(m'=(p',u')\), let \(Q_a(m,m')=-u_a{}^bp_b+u'_a{}^bp'_b\), which is real. Equation~\eqref{eq:Smatrix} gives
\[
S_2=\frac1{2g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2}\,\eta^{ab}\mathrm{Tr}\bigl(A_a(\mathcal EA)_b\bigr),\qquad
(\mathcal EA)_b(m,m')=Q\cdot Q\,A_b(m,m')-Q_b\,Q^aA_a(m,m'),
\]
and the same form holds exactly for commuting \(N\times N\) matrices, with \(Q_a\) the differences of eigenvalues. So \(\mathcal EA=0\) at non-null \(Q\) forces \(A_a\propto Q_a\), which is the gauge direction; at null \(Q\neq0\) it forces \(Q\cdot A=0\), and the form \(\eta^{ab}\bar A_aA_b\) is positive definite on \(Q^\perp/Q\) at each pair \((m,m')\). Modulo the gauge directions \(A_a=Q_a\Lambda\), on which the form has the value \(Q\cdot Q\,|\Lambda|^2\) of either sign, the form is indefinite on these kernels only off shell and at \(Q=0\), the moduli of the commuting set. This statement concerns the signature, that is the relative sign of modes; the overall sign, relative to matter and to the weight of the integral, is not addressed. In background gauge the operator is multiplication by \(Q\cdot Q\): on Hilbert--Schmidt kernels it is self-adjoint, with no eigenvector and no Jordan block, and every probe with polarisation of positive norm has a positive spectral measure. At finite \(N\) this pairing is the trace form in which the action is written, so no regulator enters there; the truncations of first-order operators below are a choice. For a commuting background without a fibre, positivity in the bilocal description is stated in~\cite{HoKawaiSteinacker2025} for the planar sector with quenched diagonal elements, and a no-ghost theorem based on the same kind of inner product is given in~\cite{Steinacker2019NoGhost} on a different, noncommutative background.

The first-order operators sit on this space as singular kernels at \(u=u'\), and they can be labelled. The operators commuting with all \(\hat X_a\) include the functions of \(u\), the \(\mathcal P_g=-\mathrm i\partial_g\) and, for \(\lambda\in\mathfrak{so}(3,1)\), the derivation \(\mathcal J_\lambda=\tfrac12\lambda^{cd}u^{r}{}_cu^{s}{}_d\mathcal S_{rs}-y_g\lambda^{gc}\partial_c\), which generates a unitary right translation of \(u\) combined with a Lorentz transformation of \(y\). The last two are the right-invariant fields on \(Z=\mathrm{ISO}(3,1)\); the identification of the flat bundle with the Poincar\'e group, and the commuting right action on a bundle that is a Lie group, are in~\cite{Hanada2006}. If \(\mathcal C\) commutes with all \(\hat X_a\), then the linearised operator obeys \(\mathcal E_b[\tfrac12\{f,\mathcal C\}]=\tfrac12\{\mathcal E_b[f],\mathcal C\}\) for every \(f\), since it is built from \(\mathrm{ad}\,\hat X_a\) and \(\mathrm{ad}\,[\hat X_a,\hat X_b]\). In section values, write
\[
h_b{}^c=f_b{}^c+y_gf_b{}^{gc},\qquad\omega_b{}^{cd}=f_b{}^{cd},
\]
with arbitrary functions \(f_b{}^c\), \(f_b{}^{cd}=-f_b{}^{dc}\) of \(y\), and let \(F\) denote \(\partial_af_b-\partial_bf_a\) on the first index of each. Then, exactly and for arbitrary functions,
\[
\mathsf T_{ab}{}^c=F_{ab}{}^c+y_gF_{ab}{}^{gc},\qquad\mathsf R_{ab}{}^{cd}=F_{ab}{}^{cd},
\]
and with \(\mathcal E^{(1)}{}_b{}^e=-\partial^a\mathsf T_{ab}{}^e+\mathsf R_{ab}{}^{ae}\), \(\mathcal E^{(2)}{}_b{}^{de}=\partial^a\mathsf R_{ab}{}^{de}\), \(\mathcal E^{(3)}{}_b=\partial^a(\partial_a\mathsf t_b-\partial_b\mathsf t_a)\), the linearised brackets of the matrix equation,
\[
\mathcal E^{(2)}{}_b{}^{de}=\partial^aF_{ab}{}^{de},\qquad
\mathcal E^{(1)}{}_b{}^e+y_g\mathcal E^{(2)}{}_b{}^{ge}=-\partial^aF_{ab}{}^e,\qquad
2\mathcal E^{(3)}{}_b=\partial_c\mathcal E^{(1)}{}_b{}^c-\eta_{bg}\mathcal E^{(2)}{}_c{}^{gc}.
\]
So the forty equations about flat space are ten Maxwell equations, one for each label in \(\mathfrak{iso}(3,1)\), with \(A_a\) an eleventh, and with one gauge parameter each, the count \(6+4+1\) of the class; each label has two polarisations, so \(22=20+2\). Here \(\mathsf T\) and \(\mathsf R\) are the linearised torsion and curvature, which in the gauge theory of the Poincar\'e group are read as the translational and Lorentz field strengths~\cite{Hehl2017}. For fixed label all zeros at \(q^2=0\) are simple: \(\mathcal E_b[e^{\mathrm iq\cdot y}\mathcal C]\) with \(\mathcal C=\mathcal P_g\) or \(\mathcal J_\lambda\) is \(q^2\) times the mode on polarisations transverse to \(q\). The double zeros of the preceding paragraph are a statement at fixed eigenvalue of the translations and they stand as such; they are the nilpotent action \([\mathcal P_g,\mathcal J_\lambda]\propto\lambda_g{}^c\mathcal P_c\) of translations on the labels, whose rank at null \(q\) is that of \(\lambda\mapsto q_g\lambda^{gc}\), three, for each of the two polarisations. The redefinition contains \(y\) explicitly, so it is not translation invariant and it does not remove the solutions linear in \(y\). Those solutions are \(e^{\mathrm ik\cdot y}\mathcal J_\lambda\) at null \(k\), the derivative at \(t=0\) of \(e^{\mathrm ik\cdot y}\exp(t\mathcal J_\lambda)\), and these are unitary operators on the Hilbert space that, times a transverse polarisation \(u_a{}^r\varepsilon_r\), solve the same equation for all \(t\). The argument carries over where \(Z\) is a group with left-invariant background: on \(\mathrm{SO}(4,1)\) with the bilinear term the labels are \(\mathfrak{so}(4,1)\) and the identity \(\mathbb 1\), and every equivariant first-order fluctuation is a sum of terms \(\tfrac12\{f_a,\mathcal C\}\) with \(f_a\) a dressed one-form, so the same order-zero equation \(\nabla^a\mathsf F_{ab}=0\) of the preceding paragraph governs each label on the unit de Sitter space.

Each label retains its gauge parameter, so these modes stay massless at this order; that the fields of the expansion in differential operators are massless at tree level is stated in~\cite{Sakai2019}. The first two brackets of the matrix equation contain neither \(\mathsf F_{ab}\) nor order-zero fields, so the constant \(\Theta_{ab}\) of the preceding paragraph and singlets of a fixed factor do not change the linear equations of \(h\) and \(\omega\). The torsion modes are therefore not removed by anything found here. That is a bounded statement: quadratic order, background torsion zero, operators of the stated order and the commutant labels above.

The pairing of a label is not contracted with \(\eta\). A kernel \(A_a(m,m')=u_a{}^r\varepsilon_r\mathcal O(m,m')\), with \(u\) the fibre point of \(m\) and \(\eta^{rs}\varepsilon_r\varepsilon_s=1\), has pairing \(\eta^{ab}\mathrm{Tr}(A_a^\dagger A_b)=\int|\mathcal O|^2\), whatever \(\mathcal O\) is, and if \(\mathcal O\) carries nonzero translation charge then \(\eta^{ab}\mathrm{Tr}(A_aA_b)=0\), so the Hermitian part of \(A_a\) has half of that value. For \(\mathcal O\) a truncation of \(\tfrac12\{f,\mathcal P_g\}\) with a positive weight \(w(p)\) of unit total mass and finite second moments, and \(f\) slowly varying on the scale of \(w\), the pairing of the truncations for \(g\) and \(h\) is \(\int|f|^2\,\mathsf G_{gh}\) with \(\mathsf G_{gh}=\int w\,p_gp_h\), up to a positive fibre factor common to all \(g,h\) and infrared volumes. The matrix \(\mathsf G\) is positive semi-definite, positive definite unless \(w\) lies on a hyperplane through the origin, and a Lorentz transformation of the weight changes it by a congruence. It is never a nonzero multiple of \(\eta_{gh}\), since \(\mathsf G_{00}\ge0\) and \(\mathsf G_{11}\ge0\) while \(\eta_{00}\) and \(\eta_{11}\) have opposite signs. So in this pairing \(\omega_i{}^{0j}\), with \(i\) transverse in this sense and \(\mathcal O\) a truncation of \(f\mathcal J_\lambda\), has a positive value, the boost part of \(\omega_i\) included, and for \(w\) not on such a hyperplane so do \(h_i{}^0\) and the direction \(h_i{}^\mu\propto q^\mu\), whose value is not zero. This differs from the reading in~\cite{FurutaHanadaKawaiKimura2007}, where those components are expected to be negative were the equation derived from a field theory action, and from the statements in~\cite{HoKawaiSteinacker2025} on tensor fields, that the time component of each tensor field has negative norm and that, in a minimal model without a fibre, the transverse zero-norm components decouple; that reference records that this is very different from the positivity of bilocal fields and that there is no clear correspondence between the bilocal and the tensor inner products. The pairing here is of the bilocal kind, so this is a tension between readings and not a contradiction, and the truncation is one regulator-dependent map from that pairing to a form on the label coefficients, not a derivation of the tensor inner product. It does not settle the question and does not show that negative norm is absent for effective fields: the form diverges as the cutoff is removed, it depends on the frame and on \(w\), it is not an invariant form on \(\mathfrak{iso}(3,1)\), which has no positive invariant form, and the kinetic term of an effective field, such as one induced at one loop, need not equal it. The published caution therefore stands for the covariant reading.

The double zero itself can be followed. Fix a null \(k\), a one-parameter subgroup \(\Lambda_t=\exp(t\lambda)\) with \(k\cdot\lambda\neq0\) and \(\phi^2=e^{-t^2/\sigma^2}/(\sigma\sqrt\pi)\), and take the kernels \(e^{\mathrm ik\cdot y}\,w(\mathcal P)^{1/2}\int\mathrm dt\,\psi(t)R_{\Lambda_t}\), with \(R\) the right translation of \(u\) alone, not \(\exp(t\mathcal J_\lambda)\), on which the background-gauge operator is multiplication by \((k+(1-\Lambda_t)p)^2\). Let \(v^g\) be defined by \(\partial_t(k+(1-\Lambda_t)p)^2|_{t=0}=v^gp_g\), which is \(\pm2(k\cdot\lambda)^g\). The probes with \(\psi=\phi\), multiplied by \(v\cdot\mathcal P\) and normalised, and with \(\psi=\sqrt2\sigma\phi'\), are orthonormal, and they are cutoffs of the frame and fibre parts. The compression of the operator to their span is
\[
\begin{pmatrix}O(\sigma^2)&\varkappa\\\varkappa&O(\sigma^2)\end{pmatrix},\qquad
\varkappa=-\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt2}\sqrt{v^g\mathsf G_{gh}v^h}\,\bigl(1+O(\sigma^2)\bigr),
\]
as \(\sigma\to0\) at fixed \(w\), so its eigenvalues are real, with orthonormal eigenvectors: the resolvent of this compression has two simple poles with residues of one sign, which merge as \(\sigma\to0\). These are not poles of the two-point function of the probes, whose spectral measure under the full multiplication operator is positive and continuous. The entries of this compression are evaluated exactly on a Gaussian \(w\) for one boost and for one rotation on its covering line (\(\varkappa=-\sqrt2\sigma P\,(1+O(\sigma^2))\) for the Gaussian \(w\) of variance \(P^2\) in each component, \(v\) of Euclidean norm \(2\)), with a seeded Monte Carlo agreeing; smearing over the other five fibre directions is not treated. A Jordan block is read only in the basis where the second probe is divided by \(\sqrt2\sigma\), whose squared norm \(1/(2\sigma^2)\) diverges. The limits do not commute: the spectral width is of order \(\sigma|k|P+\sigma^2P^2\), and removing the momentum cutoff first spreads the measure.

None of this extends automatically. On any background \((\mathcal MA)_b=[\hat X^a,[\hat X_a,A_b]]-2[[\hat X_a,\hat X_b],A^a]\) is symmetric for \(\eta^{ab}\mathrm{Tr}(A_a^\dagger B_b)\), which forces a neutral eigenvector at any Jordan block, and it is symmetric for the Hilbert--Schmidt form when \(\mathrm{ad}[\hat X_0,\hat X_i]=0\). On a curved first-order background \([\hat X_a,\hat X_b]\) contains \(\mathsf R_{ab}{}^{cd}\mathcal S_{cd}\), and without vector fields commuting with all \(\mathbb D_a\) the first-order labels are absent. For random Hermitian \(3\times3\) matrices, not solutions, with \(\hat X_0\to\tau\hat X_0\), a complex pair forms at small \(\tau\) (about \(0.009\) at the seed used), with the two colliding eigenvectors tending to neutral (numerical). So the positivity of the pairing on shell and the absence of Jordan blocks on Hilbert--Schmidt kernels found above hold about commuting backgrounds and are not claimed for curved ones.

These statements are for Equation~\eqref{eq:Smatrix} with \(C_{\mu\nu\lambda}=0\), quadratic order about commuting matrices and the group cases named, the formal adjoints and kernels stated, with domains of the unbounded operators and the infrared volumes untreated. They do not derive that the matrix integral selects this class, or the frame and its non-degeneracy, so Assumptions~\ref{ass:frame-lock}, \ref{ass:nondeg}, \ref{ass:lorentzian} and~\ref{ass:local-lorentz} are not discharged. They are not a statement on the graviton two-point function of Section~\ref{sec:status-N} and do not discharge Assumption~\ref{ass:EFT-a2}: loops, masses of the labels, the dynamics of the diagonal elements, background torsion, sources, supersymmetry and a value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\) are not treated, and the frame of this class is not identified with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}. Problems~D, F and~N remain open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\paragraph{First-order equivariant class: one loop about commuting matrices, the reflected form on labels and the sign of the torsion modes (conditional; bounded no-go).}
The preceding paragraph leaves open which form governs the label modes. Two candidates are tested here at the flat point: a form induced at one loop, and the form that a reflection symmetry of the matrix action assigns to them.

\emph{One loop.} Supply the content of~\cite{IKKT1997}, an input since Equation~\eqref{eq:Smatrix} is bosonic: the ten-component vector with weight \(1\), the ghosts with weight \(-2\) and one sixteen-component Weyl spinor with weight \(-\tfrac12\), with the operators \(H_i=-\Box_{\hat X}\otimes1+\Sigma_i^{ab}\otimes\mathrm{ad}[\hat X_a,\hat X_b]\) of the paragraph on the supertrace at an arbitrary matrix fluctuation in Section~\ref{sec:status-F}, now for operators or finite matrices rather than Poisson brackets, and let \(\Gamma_\varsigma=\tfrac12\sum_iw_i\operatorname{Tr}\ln(H_i+\varsigma)\) with one shift \(\varsigma\) common to all species, in background Feynman gauge off shell, at vanishing fermion background and without the phase of the fermion determinant. With diagonal fermion zero modes, integrating out the off-diagonal fermions gives a nonzero quadratic term in the off-diagonal \(A\)~\cite{AokiIsoKawaiKitazawaTada1998}. The species identities of the paragraph on the supertrace, \(\sum_iw_id_i=0\) and \(\sum_iw_i\operatorname{tr}(\Sigma_i\cdots\Sigma_i)=0\) with up to three factors, concern constant matrices, and the Duhamel expansion needs no commutation of its operators. For \(\hat X_a=\hat X^{(0)}_a+A_a\) with \([\hat X^{(0)}_a,\hat X^{(0)}_b]=0\) each commutator is of first order in \(A\), so
\[
\Gamma_\varsigma[\hat X^{(0)}+A]-\Gamma_\varsigma[\hat X^{(0)}]=O(A^4)
\]
at finite \(N\) and fixed \(\varsigma\neq0\), for all kernels \(A_a(m,m')\) and all \(Q\), and formally for operators. The cancellation to third order in the commutators is the operator-level statement of~\cite{IKKT1997}, with the first nonvanishing term at fourth order also in~\cite{Steinacker2023}; the equation above is its corollary for commuting backgrounds. At \(N=3\), with random data, the difference scales with exponent \(4.0\) in the amplitude for the Euclidean contraction at \(\varsigma=0.3\) and \(1\), and for the Lorentzian \(\eta\) with complex \(\varsigma\) (numerical); the coefficient depends on \(\varsigma\): at the data tested it grows as \(\varsigma^{-2}\) when \(\varsigma\to0\), through the modes with \(Q=0\), and the exponent \(4.0\) is reached only for amplitudes small compared with \(\varsigma^{1/2}\) (numerical), so the limits \(A\to0\) and \(\varsigma\to0\) do not commute and nothing is stated at \(\varsigma=0\). This trace includes the modes with \(Q=0\), which the standard one-loop object excludes as zero modes~\cite{IKKT1997,AokiIsoKawaiKitazawaTada1998}; no statement is made about that object. So at one loop, with this content and a common regulator at fixed \(\varsigma\neq0\), the second variation about commuting matrices vanishes, and no mass, kinetic form or term of higher order in \(Q\) is induced on the labels at second order in the fluctuation; the form at this order remains the tree form. About commuting matrices this gives at all \(Q\) the vanishing, at this content, of the mass term found in~\cite{Sakai2017} in the semiclassical limit at vanishing base momentum, with coefficient proportional to \(d-2-2^{[d/2]-\kappa}\), which vanishes for \(d=10\), \(\kappa=2\), and which that reference finds positive when bosons exceed fermions. The vanishing at all \(Q\) uses the content: with \(\sum_iw_id_i=0\) alone (\(d=4\), one Majorana spinor) the second-order term is nonzero (exponent \(2.0\)), and without fermions there is a term linear in the diagonal part of \(A\), the potential of the eigenvalues, and a second-order term for off-diagonal \(A\). The mass from quantum corrections suggested in~\cite{FurutaHanadaKawaiKimura2007} therefore does not arise at this order about commuting matrices with this content and regulator. A second-order term requires nonzero commutators of the background: for four commuting matrices tensored with \(\mathbb 1\) on \(\mathbb C^2\), three further matrices \(\mathbb 1\otimes\sigma^i/2\) and \(A\) along the first four, the exponent is \(2.0\) at this content. There the second-order terms are those of the factorisation in the paragraph on product backgrounds, and their value on the labels is not computed.

\emph{Commutant labels at nonzero torsion.} If \(\mathcal C\) commutes with all \(\hat X_a\) on any background, the adjoint compatibility of the preceding paragraph gives \(\mathcal E_b[\tfrac12\{f,\mathcal C\}]=0\) for constant \(f\), since \(\mathcal E_b\) is built from \(\mathrm{ad}\,\hat X_a\) and \(\mathrm{ad}[\hat X_a,\hat X_b]\); a commutant label therefore acquires no term without a derivative of \(f\) in its linearised equation, background torsion included. Such a term can come only from a mode that stops being commutant. On \(\hat X_a=\mathrm iu_a{}^bE_b\), with \(E_b\) left-invariant fields on a four-dimensional Lie group, structure constants \(\mathsf c_{ab}{}^d\) and adjoint matrices \(\mathrm{ad}_a\), one has \(\mathsf R_{ab}{}^{cd}=0\) and \(\mathsf T\) built from \(\mathsf c\), and the matrix equation becomes \(\eta^{ac}\mathrm{ad}_a\mathrm{ad}_c=0\), which has the form of the equation \(h_{ab}[t^a,[t^b,t^c]]=0\) of~\cite{Liao2026} for a form \(h\) on the algebra, with the indices placed oppositely there; Hermiticity constants change no commutator. An operator commuting with all \(\hat X_a\) that is a right translation of \(u\) by \(\lambda\) plus a vector field \(V_\lambda\) on the group needs \([E_b,V_\lambda]=\lambda_b{}^cE_c\), so \(\lambda\) must be a derivation of the algebra. For \(E_0-E_3\) central, \([E_0+E_3,E_1]=E_2\) and \([E_0+E_3,E_2]=-E_1\) (a null rotation generator, as discussed in~\cite{Liao2026}), these \(\lambda\) in \(\mathfrak{so}(3,1)\) form a line, and for \([E_0,E_2]=E_2\), \([E_0,E_3]=E_3\), \([E_1,E_2]=E_2\), \([E_1,E_3]=-E_3\) only \(\lambda=0\) (exact), against six at \(\mathsf c=0\). Whether the remaining modes acquire a mass term of definite sign is not computed.

\emph{Reflected form.} Use the Euclidean contraction and the compact fibre \(\mathrm{SO}(4)\), as for \(\mathrm{Spin}(n)\) in~\cite{Hanada2006}, so that \(\hat X_a=-u_a{}^bp_b\) on \(L^2(\mathbb R^4\times\mathrm{SO}(4))\). Let \(\theta=\mathrm{diag}(-1,1,1,1)\), with entries \(\theta_r\), \(U_\theta\psi(p,u)=\psi(\theta p,\theta u\theta)\) and \(\mathcal I[A]_a=\theta_a{}^cU_\theta A_cU_\theta^{-1}\). Then \(U_\theta\hat X_aU_\theta^{-1}=\theta_a{}^c\hat X_c\), and \(\mathcal I\) is a unitary involution of the Hilbert--Schmidt kernels which preserves the background and the action of Equation~\eqref{eq:Smatrix} at \(C_{\mu\nu\lambda}=0\). The form \(\mathrm{HS}(A,\mathcal I[B])\) is therefore indefinite and nondegenerate, with the Hilbert--Schmidt form as its majorant in the sense of Krein spaces. Let \(w\ge0\) be a function of the mean momentum, invariant under \(\theta\), of unit total mass and finite second moments, and define for \(r,g=0,\dots,3\), with the kernel \(A_a\) between \(m=(\bar p+q/2,u)\) and \(m'=(\bar p-q/2,u)\),
\[
\phi_r{}^g(y)=\int\mathrm d\mathrm{Haar}(u)\int\mathrm d^4\bar p\,\frac{\mathrm d^4q}{(2\pi)^4}\;e^{\mathrm iq\cdot y}\,\bar p_g\,w(\bar p)^{1/2}\,u^a{}_rA_a.
\]
A gauge variation \(A_a=Q_a\Lambda\) shifts \(\phi_r{}^g\) by a multiple of the gradient \(\partial_r\Lambda^g\), with \(\Lambda^g\) the same projection of \(\Lambda\), so \(\partial_r\phi_s{}^g-\partial_s\phi_r{}^g\) is invariant at this order, and \(\phi_r{}^g\) evaluated on \(\mathcal I[A]\) is \(\theta_r\theta_g\,\phi_r{}^g(\theta y)\) evaluated on \(A\). The Gaussian integral of the background-gauge form gives, for \(y\neq y'\),
\[
\bigl\langle\phi_r{}^g(y)\,\phi_s{}^h(y')\bigr\rangle=g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2\,\mathcal V\,\delta_{rs}\,\mathsf G_{gh}\,\frac1{4\pi^2|y-y'|^2},
\]
with \(\mathsf G_{gh}=\int w\,p_gp_h\) and \(\mathcal V\) a positive factor common to all indices that contains the fibre delta at \(u=u'\), so it is singular; smearing the fibre to make it finite changes \(Q\), and that case is not treated. For a test function supported at \(y^0>0\) the reflected form of \(\phi_r{}^g\) is therefore \(\theta_r\theta_g\mathsf G_{gg}\) times the reflection-positive form of the massless scalar, and that of the invariant field strength with spatial \(r,s\) is \(\theta_g\mathsf G_{gg}\) times the reflection-positive form of the Maxwell field. So on these gauge-invariant probes with \(g=0\) and \(\mathsf G_{00}>0\) the form is negative for generic test functions: the Euclidean flat point with the reflection \(\mathcal I\) does not satisfy the positivity of~\cite{OsterwalderSchrader1973,OsterwalderSchrader1975} on them at tree level. For a weight invariant under \(\mathrm O(4)\), \(\mathsf G_{gh}=c\,\delta_{gh}\) with \(c>0\), and the reflected form on the translations is \(c\,\theta_g\delta_{gh}\), which is \(c\,\eta_{gh}\). For the probes \(\int\mathrm d\Lambda\,\psi_\lambda(\Lambda)R_\Lambda\,w^{1/2}\) of the fibre directions, with \(R_\Lambda\) the right translation of \(u\), \(\psi_\lambda=\tfrac12\lambda^{cd}(\ln\Lambda)_{cd}\,\rho\) and \(\rho\) supported near the identity and invariant under conjugation by \(\mathrm O(4)\), the Gram matrix is a positive multiple of \(\tfrac12\lambda_{cd}\lambda'^{cd}\), the only invariant, and \(\mathcal I\) acts by \(\lambda\mapsto\theta\lambda\theta\), so the reflected pairing of these kernels is the Lorentz invariant form on \(\mathfrak{so}(3,1)\) with boosts negative. A two-point sign is not stated for them at finite smearing, since \(Q\) then depends on \(p\).

The same relation holds for the real Lorentzian fibre of the preceding paragraph. For a Gaussian weight of variance \(P^2\) per component in the rest frame of a unit timelike \(n\), \(\mathsf G_{gh}=P^2(\eta_{gh}+2n_gn_h)\), which is positive and depends on \(n\), and with \(\theta_n\) the reflection along \(n\) one has \(\mathsf G_{gh}(\theta_n)^h{}_k=P^2\eta_{gk}\), independent of \(n\) (exact, also after a boost). So for this weight the positive form of the preceding paragraph is a majorant of the covariant form, the two being related by the involution \(U_{\theta_n}\) on kernels; moments not of this form give no multiple of \(\eta\). This locates the tension recorded in the preceding paragraph. The components with an odd number of time indices, which~\cite{FurutaHanadaKawaiKimura2007} calls negative modes although no action is used there, and does not expect to have positive norm were the equation derived from a field theory action, are those odd under \(\mathcal I\); here their sign is computed for the reflected form at tree level with the matrix action, and it is not a norm computed in a Lorentzian theory. In~\cite{HoKawaiSteinacker2025} the positivity for the planar sector with quenched diagonal elements is read from the large-\(N\) gauge theory with the colour factor removed, the colour degeneracy corresponding to the relative coordinate. The reading adopted here, which that reference does not state in these terms, is that this coordinate, conjugate to \(\bar p\), is inert under the reflection. The corresponding map, \(\theta_a\) with \((Q,\bar p)\mapsto(\theta Q,\bar p)\), preserves the form \(Q\cdot Q|A|^2-|Q\cdot A|^2\) and does not preserve the cubic and quartic terms at finite \(N\) (one finite model, exact matrices), so it is a symmetry of the Gaussian form and not of the matrix action. That reference records no clear correspondence between the bilocal and the tensor inner products; the reflected form supplies one for these truncations, and it does not settle which form is the physical inner product. The no-ghost theorem of~\cite{Steinacker2019NoGhost}, on a different background and established there at the semi-classical level, gives a positive-definite inner product on gauge-fixed on-shell modes modulo pure gauge; it does not conflict with a tree-level sign on probes at this background.

\emph{Bearing on Section~\ref{sec:status-E}, and the relaxations.} The statement above bounds one route to Lorentzian signature, reconstruction from a Euclidean integral. That route is not asserted in that section. A Lorentzian matrix integral with a convergence regulator is a different starting point, and its relation to the Euclidean model is disputed. At finite \(N\), in the limit and under the hypotheses recorded in Section~\ref{sec:status-E} (proved at \(N=2\), conditional for \(N\ge3\)), its polynomial moments of low degree are those of the Euclidean model up to phases, so the reflection statement is not excluded from bearing on it in that limit; for \(N\to\infty\) taken first, or for a fixed positive mass, no such relation is stated. The hypotheses of the reflection statement are the Euclidean contraction, a commuting background with a fibre, tree level, reflection by \(\mathcal I\) and probes of the translation labels. Relaxing them gives the following. (i) \emph{Singlets.} For uniform diagonal elements, quenching and planar diagrams the matrix model is the dimensionally reduced gauge theory~\cite{HoKawaiSteinacker2025}. If its singlet correlators in four dimensions satisfy the positivity, which is taken from field theory and not checked here, a Lorentzian signature there is an output of reconstruction and not a chosen rotation. That sector has no frame, and that reference places gravity in the nonplanar diagrams and the diagonal elements, so Assumption~\ref{ass:lorentzian} is not discharged. (ii) \emph{Constraint.} Take \(\eta\) on \(f_b{}^g\) and \(\tfrac12\lambda_{cd}\lambda^{cd}\) on \(f_b{}^{cd}\), at null \(q\neq0\) and for the two polarisations \(b\) transverse to \(q\). On the twenty components these forms have twelve positive and eight negative directions, the eight being those with an odd number of time indices. On \(q_gf_b{}^g=0\) and \(q_cf_b{}^{cd}=0\), the second being the kernel of the rank-three map behind the double zeros, they are nonnegative. With \(v_1,v_2\) transverse unit vectors, the kernels are spanned by \(q,v_1,v_2\) and by \(q\wedge v_1,q\wedge v_2,v_1\wedge v_2\), which gives signature \((2,0)\) with one null direction and \((1,0)\) with two null directions, so six positive and six null directions in all (also exact at three sampled \(q\)). The translation part is the constraint imposed in~\cite{HoKawaiSteinacker2025} without a fibre. Its bivector analogue, for gauge parameters as well, leaves three of six parameters at each momentum and does not close: the coordinate \(y^2\) times \(\mathrm dy^0\wedge\mathrm dy^1\) and the coordinate \(y^3\) times \(\mathrm dy^1\wedge\mathrm dy^2\) have vanishing divergence and their commutator does not. It therefore conflicts with six-parameter local rotations, and it is not derived. (iii) \emph{Mass.} The one-loop result above excludes this at second order about commuting matrices with this content; nonzero background commutators, higher loops and the dynamics of the diagonal elements remain. (iv) \emph{No selection by classical solutions.} The contraction is not selected by the equation at \(\mathsf R_{ab}{}^{cd}=0\): the second algebra above solves it for \(\eta\); \([E_0,E_2]=E_2\), \([E_0,E_3]=E_3\), \([E_1,E_2]=E_3\), \([E_1,E_3]=-E_2\) solves it for \(\delta\) and, with index \(0\) as time, not for \(\eta\), which does solve it with the time index on \(E_2\) or \(E_3\) (this is the algebra \(\mathcal A_{4,12}\) of~\cite{Chatzistavrakidis2011}, with \(X_1,X_2,X_3,X_4\) there equal to \(\mathrm i\) times \(E_2,E_3,-E_0,E_1\)); and the frame \(\partial_t\), \((\alpha-t)\partial_i\) of~\cite{IsonoTomino2010} solves it for both (exact). A classification of Lie-algebraic solutions by signature is in~\cite{Liao2026}.

These statements are for Equation~\eqref{eq:Smatrix} with \(C_{\mu\nu\lambda}=0\) and no matter term, the commuting and left-invariant backgrounds named, formal adjoints with domains and infrared volumes untreated, one common regulator, and tree level except for the first part, where the content is supplied. They do not derive that the matrix integral selects this class, its frame or its non-degeneracy, the Euclidean or the Lorentzian contraction, the weight \(w\) or the quenching, so Assumptions~\ref{ass:frame-lock}, \ref{ass:nondeg}, \ref{ass:lorentzian} and~\ref{ass:local-lorentz} are not discharged. The modes with odd labels have simple zeros at fixed label and for a Gaussian weight the form is diagonal in the label, so the sign above concerns the field content and is not a statement on an Ostrogradsky ghost in the graviton two-point function of Section~\ref{sec:status-N}; Assumption~\ref{ass:EFT-a2} is not discharged. Terms of fourth and higher order, loops beyond the first, a value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\) and the identification of the frame with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence} are not treated. Problems~D, E, F and~N remain open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\paragraph{A finite-\(N\) matrix toy for the class relations.}
On the model space of the class above, take the base to be the unit two-sphere with coframe \(\theta^1=\mathrm d\theta\), \(\theta^2=\sin\theta\,\mathrm d\varphi\), orthonormal frame \(e_1=\partial_\theta\), \(e_2=(\sin\theta)^{-1}\partial_\varphi\), and the circle group as structure group, with the Levi-Civita connection \(\omega^{12}=-\cos\theta\,\mathrm d\varphi\). The class data of this connection are \(\mathsf T_{12}{}^2=\cot\theta\), from \([e_1,e_2]=-\cot\theta\,e_2\), and \(\mathsf R_{12}{}^{12}=-1\) with \(\mathcal S_{12}\) the fundamental vertical field \(\xi\) (the curvature two-form \(\mathrm d\omega^{12}\) evaluates to \(+1\) on \((e_1,e_2)\)), with \(\mathsf F=0\). The horizontal lifts \(\mathbb D_a=e_a-\omega_a\xi\) satisfy exactly \([\mathbb D_1,\mathbb D_2]=-\cot\theta\,\mathbb D_2-\xi\) and \([\lambda\xi,\mathbb D_a]=-(\mathbb D_a\lambda)\xi\) for every real \(\lambda\) on the base: the vertical part of \([\mathbb D_1,\mathbb D_2]\) alone is \(-\csc^2\theta\,\xi\neq0\), so the commutator of two first-order operators is not itself first-order when the curvature is nonzero, and the zeroth-order slot \(\tfrac12\mathsf R_{ab}{}^{cd}\mathcal S_{cd}\) of the class relation is not optional. At finite \(N\) the derivation part of the same structure is carried by matrices: with spin-\(j\) generators \(J_i\), \(N=2j+1\), radius-one coordinates \(X_i=J_i/\sqrt{j(j+1)}\), so that \([X_i,X_j]=\mathrm i\hbar_{\mathrm{eff}}\varepsilon_{ijk}X_k\) with \(\hbar_{\mathrm{eff}}=1/\sqrt{j(j+1)}\), the inner derivations \(\mathcal L_a=\hbar_{\mathrm{eff}}^{-1}\operatorname{ad}_{X_a}\) obey the Leibniz rule and close the rotation algebra exactly, \([\mathcal L_i,\mathcal L_j]=\mathrm i\varepsilon_{ijk}\mathcal L_k\) and \(\mathcal L_i(X_j)=\mathrm i\varepsilon_{ijk}X_k\), for every \(N\); moving coefficient functions across the noncommutative product errs from the Poisson bracket at order \(O(\hbar_{\mathrm{eff}})\), and the frame redundancy \(\sum_i x_i\rho_i=0\) of the sphere frame is violated at the same order.
