The ghost operator \(\sum_i\operatorname{ad}_{X_i}^2/\hbar_{\mathrm{eff}}^2\), with the Euclidean contraction, has, on \(\operatorname{End}(\mathbb C^N)\), the spectrum \(\{l(l+1):0\le l\le N-1\}\) with multiplicity \(2l+1\) exactly, so it reproduces the Laplace--Beltrami spectrum of the sphere up to the removal of the modes \(l>N-1\). This is the finite-size regularization of curved-geometry matrix configurations of \cite{HattoriSekoTsuchiya2026}, read on the class of this section. The toy is compact in base and fibre, Abelian in fibre, torsion-free and two-dimensional: it retains neither \(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\) nor the noncompact fibre nor the \((7,3)\) kinetic signature nor the selection of the class by the matrix equation, and it discharges neither Assumption~\ref{ass:frame-lock} nor Assumption~\ref{ass:nondeg} nor Assumption~\ref{ass:local-lorentz}. Against the criterion of Chapter~\ref{ch:agenda} this is a conditional sub-lemma on a compact toy, and the status recorded for Problem~D above is unchanged.

\paragraph{The locked frame as a gauge-invariant composite.}
Elitzur's theorem \cite{Elitzur1975} rules out a nonzero expectation value of a gauge-variant local field for a compact structure group, and Fradkin and Shenker \cite{FradkinShenker1979} showed that the Higgs and confinement regimes of such theories are analytically connected without any local order parameter. Both statements are established for compact gauge groups on the lattice; their extension to the noncompact frame group is not established. The expectation value of Equation~\eqref{eq:VEV} is gauge-covariant under Equation~\eqref{eq:Phi-transform}, so on the gauge-invariant reading adopted here the locking of Assumption~\ref{ass:frame-lock} is to be represented not by the nonzero expectation value itself but by composites that are bilocal in the base, in the spirit of the bilocal fields of \cite{HoKawaiSteinacker2025}, with parallel transporters built from the connection of the class above relating the two points, and the composite metric of Equation~\eqref{eq:g-from-e} is invariant under tangent-space rotations. On this reading Assumption~\ref{ass:nondeg} becomes a non-degeneracy statement about the two-point composite rather than a consequence of a broken symmetry, and the locking is recorded as a complementarity of regimes rather than a local spontaneous breaking. The construction presupposes the connection content that the closure of Problem~D1 in Chapter~\ref{ch:agenda} requires to be specified, and is recorded as a mechanism whose selection by the matrix dynamics is open, with no change to the status of Problem~D above.

\section{Problem E: signature}
\label{sec:status-E}

\paragraph{Ledger label.}
Status: OPEN. Priority: CRITICAL PATH for the matrix-derived label.
Completion: PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL. A scope retaining
Assumption~\ref{ass:lorentzian} gives up the claim of emergent or
matrix-derived Lorentzian signature.

Euclidean positivity of the matrix action and the formal Wick rotation of
one coordinate are recorded. They do not select Lorentzian signature without
an additional hypothesis.

\paragraph{The regulated Lorentzian integral at finite \(N\) (conditional for \(N\ge3\)).}
Take traceless Hermitian \(N\times N\) matrices \(A_\mu\), \(\mu=0,\dots,9\), with indices contracted by \(\eta=\mathrm{diag}(-1,1,\dots,1)\), the quartic term \(S_{\mathrm b}=-\tfrac1{4g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2}\operatorname{Tr}[A_\mu,A_\nu][A^\mu,A^\nu]\) of Equation~\eqref{eq:Smatrix} and the fermion content of~\cite{IKKT1997}, an input since that equation is bosonic, with \(\operatorname{Pf}\mathcal M(A)\) the Pfaffian that the fermions give, \(\mathcal M\) being linear in \(A\). The oscillatory integral has been defined in three ways. (A) With a positive Gaussian factor~\cite{Steinacker2026Invitation}: \(I_\epsilon[O]=\int\mathrm dA\,O(A)\operatorname{Pf}\mathcal M(A)\,\mathrm e^{\mathrm iS_{\mathrm b}(A)-\epsilon\sum_\mu\operatorname{Tr}A_\mu^2}\), \(\epsilon>0\), over Hermitian matrices, which converges absolutely for polynomial \(O\). That reference has the opposite sign of the quartic term; since \(\operatorname{Pf}\mathcal M\) is real on Hermitian matrices, its integral is the complex conjugate of the one below for real \(O\). (B) As the limit \(u\to0^+\) on the contours \(\mathcal R_u\) below~\cite{Anagnostopoulos2026}. (C) By Faddeev--Popov fixing of the boosts with \(\operatorname{Tr}A_0A_i=0\)~\cite{AsanoNishimuraPiensukYamamori2025}. A Lorentz-invariant mass \(S_\gamma=-\tfrac{\gamma}{2g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2}\operatorname{Tr}A_\mu A^\mu\) may be added. The relation of these integrals to the Euclidean model is disputed. Reference~\cite{Steinacker2026Invitation} states that with the Gaussian factor the Lorentzian model is no longer related to the Euclidean model by Wick rotation; \cite{Anagnostopoulos2026} states equivalence for (B) at \(\gamma=0\) and its failure for \(\gamma>0\); and \cite{ChouNishimuraTripathi2025} reports inequivalence for (C) at \(N=2\) with four spatial matrices and no fermions.

\emph{Contours.} For \(0\le u\le1\) let \(\mathcal R_u\) be \(A_0=\mathrm e^{-3\pi\mathrm iu/8}\tilde A_0\), \(A_i=\mathrm e^{\pi\mathrm iu/8}\tilde A_i\), with \(\tilde A_\mu\) traceless Hermitian, and let \(S_{\mathrm E}(\tilde A)=\tfrac1{4g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2}\sum_{\mu,\nu}\|[\tilde A_\mu,\tilde A_\nu]\|^2\) in the Hilbert--Schmidt norm. On \(\mathcal R_u\) one has \(\operatorname{Re}\mathrm iS_{\mathrm b}=-\sin(\pi u/2)\,S_{\mathrm E}(\tilde A)\), and the regulator exponent has real part \(-\epsilon[\cos(3\pi u/4)\operatorname{Tr}\tilde A_0^2+\cos(\pi u/4)\sum_i\operatorname{Tr}\tilde A_i^2]\). For fixed \(\epsilon>0\) the integrand is holomorphic, and for \(u<2/3\) the boundary term of Stokes' theorem on a ball of radius \(R\) times \([0,u]\) is bounded by a polynomial in \(R\) times \(\mathrm e^{-\epsilon\cos(3\pi u/4)R^2}\). So \(I_\epsilon[O]\) equals the same integral on \(\mathcal R_u\) for every \(u<2/3\) (exact). On \(\mathcal R_1\) at fixed \(\epsilon\) the integrand is not absolutely integrable, since the regulator grows on \(\tilde A_0\) while \(S_{\mathrm E}\) vanishes on commuting matrices. At fixed \(\epsilon\) the contour therefore does not reach the Euclidean model.

\emph{The bound along the flat directions.} On \(\mathcal R_u\) the factor \(\operatorname{Pf}\mathcal M\) is a constant phase times the Pfaffian in \(\tilde A\) with the coefficient matrix of \(\tilde A_0\) multiplied by \(\mathrm e^{-\pi\mathrm iu/2}\). The convergence proof of~\cite{AustingWheater2001} is stated for the Euclidean Pfaffian, bounds its modulus, and uses of the coefficient matrices only the bound \(|\Gamma^\mu_{\alpha\beta}|\le1\) on their entries, as that reference says. The phase keeps that bound, and the step singling out one large matrix, written there for one index, becomes a finite sum over \(\mu\). The same proof therefore gives \(\int\mathrm d\tilde A\,(1+\|\tilde A\|)^k\,|\operatorname{Pf}\mathcal M(A(u,\tilde A))|\,\mathrm e^{-cS_{\mathrm E}(\tilde A)}<\infty\) for \(c>0\), \(0\le u\le1\) and \(k<14\). For \(N\ge3\) this extension rests on reading that proof, and no independent proof is given here. For \(N=2\) it is proved directly. With \(A_\mu=\sum_aA_\mu^a\sigma^a/2\) and \(\mathcal G_{ab}=\eta^{\mu\nu}A_\mu^aA_\nu^b\), one has \(\operatorname{Pf}\mathcal M=c_{\mathrm P}(\det\mathcal G)^4\), a constant \(c_{\mathrm P}\neq0\), for all complex \(A\): the Pfaffian is invariant under \(\mathrm{SO}(10,\mathbb C)\times\mathrm{SO}(3,\mathbb C)\), real Euclidean \(A\) has a singular-value form, the spinor branches into eight doublets of \(\mathrm{SO}(3)\), and the resulting \(6\times6\) determinant, of degree six, vanishes to second order in each singular value and is nonzero when all three equal \(1\) (also checked at random complex \(A\) to \(10^{-13}\)). By the Cauchy--Binet formula \(|\det\mathcal G(A(u,\tilde A))|\le\det\tilde{\mathcal G}\), with \(\tilde{\mathcal G}\) the Euclidean contraction of \(\tilde A\), and in the singular values the resulting integral converges exactly for \(k<14\).

\emph{Limit at finite \(N\).} Dominated convergence on \(\mathcal R_u\) at one fixed \(u\in(0,2/3)\), followed by Stokes' theorem from that \(u\) to \(u=1\) with the auxiliary factor \(\exp(-\delta[\mathrm e^{3\pi\mathrm i/8}\operatorname{Tr}A_0^2+\mathrm e^{-\pi\mathrm i/8}\sum_i\operatorname{Tr}A_i^2])\), whose exponent has negative real part on all these contours and which is removed as \(\delta\to0^+\) by the same domination, gives for polynomial \(O\) of degree \(k<14\)
\[
\lim_{\epsilon\to0^+}I_\epsilon[O]=\mathrm e^{7\pi\mathrm i(N^2-1)/4}\int\mathrm d\tilde A\;O\bigl(\mathrm e^{-3\pi\mathrm i/8}\tilde A_0,\mathrm e^{\pi\mathrm i/8}\tilde A_i\bigr)\operatorname{Pf}\mathcal M(-\mathrm i\tilde A_0,\tilde A_i)\,\mathrm e^{-S_{\mathrm E}(\tilde A)},
\]
with difference at most \(C_{O,N}\epsilon\) for \(k\le11\). Where the Euclidean partition function does not vanish, which is proved at \(N=2\) and is a hypothesis here for \(N\ge3\), ratios converge to Euclidean values times phases, for example \(\langle\operatorname{Tr}A_0^2\rangle\to\mathrm e^{-3\pi\mathrm i/4}\langle\operatorname{Tr}\tilde A_0^2\rangle_{\mathrm E}\) and \(\langle\sum_i\operatorname{Tr}A_i^2\rangle\to\mathrm e^{\pi\mathrm i/4}\langle\sum_i\operatorname{Tr}\tilde A_i^2\rangle_{\mathrm E}\), the relations stated for (B) in~\cite{Anagnostopoulos2026}. The same limit holds with \(\sum_{\mu,\nu}M_{\mu\nu}\operatorname{Tr}A_\mu A_\nu\) for any positive definite \(M\) in place of the regulator, since the argument then needs only \(u\) small. For these \(O\) the limit is therefore independent of the regulator and invariant under \(\mathrm{SO}(9,1)\) at finite \(N\), although each regulator breaks that group. It also holds for \(\gamma\to0\) with \(\gamma/\epsilon\) bounded above, the exponent then having real part \(-[\epsilon\cos(3\pi u/4)-\tfrac{\gamma}{2g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2}\sin(3\pi u/4)]\operatorname{Tr}\tilde A_0^2\) on \(\tilde A_0\) and the corresponding term with \(\pi u/4\) on \(\tilde A_i\). For fixed \(\gamma<0\) the contour argument reaches \(u=1\) with the mass retained. For fixed \(\gamma>0\) the admissible \(u\) closes like \(\epsilon/\gamma\) as \(\epsilon\to0\), and nothing is stated. At \(N=2\), reduced to three real variables (numerical), the values at fixed \(\epsilon\) and \(u=0.3,0.45,0.6\) agree to about eight digits, a direct evaluation on Hermitian matrices at \(u=0\) agrees within its grid error at the larger \(\epsilon\) tested, the Euclidean ratio of \(\sum_i\operatorname{Tr}\tilde A_i^2\) to \(\operatorname{Tr}\tilde A_0^2\) is \(9\), the limit is approached linearly in \(\epsilon\) with the slope given by the first moment, and as \(\epsilon\to0\) the moment \(\langle(\sum_i\operatorname{Tr}A_i^2)^{n}\rangle\) converges in modulus to the Euclidean value for \(2n\le12\), grows logarithmically for \(2n=14\) and grows as \(1/\epsilon\) for \(2n=16\).

\emph{Definition (C) at \(N=2\).} For at least four spatial matrices, write \(d\) for their number. The conditions \(\operatorname{Tr}A_0A_i=0\) force either \(A_0=0\), a sector which the Faddeev--Popov determinant removes as the delta functions are smeared and then sharpened, or \(\mathcal G=\mathrm{diag}(g_+,g_-,-g_3)\) up to rotation, with \(g_\pm,g_3\ge0\). The integrand is then \((g_+g_-g_3)^{(d-3)/2+p}|g_+-g_-|(g_3+g_+)(g_3+g_-)\) times the exponential, with \(p=4\) for the content above at \(d=9\) and \(p=0\) without fermions. The contour argument applies on the wedge \(g_+>g_-\), and at \(u=1\) it gives the Euclidean integrand with \(|g_+-g_3||g_--g_3|\) replaced by \((g_+-g_3)(g_--g_3)\). In an ordered triple the three positions of \(g_3\) give signs \(+,-,+\), which sum to \(1\) against the \(3\) of the Euclidean measure. So for \(\gamma\le0\), with a convergence factor as in (A) removed last, which (C) also needs since it is not absolutely convergent, and wherever the integrals converge and the partition function does not vanish, the expectation values of invariant observables of (C) at \(N=2\) equal those of the Euclidean model at the same phases (exact), namely the same \(O\) and \(S_\gamma\) on \(\mathcal R_1\) with weight \(\mathrm e^{-S_{\mathrm E}}\) and, for \(p=4\), the Pfaffian there; for \(p=0\), by the rotation \(A_\mu=\mathrm e^{\pi\mathrm i/8}\tilde A_\mu\), \(\mu=1,\dots,d+1\), with \(\tilde A_{d+1}\) the \(\tilde A_0\) above, this is the model with \(A_0=-\mathrm iA_{d+1}\) of~\cite{ChouNishimuraTripathi2025}. For \(d=4\), no fermions and \(\gamma<0\), this gives \(\langle\operatorname{Tr}A_\mu A^\mu\rangle\) of (C) equal to the Euclidean value, which grows as \(|\gamma|^{-1/2}\) when \(\gamma\to0^-\) in both models (numerical). The gap between the models that~\cite{ChouNishimuraTripathi2025} plots at \(\gamma<0\) is not reproduced by this reduction, and the difference is not resolved here. For \(\gamma>0\) no contour is available and that case is not treated.

For these observables, this order of limits, and \(\gamma\le0\) or \(\gamma\) as above, the regulated integral therefore carries at finite \(N\), conditionally for \(N\ge3\), no datum beyond the Euclidean model, with any mass term on \(\mathcal R_1\), and its phases. This is no statement about \(N\to\infty\) taken first, since the constants \(C_{O,N}\) are not shown to be uniform in \(N\); about fixed \(\gamma>0\); about degree \(14\) and above; or about non-polynomial observables such as \(\operatorname{Tr}\mathrm e^{\mathrm ik^\mu A_\mu}\), which grow on \(\mathcal R_u\), so that the domination above fails for them. The next paragraph sharpens this at \(N=2\) and \(\gamma=0\): the failure occurs for every nonzero \(k\) in the contour class (B), and it is absent at fixed \(\gamma<0\). It does not exclude a real-time reading obtained by continuation. It does not derive a signature, a background, a frame or the fermion content, and Assumption~\ref{ass:lorentzian} is not discharged. Problem~E remains open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\emph{Exponential observables \(\operatorname{Tr}\mathrm e^{\mathrm ik^\mu A_\mu}\) at \(N=2\).} Let \(O_k(A)=\operatorname{Tr}\mathrm e^{\mathrm ik^\mu A_\mu}\) with \(k\in\mathbb C^{10}\), at \(\gamma=0\), regulator (A), contours \(\mathcal R_u\) and \(\epsilon\to0^+\) last. For each fixed \(\epsilon>0\) and \(u<2/3\) the integral converges absolutely and is entire in \(k\), because on \(\mathcal R_u\) the growth of \(O_k\) is at most \(\mathrm e^{c(u,k)\|\tilde A\|}\) against a Gaussian regulator, and the fixed-\(\epsilon\) Stokes identity extends to it. At \(N=2\) the matrix \(k^\mu A_\mu\) has eigenvalues \(\pm\rho_X/2\) with \(\rho_X^2=k^\mu k^\nu\mathcal G_{\mu\nu}\), so \(O_k=2\cos(\rho_X/2)\) is branch-independent, and \(|2\cos(\omega/2)|^2=4(\cos^2(\operatorname{Re}\omega/2)+\sinh^2(\operatorname{Im}\omega/2))\). Along rank-one rays \(\tilde A_\mu=\rho_\mu H\), \(\operatorname{Tr}H^2=1\), the Euclidean weight \(\mathrm e^{-S_{\mathrm E}}\) is not exponentially damped and tends to a bounded factor along the corner (\(S_{\mathrm E}\) vanishes on the exact rank-one rays), the Pfaffian vanishes on the ray and is polynomial in the coordinates nearby, and the remaining factors are polynomial (numerical, in a reduced chart), while \(|O_k|\ge 2\sinh\!\bigl(b_u(\hat\rho;k)\rho/2\bigr)\) with \(b_u(\hat\rho;k)=|\operatorname{Im}(k^0\mathrm e^{-3\pi\mathrm iu/8}\hat\rho_0+k^i\mathrm e^{\pi\mathrm iu/8}\hat\rho_i)|\). At every \(u\in(0,1]\) the functional \(\hat\rho\mapsto\operatorname{Im}(k^0\mathrm e^{-3\pi\mathrm iu/8}\hat\rho_0+k^i\mathrm e^{\pi\mathrm iu/8}\hat\rho_i)\) is nonzero for every nonzero real \(k\) (at \(u=1\) its coefficients are \(-\sin(3\pi/8)k^0\) and \(\sin(\pi/8)\vec k\)), so the Euclidean side of the displayed limit identity diverges in modulus for every nonzero real \(k\); more generally it diverges for every \(k\) outside the slice on which \(k^0\mathrm e^{-3\pi\mathrm i/8}\) and all \(k^i\mathrm e^{\pi\mathrm i/8}\) are real, where \(|O_k|\le2\) and that side converges by the \(k<14\) Pfaffian window. For \(\operatorname{Im}k\ne0\) such that some commuting unit \(\hat\rho\) has \(|\operatorname{Im}(k\cdot\hat\rho)|>|\operatorname{Re}(k\cdot\hat\rho)|\), the commuting-ray contribution to the \(u=0\) integral has \(S_{\mathrm b}=0\) and grows along a sequence \(\epsilon\to0^+\) like \(\mathrm e^{c_2(k)/\epsilon}\) with \(c_2(k)>0\) (commuting-ray model; no transverse stationary-phase bound is claimed), and for real \(k\ne0\) the integrand at \(u=0\) carries \(|O_k|\le2\) pointwise, but no fixed-\(u\) domination in \(\epsilon\) is available at any \(u\in(0,2/3)\): the fixed-\(\epsilon\) modulus on \(\mathcal R_u\) grows without bound as \(\epsilon\to0\), numerically like \(\mathrm e^{c(k,u)/\sqrt\epsilon}\) on the sampled range. Hence within the class stated the displayed limit identity fails for every nonzero real \(k\) and for every non-real \(k\) outside the slice on which \(k^0\mathrm e^{-3\pi\mathrm i/8}\) and all \(k^i\mathrm e^{\pi\mathrm i/8}\) are real, holds at \(k=0\), and is unresolved on that slice at \(k\ne0\): there is no analyticity strip and no non-polynomial extension of the limit statement at \(\gamma=0\) off that slice, within the hypothesis class \(N=2\), regulator (A), contours \(\mathcal R_u\), \(\epsilon\to0^+\) last. The obstruction is the massless flat direction, and at fixed \(\gamma<0\) it is absent: the mass contributes on \(\mathcal R_u\) the real exponent \((\gamma/2g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2)[\sin(3\pi u/4)\operatorname{Tr}\tilde A_0^2+\sin(\pi u/4)\sum_{i=1}^{9}\operatorname{Tr}\tilde A_i^2]\), a Gaussian damping on every component that dominates \(\mathrm e^{c(k)\|\tilde A\|}\) uniformly for \(\epsilon\le\epsilon_0<|\gamma|/(2g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2)\), and the displayed limit identity then extends to \(O_k\) for every \(k\in\mathbb C^{10}\) (numerical). At fixed \(\gamma>0\) the \(u=1\) integral diverges in modulus even at \(k=0\) (numerical), so the contour family and the thimbles of~\cite{ChouNishimuraTripathi2025} act on different contour integrals and produce no competing value; the classicalization of~\cite{AsanoNishimuraPiensukYamamori2025} is tied there to a Lorentz-breaking infrared cutoff and bears neither on this paragraph nor on the statements above. Everything in this paragraph is for the hypothesis class stated in it, does not address \(N\to\infty\) taken first, and derives no signature, real-time response, background or continuation; Assumption~\ref{ass:lorentzian} is not discharged. Problem~E remains open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\emph{The slice at \(k\ne0\): refinement of the window and model-level growth control.} On the slice \(k^0\mathrm e^{-3\pi\mathrm i/8}=r^0\in\mathbb R\), \(k^i\mathrm e^{\pi\mathrm i/8}=r^i\in\mathbb R\) the Cauchy-Binet identity \(\rho_X^2=\sum_{a=1}^{3}{(k^\mu A^a_\mu)}^2\) is exact at \(N=2\), and on \(\mathcal R_1\) the contraction \(k^\mu A_\mu\) reduces to the Hermitian real-linear form \(r^\mu\tilde A_\mu\), so \(\rho_X\) is real and \(|O_k|\le2\) pointwise there; right-hand-side boundedness on the slice is structural. The commuting-ray alternative also refines inside the slice: at \(u=0\), writing the growth functional as \(B=\mathbf v\mathbf v^{\mathrm T}-\mathbf u\mathbf u^{\mathrm T}\) with \(\mathbf u=(\cos(3\pi/8)r^0,\cos(\pi/8)\vec r\,)\), \(\mathbf v=(\sin(3\pi/8)r^0,-\sin(\pi/8)\vec r\,)\), the matrix \(B\) is supported on \(\operatorname{span}\{\mathbf e_0,\hat{\mathbf r}\}\), has rank at most two, and \(\lambda_{\max}(B)=[{(r^0)}^2-r^2+\sqrt{{(r^0)}^4+6{(r^0)}^2r^2+r^4}]/(2\sqrt2)\), which is positive iff \(r^0\ne0\); for pure spatial slice momenta (\(r^0=0\)) there is no commuting ray with growth, the neutral set is \(\hat{\mathbf r}\cdot\hat\rho=0\), and \(B\) is negative off it. For \(r^0\ne0\) the one-dimensional branch integral \(\int_{\mathbb R}\mathrm e^{-Qa^2}\,2\cos(\gamma_{\mathrm c}|a|)\,\mathrm da=2\sqrt{\pi/Q}\,\mathrm e^{-\gamma_{\mathrm c}^2/(4Q)}\), \(\gamma_{\mathrm c}=\mathrm e^{3\pi\mathrm i/8}r^0/2\), gives pointwise modulus growth \(\mathrm e^{{(r^0)}^2\cos(3\pi u/4-\pi/4)/(16\epsilon)}\) for the soft temporal mode at \(\gamma=0\), saturating as \(\epsilon\to0^+\) at fixed \(\gamma<0\) to a constant of order \(\mathrm e^{{(r^0)}^2\sin(3\pi u/4-\pi/4)/(8|\gamma|)}\), which diverges as \(\gamma\to0^-\) when \(3\pi u/4-\pi/4>0\) and decays when it is negative. In the reduced \(N=2\) integral (regulator (A), contours \(\mathcal R_u\), \(\epsilon\to0^+\) last) with this insertion at pure temporal slice momenta, the modulus ratio \(|Z_\epsilon[O_k]|/|Z_\epsilon[1]|\) stays of order one and tends to \(2\) along the ladder down to \(\epsilon=3\cdot10^{-3}\) (fit slope \(0.0000\) of \(\ln\)-ratio against \(1/\epsilon\), against the pointwise corner value \(0.044/\epsilon\) at \(r^0=1\)), and at \(u=0.6\) with \(\gamma=-0.2,-0.05\) it stays between \(0.74\) and \(1.78\): in the reduction neither the commuting-ray growth \(\mathrm e^{c_2(k)/\epsilon}\) nor the pointwise saturation factor materialises, consistent with phase-space suppression of the corner branch growth near the soft corner by Vandermonde and measure powers, though no domination lemma is claimed. These are reduced-model computations with oscillation-limited resolution at \(\gamma=0\); whether the left-hand side is controlled on the slice at \(k\ne0\) with \(r^0\ne0\) remains open at full \(N=2\), the \(\gamma\to0^-\) bridge from fixed-\(\gamma<0\) success remains open, and the pure-spatial half of the slice is now ray-free; Problem~E remains open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\paragraph{Slice bridge band and modulus obstruction.} On the slice \(k^0\mathrm e^{-3\pi\mathrm i/8}=r^0\in\mathbb R\), \(k^i\mathrm e^{\pi\mathrm i/8}=r^i\in\mathbb R\), the insertion is bounded on the final contour, \(|O_k|\le2\), since \(k^\mu A_\mu\) reduces on \(\mathcal R_1\) to the Hermitian form \(r^\mu\tilde A_\mu\) and \(\rho_X^2=\sum_{a=1}^{3}(r^\mu\tilde A^a_\mu)^2\ge0\), so the \(k<14\) Pfaffian window applies with the \(k=0\) majorant. With the auxiliary factor at \(\delta=\lambda\epsilon\), the quadratic damping along \(\mathcal R_\theta\), \(\theta\in[u,1]\), is strictly positive on the whole slide if and only if \(\delta>\lambda_*\epsilon\), \(\lambda_*=\sqrt{2+\sqrt2}\): the spatial coefficient stays positive for all \(\delta\ge0\), and the temporal ratio \(-\cos(3\pi\theta/4)/\cos(3\pi(2\theta-1)/8)\) is strictly increasing with supremum \(\lambda_*\) at \(\theta=1\). For each fixed \(\lambda>\lambda_*\) the damped family at \(u=1\) converges to the right-hand side by dominated convergence within the window. Removing the auxiliary factor from either undamped side in modulus fails: on the rank-one corner \(\tilde A_0=\rho H\), \(\operatorname{Tr}H^2=1\), with transverse scale \(O(\epsilon)\), \(|O_k|\ge\mathrm e^{b_u\rho/2}/(2\sqrt2)\), \(b_u=\sin(3\pi(1-u)/8)|r^0|>0\) for \(u<1\), against regulator damping \(\epsilon\cos(3\pi u/4)\rho^2\), so the fixed-\(\epsilon\) modulus integral on \(\mathcal R_u\) obeys \(M_u(\epsilon)\ge\mathrm e^{c_0(u)/\epsilon}/\mathrm{poly}\), \(c_0(u)=\sin^2(3\pi(1-u)/8){(r^0)}^2/(16\cos(3\pi u/4))>0\) for \(u<2/3\), and diverges for \(u\ge2/3\); on the flat contour \(|O_k|\) grows like \(\mathrm e^{c(k)\rho}\) and the corresponding bound grows like \(\mathrm e^{c(k)^2/(4\epsilon)}\). The linking band \(\delta\le\mathrm{poly}\cdot\mathrm e^{-c_0(u)/\epsilon}\) is therefore incompatible, as \(\epsilon\to0^+\), with the Stokes band \(\delta\ge\lambda_*\epsilon\): within the class \(N=2\), regulator (A), \(\gamma=0\), slice momenta with \(r^0\ne0\), and linking by absolute-value estimates along the family \(\mathcal R_u\), no rate \(\delta(\epsilon)\) and no contour slide bridges the undamped slice insertion (a fixed negative mass contributes only a phase at \(u=0\)). The displayed limit identity therefore remains unresolved on the slice at \(k\ne0\) beyond such bridges; closing it requires non-modulus (oscillation-based) estimates or an independent mechanism. Problem~E remains open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.


\paragraph{The \(\gamma\to0^{-}\) link at fixed \(u\): reduction and modulus obstructions on both sides.} Let \(I_\epsilon^{(\gamma)}[O]\) denote the regularized integral (A) with the mass \(S_\gamma=-\tfrac{\gamma}{2g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2}\operatorname{Tr}A_\mu A^\mu\) added, computed on \(\mathcal R_u\) with \(u\in(0,2/3)\) fixed and \(\epsilon\to0^+\) last, and \(\Delta(\epsilon,\gamma)=I_\epsilon^{(0)}[O_k]-I_\epsilon^{(\gamma)}[O_k]\) for slice momenta with \(r^0\ne0\). On \(\mathcal R_u\) the mass exponent has real part \((\gamma/2g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2)[\sin(3\pi u/4)\operatorname{Tr}\tilde A_0^2+\sin(\pi u/4)\sum_{i=1}^{9}\operatorname{Tr}\tilde A_i^2]\le0\) for \(\gamma<0\), and \(|\mathrm e^{z}-1|\le|z|\) holds for \(\operatorname{Re}z\le0\); since \(|\operatorname{Tr}A_\mu A^\mu|\le\operatorname{Tr}\tilde A_0^2+\sum_i\operatorname{Tr}\tilde A_i^2\) on \(\mathcal R_u\), expanding the mass exponential gives \(|\Delta(\epsilon,\gamma)|\le(|\gamma|/2g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2)\,\tilde J(\epsilon,u)\), where \(\tilde J(\epsilon,u)\) is the \(\gamma=0\) modulus functional on \(\mathcal R_u\) with insertion \(\operatorname{Tr}\tilde A_0^2+\sum_i\operatorname{Tr}\tilde A_i^2\). Along the rank-one ray with transverse scale \(O(\epsilon)\), the bound \(|O_k|\ge\mathrm e^{b_u\rho/2}/(2\sqrt2)\), \(b_u=\sin(3\pi(1-u)/8)|r^0|\), against the regulator damping \(\epsilon\cos(3\pi u/4)\rho^2\) gives \(\tilde J(\epsilon,u)\ge\mathrm e^{c_0(u)/\epsilon}/\mathrm{poly}\) with \(c_0(u)=\sin^2(3\pi(1-u)/8){(r^0)}^2/(16\cos(3\pi u/4))>0\): \(\Delta\to0\) as \(\gamma\to0^-\) at each fixed \(\epsilon\), but no absolute-value estimate for \(\Delta\) is \(o(1)\) uniformly on any ladder \(\epsilon\to0^+\), so at fixed \(u\in(0,2/3)\) the \(\gamma\to0^{-}\) link by modulus bounds is obstructed by the same flat-direction divergence that obstructs the undamped slice left-hand side, and any bridge must control the \(\gamma=0\) slice integral by signed (oscillation-based) estimates uniform in \(\epsilon\). On the mass side the soft-mode scale is \(\epsilon\)-uniform but not \(\gamma\)-uniform: maximizing \(b_u\rho/2-[\epsilon\cos(3\pi u/4)+|\gamma|\sin(3\pi u/4)/(2g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2)]\rho^2\) gives \(b_u^2/(16\beta(\epsilon,\gamma))\) with \(\beta(\epsilon,\gamma)\ge|\gamma|\sin(3\pi u/4)/(2g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2)\), and the ray region contributes at least \(\mathrm e^{\Gamma_0(u)/|\gamma|}/\mathrm{poly}\) with \(\Gamma_0(u)=g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2\sin^2(3\pi(1-u)/8){(r^0)}^2/(8\sin(3\pi u/4))>0\) for every \(u\in(0,2/3)\), strictly decreasing in \(u\) (\(\Gamma_0(1/3)\approx0.0884{(r^0)}^2\) at \(g_{\mathrm{YM}}=1\)): the mass-side modulus also blows up super-polynomially as \(\gamma\to0^-\), most strongly at small \(u\), so the two sides of the link are not simultaneously controllable in modulus. With the mass Gaussian the extension of the displayed limit identity at fixed \(\gamma<0\) to \(O_k\) for every \(k\in\mathbb C^{10}\) is a derivation: for fixed \(u\in(0,2/3)\) the damping of each Hermitian component is bounded below by \(|\gamma|\sin(3\pi u/4)/(2g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2)\) on \(\tilde A_0\) and \(|\gamma|\sin(\pi u/4)/(2g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2)\) on \(\tilde A_i\), independent of \(\epsilon\), so the Stokes boundary term on balls times \([0,u]\) decays and both sides of the limit are dominated. In the one-dimensional branch model the coefficient of \(\tilde A_0\) in \(k^\mu A_\mu\) on \(\mathcal R_u\) at pure temporal slice momentum is \(\mathrm e^{3\pi\mathrm i(1-u)/8}r^0/2\), not its value at \(u=0\); with this contour-dependent coefficient the soft-mode growth exponent at \(\gamma=0\) is \({(r^0)}^2/(16\sqrt2\,\epsilon)\) and the fixed-\(\gamma\) saturation exponent is \(-g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2{(r^0)}^2/(8\sqrt2|\gamma|)\), both independent of \(u\), so the saturation factor \(\mathrm e^{{(r^0)}^2\sin(3\pi u/4-\pi/4)/(8|\gamma|)}\) and its growth as \(\gamma\to0^-\) for \(3\pi u/4-\pi/4>0\) are properties of the frozen-coefficient model, not of the contour family. Problem~E remains open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.




\paragraph{Status.}
Remains Assumption~\ref{ass:lorentzian}.

\section{Problem F: coefficients \(A,B,C\)}
\label{sec:status-F}

\paragraph{Ledger label.}
Status: OPEN. Priority: CRITICAL PATH. Completion:
PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL. Reduced-scope result: treating
\(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\) as an input leaves F OPEN and does not satisfy
the matrix-derived criterion.

The conditional, truncated heat-kernel identity is
Theorem~\ref{thm:Geff}; it is not a computed \(A,B,C\)
parameterization or physical value of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\).

\paragraph{Missing step.}
Specify the gauge-fixed fluctuation, Goldstone, diffeomorphism-ghost,
and local-Lorentz-ghost operators and weights; per-sector proper-time
limits; massive threshold functions if masses are resummed; the
continuation; and the finite-counterterm scheme. Then evaluate the
weighted \(a_1\) and curvature-linear \(a_2\) contributions in that
scheme. Until this is completed, Problem~F remains open, and the
asserted \(N_{\mathrm{eff}}=+2\) count does not close it.

\paragraph{Quadratic Hessian of the displayed action (conditional; not closure).}
Restrict to the square locked \(4\times4\) block of
Assumption~\ref{ass:locked-block}, expand~\eqref{eq:Smicro} with~\eqref{eq:V}
on the flat scaffold about \(\Phi=v\mathbb I+d\) at \(A_\mu=0\), and write
\(d=H+K\) with \(H=(d+d^\dagger)/2\) Hermitian and \(K=(d-d^\dagger)/2\)
anti-Hermitian. A direct expansion gives \(V^{(1)}=0\) and
\(V^{(2)}=\lambda v^2\,\mathrm{Tr}H^2\), independent of \(K\), and
\(\mathrm{Tr}(d^\dagger d)=\mathrm{Tr}H^2+\mathrm{Tr}(K^\dagger K)\) with no
cross term. The \(H\)--\(H\) block therefore has the Lorentzian field
equation \((\Box-\lambda v^2)H=0\) with the kinetic sign of~\eqref{eq:Smicro},
that is, the Euclidean operator \(-\nabla_E^2+\lambda v^2\) after Wick
rotation. This is the \(\Phi\)--\(\Phi\) block at fixed \(A_\mu=0\);
quadratic mixing of \(A_\mu\) with \(\Phi\) through the covariant
derivative is not evaluated, and no gauge-fixed coupled Hessian is given.
No identification of \(H\) with the \(\psi\) and \(T_{\mu\nu}\) of
Chapter~\ref{ch:lorentz-decomposition} is made here, since that split uses
\(\eta_{\mu\nu}\) and the vacuum identification of
Assumption~\ref{ass:frame-lock}. Equation~\eqref{eq:Smicro} displays no
field-strength term for \(A_\mu\), and \(\omega_\mu\) does not appear in it;
its operator is unspecified (Assumption~\ref{ass:local-lorentz}). The
displayed action also does not exhibit the Fierz--Pauli structure used as
hypothesis~5 of Theorem~\ref{thm:mT}, so any tensor-block terms beyond it
are an additional input. The gauge-fixed sector list, Goldstone and ghost
operators, measure, curvature couplings and weights are not specified by
this calculation, so it does not fix the sector sum of
Theorem~\ref{thm:Geff}, does not close F or D, and leaves the missing step
above unchanged.

\paragraph{Gauge-group dependence of the scalar block (conditional; not closure).}
The contraction \(\mathrm{Tr}\,\Phi^\dagger\Phi\) is a positive-definite norm,
and the potential in~\eqref{eq:V}, being built from it, is invariant under
constant unitary transformations of the internal index and of the tangent
index; neither is invariant under non-compact boosts of the tangent index.
The covariant kinetic term has a
\emph{local} symmetry only for those transformations for which a connection and
its transformation law are specified; for the tangent index this requires
Assumption~\ref{ass:local-lorentz} with \(\omega_\mu\), which is not part
of~\eqref{eq:Smicro}. Moreover, the compact group \(\mathrm{U}(4)\) has no
real \(\mathfrak{so}(3,1)\) subalgebra, so choosing
\(G_{\mathrm{int}}=\mathrm{U}(4)\) is not compatible with the construction of
Chapter~\ref{ch:lorentz-locking} that requires \(G_{\mathrm{int}}\) to contain
\(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\) or its algebra. It defines a different compact-symmetry
model, whose consequences for Problem~D are not examined here.

For that separate model only, work on the square locked \(4\times4\) block
about \(\Phi=v\mathbb{I}\) with \(A_\mu\) as an unspecified fluctuation. Write
\(\delta\Phi=H+K\) as above. Assume (i) the action has no
\(F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}\) term; (ii) \(G_{\mathrm{int}}\subseteq\mathrm{U}(4)\)
acts with the infinitesimal orbit \(\delta K=\alpha\,v\) for \(\alpha\) in its
Lie algebra; and (iii) the orbit directions are removed by the gauge
condition. Then the number of anti-Hermitian directions not tangent to the
orbit, which have no quadratic potential, is \(16-\dim G_{\mathrm{int}}\); for
\(\mathrm{U}(4)\) this is zero and for \(\mathrm{SU}(4)\), \(\mathrm{SO}(4)\),
\(\mathrm{U}(1)\) it is \(4\), \(10\), \(15\) respectively. For
\(G_{\mathrm{int}}=\mathrm{U}(4)\) the linearized gauge condition \(K=0\) has a
field-independent Jacobian at the vacuum, and the remaining
\(\Phi\)--\(\Phi\) block is the sixteen real components of \(H\) with
\(m^2=\lambda v^2\). This is the scalar Hessian block only: the ghost and
measure contributions beyond the linearized Jacobian, the ultralocal
determinant from the nonlinear \(A_\mu\) integral, any induced vector or tensor
operator, and the weighting of the full sector sum all remain unspecified, and
the count is not a heat-kernel trace for the Problem~F target. The
discrepancy with the \(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\) assumption is recorded, not resolved.
Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Bookkeeping for one sector of the sum: the Hermitian scalar
block (conditional; not closure).}
This paragraph illustrates how Theorem~\ref{thm:Geff} would be applied
to one block.  It is not a derivation of that block's coefficients.
The Hessian paragraph above is evaluated on the fixed flat scaffold
\(g^{(0)}\) of Assumption~\ref{ass:aux-metric}, where \(R=0\), so the
displayed action supplies no curvature coupling for the emergent
metric.  The operator on the emergent background and the curvature
coupling \(Q\) below are the Presentation-B assumptions of
Chapter~\ref{ch:heat-kernel}, not outputs of the displayed action.

Take the Hermitian block \(H\) of the Hessian paragraph above, with sixteen real
components.  Assume (i) that this diagonal block at \(A_\mu=0\) is
treated as decoupled from the other sectors, so the coupled determinant
is not given; (ii) the determinant weight is \(w=1\) per real bosonic
component; and (iii) the operator of Equation~\eqref{eq:O-eta} on the
block is \(-\nabla^2+E\) with \(E=\lambda v^2+QR_E\), where \(Q\) is
proportional to the identity on the sixteen components (otherwise
\(1/6-Q\) below is replaced by its trace over the components).  With
\(a_1=\mathrm{tr}(R/6-E)\) of~\eqref{eq:a0a1} and the standard
curvature-linear part of
\(a_2\supset\tfrac12\,\mathrm{tr}(R/6-E)^2\), which has the same sign
convention as \(a_1\) and is used here without being re-derived, the
coefficients of \(R_E\) for this block are
\(\mathcal A_{1,R,H}=16(\tfrac16-Q)\) and
\(\mathcal A_{2,R,H}=16\lambda v^2(Q-\tfrac16)=-\lambda v^2\mathcal A_{1,R,H}\).
In the truncated, unresummed identity of Theorem~\ref{thm:Geff} with
common proper-time limits, the block would contribute
\(-\frac{\mathcal A_{1,R,H}}{32\pi^2}\bigl[(\LUV^2-\mu^2)
-\lambda v^2\ln(\LUV^2/\mu^2)\bigr]\) to \(C_R^E\); that is, in this
block's contribution the coefficient of \(\lambda v^2\ln(\LUV^2/\mu^2)\)
is minus the coefficient of \(\LUV^2-\mu^2\).  Both coefficients vanish
at \(Q=\tfrac16\); this concerns only this block's \(R_E\)-coefficient
in the truncated expansion.  Because the block has mass \(\lambda v^2\),
a resummed treatment replaces these terms by threshold functions, so
the expression is not the physical coefficient.  No comparison with
\(A,B,C\) of Equation~\eqref{eq:Geff-structure} is made.  The
anti-Hermitian block, the \(A_\mu\) and \(\omega_\mu\) sectors, the
tensor block, the ghosts, the continuation and the scheme are not
specified here, so this does not determine \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\), does
not fix the sign of the Lorentzian term, and leaves Problem~F open.

\paragraph{Mass-resummed curvature threshold for the Hermitian block
(conditional; not closure).}
Keep the assumptions of the preceding one-block bookkeeping paragraph:
sixteen decoupled real components, determinant weight \(w=1\), common
proper-time limits, and
\(\mathcal O_H=-\nabla^2+E\), with
\(E=m^2+Q R_E\), \(m^2=\lambda v^2\), and constant \(m^2\) and \(Q\)
proportional to the identity. At the retained order assume
\(\nabla E=0\), and keep only terms linear in \(R_E\), dropping
curvature-squared invariants and curvature-derivative terms. Retaining
the mass exponential, the coincident-point heat kernel then contains
\[
\operatorname{tr}\langle x|e^{-s\mathcal O_H}|x\rangle
\supset
\frac{16}{(4\pi s)^2}e^{-sm^2}
s\left(\frac16-Q\right)R_E .
\]
Thus this block's contribution to \(C_R^E\), in the sign and
normalization convention of the preceding paragraph, with
\(s_{\min}=\LUV^{-2}\) and \(s_{\max}=\mu^{-2}\), is
\[
-\frac{16}{32\pi^2}\left(\frac16-Q\right)
J(m^2;s_{\min},s_{\max}),
\]
where
\[
\begin{aligned}
J(m^2;s_{\min},s_{\max})
&:=\int_{s_{\min}}^{s_{\max}}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{s^2}e^{-m^2s}\\
&=\frac{e^{-m^2s_{\min}}}{s_{\min}}
 -\frac{e^{-m^2s_{\max}}}{s_{\max}}
 -m^2\left[
 E_1(m^2s_{\min})-E_1(m^2s_{\max})
 \right],
\qquad
E_1(x):=\int_x^\infty\frac{e^{-t}}{t}\,\mathrm{d}t .
\end{aligned}
\]
The \(m\to0\) limit is \(J(0)=\LUV^2-\mu^2\). For
\(m^2s_{\max}\ll1\),
\[
J=\LUV^2-\mu^2
 -m^2\ln\!\left(\frac{\LUV^2}{\mu^2}\right)
 +O\!\left(m^4s_{\max}\right).
\]
This small-mass expansion gives the earlier truncated expression;
the resummed \(J\) is the threshold function, and the earlier
expression is not removed. At this order in curvature, the resummed
expression includes the mass insertions represented by that
curvature-linear \(a_2\) term; they must not be added a second time.
For \(m^2s_{\min}\gg1\), this block's loop contribution is
exponentially suppressed. The full coefficient still includes the
scheme-dependent local curvature counterterm of
Theorem~\ref{thm:Geff}; this block calculation does not specify that
counterterm or the other sectors, their operators and weights, the
gauge fixing, or the continuation. Problems~B, C, D, F and~G remain
open.
\paragraph{The assumed group \(G_{\mathrm{int}}\supseteq\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\)
and the displayed potential (conditional; not closure).}
Chapter~\ref{ch:lorentz-locking} takes \(G_{\mathrm{int}}\) to contain
\(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\) acting on the internal index, with orbit
\(\delta d=v\alpha\) at the vacuum.  As mixed-index matrices
\(\alpha^I{}_J\) on the locked block, its three boost generators are
real symmetric matrices, for example \(B=E_{01}+E_{10}\), and so lie in
the Hermitian block \(H\) of the Hessian paragraph above, while the
rotations are antisymmetric.  For \(d=vB\) that paragraph gives
\(V^{(2)}=\lambda v^2\,\mathrm{Tr}H^2=2\lambda v^4\neq0\), so the
potential of~\eqref{eq:V}, which is built from the positive-definite
\(\mathrm{Tr}\,\Phi^\dagger\Phi\), is not invariant under these
generators.  Assumption~(ii) of the preceding paragraph, that the orbit
lies in the anti-Hermitian block, therefore does not hold for
\(G_{\mathrm{int}}=\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\); that count is for a different
model, and \(16-\dim G_{\mathrm{int}}\) does not apply here because the
orbit has components in \(H\).  For the displayed potential, three of
the six broken directions of Proposition~\ref{prop:goldstone-count}
carry mass \(\lambda v^2\), so that proposition's flat-Goldstone count
requires a boost-invariant potential, such as an \(\eta\)-contracted
form like the candidate discussed under Problem~D, whose quadratic form
is indefinite.  This dependence is recorded, not resolved.  Declaring
\(G_{\mathrm{int}}\) therefore does not by itself specify the
anti-Hermitian or \(A_\mu\) sectors of Problem~F: those depend on the
choice of potential, which is the open content of Problem~D.  The
statement is for the displayed action at quadratic order; it does not
rule out other constructions, does not evaluate any further sector of
Theorem~\ref{thm:Geff}, and leaves Problems~D and~F open.

\paragraph{Sector sum: a bound from the universal \(a_1\) piece.}
Fix a common limit pair \(\LUV,\mu\), take \(Q_s=0\), and keep only the
universal \(a_1\) term of Equation~\eqref{eq:universal-R}; the
curvature-linear part of \(a_2\), thresholds and counterterms are
dropped. Assume, as a convention to be checked against the
continuation of Appendix~\ref{app:seeley-dewitt}, that the Euclidean
effective action contains
\(-\tfrac{1}{16\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}}}\int\sqrt g\,R_E\). Then
\[
  \frac1{G_{\mathrm{eff}}}
  =\frac{\mathcal N_0}{12\pi}\,(\LUV^2-\mu^2),
  \qquad
  \mathcal N_0=\sum_{s}w_s\operatorname{tr}_{V_s}\mathbb I_s ,
\]
that is, \(A=1/(12\pi)\) for this piece alone. This is the
weighted-trace form of the single-sector formula of
Section~\ref{sec:ch17-coeff}. Suppose in addition that the induced term
of the displayed-action time-kinetic paragraph is a linearised
Einstein--Hilbert term whose coefficient \(g=1/(16\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}})\)
is the total induced coefficient, so that its window
\(1/G_{\mathrm{eff}}<8\pi v^2\) holds. At \(\mu\to0\) this gives
\[
  0<\mathcal N_0\,\frac{\LUV^2}{v^2}<96\pi^2,
  \qquad\text{i.e.}\qquad
  \frac{\LUV}{v}<\pi\sqrt{\frac{96}{\mathcal N_0}} .
\]
For \(\mu>0\) the factor \(\LUV^2-\mu^2\) only weakens the bound.
Positivity of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\) requires \(\mathcal N_0>0\) in this
convention. If \(\LUV\gg v\), the weighted sum must therefore nearly
cancel. As an illustration only, one unit-weight sixteen-component
sector would give \(\LUV/v<\pi\sqrt6\approx7.7\). The numerics are
illustrative, not an evaluation of \(\mathcal N_0\). The unresummed
truncation also requires \(\lambda v^2\ll\LUV^2\) for the massive
sectors, which may fail for \(\LUV\) of order \(v\). The sum itself is
not computed here because the manuscript does not supply: the
gauge-fixed operators and \(E_s\) for the \(\mathrm K\) and
\(A_\mu\) sectors (which depend on the \(G_{\mathrm{int}}\) and
potential choices of Problem~D), the diffeomorphism and local-Lorentz
ghost operators and weights, the per-sector limits, the thresholds,
and the continuation convention. Problems~B, C, D, F and~G remain open.

\paragraph{Conditional gauge-fixed Hermitian block in the compact
\(U(4)\) branch (not closure).}
This calculation concerns only the separate compact \(U(4)\) model
described above, not the \(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\)-compatible construction.
Assume \(A_\mu\) is a Hermitian \(U(4)\) connection, with no
\(F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}\) term, and write
\(\Phi=v\mathbb I+H+iY\), where \(H,Y\) are Hermitian. To quadratic
order the displayed action gives
\[
  D_\mu\Phi
  =\partial_\mu H+i(\partial_\mu Y-vA_\mu)+O(\text{quadratic}),
\]
\[
  S_{\Phi,A}^{(2)}
  =\int\mathrm{d}^4x\,\sqrt{-g^{(0)}}\,
   \operatorname{Tr}\!\left[
   -g^{(0)\mu\nu}\left(
    \partial_\mu H\,\partial_\nu H+
    (\partial_\mu Y-vA_\mu)(\partial_\nu Y-vA_\nu)
   \right)-\lambda v^2H^2\right].
\]
Thus the derivative mixing is between \(Y\) and \(A_\mu\), not \(H\)
and \(A_\mu\). Choose the linearized unitary gauge \(Y/v=0\); \(Y\)
is eliminated by this gauge condition and is not integrated as a
fluctuation in this gauge. For
\(\Phi\mapsto e^{i\alpha}\Phi\), one has \(\delta Y=v\alpha\) at the
vacuum, so the Faddeev--Popov operator for this condition is
\(\mathbb I_{16}\) there. A complex ghost pair has relative
determinant weight \(w=-2\) in the convention of
Equation~\eqref{eq:ch8-sector-sum}, but this operator is ultralocal.
In the same gauge \(A_\mu\) has only an algebraic quadratic term:
the action supplies no differential operator for it. Its determinant
and the ghost determinant are therefore not Laplace-type
heat-kernel sectors; their regulated local contributions are not
evaluated here.

For the differential Hermitian block \(H\) alone, further assume
that its operator on the emergent background is
\(-\nabla^2+\lambda v^2\), with no \(R_E\) term in \(E\), and assign
it common limits \(\LUV,\mu\). These are Presentation-B and cutoff
assumptions, not consequences of the displayed action on the fixed
scaffold. In the truncated, unresummed expansion,
\[
  \mathcal N_{0,H}=16,\qquad
  \mathcal A_{1,R,H}=\frac83,\qquad
  \mathcal A_{2,R,H}=-\frac83\lambda v^2 .
\]
The \(a_2\) coefficient uses, without re-derivation, the
curvature-linear term
\(\tfrac12\operatorname{tr}(R_E/6-E)^2\) used in the preceding
Hermitian-block bookkeeping paragraph; the expressions here are its
\(Q=0\) specialization. With the Euclidean matching convention
\(C_R^E=-1/(16\pi G_H)\), Theorem~\ref{thm:Geff} then gives the
single-block contribution
\[
  \frac1{G_H}
  =\frac{4}{3\pi}\left[
     \LUV^2-\mu^2
     -\lambda v^2\ln\!\left(\frac{\LUV^2}{\mu^2}\right)
   \right].
\]
Using \(\ln(\LUV^2/\mu^2)=2\ln(\LUV/\mu)\), this block alone
corresponds formally to
\(A_H=1/(12\pi)\), \(B_H=-8\lambda/(3\pi)\), and
\(C_H=-4/(3\pi)\) in Equation~\eqref{eq:Geff-structure}. These are
formal single-block values in a parameterization whose sectors need
not share \(\mu\); they are not full-theory coefficients. The
unresummed expansion requires \(\lambda v^2\ll\LUV^2\), so these
values are not threshold-correct. The algebraic determinants, other
fluctuation and ghost sectors, sector thresholds, continuation, and
finite curvature counterterm are not included. The displayed
nonnegative potential is not boost-invariant, so this compact branch
does not supply the missing \(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\)-compatible sector
sum. Problems~B, C, D, F and~G remain open.

\paragraph{Finite-cell check of the ultralocal determinants
(conditional; not closure).}
In the compact \(U(4)\) branch and linearized unitary gauge of the
preceding paragraph, regulate the Euclidean fixed scaffold by finitely
many cells of Euclidean proper volume \(\Delta V_0\). Use a basis of
Hermitian generators orthonormal under the trace and Lebesgue measure
for the four Euclidean orthonormal components of each of the sixteen
generators. At the vacuum the linearized Faddeev--Popov matrix is
\(\mathbb I_{16}\) at each cell, so its finite-dimensional determinant
is one and its contribution \(\operatorname{Tr}\ln\mathbb I_{16}\)
vanishes in this regulator. The eliminated \(Y\) fields are not
integrated again.

After continuation, the Euclidean action for the algebraic \(A_\mu\)
variables is positive in all four orthonormal components. The
finite-cell Gaussian is
\[
  \int\mathrm{d}^{64}A\,
       \exp\!\left[-v^2\Delta V_0
          \sum_{a=1}^{16}\sum_{\hat\mu=1}^{4}
                 (A_{\hat\mu}^{a})^2\right]
  =\left(\frac{\pi}{v^2\Delta V_0}\right)^{32}.
\]
For equal cells its logarithm is
\(-32N_{\mathrm{cells}}\ln(v^2\Delta V_0/\pi)\), a
volume-type term with no curvature dependence in this scaffold
regulator. That absence of curvature dependence is a property of the
regulator built on the fixed scaffold, not a result about the
emergent curvature coefficient. The \(A_\mu\) integral is not of
Laplace type, so \(a_1\) does not apply to it as written. Its local
contribution depends on the functional-measure normalization; the
displayed action and heat-kernel cutoff alone do not select that
normalization. This calculation does not justify assigning these
ultralocal factors zero in a different measure or regulator, and does
not determine \(C_R^E\). It is confined to the compact \(U(4)\) branch
and does not provide the missing \(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\)-compatible
sector sum. Problems~B, C, D, F and~G remain open.

\paragraph{Which metric the scaffold one-loop action induces (conditional; not closure).}
Consider the displayed action~\eqref{eq:Smicro} with
potential~\eqref{eq:V} on the square locked block, on the scaffold
\(g^{(0)}\) of Assumption~\ref{ass:aux-metric}, after Wick rotation,
with the internal connection held at a background value
\(\bar A_\mu\) and not integrated. Expand \(\Phi=\bar\Phi+\varphi\)
about a smooth background \(\bar\Phi\) and write \(\varphi\) in
thirty-two real components normalized so that the kinetic term is
\(\sum_a g^{(0)\mu\nu}\bar D_\mu\varphi_a\bar D_\nu\varphi_a\). The
kinetic term is bilinear in \(\Phi\), so its second variation does not
depend on \(\bar\Phi\), and the fluctuation operator is
\[
  \mathcal O_{\bar\Phi}
  =-g^{(0)\mu\nu}\bar D_\mu\bar D_\nu+E(\bar\Phi),
\]
where \(\bar D\) is built from \(g^{(0)}\) and \(\bar A_\mu\), and
\(E(\bar\Phi)\) is one half of the Hessian of \(V\) at \(\bar\Phi\);
\(E\) contains no derivative of \(\bar\Phi\). Use this operator
in~\eqref{eq:W-again} with the limits~\eqref{eq:s-limits}. For
\(I(\LUV)=\int_{\LUV^{-2}}^{\mu^{-2}}s^{-1}f(s)\,\mathrm{d}s\) one has
\(\partial_{\LUV}I=2\LUV^{-1}f(\LUV^{-2})\), so the
\(\LUV\)-dependence of \(W_E^{(1)}\) is fixed by the small-\(s\)
expansion of the heat trace through \(a_2\), up to terms that vanish as
\(\LUV\to\infty\). By~\eqref{eq:appA-a0}--\eqref{eq:appA-a2},
\[
  W_E^{(1)}\supset
  -\int\mathrm{d}^4x\,\sqrt{g^{(0)}}\left[
    \frac{32\,\LUV^4}{64\pi^2}
    +\frac{\LUV^2}{32\pi^2}
     \operatorname{tr}\Bigl(\tfrac16R[g^{(0)}]-E(\bar\Phi)\Bigr)
    +\frac{1}{32\pi^2}\ln\Bigl(\frac{\LUV^2}{\mu^2}\Bigr)a_2
  \right],
\]
where \(a_2\) contains \(E^2\), \(R[g^{(0)}]E\), curvature-squared
terms of \(g^{(0)}\), \(\bar F_{\mu\nu}\bar F^{\mu\nu}\) and total
derivatives. No \(\LUV^4\), \(\LUV^2\) or \(\ln\LUV\) term contains a derivative of
\(\bar\Phi\) outside a total derivative; terms with derivatives of
\(\bar\Phi\) have coefficients that are independent of \(\LUV\) or vanish
as \(\LUV\to\infty\). The \(\LUV^2\) curvature term
is the term~\eqref{eq:universal-R}, with \(\mathcal N_0=32\), for the
scaffold metric \(g^{(0)}\), which Assumption~\ref{ass:aux-metric}
holds fixed.

For the composite metric the corresponding coefficient vanishes. On
the flat scaffold take \(\bar A_\mu=0\) and
\(\bar\Phi=(v+\sigma)\mathbb I\), so that
\(g=(1+\sigma/v)^2\eta\) by~\eqref{eq:repairII-trace}; in the
Euclidean section \(\sqrt g\,R[g]=6(\partial\sigma)^2/v^2\) up to a
total derivative. The \(\LUV^2\) term above is
\(\tfrac{\LUV^2}{32\pi^2}\int\mathrm{d}^4x\operatorname{tr}E(\sigma)\),
with \(\operatorname{tr}E(\sigma)=16\lambda(v^2+4v\sigma+2\sigma^2)\),
and contains no \((\partial\sigma)^2\). Building the operator on \(g\)
instead, as in Assumption~\ref{ass:emergent-op}, gives
\(-\mathcal N_0\LUV^2(\partial\sigma)^2/(32\pi^2v^2)\) at the same
order, which is the induced term of~\eqref{eq:Z-sigma} with
\(1/(16\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}})=\mathcal N_0\LUV^2/(192\pi^2)\). The
replacement supplies the whole \(\LUV^2\) coefficient for the composite
metric; it does not correct a scaffold value.

The \((\partial\sigma)^2\) term of the scaffold one-loop action has no
\(\LUV^2\) or \(\ln\LUV\) piece. Take the limits~\eqref{eq:s-limits}
and \(p^2\ll\mu^2\ll\lambda v^2\). At \(\bar A_\mu=0\) the sixteen
Hermitian components have
\(E_H=\tfrac\lambda2\bigl(3(v+\sigma)^2-v^2\bigr)\) and the sixteen
anti-Hermitian components have
\(E_K=\tfrac\lambda2\bigl((v+\sigma)^2-v^2\bigr)\). At second order in
\(\sigma\) a block of \(n\) components with mass \(m\) gives
\[
  W_E^{(1)}\supset
  -\frac n4\,E'(0)^2\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^4p}{(2\pi)^4}\,
   \tilde\sigma(p)\tilde\sigma(-p)\,B(p),
\]
\[
  B(p)=\frac{1}{16\pi^2}\int_0^1\mathrm{d}x\left[
   E_1\!\left(\frac{m^2+x(1-x)p^2}{\LUV^2}\right)
   -E_1\!\left(\frac{m^2+x(1-x)p^2}{\mu^2}\right)\right],
\]
with \(E_1(x)=\int_x^\infty e^{-t}t^{-1}\,\mathrm dt\), the exponential
integral. For the Hermitian block, \(n=16\), \(E'(0)=3\lambda v\),
\(m^2=\lambda v^2\) and
\(\partial_{p^2}B|_{p=0}=-\bigl(e^{-\lambda v^2/\LUV^2}
-e^{-\lambda v^2/\mu^2}\bigr)/(96\pi^2\lambda v^2)\). For the
anti-Hermitian block, \(n=16\), \(E'(0)=\lambda v\), \(m=0\) and
\(\partial_{p^2}B|_{p=0}=(\LUV^{-2}-\mu^{-2})/(96\pi^2)\). The local
term \(\tfrac12\Delta Z\int\mathrm{d}^4x\,(\partial\sigma)^2\) therefore
adds to the microscopic value \(2N\) of \(Z\) in~\eqref{eq:Z-sigma}
\[
  \Delta Z=\frac{3\lambda}{4\pi^2}
   \Bigl(e^{-\lambda v^2/\LUV^2}-e^{-\lambda v^2/\mu^2}\Bigr)
   +\frac{\lambda^2v^2}{12\pi^2}\Bigl(\frac1{\mu^2}-\frac1{\LUV^2}\Bigr),
\]
that is, \(3\lambda/4\pi^2\) up to \(O(\lambda v^2/\LUV^2)\) and
\(O(e^{-\lambda v^2/\mu^2})\) from the Hermitian block, plus a positive
term from the anti-Hermitian block that depends on the infrared limit
\(\mu\) and is replaced by a term non-analytic in \(p^2\) as
\(\mu\to0\). Both terms are positive for \(\mu<\LUV\) and carry
no \(\LUV^2\) or \(\ln\LUV\) piece. In this one-loop calculation the
conformal-mode kinetic term keeps the sign of Lemma~\ref{lem:msigma}.
The opposite-sign term of~\eqref{eq:Z-sigma} is not produced in this
calculation; in the manuscript it arises from the induced
Einstein--Hilbert term, that is, from the operator built on the
emergent metric.

\paragraph{Scaffold rotations exclude linearized diffeomorphism invariance in a stated class (conditional; not closure).}
In~\eqref{eq:Smicro} the components of \(\Phi^I{}_A\) are scalars of
the scaffold: a scaffold index appears only on \(\partial_\mu\) and
\(A_\mu\), and on \(\omega_\mu\) in the completion~\eqref{eq:D-both}.
On the flat Euclidean scaffold, rotations \(x\mapsto Rx\),
\(R\in\mathrm{SO}(4)\), acting on scaffold indices alone are a
symmetry, and the background \(\bar\Phi=v\mathbb I\),
\(\bar A_\mu=\bar\omega_\mu=0\) is invariant. Assume that the regulator
and the gauge fixing preserve this symmetry, as the scaffold
proper-time regulator above does, and that the composite-metric
fluctuation \(h_{\mu\nu}\) is a fixed linear function of the components
of \(\Phi\), for example the one obtained from~\eqref{eq:g-from-e}
with the identification~\eqref{eq:vev-is-frame}. Then, at any loop
order, the quadratic part of the effective action in the components of
\(\Phi\), with the connections set to zero or eliminated at quadratic
order, has the form
\(\sum_{a,b}\tilde\varphi_a(-p)F_{ab}(p^2)\tilde\varphi_b(p)\):
invariance gives \(F_{ab}(Rp)=F_{ab}(p)\), an
\(\mathrm{SO}(4)\)-invariant function of one vector depends only on
\(p^2\), and eliminating a connection, whose mixed and diagonal kernels
have the forms \(p_\mu f(p^2)\) and
\(\alpha(p^2)\delta_{\mu\nu}+\beta(p^2)p_\mu p_\nu\) with internal
labels suppressed, preserves this form. Let \(Q_p\) be the reduced form
on \(h\) obtained by eliminating the remaining components; it is the
reduced form that inherits any invariance of the full effective action
under a linearized gauge transformation, and it too depends on \(p\)
only through \(p^2\). If \(Q_p\) is invariant under
\(h\mapsto h+p\otimes\xi+\xi\otimes p\) for all \(\xi\), then \(Q_p\)
annihilates \(p'\otimes\xi+\xi\otimes p'\) for every \(p'\) with
\(p'^2=p^2\); for \(p\neq0\) these tensors span all symmetric tensors,
so \(Q_p=0\). The same holds on the traceless block with the
transformation~\eqref{eq:FP-gauge}. The linearized Einstein--Hilbert
form and the massless Fierz--Pauli form~\eqref{eq:FP} are invariant
and nonzero, hence depend on the direction of \(p\); \(Q_p\) cannot
equal \(c\) times either of them plus a form depending on \(p\) only
through \(p^2\) unless \(c=0\). A form depending only on \(p^2\) can
coincide with a gauge-fixed linearized Einstein--Hilbert operator, for
example the Feynman--de~Donder form
\(p^2\bigl(h_{\mu\nu}h_{\mu\nu}-\tfrac12h^2\bigr)\), but without the
gauge symmetry and its ghosts each nondegenerate direction of such a
form, including directions of opposite sign, is a propagating
component. This statement holds for a flat scaffold, a regulator and
gauge fixing that preserve scaffold rotations, a vacuum and background
invariant under them, and a composite-metric fluctuation that is a
fixed linear function of the components of \(\Phi\). It does not
exclude a composite-metric Einstein--Hilbert term in general: a
regulator or kinetic metric built from \(\Theta^{\mu\nu}\) in
Presentation~A (Remark~\ref{rem:ch3-varying-theta}), a vacuum or
background that is not invariant under scaffold rotations, a curved
scaffold, or a composite metric that is not a fixed linear image of
\(\Phi\) each relaxes one of these hypotheses and is not covered.

\paragraph{What the two preceding results imply for Problem~F (conditional; not closure).}
In the class of the preceding paragraph, neither the displayed action
nor its quantum corrections give the composite-metric fluctuation a
linearized diffeomorphism invariance, an induced Einstein--Hilbert
term, or the Fierz--Pauli block of hypothesis~5 of
Theorem~\ref{thm:mT}; the induced Fierz--Pauli term assumed in the
quadratic tests of Section~\ref{sec:status-D} is likewise not
generated in this way. These structures need an ingredient that ties
derivative indices to tangent indices. The
operator~\eqref{eq:O-eta} on the emergent background, used in
Theorem~\ref{thm:Geff}, is such an ingredient; it enters as
Assumption~\ref{ass:emergent-op}, which is assumed, not derived. The
one-loop \(\Phi\)--\(\Phi\) action of the displayed action on the
scaffold contributes to the \(\LUV^2\) and \(\ln\LUV\) coefficients
\(A\) and \(B\) of~\eqref{eq:Geff-structure} only through the curvature
of \(g^{(0)}\), not through that of the composite metric; the missing
step of Problem~F includes discharging
Assumption~\ref{ass:emergent-op}, or replacing it by another derived
ingredient. Not covered here: integration of the auxiliary \(A_\mu\) in
the first result, the hypotheses relaxed in the second, and
Presentation~A in general. No value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\)
follows. Problems~B, C, D and~F remain open.

\paragraph{Presentation~A in the Poisson limit: the \(\Theta\)-built metric and the connection sector (conditional; not closure).}
Let \(\Theta^{\mu\nu}(x)\) be a nondegenerate Poisson bivector and
\(\rho=1/|\operatorname{Pf}\Theta|\), so that
\(\partial_\mu(\rho\,\Theta^{\mu\nu})=0\). Keep the leading
semiclassical order only:
\([\cdot,\cdot]\to i\{\cdot,\cdot\}\) with
\(\{f,g\}=\Theta^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu f\,\partial_\nu g\),
\(\operatorname{Tr}\to\int\mathrm{d}^4x\,\rho\), and matrix-coordinate
indices contracted with \(\eta\). With
\(K^{\mu\nu}=\eta_{\alpha\beta}\Theta^{\alpha\mu}\Theta^{\beta\nu}\) and
\(g_\rho^{\mu\nu}=\rho K^{\mu\nu}\) as in
Remark~\ref{rem:ch3-varying-theta}, let \(G\) be the metric with
\(G^{\mu\nu}=K^{\mu\nu}\); then \(G=\rho\,g_\rho\),
\(|\det g_\rho|=1\) and \(\sqrt{|G|}=\rho^2\). Higher Moyal terms are
dropped. They are not small at momenta of order \(\LUV\), so the
coefficients below are those of the Poisson-limit operators within the
truncated expansion of Theorem~\ref{thm:Geff}. Signs and eigenvalues
refer to the Euclidean section \(\eta=\delta\); the algebraic identities
do not depend on the signature.

\emph{Scalar sector.} A real fluctuation whose kinetic term is the
Poisson limit of \(\operatorname{Tr}[\hat X_\mu,\varphi]^2\) has the
operator
\(\mathcal O_s=-\rho^{-1}\partial_\mu(\rho K^{\mu\nu}\partial_\nu\,\cdot\,)\).
On the metric \(G\) it has the form~\eqref{eq:O-eta}, with
\(D=\nabla+\omega\), \(\omega_\mu=-\tfrac12\partial_\mu\ln\rho\) and
\(E=-\tfrac12\Box_G\ln\rho
+\tfrac14G^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\ln\rho\,\partial_\nu\ln\rho\).
Since \(G=\rho\,g_\rho\) in four dimensions,
\(R_G=\rho^{-1}\bigl[R-3\Box_{g_\rho}\ln\rho
-\tfrac32|\nabla\ln\rho|^2_{g_\rho}\bigr]\) and
\(E=\rho^{-1}\bigl[-\tfrac12\Box_{g_\rho}\ln\rho
-\tfrac14|\nabla\ln\rho|^2_{g_\rho}\bigr]\), so that
\[
  \sqrt{|G|}\Bigl(\tfrac16R_G-E\Bigr)=\tfrac16\,\rho\,R[g_\rho]
\]
pointwise. For a constant \(\LUV\), one such component contributes
\(-\tfrac{\LUV^2}{192\pi^2}\int\mathrm{d}^4x\,\rho\,R[g_\rho]\)
to~\eqref{eq:W-expand}: the coefficient of
Theorem~\ref{thm:Geff}, multiplying the curvature of the
\(\Theta\)-built metric \(g_\rho\) with a factor \(\rho\). The
component is minimally coupled to \(g_\rho\).

\emph{Frame.} Let \(\omega=\Theta^{-1}\), \(\omega_{\mu B}\Theta^{B\nu}
=\delta_\mu{}^\nu\), and \(e^A{}_\mu=\eta^{AB}\omega_{\mu B}\). Then
\(G_{\mu\nu}=\eta_{AB}e^A{}_\mu e^B{}_\nu\), which is the
form~\eqref{eq:g-from-e} of Definition~\ref{def:composite-metric} for
a coframe with \(\eta_{AB}e^B{}_\mu\) antisymmetric in \((A,\mu)\).
At the sample points tested the map \(\Theta\mapsto G\) has rank six,
so that the \(\Theta\)-built metrics form a six-parameter subfamily of
the metrics, and no pure-gauge perturbation
\(p_{(\mu}\xi_{\nu)}\) of \(G\) lies in its tangent space. If
\(\langle\Phi\rangle/v\) of~\eqref{eq:vev-is-frame} were identified with
this coframe, the operator above would be that of
Assumption~\ref{ass:emergent-op} for the metric \(G\). This
identification is not derived.

\emph{Connection sector.} In background gauge with gauge-fixing term
\(\tfrac12\rho\,(D_\mu a^\mu)^2\), \(D^\mu=\{x^\mu,\cdot\}\), the part of
\eqref{eq:Smatrix} quadratic in the matrix gauge potential \(a^\mu\)
is, up to a constant factor,
\(\int\mathrm{d}^4x\,\rho\,[K^{\rho\sigma}\partial_\rho a_\nu\partial_\sigma a^\nu
-2a^\mu\{\Theta_{\mu\nu},a^\nu\}]\), the term linear in \(a^\mu\) not
being assumed to vanish. The ghost operator is
\(D_\mu D^\mu=-\mathcal O_s\), of weight \(-2\). The operator
\(\mathcal O_va^\mu=\mathcal O_sa^\mu
-2\eta^{\mu\alpha}\{\Theta_{\alpha\nu},a^\nu\}\) has the
form~\eqref{eq:O-eta} on \(G\) with
\(\omega_\sigma=-\tfrac12\partial_\sigma\ln\rho\,\mathbb I+\mathcal B_\sigma\),
\((\mathcal B^\sigma)^\mu{}_\nu=\eta^{\mu\alpha}\Theta^{\rho\sigma}
\partial_\rho\Theta_{\alpha\nu}\), and
\(E=\nabla_\sigma\omega^\sigma+\omega_\sigma\omega^\sigma\). Then
\[
  \sqrt{|G|}\operatorname{tr}\Bigl(\tfrac16R_G-E\Bigr)
  =\tfrac46\,\rho\,R[g_\rho]+\rho^2X,\qquad
  X=\eta^{\rho\lambda}\eta_{\mu\alpha}\eta_{\nu\beta}\,
    \partial_\rho\Theta^{\mu\nu}\,\partial_\lambda\Theta^{\alpha\beta},
\]
and with the ghosts the connection sector contributes
\(\tfrac26\rho R[g_\rho]+\rho^2X\). The derivative index of \(X\) is
contracted with \(\eta\) and not with \(G\). For
\(\Theta^{01}=\vartheta_1(x^0,x^1)\), \(\Theta^{23}=\vartheta_2(x^2,x^3)\)
one finds
\(X=2K^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\ln\rho\,\partial_\nu\ln\rho\); for a
generic Poisson bivector \(X\) differs from this one expression.

\emph{Quadratic form about constant \(\bar\Theta\).} Let
\(\Theta=\bar\Theta+\epsilon T\), \(T^{\mu\nu}
=\bar\Theta^{\mu\rho}\partial_\rho a^\nu-\bar\Theta^{\nu\rho}\partial_\rho a^\mu\),
\(a^\mu=u^\mu\cos(p\cdot x)\). With \(q^\mu=\bar\Theta^{\mu\rho}p_\rho\),
the phase-averaged second-order terms of \(\rho R[g_\rho]\) and
\(\rho^2X\) are quadratic forms on the three-dimensional quotient by
\(u\propto q\). Relative to the tree-level kernel
\(M=q^2\eta-qq\) of Remark~\ref{rem:ch3-trace-quadratic}, with
\(p_\eta^2=\eta^{\mu\nu}p_\mu p_\nu\) and
\(\bar\rho=1/|\operatorname{Pf}\bar\Theta|\), their Euclidean
eigenvalues are
\(\bar\rho^{2}p_\eta^2\) (three modes) for \(\rho^2X\), and
\(-\tfrac14\bar\rho^{2}p_\eta^2\) (two modes with \(\delta\rho=0\), for
which the linearized Einstein--Hilbert form of \(\delta g_\rho\) equals
that of its transverse-traceless part with respect to \(p\) and
\(\bar g_\rho\)) and \(-\tfrac34\bar\rho^{2}p_\eta^2\) (one mode with
\(\delta\rho\ne0\)) for \(\rho R[g_\rho]\). Let
\(N_{\mathrm{tot}}=N_s+2\), where \(N_s\) counts, with statistical
sign, the real components whose fluctuation operator is
\(\mathcal O_s\) with the same cutoff; it is an input, not a derived
count. In the Euclidean section the \(\LUV^2\) quadratic form is
\(-\tfrac{\LUV^2}{32\pi^2}\bar\rho^{2}p_\eta^2\,
(1-N_{\mathrm{tot}}/24)\,M\) on the two transverse-traceless-type
modes and \((1-N_{\mathrm{tot}}/8)\,M\) on the third. The net
coefficient on the two transverse-traceless-type modes has the sign of
a positive induced Einstein--Hilbert term only if
\(N_{\mathrm{tot}}>24\); the Einstein--Hilbert-type piece itself always
has that sign. For the connection sector alone, \(\rho^2X\) has the
opposite sign and dominates. This is a statement about the
Maxwell-kernel coefficient on these modes. Both pieces are multiples of
\(M\) by functions of \(p_\eta^2\), so they do not move the cone
\(q^2=0\), and about a constant background they do not fix
\(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\).

\emph{Beyond quadratic order.} Nondegenerate Poisson bivectors near
\(\bar\Theta\) are \(\Theta=(\psi^*\bar\omega)^{-1}\),
\(\bar\omega=\bar\Theta^{-1}\), \(\psi=x+w(x)\). Modulo total
derivatives, the scalar densities of weight \(\rho\) with two
derivatives built covariantly from \(g_\rho\) and \(\rho\) are the
combinations of \(\rho R[g_\rho]\) and
\(\rho\,g_\rho^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\ln\rho\,\partial_\nu\ln\rho\). At
quadratic order about constant \(\bar\Theta\), \(\rho^2X\) coincides
with \(-4\rho R[g_\rho]-4\rho\,g_\rho^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\ln\rho\,
\partial_\nu\ln\rho\) for the random backgrounds and both signatures
sampled. In a numerical test (double precision, \(w\) a sum of three
plane waves with \(\sum p_j=0\), forty random samples) the cubic
coefficient of \(\rho^2X\) is not of the form
\(c_1\rho R[g_\rho]+c_2\rho\,g_\rho^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\ln\rho\,
\partial_\nu\ln\rho\) for any constants (relative residual about
\(0.3\) for the best fit). So, on this sample, \(\rho^2X\) is not a
combination of these two covariant densities modulo total derivatives,
and the quadratic agreement does not extend. Rewritings that use
further structure, such as the constant \(\eta\), are not tested.

If \(\LUV^2\) is replaced by \(\LUV^2f(\rho)\) (a local proper-time
cutoff on the diagonal heat kernel), the coefficient of
\(R[g_\rho]\) becomes \(f(\rho)\rho\); with
\(\hat g=f\rho\,g_\rho\),
\(\int f\rho R[g_\rho]=\int\sqrt{\hat g}\,[\hat R
-\tfrac32\hat g^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\ln(f\rho)\partial_\nu\ln(f\rho)]\).
The factor on the two transverse-traceless-type modes is unchanged, and
for \(f=\rho^s\) the third becomes \(1-N_{\mathrm{tot}}(2s+3)/24\).

This is a computation for Poisson-limit operators of a bosonic
four-dimensional matrix gauge sector in a stated gauge, off shell or
about a constant background. It does not identify \(G\) with the
emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}, does not
treat Moyal corrections at the cutoff, fermionic or additional scalar
fields, the mixing of \(\Phi\) with \(a^\mu\), the locked-block
diffeomorphism and local-Lorentz ghosts, or the relation of the induced
term to the tree-level term of~\eqref{eq:Smatrix}, and gives no value or
sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). The kinetic metric and regulator here
are built from \(\Theta\), and the map \(\Theta\mapsto G\) is nonlinear,
so the hypotheses of the scaffold-rotation statement above (a flat
scaffold, a rotation-preserving regulator, an invariant vacuum, and a
composite-metric fluctuation that is a fixed linear image of \(\Phi\))
do not apply; no linearized-diffeomorphism invariance is claimed.
Problems~B, C, D and~F remain open.

\paragraph{Presentation~A in the Poisson limit: what four matrix coordinates can supply (conditional; not closure).}
Keep the setting of the preceding paragraph: four matrix coordinates, the Poisson limit, matrix indices contracted with \(\eta=\mathrm{diag}(-1,1,1,1)\), a real nondegenerate \(\Theta\), \(K^{\mu\nu}=\eta_{\alpha\beta}\Theta^{\alpha\mu}\Theta^{\beta\nu}\), \(G=K^{-1}\), \(\rho=1/|\operatorname{Pf}\Theta|\), \(g_\rho^{\mu\nu}=\rho K^{\mu\nu}\). Let \(N=\Theta\eta\in\mathfrak{so}(3,1)\) with electric and magnetic parts \(\mathbf E,\mathbf B\), so \(\operatorname{Pf}\Theta=\pm\mathbf E\cdot\mathbf B\). For vectors \(a,b\) write \(a\circ b:=ab^T+ba^T\).

\emph{Image of \(\Theta\mapsto G\).} The characteristic polynomial of \(N\) is \(z^4+(\mathbf B^2-\mathbf E^2)z^2-(\mathbf E\cdot\mathbf B)^2\), so \(K\eta=-N^2\) has two doubly degenerate eigenvalues and \((K\eta)^2-\tfrac12\operatorname{tr}(K\eta)\,K\eta-(\operatorname{Pf}\Theta)^2\,\mathbb I=0\). Hence the six-dimensional image has codimension four in the ten-dimensional space of metrics and lies in the set cut out by this relation. Decomposing \(N=(n_+,n_-)\) in \(\mathfrak{sl}_2\oplus\mathfrak{sl}_2\), \(N^2=(\nu_++\nu_-)\mathbb I+2\,n_+\otimes n_-\), and a kernel element of the differential of \(\Theta\mapsto K\) must satisfy \(\delta n_\pm=\mp\lambda n_\pm\) and \(\lambda(\nu_+-\nu_-)=0\); since \(\operatorname{Im}\nu_+=0\) only if \(\mathbf E\cdot\mathbf B=0\), the map \(\Theta\mapsto G\) has rank six at every \(\Theta\) with \(\operatorname{Pf}\Theta\neq0\). The differential of \(\Theta\mapsto K\) has rank five when \(\Theta\neq0\) and \(\operatorname{Pf}\Theta=0\) (symbolic and exact-rational checks).

\emph{Linear response.} Let \(\Theta=\bar\Theta+\epsilon T\), \(T^{\mu\nu}=\bar\Theta^{\mu\rho}\partial_\rho a^\nu-\bar\Theta^{\nu\rho}\partial_\rho a^\mu\), \(a^\mu=u^\mu\cos(p\cdot x)\), \(q=\bar\Theta p\). The amplitude of \(\sin(p\cdot x)\) in \(\delta G\) is \((\bar Gu)\circ p+n\circ m\), with \(n=\bar G\bar\Theta p\), \(m=\bar G\bar\Theta\eta u\), and \(\delta\ln\rho=p\cdot u\). For \(u\propto q\) one has \(T=0\) (the matrix gauge). The first term is a linearised diffeomorphism. Since \(n\) and \(p\) are covectors, orthogonality is taken with the metric \(\bar K\): \(n^T\bar Kp=0\), while \(n\) is not parallel to \(p\) for generic \(p\), so the second term is not a diffeomorphism. The images of \(u\mapsto(\delta G,\delta\rho)\) and \(u\mapsto(\delta g_\rho,\delta\rho)\) are three-dimensional. For generic \(p\) the linearised Einstein operator about the constant background has rank three on this image, and its image lies in the six-dimensional space of symmetric tensors with \(p^\mu T_{\mu\nu}=0\) (``conserved''; exact-rational check for \(G\) and for \(g_\rho\)). Hence the tensor equation \(\mathcal E[h]=T\), and any linear local relation between \((\delta G,\delta\rho)\) and \(T\), can hold only for \(T\) in a subspace of dimension at most three of the six-dimensional space of conserved sources; varying the four functions \(a^\mu\) gives only the projection of the equation onto that image. This does not exclude an additional conserved stress that absorbs the codimension; such a stress would have to be derived. As a counting argument (not a derived result), four coordinate functions minus one matrix gauge function leave three gauge-invariant functions, against \(10-4+1=7\) for a diffeomorphism-invariant theory of a metric and one scalar. The tree-level quadratic action of Remark~\ref{rem:ch3-trace-quadratic} is proportional to \(\int\rho\,\eta_{\mu\alpha}\eta_{\nu\beta}T^{\mu\nu}T^{\alpha\beta}\), algebraic in \((\delta G,\delta\rho)\); every term with derivatives of the metric perturbation is a loop term. Whether the algebraic term is cancelled is the content of Assumption~\ref{ass:EE} and is not decided here.

\emph{Metric seen by the operators.} Suppose, as an assumed dictionary not contained in the four-matrix class, that there are \(D\ge4\) coordinate matrices \(X^I\) with a Lorentzian \(\eta_{IJ}\), and put \(\Phi^I{}_\mu:=\partial_\mu x^I\), \(g:=\Phi^T\eta\Phi\). Then \(K=\theta^{T}g\,\theta\) exactly; for \(D=4\), \(x^I=y^I\) and \(g=\eta\). The relation \(K=c\,g^{-1}\), \(c\neq0\), would give \(N^2=-c\) with \(N=\theta g\in\mathfrak{so}(g)\), whose characteristic polynomial has constant term \(-(\mathbf E\cdot\mathbf B)^2\le0<c^2\); \(c=0\) contradicts invertibility. So, pointwise for any real Lorentzian \(g\), the operator metric \(G=K^{-1}\) is not \(g\); this extends Remark~\ref{rem:ch3-moyal-check}, and Assumption~\ref{ass:emergent-op} read with the metric of that embedding reading is not discharged. The metric \(G\) itself has the form~\eqref{eq:g-from-e} for the coframe of the preceding paragraph. Let \(\ell\) be the fixed length scale in \(\Theta/\ell^2\), and \(e=\eta^{-1}\hat\omega\) with \(\hat\omega=(\Theta/\ell^2)^{-1}\). If \(e\) is identified with \(\langle\Phi\rangle/v\) as in~\eqref{eq:vev-is-frame}, then, with the internal index contracted with \(\delta\) and the locked block real, \(e^Te=\mathbb I\) on the vacuum manifold of~\eqref{eq:V} holds iff \(\hat\omega^2=-\mathbb I\), i.e. \(J:=\hat\omega\) is an orthogonal complex structure. Then \(G=\mathbb I-2nn^T\) with \(n=-Je_0\), where \(e_0\) is the first basis vector, so \(n\) is a spatial unit vector, and only two of the six directions of \(\delta\Theta\) are tangent to that manifold.

This is a statement for four matrix coordinates, the Poisson limit, a constant \(\eta\), linear order about constant \(\bar\Theta\) and generic \(p\); it does not apply to an independent field \(\Phi\) of Presentation~B, to the embedding statement above beyond four coordinates, or to Moyal corrections. It does not derive \(\langle\Phi\rangle/v\) as a coframe and gives no value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). Problem~F, and Problems~A, B, C and~D, remain open.

\paragraph{More than four matrix coordinates in the Poisson limit: linear response (conditional; not closure).}
Keep Definition~\ref{def:matrix-coords-ext}, the Poisson limit, a four-dimensional symplectic leaf, a real Lorentzian induced tensor \(g\), \(\omega=\theta^{-1}\), \(K=\theta^{T}g\,\theta\) and \(G=K^{-1}\).

\emph{Operator metric versus induced metric.} The relation \(G=c\,g\) is excluded for every \(D\ge4\) by the argument of the preceding paragraph, because only the real four-dimensional leaf and the real Lorentzian \(g\) enter it; for a Euclidean induced \(g\) an orthogonal complex structure gives \(G\propto g\), but then \(G\) is Euclidean.

\emph{Linear response.} In a chart with constant \(\theta\), let \(\delta x^{I}=A^{I}e^{ip\cdot y}\), \(A=\xi^{\mu}\partial_\mu x+\phi_i n^{i}\) with \(n^{i}\) a normal frame and \(\mathrm{II}^{i}_{\mu\nu}=n^{i}\cdot\partial_\mu\partial_\nu x\); a factor \(i\) is absorbed in \(\xi\). To first order \(\delta g=p\circ(g\xi)-2\phi_i\mathrm{II}^{i}\) with \(a\circ b=ab^{T}+ba^{T}\), and \(\delta G=-G\,\theta^{T}\delta g\,\theta\,G\); the Hamiltonian choice \(\xi\propto\theta^{T}p\) gives \(\delta G=p\circ(G\xi)\), a linearised diffeomorphism of \(G\). About a flat brane the transverse amplitudes do not enter at first order, so the rank of the linearised Einstein operator of \(G\) on the image is exactly three for every \(D\), against six conserved sources \(p^{\mu}T_{\mu\nu}=0\). About a background with generic \(\mathrm{II}^{i}\) the rank is at most \(\min(6,D-1)\), because the image contains a gauge direction and lies in the space of conserved tensors; one exact-rational draw for each \(4\le D\le11\) attains this bound, so it is the generic rank by lower semicontinuity, and the same holds when \(K^{\mu\nu}\mathrm{II}^{i}_{\mu\nu}=0\). All six sources are then reachable from \(D=7\). The analysis freezes \(g\), \(\theta\) and \(\mathrm{II}\) at a point and does not impose the Gauss--Codazzi relations. Counting the density scalar as well, the number \(D-1\) of gauge-invariant functions must be at least \(7\), that is \(D\ge8\); this is a necessary count. No field equation of Einstein type for \(G\) is derived.

\emph{Content.} The \(D-4\) transverse coordinates are not covered by Assumption~\ref{ass:locked-block}; they are additional infrared fields unless they are gapped, and if they are gapped and decouple the flat-brane statement above applies. One-loop IKKT calculations on a brane with compact fuzzy extra dimensions report an induced Einstein--Hilbert term with the Newton constant set by the Kaluza--Klein scale~\cite{Steinacker2023}, and modified Einstein equations with extra tensors are reported in~\cite{KumarSteinacker2023,Steinacker2026}. A leaf of dimension greater than four is the hypothesis of the first statement above that such a construction would relax. These results have not been checked against the present operators.

This is a statement for \(D\) matrix coordinates, the Poisson limit, a four-dimensional symplectic leaf, a constant \(\eta_{IJ}\), locally frozen background data and generic \(p\). It does not identify \(G\) or \(g\) with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}, does not identify \(\Phi^{I}{}_{A}\) with a tangent-plane frame, does not derive a local Lorentz symmetry, a signature or an extra conserved stress, and gives no value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\) in Equation~\eqref{eq:Geff-structure}. This changes no ledger label; Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Leaf dimension above four: fibre averages, a product factor and a conjugate-coordinate class (conditional; not closure).}
Work in the Poisson limit with matrix coordinates \(x^I\), \(I=0,\dots,D-1\), contracted with \(\eta=\mathrm{diag}(-1,1,\dots,1)\), on a symplectic leaf of dimension \(2m\ge4\), and four base functions \(y^\mu\) on the leaf. For a function \(\phi(y^\mu)\) the kinetic tensor is \(K^{\mu\nu}=\langle\eta_{IJ}V^{I\mu}V^{J\nu}\rangle\) with \(V^{I\mu}=\{x^I,y^\mu\}\) and \(\langle\cdot\rangle\) a positive average over the fibre; for a four-dimensional leaf it is \(\theta^{T}g\theta\).

\emph{Matrices as base coordinates.} Let \(y^\mu=x^\mu\), \(\mu=0,\dots,3\), so that \(V^{I\mu}=\Theta^{I\mu}\) is real and antisymmetric. Then \(K^{00}=\langle\sum_{I\ge1}(\Theta^{I0})^2\rangle\ge0\) and \(\sum_\mu K^{\mu\mu}=\langle\sum_{I\ge4}(\Theta^{I0})^2+\sum_{I\ge1,\,k\ge1}(\Theta^{Ik})^2\rangle\ge0\), whereas \(K=c\,\eta^{-1}\) has \(K^{00}=-c\) and \(\sum_\mu K^{\mu\mu}=2c\); hence \(c=0\). The case \(K=0\) forces \(\Theta^{I\mu}=0\) for \(\mu\le3\), so the \(x^\mu\) would be Casimir functions, contradicting nondegeneracy. Therefore \(K\ne c\,\eta^{-1}\) for every \(c\ne0\), for every \(D\), every compact fibre and every positive average: in this class, enlarging the leaf by a fibre does not remove the proportionality obstruction of the preceding paragraphs. For any nondegenerate real Lorentzian \(g_L\) on a leaf of dimension \(2m\ge4\), \(G_L=(\theta^{T}g_L\theta)^{-1}\) is not proportional to \(g_L\). Indeed \(K=c\,g_L^{-1}\) with \(c\neq0\) gives \(N^2=-c\,\mathbb I\) for \(N=\theta g_L\). For \(c>0\), \(\det N=c^m>0\), whereas \(\det N=(\operatorname{Pf}\theta)^2\det g_L<0\). For \(c<0\), \(N\) is \(g_L\)-skew with \(N^2=|c|\,\mathbb I\), so its \(\pm\sqrt{|c|}\) eigenspaces are totally null and have dimension at most one in Lorentzian signature, which forces \(2m\le2\); for \(2m=2\) the relation can hold (\(g_L=\mathrm{diag}(-1,1)\), \(\theta=2\epsilon\) gives \(K=-4g_L^{-1}\)). For a product leaf \(M\times\mathcal C\) with block-diagonal \(\theta\) and embedding, \(V^{i\mu}=0\) for the coordinates along \(\mathcal C\), so \(K\) is the tensor of the \(M\) factor, and to first order the response of \(K\) to \(\delta x^I=A^I(t)e^{ip\cdot y}\) is the four-dimensional one with amplitude \(\langle A^\mu\rangle\); transverse amplitudes and non-constant fibre parts do not enter. The rank statements of the preceding paragraph therefore hold with \(D\) the number of coordinates that vary over \(M\), and Kaluza--Klein modes enter only quadratically.

\emph{Conjugate coordinates.} The proportionality is not excluded when the base functions are not among the matrix coordinates. Let \(M^{AB}\), \(A,B=0,\dots,5\), generate \(\mathfrak{so}(4,2)\) with invariant form \(\mathrm{diag}(-,+,+,+,+,-)\) on the coadjoint orbit \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\) (six-dimensional leaf), take the four matrix coordinates \(T^a=M^{a4}/R\), \(a=0,\dots,3\), and the base functions \(y^\mu=M^{\mu5}/R\). The functions \(y^\mu\) are not among the \(T^a\), and the leaf is not the four-dimensional leaf of Definition~\ref{def:matrix-coords-ext}. The Lie--Poisson relation gives \(\{T^a,y^\mu\}=\pm\eta^{a\mu}M^{45}/R^2\), because the form has \(\eta^{45}=\eta^{a5}=\eta^{4\mu}=0\), hence \(K^{\mu\nu}=(M^{45}/R^2)^2\eta^{\mu\nu}\) pointwise, with \(M^{45}\) a function of \(y^\mu\) on the orbit (checked on an explicit realisation). Here \(\eta^{\mu\nu}\) is the Cartesian form of the functions \(y^\mu\), not the pull-back of the matrix contraction \(\eta_{IJ}\), and the matrix-induced tensor on the six-dimensional leaf has rank four.
