This \(K\) is conformally flat, so the exact class carries one scalar function and no metric waves; whether fluctuations of \(T^a\) that are not fibre-constant supply a sufficient number of gauge-invariant functions is not computed here, and no argument is given that the \(y^\mu=M^{\mu5}/R\) are the base functions of the reduced theory.

\emph{Heat kernel over a fuzzy factor.} For the product leaf with a finite fuzzy factor, the scalar operator of the Poisson-limit paragraph is \(\mathcal O_s\otimes1+1\otimes\Box_{\mathcal C}\), so each four-dimensional sector is replicated over the \(\dim\mathrm{Mat}(\mathcal H_{\mathcal C})\) modes, the mode of mass \(m\) with the threshold factor \(F(m^2/\Lambda_{\mathrm{UV}}^2)=\int_1^\infty u^{-2}e^{-m^2u/\Lambda_{\mathrm{UV}}^2}\mathrm du\in(0,1]\) multiplying the coefficient of Theorem~\ref{thm:Geff}. In the truncated expansion the curvature coefficient is thus of order \(\Lambda_{\mathrm{UV}}^2\) times the weighted number of light modes; it is not set by the Kaluza--Klein scale. The supersymmetric one-loop mechanism of~\cite{Steinacker2023}, in which the corresponding terms cancel and the Newton constant is set by a finite mixed term, rests on field content that is not part of the present sector list.

This is a statement for the Poisson limit, the stated fibre classes and off-shell backgrounds; it does not identify \(G\) or \(\eta_{\mu\nu}\) with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}, gives no field equation, gap of the transverse scalars, \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\) value or sign, and does not compute the rank of the linearised Einstein operator in the conjugate-coordinate class.

\paragraph{Connection-sector term \(X\) as a torsion invariant (conditional; not closure).}
Take the setting of the Poisson-limit paragraph on Presentation~A above: four matrix coordinates, a real nondegenerate \(\Theta\) with Jacobi identity, \(\eta=\mathrm{diag}(-1,1,1,1)\) or \(\mathrm{diag}(1,1,1,1)\), \(K^{\mu\nu}=\eta_{\alpha\beta}\Theta^{\alpha\mu}\Theta^{\beta\nu}\), \(G=K^{-1}\), \(\rho=1/|\operatorname{Pf}\Theta|\), \(g_\rho^{\mu\nu}=\rho K^{\mu\nu}\). Let \(\omega=\Theta^{-1}\) (closed), \(e^A{}_\mu=\eta^{AB}\omega_{\mu B}\) so that \(G_{\mu\nu}=\eta_{AB}e^Ae^B\), \(T^{A}{}_{\mu\nu}=\partial_\mu e^A{}_\nu-\partial_\nu e^A{}_\mu\), \(T_{\lambda\mu\nu}=\eta_{AB}e^A{}_\lambda T^B{}_{\mu\nu}\) and \(S_{\lambda\mu\nu}=T_{[\lambda\mu\nu]}\). The matrix indices \(\alpha,\beta,A,B\) are lowered and raised with \(\eta\); coordinate indices, and \(S_{\lambda\mu\nu}S^{\lambda\mu\nu}\), with \(G\). The term \(X\) of the connection sector is \(X=\eta^{\rho\lambda}\eta_{\mu\alpha}\eta_{\nu\beta}\partial_\rho\Theta^{\mu\nu}\partial_\lambda\Theta^{\alpha\beta}\). Since \(\Theta^{T}\eta\,\Theta=G^{-1}\) and \(\partial\Theta=-\Theta(\partial\omega)\Theta\), \(X=G_{\mu\nu}T^{\alpha\beta\mu}T_{\alpha\beta}{}^{\nu}\) with \(T^{\alpha\beta\mu}=\Theta^{\mu\rho}\partial_\rho\Theta^{\alpha\beta}\), and \(X=T_{\lambda\mu\nu}T^{\lambda\mu\nu}\) because \(d\omega=0\) gives \(T^A{}_{\mu\nu}=-\eta^{AB}\partial_B\omega_{\mu\nu}\). Moreover \(\partial_\mu\ln\rho=G^{\lambda\nu}T_{\lambda\mu\nu}\), which follows from \(d\omega=0\) through \(\partial_\sigma\ln\operatorname{Pf}\omega=-\Theta^{\nu\mu}\partial_\mu\omega_{\nu\sigma}\). With the identity for the Weitzenb\"ock connection of the coframe \(e\) (in the present index convention, checked by direct computation) \(R[G]=-\bigl(\tfrac14T_{\lambda\mu\nu}T^{\lambda\mu\nu}+\tfrac12T_{\lambda\mu\nu}T^{\mu\lambda\nu}-V_\mu V^\mu\bigr)-2\nabla_\mu V^\mu\), \(V_\mu=G^{\lambda\nu}T_{\lambda\mu\nu}\), the relation \(S_{\lambda\mu\nu}S^{\lambda\mu\nu}=\tfrac13\bigl(T_{\lambda\mu\nu}T^{\lambda\mu\nu}-2T_{\lambda\mu\nu}T^{\mu\lambda\nu}\bigr)\), and the conformal transformation \(G=\rho\,g_\rho\) with \(\det g_\rho=1\), one finds the exact pointwise identity
\[
  \rho^2X=-2\rho R[g_\rho]-\rho\,g_\rho^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\ln\rho\,\partial_\nu\ln\rho+\tfrac32\rho^2S_{\lambda\mu\nu}S^{\lambda\mu\nu}+2\partial_\mu\bigl(g_\rho^{\mu\nu}\partial_\nu\rho\bigr).
\]
A rewriting using the coframe \(e=\eta^{-1}\omega\) is therefore the squared axial torsion. The identity was also verified in exact rational arithmetic on finite-amplitude generic two-jets of a closed \(\omega\) for both signatures. At quadratic order about constant \(\bar\Theta\), for the perturbations of the Poisson class, the invariants \(\rho R[g_\rho]\), \(\rho\,g_\rho^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\ln\rho\,\partial_\nu\ln\rho\) and \(\rho^2S_{\lambda\mu\nu}S^{\lambda\mu\nu}\) are linearly dependent modulo total derivatives, so this decomposition does not alter the net coefficient \(1-N_{\mathrm{tot}}/24\) stated above.

This is a statement for the Poisson-limit operators of the preceding paragraphs. It does not identify \(G\) with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}, does not treat Moyal corrections, fermions or other fields, and gives no value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). This changes no ledger label; Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class: first-order response and linearised Einstein rank (conditional; not closure).}
This paragraph supplements the preceding one, which left the rank of the linearised Einstein operator in this class uncomputed. Keep the conjugate-coordinate class of the preceding paragraphs in the Poisson limit: the leaf \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\) with the Lie--Poisson bracket \(\{M^{AB},M^{CD}\}=s(\eta^{AC}M^{BD}-\eta^{AD}M^{BC}-\eta^{BC}M^{AD}+\eta^{BD}M^{AC})\), \(s=\pm1\) a convention, the matrices \(X^a=T^a+A^a\) with \(A^a\) arbitrary functions on the leaf, \(\eta=\mathrm{diag}(-1,1,1,1)\), and the base functions \(y^\mu\) held fixed. Put \(m=M^{45}/R^2\) and \(y^4=M^{45}/R\). Then \(\{T^a,y^\mu\}=s\,\eta^{a\mu}m\), \(\{y^\mu,y^\rho\}=-s\,M^{\mu\rho}/R^2\), and with \(A^\nu:=A^a\delta_a{}^{\nu}\) the first-order change of \(K^{\mu\nu}=\eta_{ab}\{X^a,y^\mu\}\{X^b,y^\nu\}\) is \(\delta K^{\mu\nu}=s\,m\,(\{A^\nu,y^\mu\}+\{A^\mu,y^\nu\})\). Additional matrices \(X^i\) with \(\{X^i,y^\mu\}=0\) in the background enter \(K\) only through \(\eta_{ij}\{X^i,y^\mu\}\{X^j,y^\nu\}\), which is quadratic in their fluctuations.

\emph{Fibre structure.} On an explicit realisation one finds \(M^{ab}y_b=0\) for \(a,b=0,\dots,4\) (indices lowered with \(\mathrm{diag}(-,+,+,+,+)\)), hence \(T^\mu=-M^{\mu\sigma}y_\sigma/y^4\) and \(y_\mu T^\mu=0\). Over a point of the base with \(y^4\neq0\) the fibre of \(y^a\) is a two-sphere on which the six functions \(M^{\mu\rho}\) have zero average, are first spherical harmonics and span a three-dimensional space \(W_y\), the projection to the indices \(0,\dots,3\) of the self-dual two-forms of the Euclidean space \(y^\perp\subset\mathbb R^{1,4}\); the four functions \(T^\mu\) span the same space of functions. Consequently the first-harmonic part of \(A^\nu\) consists of twelve amplitudes \(b^{\nu;\mu\rho}(y)\in W_y\) and coincides with the frame-type fluctuations \(A^\nu=\kappa^\nu{}_\sigma(y)T^\sigma\), defined modulo \(\kappa^\nu{}_\sigma\to\kappa^\nu{}_\sigma+k^\nu y_\sigma\).

\emph{Response.} Let \(\mathrm d\mu\) be the fibre measure induced by the Liouville measure on a sheet \(y^4>0\) (the other sheet is not included) and \(B^{\nu;\mu\rho}=\int\mathrm d\mu\,A^\nu M^{\mu\rho}\). Liouville invariance of the bracket gives \(\int\mathrm d\mu\,\delta K^{\mu\nu}=(m/R^2)\,\partial_\rho\bigl(B^{\nu;\mu\rho}+B^{\mu;\nu\rho}\bigr)\), so the fibre-integrated variation \(\delta\mathcal K^{\mu\nu}\) satisfies \(\partial_\mu\partial_\nu(\delta\mathcal K^{\mu\nu}/m)=0\) for every \(A\). Only the first-harmonic part of \(A\) contributes: fibre-constant fluctuations, the ordinary gauge fields, and harmonics of order two and higher give no linear response. Take a plane wave \(e^{iq\cdot y}\), frozen coefficients at a base point, \(|q|\ell\gg1\) with \(\ell^2=-y_ay^a\), and \(q^2\neq0\). To leading order \(\delta G_{\mu\nu}\propto\eta_{\mu\alpha}\eta_{\nu\beta}h^{\alpha\beta}\) with \(h^{\mu\nu}=q_\rho(b^{\nu;\mu\rho}+b^{\mu;\nu\rho})\). Antisymmetry in \((\mu,\rho)\) gives \(q_\mu q_\nu h^{\mu\nu}=0\), so the image has dimension at most nine. The rank is a consequence of the image being the whole hyperplane \(q_\mu q_\nu h^{\mu\nu}=0\) and does not depend on the particular three-dimensional space \(W_y\); this hyperplane is not invariant under longitudinal diffeomorphisms, so the gauge group of the class is that of divergence-free diffeomorphisms. In exact rational arithmetic, at base points \((y^0,y^4)=(5,3),(13,5),(5,4),(25,7)\) in units \(R=1\) and for generic rational \(q\), the image has dimension nine, the linearised Einstein operator of \(\eta\) has rank six on it, its image is the whole six-dimensional space of conserved tensors \(q^\mu T_{\mu\nu}=0\), and its kernel on the image is three-dimensional. Six is the largest possible rank, so it is the generic rank by lower semicontinuity. For null \(q\) the rank is three, and at \(y^4=0\) the image has dimension seven and the rank is five. For \(A^\nu=\{\Lambda,T^\nu\}\) the leading image is \(h=q\circ(\Lambda q)\) with \(\Lambda^{\mu\rho}\in W_y\), the three-dimensional space of transverse linearised diffeomorphisms \(\xi\cdot q=0\), which is the kernel found above; the longitudinal diffeomorphism is neither a matrix gauge transformation nor in the image. The twelve amplitudes thus split into three that leave \(G\) unchanged, three gauge directions and six gauge-invariant functions, against six conserved sources and against \(10-4+1=7\) for a metric and a density in a diffeomorphism-invariant theory; the density is fixed by the Liouville measure and the image obeys the one relation above. A change \(y^\mu\to y^\mu+\delta y^\mu\) of the base functions acts at leading order as a linearised diffeomorphism with parameter \(\int\mathrm d\mu\,\delta y^\mu\) on the fibre-integrated tensor and the fibre volume, and so adds no gauge-invariant function. The algebraic parts of the response and of the gauge variation are of relative order \(1/(|q|\ell)\) and are not computed, so the gauge invariance of \(\mathcal E[h]=T\) holds at the leading symbol only.

This is a kinematic statement for the Poisson limit, the stated class, linear order, frozen coefficients and the fibre-integrated tensor; fibre-non-constant parts of \(\delta K\) are not treated. It does not identify \(G\) with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}, derives no field equation, and does not show that the first-harmonic amplitudes are light, positive-norm or decoupled. A Yang--Mills-type tree-level quadratic action, analogous to that of Remark~\ref{rem:ch3-trace-quadratic} and built from first derivatives of \(A\), gives equations of second order in these amplitudes (no such action is computed in this class), whereas \(\mathcal E[h]\) is of third order; an equation \(\mathcal E[h]=T\) would therefore require an induced term or another structure, which is not decided here. Fibre harmonics of the fluctuation matrices form the higher-spin tower described in~\cite{SperlingSteinacker2019,MantaSteinacker2025}; the relation of the amplitudes \(b^{\nu;\mu\rho}\) to that tower is not examined here.

\paragraph{Why \(y^\mu=M^{\mu5}/R\) in this class (partial; conditional; not closure).}
The only \(\mathrm{SO}(3,1)\) vector generators of \(\mathfrak{so}(4,2)\) are \(T^\mu=M^{\mu4}/R\) and \(y^\mu=M^{\mu5}/R\), so take \(u^\mu=\alpha T^\mu+\beta y^\mu\). Let \(\Box=\eta_{ab}\{T^a,\{T^b,\cdot\}\}\) be the Poisson-limit scalar operator. A reduction to an operator on functions of four variables without a fibre average requires that \(\Box\) maps functions of \(u\) to functions of \(u\), that is, that \(K_u^{\mu\nu}=\eta_{ab}\{T^a,u^\mu\}\{T^b,u^\nu\}\) and \(\Box u^\mu\) are functions of \(u\). The Lie--Poisson relations give \(\Box y^\mu=-y^\mu/R^2\), \(\Box T^\mu=-3T^\mu/R^2\) and \(K_u^{\mu\nu}=\alpha^2\eta_{ab}M^{a\mu}M^{b\nu}/R^4+\beta^2m^2\eta^{\mu\nu}\), the cross term cancelling by antisymmetry of \(M^{\mu\nu}\). For \(\alpha=0\) both conditions hold. For \(\alpha\neq0\) the term \(M^{a\mu}M_a{}^{\nu}\) depends on the position on the fibre, and for \(\beta\neq0\) also \(\Box u^\mu=-(3\alpha T^\mu+\beta y^\mu)/R^2\) is not a function of \(u\) (a gradient-rank test at random points of the leaf, using these expressions, confirms both failures; for \(\alpha=\beta\) the \(u^\mu\) are not independent and the test does not apply). Within this family the base functions are therefore \(y^\mu\) up to normalisation. This presupposes the orbit \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\), the matrices \(T^a=M^{a4}/R\) and the Lorentz-vector family; it is a property of the operator \(\Box\), not a consequence of the matrix equations of motion, and it does not show that the physical reduction is the one by \(y^\mu\).

This is a statement for the Poisson limit, the class above, the Lorentz-vector family and generators only; it gives no field equation, no value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\), and does not derive the background, the signature or a coframe from \(\Phi\); in particular it does not bear on Assumption~\ref{ass:EE}. This changes no ledger label; Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class: tree-level quadratic form of the level-one amplitudes (conditional; not closure).}
This paragraph supplements the preceding ones, in which the tree-level action of the amplitudes \(b^{\nu;\mu\rho}\) was not computed. Keep the Poisson limit, the leaf \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\), \(X^a=T^a+A^a\), the base functions \(y^\mu\) and the notation of the preceding paragraphs, in units \(R=1\), and write \(D^a=\{T^a,\cdot\}\), \(\mathcal J_{ab}=\{M_{ab},\cdot\}\), \(\Box=D_bD^b\). The quartic term of~\eqref{eq:Smatrix} with \(C_{\mu\nu\lambda}=0\), read with \([\cdot,\cdot]\to i\{\cdot,\cdot\}\) and \(\operatorname{Tr}\to\int\mathrm{vol}\), is \(S=(4g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2)^{-1}\int\mathrm{vol}\,\{X^a,X^b\}\{X_a,X_b\}\), with field equation \(\{X^b,\{X_b,X_a\}\}=0\).

\emph{Background.} The Lie--Poisson relations give \(\{T_a,\{T^a,T^b\}\}=-3T^b\), an identity of the polynomial Poisson algebra of \(\mathfrak{so}(4,2)\) that does not depend on the sign \(s\). Hence \(T^a\) is not stationary for the quartic term alone. It is stationary for \(S+(2g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2)^{-1}\mu^2\int\mathrm{vol}\,X_aX^a\), whose field equation is \(\{X^b,\{X_b,X_a\}\}=\mu^2X_a\), exactly when \(\mu^2=-3\). A constant \(C_{\mu\nu\lambda}\) cannot take the place of this term: it would add \(C_{abc}M^{bc}\) to the field equation, and these functions are linearly independent of \(T_a=M_{a4}\) on the orbit. The bilinear term is not part of~\eqref{eq:Smatrix}; it is an input of this class with a coefficient fixed by stationarity, and a mass term is reported for the covariant backgrounds of~\cite{SperlingSteinacker2019}.

\emph{Quadratic form.} With this term, \(S^{(2)}=(2g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2)^{-1}\int\mathrm{vol}\,A^aL(A)_a\) with \(L(A)_a=-\Box A_a+D_a(D_bA^b)-2s\,\mathcal J_{ab}A^b+\mu^2A_a\), an identity verified in the polynomial Poisson algebra for both signs \(s\). Gauge covariance of the field equation about a stationary point gives \(L(\{\Lambda,T\})=0\) for every function \(\Lambda\), and the same check fails for \(\mu^2\neq-3\). The quadratic form is therefore exactly invariant under \(\delta A^a=\{\Lambda,T^a\}\), not only at a leading symbol.

\emph{Leading symbol.} Since \(D^ay^\mu=s\,m\,\eta^{a\mu}\) with \(m=M^{45}\) a function of \(y\) alone, the only part of \(L\) with two derivatives along \(y\) is \(-\Box A_a+D_a(D_bA^b)\). The operator \(\mathcal J_{ab}\) acts on \(y^\mu\) as a Lorentz rotation with a coefficient linear in \(y\), and the fibre derivatives of \(A\) and the derivatives of \(m\) enter with coefficients of relative order \(1/(|q|\ell)\). For \(A^a=c^a(y)F\) with \(F\) a fixed fibre function and \(|q|\ell\gg1\), then, \(L(A)_a=-m^2(\partial^2c_a-\partial_a\partial_bc^b)F\) plus terms with at most one derivative of \(c\), so that
\[
S^{(2)}\simeq\frac{1}{4g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2}\int\mathrm{vol}\;m^2\,\mathcal F_{ab}\mathcal F^{ab},\qquad
\mathcal F_{ab}=\partial_aA_b-\partial_bA_a ,
\]
with \(\partial_a\) the derivative along \(y\) at fixed fibre argument. At this order each fibre function is an independent Maxwell field with the same coefficient, and there is no coupling between fibre harmonics. On the explicit realisation of the preceding paragraphs, evaluating \(L\) on \(A^a=c^a\mathrm e^{i\lambda q\cdot y}M^{12}\) reproduces \(\lambda^2m^2M^{12}\mathrm e^{i\lambda q\cdot y}(q^2c_a-q_aq\cdot c)\) with a relative remainder that falls as \(1/\lambda\).

\emph{Level-one sector.} Write \(M^{\mu\rho}=\sum_ie^iS_i^{\mu\rho}\) with \(S_i\) an orthogonal, equal-norm basis of \(W_y\) and \(e^i\) the three first spherical harmonics on the fibre; by the \(\mathrm{SU}(2)\) invariance of the fibre measure, \(\int\mathrm d\mu\,e^ie^j=\varkappa(y)\,\delta^{ij}\) with \(\varkappa>0\). For \(A^\nu=c^\nu_i(y)e^i\) one has \(b^{\nu;\mu\rho}=\varkappa\,c^\nu_iS_i^{\mu\rho}\), and the twelve amplitudes are three vector fields \(c_i\) with the action \((m^2\varkappa/4g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2)\sum_i\int\mathrm d^4y\,\mathcal F_{i,ab}\mathcal F_i^{ab}\). The kinetic matrix is \(\delta_{ij}(q^2\eta_{ab}-q_aq_b)\), definite in the index \(i\); the leading symbol has no mass term and no coupling to the other harmonics. The overall sign is that of the quartic term as displayed, common to all fibre harmonics including the fibre-constant gauge field, so no relative ghost arises at this order. It is opposite to the sign of the Lorentzian Maxwell action \(-\tfrac14F_{ab}F^{ab}\) in the same \(\eta\), and whether it is the healthy sign depends on the convention for the weight, which~\eqref{eq:Smatrix} does not fix. The Maxwell transformation \(\delta c_i^\nu=\partial^\nu\lambda_i\) is the leading form of \(\delta A^\nu=\{\Lambda,T^\nu\}\) with \(\Lambda=\lambda_ie^i\), under which \(h\) changes by a divergence-free linearised diffeomorphism. Masses, the connection induced by the \(y\)-dependence of \(S_i\) and \(m\), and the mixing of harmonics enter at relative orders \(1/(|q|\ell)\) and \(1/(|q|\ell)^2\), which frozen coefficients do not determine; they are not computed.

This is a statement for the Poisson limit, the stated class with the bilinear term, and the leading symbol. It does not determine masses, the signs of subleading terms or decoupling beyond leading order, and it does not identify the fibre-averaged tensor with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class: what the tree-level equations of the level-one amplitudes imply for \(\mathcal E[h]\) (conditional; bounded no-go).}
Keep the setting of the preceding paragraph. Let \(\mathcal E\) be the linearised Einstein operator of \(\eta\), \(h^{\mu\nu}=q_\rho(b^{\nu;\mu\rho}+b^{\mu;\nu\rho})\) and \(s_i^\mu=S_i^{\mu\rho}q_\rho\), so that \(h^{\mu\nu}=\varkappa\sum_i(s_i^\mu c_i^\nu+s_i^\nu c_i^\mu)\). The order count of the preceding paragraphs becomes precise at the leading symbol. In exact rational arithmetic at the base points \((y^0,y^4)=(5,3),(13,5),(5,4)\) in units \(R=1\) the following hold.

\emph{Free equations.} The equations \(q_a\mathcal F_i^{ab}=0\) make \(c_i\) pure gauge for \(q^2\neq0\), so \(h\) is then a divergence-free linearised diffeomorphism and \(\mathcal E[h]=0\). For null \(q\) in Lorenz gauge there are six physical polarisations (three Maxwell fields): three give \(h=0\), and the other three give \(h\) modulo diffeomorphisms spanned by the two transverse-traceless polarisations and one further polarisation with nonzero trace (base points \((5,3)\) and \((13,5)\)). For these solutions \(\mathcal E[h]_{\mu\nu}=\tfrac12q_\mu q_\nu h^\alpha{}_\alpha\), which vanishes on the transverse-traceless polarisations and not on the polarisation with nonzero trace; the longitudinal diffeomorphism is not a symmetry of the class, so this trace is not removed by gauge.

\emph{No local relation.} The map \(c\mapsto\mathcal E[h(c)]\) is cubic in \(q\), and the Maxwell operator \(c\mapsto q^2c^b-q^b\,q\cdot c\) is quadratic. No matrix \(P(q)\) linear in \(q\) satisfies \(\mathcal E[h(c)]=P(q)\cdot(\text{Maxwell operator on }c)\), and none of the ten components of \(\mathcal E[h(c)]\) is divisible by \(q^2\). Hence no local equation \(\mathcal E[h]=P(\partial)J\) follows from the second-order equations \(\partial_a\mathcal F_i^{ab}=J_i^b\) for a source \(J_i\).

\emph{Response to a source.} Take as input a conserved source, \(q_\mu T^{\mu\nu}=0\), coupled to the amplitudes through \(h\), that is \(J_{i\nu}\propto s_i^\mu T_{\mu\nu}\) (gauge invariance of the coupling requires the conservation). Then \(c_i\) is determined up to gauge, \(c_i^\nu\propto J_i^\nu/q^2\), and
\[
h^{\mu\nu}=\frac{N}{q^2}\bigl(Q^{\mu\alpha}T_\alpha{}^\nu+Q^{\nu\alpha}T_\alpha{}^\mu\bigr),\qquad
Q^{\mu\alpha}=\sum_is_i^\mu s_i^\alpha=q_\perp^2P_\perp^{\mu\alpha}-q_\perp^\mu q_\perp^\alpha ,
\]
with \(N\) a nonzero constant fixed by the couplings, \(P_\perp\) the projector on \(y^\perp\) and \(q_\perp=P_\perp q\), both restricted to the indices \(0,\dots,3\). Modulo \(q\circ\xi\) this is \(h^{\mu\nu}\simeq N\bigl[2(1+q_\parallel^2/q^2)T^{\mu\nu}+\hat y^\mu\tau^\nu+\tau^\mu\hat y^\nu\bigr]\) with \(\hat y=y/\ell\), \(q_\parallel=q_\mu\hat y^\mu\) and \(\tau^\nu=\hat y_\alpha T^{\alpha\nu}\). The response is homogeneous of degree zero in \(q\): \(h\) is algebraic in \(T\), local for \(q_\parallel=0\), in particular for a static source at a base point with \(y^i=0\), and otherwise carries the factor \(q_\parallel^2/q^2\). Since \(\mathcal E\) is of degree two, \(\mathcal E[h]\) is homogeneous of degree two in \(q\) and is nonzero whenever \(h\) is not a linearised diffeomorphism; it therefore cannot equal a constant multiple of \(T\) (the sampled cases also exclude \(q^2T\)). A relation of second order in \(c\) is zeroth order in \(h\) once the common derivative is removed, whereas \(\mathcal E[h]=T\) is second order in \(h\).

\emph{Induced term.} Suppose a term \((16\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}})^{-1}\int\sqrt{-G}\,R\), reported to arise at one loop in~\cite{Steinacker2023} and not computed here, is added as an input. Its quadratic form in \(h\) has degree two in \(q\) and the tree-level form has degree zero, so the tree-level form dominates at small \(q\), and an Einstein-type response \(h\sim G_{\mathrm{eff}}T/q^2\) can appear only above the scale set by the ratio of the two coefficients, provided that scale lies within \(|q|\ell\gg1\); otherwise the induced form is subdominant throughout the regime of the class. Removing the tree-level term would require a loop contribution to the coefficient of \(\sum_i\mathcal F_i^2\) that cancels it; this is not examined.

This is a statement for the Poisson limit, the stated class, the leading symbol, tree level, the level-one sector coupled to a conserved source through \(h\), and the sheet \(y^4>0\). It does not exclude an Einstein-type equation from the induced term, from subleading orders in \(1/(|q|\ell)\), from other sectors or from the entanglement route of Assumption~\ref{ass:EE}, and it derives no value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). This changes no ledger label; Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class: matrix Noether identity and the divergence of the stress (conditional; not closure).}
Work in the Poisson limit with \(F^{ab}=\{X^a,X^b\}\) and \(E_c=-\{X^b,\{X_b,X_c\}\}+\mu^2X_c\), the field equation of \((4g_{\mathrm{YM}}^2)^{-1}\int\mathrm{vol}\,(F_{ab}F^{ab}+2\mu^2X_aX^a)\), for arbitrary functions \(X^a\) on the leaf. The Jacobi identity and the Leibniz rule give \(\{X^a,E_a\}=0\) identically. The tensor \(\mathbb T^{ab}=F^{ac}F^{b}{}_{c}+\mu^2X^aX^b\), which is the variation of the action with respect to the contraction form, satisfies \(\{X_a,\mathbb T^{ab}\}=-F^{b}{}_{c}E^c+\tfrac14\{X^b,F_{cd}F^{cd}\}\); its divergence therefore vanishes on the field equation up to a gradient, and \(\mathbb T^{ab}-\tfrac14\eta^{ab}F_{cd}F^{cd}\) is divergence-free on the field equation exactly, the subtracted term being \(\tfrac14\eta^{ab}F_{cd}F^{cd}\) and not a trace of \(\mathbb T^{ab}\). About \(X^a=T^a\) the linear form obeys \(\{T^a,L(A)_a\}=-(3+\mu^2)\{A^a,T_a\}\), which vanishes exactly for \(\mu^2=-3\). For the massless model the matrix-level identity \([X_a,\mathbb T^{ab}]\propto\) equation of motion is standard; the Poisson-limit form \(\{X^a,E_a\}=0\) and the divergence identity with the \(\mu^2\) term are recorded here as exact identities in the polynomial Poisson algebra, and were verified there for both signs of the bracket. The tensor \(\mathbb T^{ab}\) carries matrix indices and \(\{X_a,\cdot\}\) is a frame derivative, not \(\nabla_\mu\mathbb T^{\mu\nu}\); it differs from the coordinate divergence of the fibre-averaged tensor by terms containing derivatives of the frame coefficients, of relative order \(1/(|q|\ell)\), which are not computed. The paragraph does not identify \(\mathbb T^{ab}\) with the source of an Einstein-type equation.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class: extra stress and crossover response at the leading symbol (conditional; not closure).}
Keep the setting and notation of the preceding paragraphs at the base points \(y^i=0\), with \(s_i^\mu=S_i^{\mu\rho}q_\rho\), \(Q^{\mu\nu}=\sum_is_i^\mu s_i^\nu\) and \(G_{ij}=s_i\!\cdot\! s_j\) (four-dimensional contraction). Add as inputs a term \(\tfrac12w\int h^{\mu\nu}\mathcal E_{\mu\nu}[h]\), the quadratic form of an induced Einstein--Hilbert term with \(w=-\zeta^2/(16\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}})\) when \(\delta g=\zeta h\), and a conserved source \(T_{\mu\nu}\) coupled as \(\int h^{\mu\nu}T_{\mu\nu}\); the normalisation of the tree-level term and of the coupling is absorbed in \(w\) and in \(\Lambda\), the coefficient of \(\tfrac12c_i\cdot Mc_i\), \(Mc^\nu=q^2c^\nu-q^\nu q\!\cdot\! c\). Since \(s_i\!\cdot\! q=0\), \(\{s_1,s_2,s_3,q\}\) is a basis for \(q^2\neq0\); \(c_i=D_{ij}s_j+\gamma_iq\) with \(\gamma_i\) the gauge part, \(h=H(c)\) with \(H\) the linear map \(c\mapsto h\), which depends only on the symmetric part \(D^{S}\) of \(D\), the three antisymmetric components of \(D\) are the inert amplitudes, \(c^TMc=q^2\mathrm{tr}(DGD^T)\), and \(G=q_\perp^2\mathbf 1-(y^0/\ell)^2\mathbf k\mathbf k^T\) with \(q_\perp^2=q^2+(q_\mu y^\mu)^2/\ell^2\) and \(\mathbf k=\mathbf q/|\mathbf q|\); its eigenvalues are \(g=q_\perp^2\) (twice) and \(\xi=-\sigma q^2\), \(\sigma=(y^4)^2/\ell^2\), along the spatial \(\mathbf q\). The restriction to \(y^i=0\) only provides these closed forms.

\emph{Extra stress.} The amplitude equations are equivalent to \(H^{T}X=-\Lambda Mc\) with \(X=w\mathcal E[h]-T\). In exact rational arithmetic at \((y^0,y^4)=(5,3),(13,5)\) and several rational \(q\) one finds that \(X\) is symmetric (equivalent to the three inert-amplitude equations), that \(q^\mu X_{\mu\nu}=0\), and that \(X_{\mu\nu}=-\tfrac12\Lambda\sum_i\tilde s^i_\mu(Mc_i)_\nu\) with \(\tilde s^i=\sum_kG^{-1}_{ik}s_k\). The amplitude equations determine \(X\) up to \(q_\mu q_\nu\), which \(H^T\) annihilates; \(q^\mu X_{\mu\nu}=0\) follows from the Bianchi identity of \(\mathcal E\) and the conservation of \(T\), which fixes that term, as the ambiguity of the anharmonicity tensor is fixed in~\cite{KumarSteinacker2023,Steinacker2026}. The closed form \(X=-\tfrac12\Lambda\sum_i\tilde s^i\otimes Mc_i=-\Lambda\mathcal L\mathcal E\) expresses the extra stress through the tree-level equations; it is not an independent derivation of its conservation, and the Einstein part \(w\mathcal E\) is the supplied input.

\emph{Crossover.} The operator \(\mathcal L\) is defined on the six-dimensional span of the symmetrised products \(s_is_j\) and is invertible for \(q^2\neq0\) and \(\xi\neq0\). With \(\mathbb L[Z]=(\eta Q)Z+Z(Q\eta)\) one has \(h_T=\mathbb L[Y]\), \(Y=-2X/(\Lambda q^2)\), and eliminating the amplitudes gives exactly \((w+\Lambda\,\mathcal L)\mathcal E=T\), \(\mathcal L=\tfrac12\mathbb L^{-1}\circ(2-P\,\mathrm{tr})\), where \(P\) projects on \(q^\perp\). The operator \(\mathcal L\) has eigenvalues \(1/(2g)\) (two modes), \(1/(g+\xi)\) (two), \((g\pm\sqrt{g(g+8\xi)})/(8g\xi)\) (one each) and zero on the three inert modes. In the first channel \((1+\varrho/(2q_\perp^2))\mathcal E=T/w\) with \(\varrho=\Lambda/w\) the ratio of the tree-level to the induced coefficient, the form \((1-m^2\Box^{-1})\) with \(\Box\to-q_\perp^2\) and \(m^2=\varrho/2\); \(q_\perp^2=k^2+\sigma\omega^2\) is positive definite in \((\omega,\mathbf k)\), not the form \(q^2\). The result is the supplied Einstein part \(w\mathcal E\) plus a non-local, frame-dependent tree-level correction.

\emph{Roots.} \(\det(\Lambda A+wB)\propto\Lambda^3(q^2)^9(\Lambda+2gw)^2(\Lambda+(g+\xi)w)^2(-\Lambda^2+2\Lambda gw+8gw^2\xi)\), which reproduces the generalised eigenvalues of the tree-plus-induced form at the sampled points. Besides \(q^2=0\) there are roots that depend on the direction of \(q\) through the frame: in the first channel \(\omega^2=-(k^2+\varrho/2)/\sigma\), complex for every \(k\) if \(\varrho>0\) and for \(k^2>-\varrho/2\) if \(\varrho<0\); the helicity-zero roots are complex when \(g+8\xi<0\). The relation is elliptic in \(\omega\) rather than wave-like: for \(\varrho>0\) the first channel has no real-frequency root, and for \(\varrho<0\) real roots exist only for \(k^2<|\varrho|/2\), with \(\omega^2<0\) and growth rate proportional to \(k\) above that. The anisotropy is that of the background direction \(\hat y=y/\ell\), through \(q_\perp^2=q^2+(q\!\cdot\!\hat y)^2\), and \(G\) is indefinite for spacelike \(q\); the anisotropy is \(SO(3,1)\)-covariant if \(y^\mu\) is treated as a vector, and is not an explicit breaking of covariance. The sign of \(\varrho\) is that of \(\Lambda/w\); the sign of the induced coefficient is not determined here. These are roots of the leading-symbol dispersion relation, not shown to be poles of a retarded response (constraints and gauge fixing are not treated). The extra modes reported in~\cite{Steinacker2026} are, as extracted, rotation-isotropic with a reduced velocity from the cosmic frame; they belong to the same family of non-boost-invariant effects, and no match is claimed, since the map between the present \(q_\perp,\xi,\sigma\) and the notation there is not established.

This is a statement for the Poisson limit, the stated class with the bilinear term, the leading symbol, tree level plus the supplied induced form and conserved source, and the base points with \(y^i=0\). It derives no value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\) or of \(\varrho\), no masses or subleading terms, no matrix-derived matter coupling, no new derivation of the conservation of the extra stress, and no identification of \(G\) with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}; the comparison with the modified Einstein equation of~\cite{KumarSteinacker2023,Steinacker2026} is structural. This changes no ledger label; Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class: the \(\Theta\)-built heat kernel and the induced coefficient (conditional; bounded no-go).}
Keep the Poisson limit, the leaf \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\), \(X^a=T^a+A^a\), the bilinear term with \(\mu^2=-3\) and the notation of the preceding paragraphs; \(D^a=\{T^a,\cdot\}\), \(J^{AB}=\{M^{AB},\cdot\}\), \(\Box=\eta_{ab}D^aD^b\). The regulator is the proper-time cutoff \(s\ge\LUV^{-2}\) of the operator \(\Box_X\) itself, applied to every sector; it is built from Poisson brackets and the matrix contraction only. Background gauge fixing \(D_aA^a\) gives the ghost operator \(-\Box\) (weight \(-2\)), the vector operator \(H_V=-\Box-2sJ_{ab}\Sigma^{ab}\) (the same \(J_{ab}A^b\) as in the quadratic form above, with \(\mu^2\) added), and, for \(n_{\mathrm{ex}}\) further matrices with trivial background, \(-\Box+\mu_i^2\) with \(\mu_i^2\) an additional input. Fermions are absent. In exact arithmetic in the polynomial Poisson algebra, for both signs \(s\): \(\Box=C_{\mathfrak{so}(4,1)}-C_{\mathfrak{so}(3,1)}\) with the generators \(J^{AB}\), \(A,B\le4\), and \(J^{ab}\), \(a,b\le3\); \([\Box,J^{ab}]=0\) while \([\Box,D^a]\ne0\); and on functions of \(y^A=M^{A5}\), \(A\le4\), one has \(D^a=s\,(y^4\partial^a-y^a\partial_4)\), a rotation field of the hyperboloid \(y\cdot y=-\ell^2\). Fibre-constant functions therefore form an invariant subspace of \(\Box\), on which \(\Box=\rho^{-1}\partial_\mu(\rho\,m^2\eta^{\mu\nu}\partial_\nu\,\cdot)\) with \(m=y^4\), \(\rho=1/m\); for \(G^{\mu\nu}=m^2\eta^{\mu\nu}\) the Seeley--DeWitt density of this sector is \(\sqrt{|G|}\,(R_G/6-E)=(5m^2-3\ell^2)/(4m^6)\), which is not of definite sign (a formal evaluation for \(\eta=\mathrm{diag}(-1,1,1,1)\); no Euclidean heat-kernel statement is made for this sector). Fibre harmonics of order \(\ge1\) are not an invariant subspace of the perturbed operators, so this sector does not by itself give the response to the level-one amplitudes.

\emph{Hypoelliptic structure.} At every sampled point of the orbit (random points, and the sequence \(y^4\to0\)) the fields \(D^a\) have rank four and \(D^a\), \(J^{ab}\) together span the six-dimensional tangent space; the horizontal rank drops on \(y^4=0\) (one singular value proportional to \(y^4\)), which is excluded. Hence, for the Euclidean contraction \(\eta\to\delta\) (the operator \(\Box_E=\sum_a(D^a)^2\), which is not the Casimir difference above), the operator is a sub-Laplacian of step two with homogeneous dimension \(4+2\cdot2=8\), not an elliptic operator on a product; the ranks, the step and the dimension \(8\) depend only on the fields \(D^a\), \(J^{ab}\). The ghost and vector operators below are then built from \(\Box_E\). Under equiregularity the diagonal of its heat kernel is \(K(s)=c_0s^{-4}(1+O(s))\) with \(c_0\) given by the nilpotent approximation~\cite{BenArous1989}, here a group with four horizontal generators and a two-dimensional centre. After Fourier transform along the centre (variable \(\zeta\in\mathbb R^2\)) the nilpotent operator is a four-dimensional charged Laplacian in a constant field \(F_{ab}(\zeta)=\zeta_kS^{ab}{}_k\) of the two-plane strengths \(f_1,f_2\propto|\zeta|\), with Landau levels \(f_1(2n_1+1)+f_2(2n_2+1)\) and the diagonal \(\prod_j f_j/(4\pi\sinh sf_j)\) per unit horizontal volume and per \(\mathrm d^2\zeta/(2\pi)^2\). With \(\nu\) the Liouville volume of the frame \((D^0,\dots,D^3,Z_1,Z_2)\) of the nilpotent algebra, \(c_0=\nu^{-1}(64\pi^4)^{-1}\!\int_0^{2\pi}\!\mathrm d\varphi\,\phi_1\phi_2\!\int_0^\infty\!\mathrm du\,u^3/(\sinh u\phi_1\sinh u\phi_2)\), \(f_j=|\zeta|\phi_j\); for \(f_1=f_2\) this is \(3\zeta(3)/(64\pi^3\nu)\). With the Euclidean contraction \(\eta\to\delta\) of the four matrix indices (the Poisson algebra kept) the ratio \(f_1/f_2\) at \(12\) sampled points and \(9\) directions of \(\zeta\) each lies between \(0.006\) and \(0.84\), never at \(1\); no claim is made over the whole leaf. For the Lorentzian contraction \(F\eta\) has one real and one imaginary eigenvalue pair, the electric-type case, to which the Euclidean heat-kernel construction does not apply.

\emph{Consequences.} (i) The statement that a fuzzy factor replicates the four-dimensional coefficient of Theorem~\ref{thm:Geff} over its modes, with threshold weights, is the limit \(sf_j\ll1\) of the diagonal above, \(\prod_j sf_j/\sinh sf_j=1-s^2(f_1^2+f_2^2)/6+\dots\), valid when \(\LUV^2\) exceeds the largest populated \(f_j\). In the Poisson limit \(|\zeta|\) is unbounded and the regime is the hypoelliptic one; the replication formula does not apply there, and the weighted mode count is replaced by \(\nu^{-1}\int\mathrm d^2\zeta\,(2\pi)^{-2}\prod_j sf_j/\sinh sf_j\). (ii) In the same nilpotent limit \(H_V\) acquires the Zeeman term \(\pm2f_1,\pm2f_2\), so its lowest eigenvalue at fixed \(\zeta\) is \(-|f_1-f_2|\) (checked by diagonalisation in a truncated Fock space): zero modes of unbounded number for \(f_1=f_2\), and eigenvalues unbounded below for \(f_1\ne f_2\), neither regulated by the proper-time cutoff. The vector sector therefore needs a fibre truncation (a finite fuzzy fibre) or fermionic partners, and in the Euclidean continuation used here \(e^{-sH_V}\) is unbounded for every \(s>0\) at the leading symbol, so the proper-time trace of this sector diverges. (iii) For \(sf_j\ll1\), the vector, ghost and \(n_{\mathrm{ex}}\) scalar sectors together give \(2+n_{\mathrm{ex}}+\tfrac{22-n_{\mathrm{ex}}}{6}s^2(f_1^2+f_2^2)\); the \(s^2\) term has the structure of the quartic term \(\{X^a,X^b\}^2\) and renormalises its coefficient logarithmically, not the Einstein--Hilbert coefficient. (iv) An Einstein--Hilbert coefficient would be the weight-two term \(\propto s^{-3}\) of the diagonal and its second variation under \(A\); no such computation is made here, and neither the sign nor the size of \(w\), hence of \(\varrho=\Lambda/w\), follows, with \(w\) and \(\varrho\) the induced and tree-level coefficients of the preceding paragraph.

This is a statement for the Poisson limit, the proper-time cutoff of \(\Box_X\) as the regulator, one loop, the leading (nilpotent) symbol, equiregular points, the Euclidean contraction \(\eta\to\delta\) with the Lie--Poisson algebra kept, background gauge and the bosonic content listed. It does not treat Moyal terms, fermions, the subleading symbol, the dependence on \(A\), or the identification of \(G\) with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}, and it does not bear on Assumption~\ref{ass:EE}. This changes no ledger label; Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class: where the flux eigenvalues of the Euclidean symbol coincide (conditional; bounded no-go).}
Keep the setting of the preceding paragraph: Poisson limit, leaf \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\), \(D^a=\{T^a,\cdot\}\), the nilpotent (Landau) leading symbol with centre variable \(\zeta\in\mathbb R^2\), the contraction \(\eta\to\delta\) of the four matrix indices with the Lie--Poisson algebra \(\mathfrak{so}(4,2)\) kept, and the sheet realised by the orbit. Write \(y^a=M^{a5}\), \(y^4=M^{45}\), \(y\cdot y=-\ell^2\), and \(Q^{ab}=(y^4)^2\eta^{ab}+y^ay^b\), the Gram matrix of the fields \(D^a\) in the ambient metric of the hyperboloid; \(Q\) is positive definite with \(\det Q=\ell^2|y^4|^6\). At \(300\) random orbit points, with both centre directions, the matrix \(Q^{-1/2}F_{ab}Q^{-1/2}\), \(F_{ab}=\zeta_kS^{ab}{}_k\), is self-dual or anti-self-dual with respect to \(\delta\) to a relative accuracy of \(10^{-9}\), with the sign equal to \(\operatorname{sign}y^4\); this is the expected statement that the vertical curvature of the horizontal distribution over the constant-curvature base is self-dual for the ambient metric. Hence \(F=Q^{1/2}\omega Q^{1/2}\) with \(\omega\) self-dual, and the plane strengths of \(F\) are \(f_{1,2}=|p|\pm|r|\), with \(p\) and \(r\) the \(\delta\)-self-dual and anti-self-dual parts of \(F\). Let \(a\ge b\) be the eigenvalues of \(Q\) on the plane spanned by the time axis and \(\vec y\), so that \(ab=\ell^2(y^4)^2\) and the remaining eigenvalue is \((y^4)^2\) twice. For \(\zeta\) such that \(\omega\) points along the axis of that plane one finds \(f_2/f_1=\min(\ell,|y^4|)/\max(\ell,|y^4|)\); for \(\omega\) orthogonal to it, \(f_2/f_1=\sqrt{b/a}\); for other directions the ratio lies between these values. The numerical range of \(f_2/f_1\) at the sampled points lies inside this bracket to \(10^{-13}\).

\emph{Consequences.} (i) The vector sector has no negative mode at fixed \(\zeta\) only if \(f_1=f_2\), and a stable point must have this for every \(\zeta\), because the set of \(\zeta\) with \(f_1=f_2\) is a linear subspace of the \(\zeta\)-plane (a line, or the whole plane only if \(F\) is (anti-)self-dual). Conditional on the self-duality statement above, the closed forms give \(f_1=f_2\) for all \(\zeta\) exactly on \(\vec y=0\), \(|y^4|=\ell\), that is on the two-sphere fibres over the points \(y^0=-\sqrt2\,\ell\), \(y^4=\pm\ell\): codimension \(4\) in the six-dimensional leaf (the minimisation of the distance to this set found it, with \(f_2/f_1=1\) to six digits at six points). On the rest of the leaf the lowest vector eigenvalue is \(-|f_1-f_2|<0\) for generic \(\zeta\), and \(|f_1-f_2|/(f_1+f_2)=(\ell-|y^4|)/(\ell+|y^4|)\) on the time axis grows linearly with the distance from the set. (ii) The same holds for the compact real form \(\mathfrak{so}(6)\) with \(\delta\) throughout: there \(f_2/f_1=|y^4|/\ell\) for every \(\zeta\) and every fibre point (checked at \(200\) points, error \(4\cdot10^{-16}\)), equal to \(1\) only over the poles \(\vec y=0\). A compact Euclidean section with the contraction \(\delta_{ab}\) therefore does not replace the instability by zero modes; self-duality holds identically only for the contraction by the position-dependent ambient metric \(Q^{-1}\), which is not a constant contraction of the matrix indices. (iii) Constant additional fluxes \(f_3,\dots\) from transverse matrices change the lowest level to \(\sum_kf_k-2\max_kf_k\) (the Landau levels of the same form, \(2\pi(\sum_i(2m_i+1)f_i\pm2f_k)\), of \cite{Troost2000}, eq.~(35), and the no-tachyon condition of its Section~9, \(f_1\le f_2+\dots+f_n\)); because \(f_1-f_2\) grows linearly in \(|\zeta|\), a \(\zeta\)-independent transverse flux restores stability only for \(|\zeta|\) below a bound set by that flux, that is on a finite fuzzy fibre, not in the Poisson limit. (iv) At the self-dual points the vector density \(\prod_j f_j/(4\pi\sinh sf_j)\) times the Zeeman trace tends to \(f^2/(2\pi^2)\) for \(sf\gg1\), independent of \(s\), so the integral over \(\zeta\) diverges there as well; the proper-time cutoff does not regulate it.

This is a statement for the Poisson limit, the leading symbol, equiregular points, the contraction \(\eta\to\delta\) (or \(\delta\) with \(\mathfrak{so}(6)\)), constant additional fluxes and bosonic content. The self-duality of \(Q^{-1/2}FQ^{-1/2}\) is verified numerically at sampled points and is not proved, so the classification of the set \(f_1=f_2\) is conditional on it. It does not treat a contraction by a position-dependent metric, Moyal terms, fermions, a finite fibre, or other backgrounds for the transverse matrices, and it determines neither the sign nor the size of \(w\). This changes no ledger label; Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class: the contraction by the ambient metric and the five-matrix presentation (conditional; bounded no-go and conditional route).}
Keep the Poisson limit, the leaf \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\) of \(\mathfrak{so}(4,2)\) with \(\eta_6=\mathrm{diag}(-1,1,1,1,1,-1)\), the notation \(y^a=M^{a5}\), \(y^4=M^{45}\), \(Q^{ab}=(y^4)^2\eta^{ab}+y^ay^b\) of the preceding paragraph, and \(T^a=M^{a4}\), \(D^a=\{T^a,\cdot\}\). On the orbit \((\eta_6M)^2=-\ell^2\mathbf 1\) (checked at \(200\) random points to \(10^{-12}\)), whence \(y\cdot y\equiv\eta_{AB}y^Ay^B=-\ell^2\) (\(A,B=0,\dots,4\)) and \(T^ay_a=0\). The base metric induced from the ambient space is \(g=\eta+y_\mu y_\nu/(y^4)^2\) with inverse \(g^{-1}=\eta+yy/\ell^2\); it is positive definite, and the contraction of the fields \(D^a\) by \(Q^{-1}\) has base symbol exactly \(g^{-1}\) (checked numerically). The kinetic tensor of the four matrices \(T^a\) is \(K=(y^4)^2\eta=(y^4)^2\bigl(g^{-1}-yy/\ell^2\bigr)\). The contraction by \(Q^{-1}\) equals \((y^4)^{-2}\bigl[\eta_{ab}D^aD^b+(y_aD^a)^2/\ell^2\bigr]\) and therefore replaces the Lorentzian tensor \(K\) by the Euclidean metric \(g\) of the hyperboloid; it is not a Wick rotation of \(\eta\).

\emph{No supply in the four-matrix class.} Hypothesis class: the Poisson limit; the four matrices \(T^a=M^{a4}\); the quartic term plus one bilinear term, with Hessian \(L(A)_a=-\Box A_a+D_a(D_bA^b)-2s\mathcal J_{ab}A^b+\mu^2A_a\); the background gauge function \(D_aA^a\), the ghost operator \(-\Box\), and a regulator that is a function of \(\Box_X\); the matrix-index form constant, \(\eta_{ab}\). In this class every operator is a polynomial with constant coefficients in the \(D^a\) and \(J^{ab}=\{M^{ab},\cdot\}\), that is, an element of the enveloping algebra of \(\mathfrak{so}(4,1)'\), the Lie algebra generated by the \(D^a\) and \(J^{ab}\) (the subalgebra of \(\mathfrak{so}(4,2)\) on the indices \(0,\dots,4\); the generators \(M^{a5}\) and \(M^{45}\) are not in it). The operator \((y_aD^a)^2\) is not an element of that enveloping algebra: its coefficients are the non-constant functions \(y_a\), which would have to be supplied by the missing generators. Hence in this class none of the action, the bilinear term, the background gauge, the ghosts and the \(\Box_X\) regulator produces the term \((y_aD^a)^2\). The full Casimir \(\tfrac12\eta_{AC}\eta_{BD}J^{AB}J^{CD}\) of \(\mathfrak{so}(4,2)\) has base symbol \(-2\ell^2g^{-1}\) (checked numerically), but it lies outside the enveloping algebra of \(\mathfrak{so}(4,1)'\) and enters neither the Hessian nor the ghost operator. Extensions of the matrix set by \(y^a\) with a positive-norm contraction (eight matrices \(T^a,y^a\); seven matrices \(T^a,y^i\)) have rank-six horizontal span, but the equation of motion then needs different bilinear coefficients for different matrices (checked by exact Poisson algebra). The eight-matrix set \(T^a,y^a\) with the \(y\)-block contracted by \(-\eta\) is stationary for one coefficient \(\mu^2=-4\), with horizontal rank six, and is not examined further.

\emph{Five-matrix presentation.} Take \(X^A=M^{Ac_0}\), \(A\ne c_0\), with the restriction of \(\eta_6\) as contraction. Definition~\ref{def:matrix-coords-ext} (\(D=5\)) covers \(c_0\in\{0,5\}\) up to relabelling; the forms for \(c_0\in\{1,\dots,4\}\), of signature \((3,2)\), lie outside it and are included as a comparison. The orbit relation gives \(y\cdot y=\eta_{c_0c_0}\ell^2\) for \(y^A=M^{Ac_0}\). At \(25\) random points for each \(c_0\) the horizontal rank is \(4\) and the centre rank \(2\); the induced contraction \(h=c^{T}\eta c\) on the horizontal space is positive definite for \(c_0\in\{0,5\}\) and has signature \((2,2)\) for \(c_0\in\{1,\dots,4\}\); a Lorentzian signature does not occur. For \(X^A=M^{A5}\), \(A=0,\dots,4\) (stabiliser \(\mathfrak{so}(4,1)\)): \(y_AD^A=0\) on the orbit, the base symbol of \(\eta_{AB}D^AD^B\) is \(\ell^2g^{-1}\) (checked to \(10^{-12}\)), and at \(300\) random points and \(91\) directions of \(\zeta\), for both signs of the bracket, the vertical flux in an \(h\)-orthonormal frame is self-dual or anti-self-dual to \(10^{-11}\), so \(f_1=f_2\) identically. This constant contraction of the five matrices is consistent with the statement that self-duality holds identically only for the contraction by the position-dependent \(Q^{-1}\) of the four matrices \(T^a\): its base symbol \(\ell^2g^{-1}\) is that of \(Q^{-1}\) up to the factor \((y^4)^2\). The bilinear coefficient required by stationarity is \(\mu^2=+4\) (exact, both signs of \(s\)), the Zeeman term of the gauge-fixed vector operator has the same modulus as in the four-matrix class, and in the polynomial Poisson algebra, exactly,
\[
H_V=C_{\mathfrak{so}(4,2)}-C_{G}+C_{\Sigma}+\mu^2,\qquad \Box=C_{\mathfrak{so}(4,1)}-C_{\mathfrak{so}(4,2)},
\]
where \(C_G\) is the Casimir of the total \(\mathfrak{so}(4,1)\) action on vector-valued functions and \(C_\Sigma\) that of the vector index. By the Weyl formulae the \(\mathfrak{so}(4,1)\) component \((k+1,k)\) of the degree-\(k\) vector polynomials has \(C_{\mathfrak{so}(4,2)}-C_G+C_\Sigma=0\) for every \(k\), so it is an eigenspace of \(H_V\) with eigenvalue \(\mu^2\) (verified directly for \(k=1\), dimension \(35\)). On the \(75\)-dimensional space of degree-one vector polynomials the formal eigenvalues of \(H_V\) are \(-8,-4,-2,+2,+4\) with multiplicities \(1,5,20,14,35\) (checked); polynomials are not normalisable, so the negative values have no spectral meaning. At the leading symbol the vector operator then has the lowest Landau level at zero for the four horizontal components, a zero-mode family with density \(f^2/(2\pi^2)\) in \(\zeta\), independent of the proper time; the normal component has no Zeeman term, and the bosonic density per \(\mathrm d^2\zeta/(2\pi)^2\) is \(f^2(16\pi^2)^{-1}\bigl[8+(3+n_{\mathrm{ex}})/\sinh^2(sf)\bigr]\). The \(\zeta\)-integral of the second term converges; that of the first diverges and is not regulated by the proper-time cutoff. The fibre of the leaf is compact, so \(\zeta\) is expected to be bounded by the fibre harmonic number of the quantum space; the divergence is a feature of the Poisson limit.

\emph{Compact check at finite size.} For the compact form \(\mathfrak{so}(6)\) with \(\delta\) throughout, on the \(N\)-th symmetric power of the fundamental representation, the exact Hessian of the quartic term plus \(\tfrac12m^2\operatorname{Tr}X^2\) (finite matrices, \(N=1,\dots,4\)) satisfies \(H+G^\dagger G=C_6-C_5^{\mathrm{tot}}+C_5^{\mathrm{vec}}+m^2\) for \(X^A=M^{A6}\), \(m^2=-4\). Its lowest eigenvalue is \(-4=m^2\) for \(N\le4\), with the family \((k+1,k)\), \(1\le k\le N\), of total multiplicity \(\sum_{k=1}^{N}(2k+1)(2k+3)(2k+5)/3\) and \(14\) further modes at \(-2\), so that the number of negative modes is \(49,154,385,814\) for \(N=1,\dots,4\) and this compact background is unstable; for the four-matrix background \(X^a=M^{a5}\), \(m^2=-3\), it is \(-(N+3)\), growing with \(N\). The sign of \(m^2\) is \(-4\) on the sphere and \(+4\) on the hyperboloid.

\emph{Consequences.} (i) In the matrix sets examined (\(T^a\); \(M^{Ac_0}\); the extended sets listed above), the term \((y_aD^a)^2\) is supplied by the matrix data only if the matrices are the position functions \(X^A=M^{A5}\); the price is that the horizontal metric induced by the contraction is then the Euclidean metric \(g\), and the conformally flat Lorentzian \(K\) of the four-matrix class is not obtained, so the level-one and stress results of the four-matrix class are not obtained in this presentation. (ii) For the hyperboloid, the vector zero modes of the leading symbol are lifted by \(+\mu^2\) only if the vanishing of the Casimir combination, which is exact on polynomial vector fields and on the compact form, continues to the normalisable sector; this continuation is not shown, and the negative formal eigenvalues \(-8,-4,-2,+2\) of the same operator on degree-one polynomials, like the negative modes of the compact background, may persist on normalisable states. (iii) Neither the sign nor the size of \(w\) follows: the weight-two coefficient and its variation under \(A\) are not computed, and the passage from a Euclidean coefficient to the Lorentzian one is a Wick-rotation hypothesis tied to Problem~E.

This is a statement for the Poisson limit, the leading symbol, equiregular points, the bosonic content and the single bilinear term; the self-duality of the flux is verified numerically and not proved, and the finite-size spectrum is for the compact form. It does not treat fermions, Moyal terms for the hyperboloid, or the Lorentzian emergent metric. It does not identify \(g\) with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}, does not derive Lorentzian signature, and leaves Assumption~\ref{ass:lorentzian} in place (Problem~E). This is a scope statement and not a retraction of any earlier result. This changes no ledger label; Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class: the Casimir of \(\mathfrak{so}(4,2)\) and its combinations as proper-time regulators in the four-matrix class (conditional; bounded no-go and conditional route).}
Keep the Poisson limit, the leaf \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\) of \(\mathfrak{so}(4,2)\), \(T^a=M^{a4}\), \(D^a=\{T^a,\cdot\}\), \(J^{AB}=\{M^{AB},\cdot\}\), \(\Box=\eta_{ab}D^aD^b\), the vector operator \(H_V\) with \(\mu^2=-3\) and the notation of the preceding paragraphs. Write \(C_{42}\), \(C_{41}\), \(C_{31}\) for \(\tfrac12\eta_{AC}\eta_{BD}J^{AB}J^{CD}\) summed over the indices \(0,\dots,5\), \(0,\dots,4\), \(0,\dots,3\), and \(\sigma(\cdot)\) for the quadratic form \(\sum w\,(\xi\!\cdot\!v)^2\) of the cotangent vector \(\xi\) of an operator \(\sum w\,v^2\) of vector fields \(v\) (the principal symbol is its negative, so that the principal symbol of \(H_V\) is \(\sigma(\Box)\) and that of \(\Delta_\kappa\) below is \(-\sigma(\Delta_\kappa)\)).

\emph{Exact relations.} In the polynomial Poisson algebra, for both signs of the bracket, \(\Box=C_{41}-C_{31}\), \(\mu^2=-3\) and, with \(C_G\) the Casimir of the total \(\mathfrak{so}(3,1)\) action on vector-valued functions (orbital part plus the vector index, the sign of the index action correlated with the sign of the bracket) and \(C_\Sigma\) that of the vector index,
\[
H_V=C_G-C_{41}-C_\Sigma+\mu^2 ,
\]
the unique solution of the linear fit \(H_V=xC_G+yC_{31}+zC_{41}+wC_{42}+uC_\Sigma+\mu^2\) with \(w=0\). Hence \(C_{42}\) does not occur in \(H_V\), and \(C_{42}-C_{41}=-\sum_{A\le4}\eta_{AA}(J^{A5})^2\). Since \(C_{42}\) is central in \(U(\mathfrak{so}(4,2))\) and \(C_{41}\) commutes with \(\mathfrak{so}(4,1)\), both commute with \(H_V\), with \(\Box\) and with the ghost operator \(-\Box\); a function of \(C_{42}\) and \(C_{41}\) therefore commutes with every operator of the class and no ordering ambiguity arises.

\emph{Symbols.} At \(40\) random orbit points the form \(\sigma(C_{42})\) on the six-dimensional cotangent space has signature \((2,4)\), and so has \(\sigma(C_{41})\). The pencil \(\sigma(C_{41})v=\lambda\,\sigma(C_{42})v\) has the eigenvalues \(\tfrac12\) (four times) and \(1\) (twice) at \(30\) points (deviation \(3\cdot10^{-13}\)); on the four-dimensional eigenspace (the span of the \(dy^C\), \(y^C=M^{C5}\)) \(\sigma(C_{42})\) is negative definite and on the two-dimensional (vertical) eigenspace positive definite. The base symbol \(-2\ell^2g^{-1}\) of the preceding paragraph is the first of these; the fibre carries the opposite sign. Write \(\sigma(C_{42})=-\alpha q_b+\beta q_f\) in this splitting, so that \(\sigma(C_{41})=-\tfrac12\alpha q_b+\beta q_f\). The operators \(C_{41}-C_{42}\) (the five-matrix \(\Box\), purely horizontal) and \(2C_{41}-C_{42}\) (purely vertical) are the two degenerate members of the family
\[
\Delta_\kappa=\kappa\,C_{42}-C_{41},\qquad -\sigma(\Delta_\kappa)=\alpha(\kappa-\tfrac12)q_b+\beta(1-\kappa)q_f ,
\]
which, as the principal symbol of \(\Delta_\kappa\), is positive definite exactly for \(\tfrac12<\kappa<1\) (checked at \(15\) points for nine values of \(\kappa\)); the value \(\kappa=\tfrac34\) gives \(-\sigma(\Delta_{3/4})=\tfrac14(\alpha q_b+\beta q_f)\), the absolute value of \(\sigma(C_{42})\) in the splitting. The horizontal planes \(\operatorname{span}\{D^a\}\) of the four-matrix class and \(\operatorname{span}\{J^{A5}\}\) of the five-matrix class are different four-planes (their sum has dimension \(6\) at six points).

\emph{Consequences.} (i) No function of \(C_{42}\) alone is an elliptic regulator: the level sets of an indefinite form in the cotangent fibre are non-compact, so \(\int\mathrm d^6\xi\,\chi(\sigma(C_{42})/\LUV^2)\) diverges for every \(\chi\) of compact support, and a cutoff of \(C_{42}\) leaves the null cone \(q_b=\beta q_f/\alpha\) unregulated; this is a statement about the leading symbol. (ii) \(\Delta_\kappa\), \(\tfrac12<\kappa<1\), is built from the matrix data \(M^{AB}\) and the contraction \(\eta_{AB}\) only, commutes with \(H_V\), \(\Box\) and the ghost operator, and has a definite symbol on the whole leaf. For a cutoff function \(\chi\) of compact support, the density \((2\pi)^{-6}\!\int\mathrm d^6\xi\,\chi(-\sigma(\Delta_\kappa)/\LUV^2)\,F(\sigma(\Box))\) is finite at every point for every continuous \(F\) (at the level of the leading symbol); in particular the central variable \(\zeta\) of the preceding paragraphs is confined to a ball \(|\zeta|\lesssim\LUV\), which regulates the \(\zeta\)-integral left divergent there. It is a different regulator from a function of \(\Box_X\), and it uses the generators \(J^{A5}\), which are not in \(U(\mathfrak{so}(4,1)')\); it is an additional element of the regularisation and not derived from the action. (iii) The volume of the window \(\{-\sigma(\Delta_\kappa)\le\LUV^2\}\) is proportional to \(\LUV^6[(\kappa-\tfrac12)^2(1-\kappa)]^{-1}\) (checked against \(\det^{-1/2}\) at five values of \(\kappa\)), so the leading coefficient depends on \(\kappa\) and diverges at both ends of the interval, where the horizontal and the vertical directions respectively are unregulated; no value of \(\kappa\) is singled out by the algebra, and the quadratic coefficient, which is the one an Einstein--Hilbert term needs, is not independent of the choice of \(\kappa\) and \(\chi\) without a further argument. (iv) For the Lorentzian \(\eta\) the form \(\sigma(\Box)\) has signature \((3,1)\) with two null directions, and in the window of \(\Delta_{3/4}\) the generalised eigenvalues of \((\sigma(\Box),-\sigma(\Delta_{3/4}))\) are \(-2\) (at eight points, the same value at each) and three positive values that grow with \((y^4)^2\) (so the negative bound is uniform in position and the positive range is not); the principal symbol \(\sigma(\Box)\) of \(H_V\) is therefore bounded by \(\mp\) a multiple of \(\LUV^2\) on the window but is negative on part of it, so the symbol of \(e^{-sH_V}\) is bounded by \(e^{2s\LUV^2}\) there and the Euclidean proper-time integral at large \(s\) still diverges. The window removes the divergence at large \(\zeta\), not the negative direction: for the Euclidean contraction \(\eta\to\delta\) of the preceding paragraphs the lowest Zeeman level \(-|f_1-f_2|\) is bounded in the window of \(\Delta_{3/4}\) by \(d_*\LUV\) (in the normalisation of the forms above) with \(1.7\le d_*\le5.6\) at six points, linear in the cutoff.

\emph{Compact check.} For the compact form \(\mathfrak{so}(6)\) with \(\delta\), \(X^a=M^{a5}\), \(a=1,\dots,4\), \(m^2=-3\), on the \(N\)-th symmetric power (the matrices \(\operatorname{End}(\mathrm{Sym}^N\mathbb C^4)=\bigoplus_{k\le N}(k,k,0)\) of \(\mathfrak{so}(6)\), so that a cutoff on \(C_{\mathfrak{so}(6)}=2k^2+6k\) is the truncation of the matrix algebra), the gauge-fixed Hessian \(H+G^\dagger G\), \(Ga=\sum_a[X^a,a^a]\), has the spectrum
\[
\lambda=c_5(a,b)-c_4(c',d')+3+m^2,
\]
with \(c_5(a,b)=a(a+3)+b(b+1)\), \(c_4(c,d)=c(c+2)+d^2\), \(a=k\) the label of \(\mathfrak{so}(6)\), \(0\le b\le a\), \((c,d)\) the \(\mathfrak{so}(4)\) content of \((a,b)\) and \((c',d')\) that of \((c,d)\otimes(1,0)\); this was checked against the numerical spectrum for \(N=1,2,3\) (differences below \(10^{-12}\)). The lowest eigenvalue in the window \(k\le A\) is \(-(A+3)\), the number of negative modes is \(38,124,344,750\) for \(A=1,\dots,4\), and their fraction of the \(4\binom{A+3}{3}^2\) modes falls numerically to \(2.5\cdot10^{-3}\) at \(A=48\) (local exponents \(3.9\) and \(5.7\), approaching \(4\) and \(6\); not proved). The cutoff by the Casimir therefore bounds the negative direction by a quantity growing linearly in the cutoff and removes none of it.

\emph{Eight matrices.} For \(T^a\) and \(y^a=M^{a5}\), \(a=0,\dots,3\), with the contraction \(\eta\) on \(T\) and \(\lambda\eta\) on \(y\), exact stationarity requires the bilinear coefficients \(\lambda-3\) for \(T\) and \(3\lambda-1\) for \(y\), equal only at \(\lambda=-1\) (common value \(-4\)). The contraction form on the tangent space has signature \((3,3)\) at \(\lambda=-1\) and \(-100\le\lambda\le-0.01\) (thirty points each), \((5,1)\) at \(\lambda=0.01,0.1,10\) and is degenerate of rank \(4\) at \(\lambda=1\); the horizontal span has rank \(6\) throughout, so there is no centre variable. A \((5,1)\) induced form with no centre variable thus occurs at the sampled values \(\lambda=0.01,0.1,10\), and requires two independent bilinear coefficients; the Hessian of that set is not examined.

This is a statement for the Poisson limit, one loop, the leading symbol, the four-matrix class with the bilinear term, the regulators that are functions of \(C_{42}\) and \(C_{41}\), and the sampled points; the signatures and the pencil are numerical, the relations in the first paragraph exact. It does not treat fermions, the Moyal terms, a Lorentzian \(i\epsilon\) integral, the identification of the window with any admissibility condition of the fibre, or the subleading symbol, it does not compute the weight-two coefficient or its variation under \(A\), and neither the sign nor the size of \(w\) follows. It identifies no induced metric with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence} and, since a Euclidean coefficient is not a Lorentzian one, leaves Assumption~\ref{ass:lorentzian} in place (Problem~E). This changes no ledger label; Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class: fibre admissibility and the Lorentzian \(i\epsilon\) window (conditional; bounded no-go).}
Keep the setting and notation of the preceding paragraph (Poisson limit, leaf \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\), \(T^a=M^{a4}\), \(\Box=\eta_{ab}D^aD^b\), \(H_V\) with \(\mu^2=-3\), \(C_{42}\), \(C_{41}\), \(C_{31}\), \(\sigma(\cdot)\), \(\Delta_\kappa\)); write \(S^2=2C_{41}-C_{42}\), whose symbol \(\beta q_f\) is purely vertical.

\emph{Fibre cutoffs.} In the Poisson limit the fibre of the leaf is a compact two-sphere and \(|\zeta|\) is unbounded; it is expected to be bounded by the fibre harmonic number of the quantum space. Two bounds on the harmonic number are in the literature. (a) \(S^2=2s(s+1)\) on the sectors \(\mathcal C^s\) with \(s\le n\), from the \((n+1)\)-dimensional representation of the stabiliser \(SU(2)_L\) on the lowest-weight state~\cite{Steinacker2019NoGhost}; the classical counterpart is the radius of the fibre as a coadjoint orbit of \(SU(2)_L\), the self-dual part of \(M^{ab}\) on the \(\eta\)-orthogonal complement of \(y\): at \(10\) orbit points exactly one of the two radii vanishes and the other equals \(\ell\), independent of \(y\). (b) A local bound \(s\le m=r^{-1}x^0\) from the uncertainty of the coherent states, which the same group states to correct earlier statements on the higher-spin cutoff~\cite{MantaSteinacker2025}; it grows with the point and is not the area bound of (a), and the two are not reconciled here. Neither is a sharp cutoff of commuting matrix-built operators.

\emph{Admissible cone.} For admissible modes, the principal series of \(SO(4,1)\), one has \(r^{-2}\Box_H>s^2+s+9/4\) with \(\Box_H=-C_{42}+C_{41}\) in the convention of~\cite{Steinacker2019NoGhost}, equivalent, by (2.7) and (3.43) of that reference, to \(-C_{42}>9/2\) in that convention (its footnote~8 prints \(C_2[\mathfrak{so}(4,2)]>9/2\) and states the identification with the \(SO(4,2)\) content of \(\operatorname{End}(\mathcal H_n)\) as plausible, not investigated). With \(\Box_H\) horizontal, \(S^2\) vertical and \(\sigma(C_{42})=-\alpha q_b+\beta q_f\) of the preceding paragraph, the semiclassical content of this bound is the cone \(-\sigma(C_{42})\ge0\), that is \(\alpha q_b\ge\beta q_f\); the Casimirs here and there differ by a sign at the symbol level. Conditional on this identification, a cutoff of \(C_{42}\) restricted to the cone together with a bound \(\beta q_f\le Q\) is a compact region, so a function of the central operator \(C_{42}\) regularises once the fibre is bounded. The cone restricts to the admissible sector and is not what bounds the window: the bound \(\beta q_f\le Q\) is a cutoff of \(S^2=2C_{41}-C_{42}\), so this relaxes the hypothesis of the preceding paragraph and does not contradict it, whose consequence~(i) for functions of \(C_{42}\) alone stands. The Lorentzian moments, the flux and the exact-sector results below are for the Gaussian window of \(\Delta_\kappa\), not for this fibre-bounded window. For fixed \(Q\) and \(\LUV^2\to\infty\) the window \(\{-\sigma(\Delta_\kappa)\le\LUV^2\}\) has its vertical term bounded by \(\beta(1-\kappa)Q\), so \(\kappa\) only rescales \(\LUV^2\) by \(2\kappa-1\) at leading order and the divergence at \(\kappa\to1\) is absent. The cutoff function and the base scale remain external: the quantisation map is \(SO(4,2)\)-equivariant and so acts on each irreducible component by a scalar, but this multiplier is not computed, and neither \(\chi\) nor the base scale is fixed. The window uses \(M^{AB}\), which are background data and not dynamical matrices, so the regulated trace is not shown to be a diffeomorphism-covariant functional of \(X^a\).

\emph{Lorentzian trace in a window.} Define the one-loop sum as \(\sum\ln(\lambda-i\epsilon)=-\int_0^\infty\frac{\mathrm ds}{s}\operatorname{Tr}\bigl[\chi\,e^{-is(H_V-i\epsilon)}\bigr]\) up to a scale-dependent constant, with \(\chi\) a function of the commuting operator \(\Delta_\kappa\); the contour is the proper time of \(H_V\) with the factor \(e^{-\epsilon s}\), and uses no metric. In a window of compact support (or a Gaussian) the number of modes per unit volume is finite, so the trace is finite for every \(s\), and \(\sum\ln(\lambda-i\epsilon)\) is a finite product over the window whether or not the Schwinger integral converges. For \(\chi=e^{-\sigma_W/\LUV^2}\), \(\sigma_W=-\sigma(\Delta_\kappa)\), the leading-symbol density is the Gaussian \((4\pi)^{-3}\LUV^6\det(\sigma_W)^{-1/2}\prod_{k=1}^{4}(1+is\LUV^2\lambda_k)^{-1/2}\), with \(\lambda_k\) the nonzero generalised eigenvalues of \((\sigma(\Box),\sigma_W)\); the kernel of \(\sigma(\Box)\) is not the vertical plane (checked at eight points). The mass-type moment is \(\langle(\sigma-i0)^{-1}\rangle=i\int_0^\infty\mathrm ds\prod_k(1+is\lambda_k)^{-1/2}\), whose real part is the principal value. For \(\lambda=(-c,a,a,a)\) the real part is \(2/(a+c)\) (closed form, checked), and for \(4000\) samples with \(c\in[0.1,10]\), \(a_k\in[10^{-3},10^{3}]\) it is positive. At eight orbit points (\(\kappa=\tfrac34\), \((y^4)^2\) from \(0.08\) to \(144\)) it is \(1.1\) to \(2.3\) times the value for the Euclidean contraction \(\eta\to\delta\), with the same sign, and the imaginary part is positive.

\emph{Flux.} At the same points the Lorentzian centre flux \(F_{ab}(\zeta)\) has \(\eta F\) with one real pair \(\pm a\) and one imaginary pair \(\pm ib\), \(a,b>0\) at all sampled \(\zeta\), and \(F_{ab}F^{ab}\) takes both signs (negative for \((y^4)^2\lesssim0.4\), positive above). The vector block therefore has Zeeman eigenvalues \(\pm2b\) (phases in \(e^{-is(H_V-i\epsilon)}\)) and \(\pm2ia\) (a factor \(e^{\pm2sa}\)). The Schwinger integrand of the vector sector then grows for the electric pair, bounded by \(e^{2sa}\) with \(a\) linear in the cutoff, while \(\ln\det(\sigma+Z)=\ln(\sigma^2-4b^2)+\ln(\sigma^2+4a^2)\) has integrable logarithmic singularities at \(\sigma=\pm2b\). The exponential growth of the Euclidean contraction is absent order by order in the flux (each order is a polynomial in \(s\) times \(e^{-is\sigma}\)) and for the magnetic pair, at the leading symbol and at the sampled points, and is present for the electric pair as a property of the integral representation and not of the determinant. The order-\(F^2\) term is proportional to \(F_{ab}F^{ab}\), so its sign is not definite on the leaf.

\emph{Invariant sector, exactly.} On fibre-constant functions \(\Box=-(\Delta_{H^4}-\Delta_{H^3})\) with \(H^4=\mathbb R_\tau\times H^3\), metric \(\mathrm d\tau^2+\cosh^2\tau\,\mathrm dH_3^2\), \(y^4=\ell\sinh\tau\), and the diagonal of \(e^{z\Delta_{H^4}+w\Delta_{H^3}}\) is the \(H^3\) integral of the product of the two heat kernels. Its expansion gives \(D_0=(4\pi)^{-2}z^{-1/2}(z+w\cosh^2\tau)^{-3/2}\), the Gaussian symbol formula above, and, for the pure \(\Box\) term (window removed), the Seeley--DeWitt coefficient \((5m^2-3\ell^2)/(4m^2)\), \(m=y^4\), whose product with \(\sqrt{|G|}=m^{-4}\) is the density \((5m^2-3\ell^2)/(4m^6)\) of the preceding paragraphs, obtained by an independent route. With the window the weight-two part of \(\sum\ln(\lambda-i\epsilon)\) is \(-(4\pi)^{-2}\LUV^2\,Q(a)\), \(a=\sinh\tau\), plus \(-2\) times a scale-dependent logarithm proportional to the number of modes, which is the \(R/6\) curvature term of the auxiliary \(H^4\) window geometry; \(\operatorname{Re}Q\) is indefinite, and with the subtraction at \(x=1\) it changes sign at \(a\simeq0.62\) (not at \(a^2=\tfrac35\), the zero of the coefficient above), a location that moves with the subtraction point and has no invariant meaning; \(\operatorname{Im}Q\to\pi\). This is the Lorentzian density of one scalar sector at the background \(A=0\), with a scale-dependent additive constant; it is not the Einstein--Hilbert coefficient.

\emph{Consequences.} (i) The coefficient carrying \(w\) is the weight-two term, a local Poisson-algebraic coefficient multiplied by window moments; the signature enters through the moments and the window, and in the potential channel the Lorentzian moment is finite and positive in the class sampled. (ii) The mode-count term is proportional to the difference between the numbers of bosonic and fermionic modes in the window, so its logarithmic scale dependence cancels only if that difference vanishes at this order. (iii) The weight-two coefficient as a quadratic form in \(A\) is not computed, the vector, ghost and fibre-harmonic sectors are not computed, and neither the sign nor the size of \(w\), hence no sign of an attractive coupling, follows.

This is a statement for the Poisson limit, one loop, the leading symbol and the invariant sector, the four-matrix class with \(\mu^2=-3\), Gaussian windows and the sampled points; the admissible cone is conditional on the identification stated, the Lorentzian moments are not computed in the fibre-bounded window, and the positivity of the moment is numerical. It does not treat fermions, Moyal terms, the dependence on \(A\), or a window built from dynamical matrices only; it identifies no induced metric with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence} and, since a Euclidean coefficient is not a Lorentzian one, leaves Assumption~\ref{ass:lorentzian} in place (Problem~E). This changes no ledger label; Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class: Lorentzian moments in the fibre-bounded cone window, and the vector, ghost and spin terms of the invariant sector (conditional; bounded no-go).}
Keep the setting and notation of the two preceding paragraphs (Poisson limit, leaf \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\), \(T^a=M^{a4}\), \(\Box\), \(H_V\) with \(\mu^2=-3\), \(C_{42}\), \(C_{41}\), \(C_{31}\), \(\sigma(\cdot)\), \(S^2=2C_{41}-C_{42}\) with symbol \(\beta q_f\), the base symbol \(\alpha q_b\), \(a=\sinh\tau=y^4/\ell\), and the function \(Q(a)\) of the exact invariant sector). At a point of the leaf take coordinates \((u,\zeta)\in\mathbb R^4\times\mathbb R^2\) on the cotangent space with \(|u|^2=\alpha q_b\) and \(|\zeta|^2=\beta q_f\), so that \(-\sigma(C_{42})=|u|^2-|\zeta|^2\), and write \(\mathsf K\) for the form \(\sigma(\Box)\) (rank four, signature \((3,1)\)) in these coordinates, with blocks \(\mathsf K_{uu}\), \(\mathsf K_{u\zeta}\), \(\mathsf K_{\zeta\zeta}\).

\emph{Gaussian windows.} Let \(\lambda_0=-c\) and \(\lambda_1,\lambda_2,\lambda_3\) be positive and \(\mathcal M=\mathrm i\int_0^\infty\mathrm ds\prod_{k=0}^3(1+\mathrm is\lambda_k)^{-1/2}\), the Gaussian average of \((\sigma-\mathrm i0)^{-1}\). Rotating \(s=-\mathrm iu\) (the fourth quadrant contains no singularity) gives \(\mathcal M=\int_0^\infty\mathrm du\,(1-cu-\mathrm i0)^{-1/2}\prod_{k\ge1}(1+\lambda_ku)^{-1/2}\), whose first factor is real for \(u<1/c\) and equals \(\mathrm i(cu-1)^{-1/2}\) for \(u>1/c\). With \(u=(1\mp v^2)/c\),
\[
  \operatorname{Re}\mathcal M=\frac2c\int_0^1\!\mathrm dv\prod_{k\ge1}\Bigl(1+\frac{\lambda_k(1-v^2)}c\Bigr)^{-1/2}>0,\qquad
  \operatorname{Im}\mathcal M=\frac2c\int_0^\infty\!\mathrm dv\prod_{k\ge1}\Bigl(1+\frac{\lambda_k(1+v^2)}c\Bigr)^{-1/2}>0 ,
\]
and \(\operatorname{Re}\mathcal M=2/(\lambda+c)\) for \(\lambda_k=\lambda\). The pencil of \(\sigma(\Box)\) with any positive definite window form has one negative, three positive and two zero generalised eigenvalues, so the principal-value mass moment of every Gaussian window of the class is positive; this proves, for Gaussian windows, the positivity that the preceding paragraph found by sampling.

\emph{Geometry of the cone window.} At \(60\) random orbit points (agreement \(10^{-9}\) or better, not proved): the eigenvalues of \(\mathsf K_{uu}\) are \(-\tfrac12\) and \(\tfrac12\sinh^2\tau\) (three times); the Schur complement of \(\mathsf K_{uu}\) in \(\mathsf K\) vanishes, so \(\sigma=Q_L(u+w\zeta)\) with \(Q_L=\mathsf K_{uu}\) and \(w=\mathsf K_{uu}^{-1}\mathsf K_{u\zeta}\); \(w\) is conformal, \(|w\zeta|=\coth\tau\,|\zeta|\), and its image is orthogonal to the time eigenvector of \(Q_L\). In the variable \(\boldsymbol x=u+w\zeta\) the removed region \(|u|<|\zeta|\) is therefore the ball of radius \(r=|\zeta|\) about the spacelike point \(p=w\zeta\), at distance \(r\coth\tau=r\sqrt{1+c/\lambda}\) from the apex \(\boldsymbol x=0\) with \(c=\tfrac12\), \(\lambda=\tfrac12\sinh^2\tau\): the cone \(|u|=|\zeta|\) touches the null cone of \(Q_L\).

\emph{Mass moment in the fibre-bounded cone window.} Let \(\chi\ge0\) be bounded and integrable on \([0,\infty)\) and take the window function \(\chi(|u|^2-|\zeta|^2)\) on \(|\zeta|^2\le Q_f\) and \(|u|\ge|\zeta|\). For a ball of radius \(R\) about \(p\) with \(|p|<R\), polar coordinates about the apex give \(\operatorname{PV}\!\int_{|\boldsymbol x-p|<R}\mathrm d^4\boldsymbol x\,Q_L^{-1}=\tfrac12(R^2-|p|^2)I_0+p^{\mathsf T}\mathsf Np\), where \(I_0=\operatorname{PV}\oint\mathrm d\Omega\,Q_L^{-1}=8\pi^2/\cosh^2\tau\) and \(\mathsf N=\operatorname{PV}\oint\mathrm d\Omega\,nn^{\mathsf T}Q_L^{-1}\), because the remaining term of the exit radius is odd in the direction \(n\) and \(Q_L\) is even (checked against a direct quadrature to \(10^{-4}\)). For spacelike \(p\) with \(|p|\ge R\), quadrature indicates that the same expression continues to hold up to the tangency distance \(R\coth\tau\) (agreement to \(10^{-3}\) in the sampled cases) and departs from it beyond; this is a numerical observation and is not proved here. Under this observation every shell \(r\le|u|\le\sqrt{r^2+\Lambda^2}\) has principal-value moment \(\tfrac12\Lambda^2I_0\), independent of \(p\), and so
\[
  \operatorname{Re}\,\mathcal N_W=\tfrac12\,\varrho\,\pi Q_f\,I_0\int_0^\infty\chi(\lambda)\,\mathrm d\lambda>0 ,\qquad
  \mathcal N_W=\int_W\varrho\,\mathrm d^4u\,\mathrm d^2\zeta\;\chi\,(\sigma-\mathrm i0)^{-1},
\]
with \(\varrho>0\) the Jacobian of the coordinates \((u,\zeta)\); the sign of the real part is conditional on the numerical observation, and the real part depends on the cutoff function only through \(\int\chi\) and on the fibre bound only through the area \(\pi Q_f\). The imaginary part is \(\pi\int_W\varrho\,\chi\,\delta(\sigma)\ge0\). Finiteness of \(\mathcal N_W\) is likewise rested on the shell formula: the apex singularity is of order \(|\boldsymbol x|^{-2}\) in four dimensions and integrable, but the window function has a jump at the cone edge when \(\chi(0)\ne0\), and the edge touches the null cone of \(Q_L\) at one point, so finiteness there is not shown independently.

\emph{Zeeman field and electric growth.} The first-order term of \(H_V\) is exactly the Zeeman matrix of the field \(F_{ab}(\xi)=\xi(\mathcal J_{ab})\), linear in the covector \(\xi\); let \(\eta F\) have eigenvalues \(\pm f_e\) (electric pair, factor \(\mathrm e^{\pm2sf_e}\) in \(\mathrm e^{-\mathrm isH_V}\)) and \(\pm\mathrm if_m\) (magnetic pair, phases), so \(f_e^2-f_m^2=-F_{ab}F^{ab}/2=-\sigma(C_{31})(\xi)\). At \(59\) of the \(60\) points (one point is numerically singular in the whitening, a fibre Gram eigenvalue vanishing in double precision), to \(10^{-9}\) except for the maximum over the base plane, which is obtained from \(3000\) sampled directions to \(6\cdot10^{-5}\): on the base plane \(f_m=0\) and \(\max f_e=|u|\cosh\tau/\sqrt2\); on the vertical plane of the pencil \(f_e=|\zeta|\sinh\tau/\sqrt2\), \(f_m=|\zeta|/\sqrt2\) in every direction; on the kernel plane of \(\mathsf K\) (the centre variable of the nilpotent model) \(f_e^2=|\zeta|^2/(2(1+2\sinh^2\tau))\) and \(f_m^2=|\zeta|^2\sinh^2\tau/(2(1+2\sinh^2\tau))\), so that on this plane \(F_{ab}F^{ab}/2\) is negative for \(|y^4|<\ell\), positive for \(|y^4|>\ell\) and changes sign exactly at \(|y^4|=\ell\); this exact threshold replaces the sample-dependent value of the preceding paragraph on that plane (on the vertical plane the signs are opposite). The fibre-driven electric rate \(2f_e\) is \(2s_f\sinh\tau\) with \(\beta q_f=2s_f^2\le2s_{\max}^2\), whereas the base-driven rate grows linearly with \(|u|\); the electric rate is thus bounded by a quantity linear in the base cutoff, whose fibre part is bounded independently of the base cutoff. The logarithm of the determinant of the Zeeman block, \(\ln(\sigma^2-4f_m^2)+\ln(\sigma^2+4f_e^2)\), has only integrable logarithmic singularities, at \(\sigma=\pm2f_m\), at the leading symbol; the growth is a property of the Schwinger representation and is not removed by the fibre bound.

\emph{Vector and ghost sectors of the invariant sector.} The ghost operator is \(-\Box\), the same as the scalar of the preceding paragraph. On fibre-constant vector fields \(A_c(y)\) the cross term \(\eta_{ac}\eta_{bd}\mathcal J^{ab}\Sigma^{cd}\) of \(C_G\) equals \(-2s\mathcal J_{ab}A^b\) exactly (symbolic check), so \(H_V=-\Box-2s\mathcal J_{ab}A^b+\mu^2\) is \(C_G-C_{41}-C_\Sigma+\mu^2\) there. Write the diagonal of \(\mathrm e^{z\Delta_{H^4}+w\Delta_{H^3}}\) as \(D=D_0(1+D_1+\dots)\), with \(D_1\) depending on \(a\) and homogeneous of degree one in \((z,w)=(t-\mathrm is,\mathrm is)\); \(\operatorname{tr}(E+R/6)\) is the first Seeley--DeWitt density of \(\mathrm e^{t\Delta_{H^4}-\mathrm isH_V}\) in the convention that the exponent is \(\Delta+E\), and for one scalar component it equals \(D_1\). For the four components, with the \(4\times4\) matrix connection,
\[
  \operatorname{tr}\bigl(E+\tfrac16R\bigr)=4D_1+\frac{6s^2(1+a^2)}{t+\mathrm isa^2}-4\mathrm is\mu^2 ,
\]
the spin term being \(2s^2\operatorname{tr}[G\,\sigma(\Delta_{H^3})]=-2s^2\operatorname{tr}[G\,\sigma(C_{31})]\), with \(G\) the inverse of the symbol of the exponent, that is the Zeeman square \(f_e^2-f_m^2\) traced against \(G\). The route reproduces the exact scalar coefficient \(D_1\) to \(120\) digits at three points, and the vector and Dirac traces were verified to the same accuracy; the vector kernel itself was not computed. With \(x=s/t\) and the subtraction at \(x=1\) of the preceding paragraph, the weight-two functions are \(Q_\mu=-\mu^2j(a)\) with \(j(a)=2(1+\mathrm i/a)/(1+a^2)\), and \(Q_{\mathrm{sp}}=-8/(1+a^2)-\mathrm i\,4(3a^2+1)/(a^3(1+a^2))\) for the spin term, so \(\operatorname{Re}[Q_{\mathrm{sp}}+4Q_{\mu}]=16/(1+a^2)\) for \(\mu^2=-3\). For \(n_{\mathrm{ex}}\) further scalars of mass \(\mu_i^2\) the bosonic content has
\[
  \operatorname{Re}Q_{\mathrm{bos}}=(2+n_{\mathrm{ex}})\operatorname{Re}Q+\frac{16-2n_{\mathrm{ex}}\mu_i^2}{1+a^2},
\]
plus the scale-dependent term proportional to \(2+n_{\mathrm{ex}}\) of the preceding paragraph. The first term is indefinite and the second has the sign of \(8-n_{\mathrm{ex}}\mu_i^2\); the scale-dependent term has the sign of a logarithm. On the full leaf the spin term in a Gaussian window has a large-\(s\) tail \(C\,\mathrm ds/s\) with \(C\) purely imaginary and proportional to \(\sigma(C_{31})\) on the kernel plane, that is to \((1-a^2)/(1+2a^2)\), so its logarithmic coefficient is of indefinite sign on the leaf, and its real part depends on the contour angle.

\emph{Supertrace.} The matrix part of the coefficient is traceless, so it enters only through \(\operatorname{tr}\Sigma^{ab}\Sigma^{cd}\), and the species-independent part \(R/6+E\) is multiplied by the number of components.
