For the Dirac representation \(\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_D\Sigma_D=\tfrac12\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_V\Sigma_V\) for all index pairs. Supply, as an input not contained in the class, the sixteen real fermion components of the ten-dimensional supersymmetric matrix model with the square of the Dirac operator equal to \(\Box+s\Sigma^{ab}\mathcal J_{ab}\) on each component, no bilinear mass, and \(n_{\mathrm{ex}}=6\), and weight \(-\tfrac12\) per real fermion component. Then the component count \(4+6-2-8\) vanishes, the weighted index \(1-\tfrac12\cdot4\cdot\tfrac12\) vanishes, the Zeeman character is \(4(\cos sf_m-\cosh sf_e)^2=s^4(f_e^2+f_m^2)^2+\dots\) (the order \(s^4\) is a sum of the squares of the two invariants and does not vanish for any non-zero flux in Lorentzian signature), and what remains of the weight-two coefficient in the invariant sector is \(-\bigl(4\mu^2+n_{\mathrm{ex}}\mu_i^2\bigr)j(a)\), the mass supertrace times the mass moment \(j(a)\), with \(\operatorname{Re}j=2/(1+a^2)>0\); no sign of the mass supertrace itself is claimed. The mode-count term and the spin term of the order \(F^2\) both cancel identically; the curvature and connection part of the weight-two term therefore does not by itself carry the Newton coefficient for this content; whether it arises at higher order of the expansion or through the dependence on \(A\) is not computed.

This is a statement for the Poisson limit, one loop, the leading symbol and the invariant sector, the four-matrix class with \(\mu^2=-3\), Gaussian windows and the cone window of the stated form, the sampled points and the supplied fermion content with no bilinear fermion mass and the same window for bosons and fermions. The continuation of the shell identity to spacelike \(p\) with \(|p|\ge R\) is a numerical observation, and the sign of the cone-window moment is conditional on it; the vector kernel and the second variation under \(A\) are not computed; fermions are not part of the class; the Jacobian \(\varrho\) and the Liouville normalisation are not computed as functions of position. It does not treat Moyal terms or a window built from dynamical matrices, it identifies no induced metric with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence} and, since a Euclidean coefficient is not a Lorentzian one, leaves Assumption~\ref{ass:lorentzian} in place (Problem~E). This changes no ledger label; Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class: the supertrace at an arbitrary matrix fluctuation, and the species constants that can multiply an Einstein--Hilbert-type term (conditional; bounded no-go).}
Keep the setting of the preceding paragraph: Poisson limit, leaf \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\), \(T^a=M^{a4}\), background gauge function, ghost, vector with \(\mu^2=-3\), \(n_{\mathrm{ex}}\) further scalars of mass \(\mu_i^2\), the supplied sixteen real fermion components of weight \(-\tfrac12\) with no bilinear fermion mass, and one window function \(\chi\) of the commuting operator \(\Delta_\kappa\) for all species. Let \(X^a\) be arbitrary functions on the leaf, \(D_a=\{X_a,\cdot\}\), \(F_{ab}=\{X_a,X_b\}\), \(\mathcal Z_{ab}=\{F_{ab},\cdot\}\) and \(\Box_X=\eta^{ab}D_aD_b\).

\emph{Operators at arbitrary \(X\).} The Jacobi identity gives \([D_a,D_b]=\mathcal Z_{ab}\). The second variation of the quartic term plus the bilinear term about \(X\) is \(\tfrac12\int\varepsilon^aL_a\) with \(L_a=-\Box_X\varepsilon_a+D_aD_b\varepsilon^b-2\{F_{ab},\varepsilon^b\}+\mu^2\varepsilon_a\); in the background gauge \(D_a\varepsilon^a\) the ghost operator is \(-\Box_X\), the further scalars at vanishing transverse matrices have \(-\Box_X+\mu_i^2\), and the square of the Dirac operator is \(\mathcal D^2=\Box_X+\tfrac12\Gamma^{ab}\mathcal Z_{ab}\) (these three identities were checked symbolically for random polynomial \(X\) on a four-dimensional phase space). Every species is therefore \(H_i=(-\Box_X)\otimes1+Z_i+m_i\) with \(Z_i=\Sigma_i^{ab}\otimes\mathcal Z_{ab}\), the same operator \(-\Box_X\) and the same \(\mathcal Z_{ab}\) for all species, constant masses \(m_i\) (\(\mu^2\), \(\mu_i^2\), and zero for the ghost and the fermions), and constant matrices \(\Sigma_i^{ab}\) acting on the species index (for the vector, minus the generator of the vector index, which gives \(-2\{F_{ab},\varepsilon^b\}\); for the fermions, minus \(\tfrac14[\Gamma^a,\Gamma^b]\) on four copies of the real four-component spinor, because the operator compared with \(-\Box_X\) is \(-\mathcal D^2\); the traces satisfy \(\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_D\Sigma_D=\tfrac12\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_V\Sigma_V\) for all index pairs).

\emph{Species sums.} With weights \(w_i\) and dimensions \(d_i\) (\(1\cdot4\), \(1\cdot n_{\mathrm{ex}}\), \(-2\cdot1\), \(-\tfrac12\cdot16\)) one has, as exact tensor identities checked for all index tuples, \(\sum_iw_id_i=0\) and \(\sum_iw_i\operatorname{tr}(Z_i^{a_1b_1}\cdots Z_i^{a_nb_n})=0\) for \(n=1,2,3\) (not only for the totally symmetrised products); for \(n=4\) the symmetrised sum is the \(s^4\) coefficient \((f_e^2+f_m^2)^2\) of the character of the preceding paragraph and does not vanish. Because \(\Sigma_i\) acts on the species index only, the term of any Duhamel expansion of \(\sum_iw_i\operatorname{Tr}[\chi\,\Phi(H_i)]\) in powers of \(m_i\) and \(Z_i\) is a species constant times a species-independent operator trace; no commutation of \(\chi\), \(\mathcal Z_{ab}\) and \(\Box_X\) is needed. Hence, for every \(X\) and every \(\Phi\), the terms without masses and with at most three insertions of \(\mathcal Z_{ab}\) cancel identically (a check with random non-commuting matrices confirms this), and the term linear in the masses and without insertions of \(\mathcal Z_{ab}\) is \(+M_{\mathrm{str}}\operatorname{Tr}[\chi\,\Phi'(H_X)]\) (for \(\Phi=e^{-(\cdot)}\) this is \(-M_{\mathrm{str}}\operatorname{Tr}[\chi\,e^{-H_X}]\)) with \(M_{\mathrm{str}}=\sum_iw_id_im_i=4\mu^2+n_{\mathrm{ex}}\mu_i^2\). The other constants that survive at the next order are \(\sum_iw_id_im_i^2\), \(\sum_iw_im_i\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_i^{ab}\Sigma_i^{cd}\) (the vector mass alone) and the quartic constant.

\emph{Consequences.} (i) The weight-two coefficient of the preceding paragraph is, for every \(X\) and not only at \(X=T\), the mass-supertrace term: no count term \(\propto R\), no spin term quadratic in \(\mathcal Z_{ab}\) and no term cubic in it appears, whatever the dependence of \(\Box_X\) and \(\mathcal Z_{ab}\) on \(A\). (ii) At the leading symbol that term is proportional to \(M_{\mathrm{str}}\int\chi\,(\sigma_X-\mathrm i0)^{-1}\) with \(\sigma_X=\eta_{ab}\,\xi(D^a)\xi(D^b)\), which depends on \(X\) through \(\mathrm dX^a\) only. For \(A^a=\alpha^a\cos\phi\), \(\phi=q\cdot y\), with constant \(\alpha^a\), the Hamiltonian field of \(X^a\) is \(W^a-\epsilon\sin\phi\,\alpha^aY_q\), where \(W^a\) is that of \(T^a\) and \(Y_q=q_\mu\{y^\mu,\cdot\}\) (chain rule; the first-order change of the field of \(F_{ab}\) was checked through the Poisson algebra for the linear ansatz \(A^a=\alpha^a\,q\cdot y\)). This leading-symbol term depends on \(A\) only through its first derivatives, so its second variation contains no second derivative of the fluctuation and no term of Einstein--Hilbert form; the next-to-leading symbol, which involves the second jet of \(X\), is not computed. With the Gaussian window of \(\Delta_{3/4}\) the Hessian of the real (principal-value) part of this moment along \(\alpha\), at frozen coefficients and \(\phi=\pi/2\), was evaluated numerically in \(59\) random draws of orbit point and \(q\): it is indefinite in \(40\), positive definite in \(5\) and negative definite in \(14\), and along \(\alpha^a\propto q^a\) it is numerically nonzero in all draws with non-null \(q\). (iii) A term of Einstein--Hilbert form can therefore enter only through the constants listed above. The one proportional to \(M_{\mathrm{str}}\) is the mass-insertion term of the weighted \(a_2\) in the Euclidean class (there a shift of the operator of species \(i\) by \(m_i\) changes the weighted \(a_2\) by \(-m_ia_1^{(i)}\), with curvature part \(-\tfrac16M_{\mathrm{str}}\,\rho R[g_\rho]\) and connection part \(-(\sum_iw_i\kappa_im_i)\rho^2X\), \(\kappa_i\) being the weight of \(\rho^2X\) of the species; the second constant above is the vector mass times \(\kappa_V\)); its value in the six-dimensional leaf requires the next-to-leading symbol and is not computed. The pure flux constant is the order-\(F^4\) term: at the phase \(\phi=0\), where the first-jet terms vanish, and at the leading order in \(q\), the flux in the leading symbol is shifted as \(F_{ab}(\xi)\to F^0_{ab}(\xi)+\epsilon\,c\,(\xi\cdot Y_q)(q_a\alpha_b-q_b\alpha_a)\), \(c=-s\,y^4\cos\phi\), and at fixed proper time and \(\kappa=\tfrac34\) the Gaussian averages in the integrand depend on \(q\cdot y\) at fixed \(q\cdot q\), through the vector \(y^\mu\) and the window (at an orbit point with \(y^i=0\), \(\langle(\xi\cdot Y_q)^2\rangle\) changes by a factor above \(2\) over a boost of rapidity \(1.2\) of a unit \(q\)); its integrand is therefore not Lorentz invariant (the window and the leaf both single out \(y^\mu\), and this diagnostic does not separate them). (iv) No finite coefficient of this term follows in the present window: at four orbit points the integrand of the order-\(F^4\) term at \(A=0\) grows as \(s^2\) at large proper time (log-slope \(2.00\)), because the flux does not vanish on the kernel plane of \(\sigma(\Box)\); the expansion in \(\mathcal Z_{ab}\) is not the expansion in a small parameter there, and a sign of an Einstein--Hilbert-type coefficient does not follow. The non-expanded leading-symbol logarithm is \(\sum_iw_i\ln\det(\sigma+Z_i)=\ln(\sigma^2-4f_m^2)+\ln(\sigma^2+4f_e^2)+4\ln\sigma-2\ln[(\sigma+f_m)^2+f_e^2]-2\ln[(\sigma-f_m)^2+f_e^2]\), whose large-\(\sigma\) series starts with \(-6(f_e^2+f_m^2)^2/\sigma^4\) (checked); its second variation under \(A\) is not computed.

This is a statement for the Poisson limit, one loop, the commuting-symbol (leading) level, the four-matrix class with \(\mu^2=-3\), the supplied fermion content with no bilinear fermion mass and the same window for all species, applied term by term in an expansion that is not shown to converge. It does not compute the next-to-leading symbol, the dependence on \(A\) of the terms of Einstein--Hilbert form, the case of transverse matrices with flux or of species-dependent windows, Moyal terms, or a window built from dynamical matrices; it identifies no induced metric with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence} and gives no value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). This changes no ledger label; Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class: the next-to-leading symbol of the mass-supertrace term at order \(\epsilon^2\) (conditional; bounded no-go with a conditional Einstein--Hilbert-form limit).}
Keep the setting of the preceding paragraph and the term \(+M_{\mathrm{str}}\operatorname{Tr}[\chi\,(H_X-\mathrm i0)^{-1}]\), \(H_X=-\Box_X\). Take \(A^a=\epsilon\alpha^a\cos\phi\), \(\phi=q\cdot y\), \(W_a\) the Hamiltonian field of \(T^a\), \(Y=q_\mu\{y^\mu,\cdot\}\), \(\kappa_a=W_a(\phi)\) (so \(Y(\phi)=0\)) and the Gaussian window of \(\Delta_{3/4}\). Terms of fourth order in \(q\) at order \(\epsilon^2\) contain all derivatives on \(A\); they are computed with the first jet of \(T\) frozen and the window symbol constant, since window gradients and \(\partial^2T\) carry fewer powers of \(q\) (derivative counting; the window symbol being constant makes the composition terms with \(\chi\) total derivatives). In this sector \(H_X=\eta^{ab}\Pi_a\Pi_b\), \(\Pi_a=\omega_a-\epsilon\alpha_a\zeta\sin\phi\), \(\omega_a=\xi(W_a)\), \(\zeta=\xi(Y)\), \([\omega_a,\phi]=-\mathrm i\kappa_a\), with \(\zeta\) commuting with everything.

\emph{Symbol and checks.} The weight-two symbol of the resolvent was derived by the left-symbol recursion, expanded to \(\epsilon^2\) and averaged over \(\phi\) (the weight-one terms average to zero). It reproduces (a) the one-dimensional coefficient \(-\tfrac1{12}V'^2\) and the two-dimensional Maxwell coefficient \(-\tfrac1{12}F_{ab}F^{ab}\) to \(10^{-11}\); (b) the leading-symbol Hessian of the preceding paragraph; (c) in the non-degenerate case, with the vector fields divergence free, the Seeley--DeWitt coefficient \(\sqrt{|g|}(R/6-\tfrac14(\nabla\lambda)^2)\), \(\lambda=\ln\sqrt{|g|}\), at second order (Fierz--Pauli form), with relative phase \(\mathrm i\) to \(12\) digits.

\emph{Result in the class.} The integrand \(K(s)\) of the proper time \(s\) is finite at small \(s\) and has at large \(s\) the form \(c_0+c_1/s+O(s^{-2})\), checked at orbit points with random \(q,\alpha\). (i) \(c_0\) is real and is the Maxwell coefficient of the gauge field \(a_b=\epsilon\zeta\alpha_b\sin\phi\) induced by the kernel momenta: \(c_0=\lambda_0\,G(\alpha)\) with \(G=(\kappa\cdot\kappa)(\alpha\cdot\alpha)-(\kappa\cdot\alpha)^2\) (agreement to \(10^{-8}\) or better in the tensor, \(\lambda_0>0\) at all sampled points). It is gauge invariant (\(\alpha\propto\kappa\)), of the sign of \(G\), which is indefinite in Lorentzian signature, and the integral \(\int^{s_{\max}}\mathrm ds\,c_0\) diverges linearly: the first-order mass term has no finite local coefficient at this order, and the derivative expansion is expected to be non-uniform at small \(\xi\) of the non-kernel directions (a mass insertion into the massless non-kernel propagator; not computed). (ii) \(c_1\) is purely imaginary and, for the window of \(\Delta_{3/4}\) at the sampled points, is neither invariant under \(\alpha\to\alpha+\lambda\kappa\) (relative violation \(0.04\)--\(0.6\)) nor of covariant form, and has eigenvalues of both signs. (iii) Let \(V'\) be the vertical tangent space (common kernel of the \(\mathrm dy^\mu\)) and scale by \(f\) the part of the window quadratic form in \(\omega_a\) in the splitting \(\xi\mapsto(\xi(W_a),\xi(v_k))\). As \(f\to0\), \(c_1\to\mathrm i\lambda_1 A\) with \(\lambda_1=|\det\Lambda|^{-1}(4\pi)^{-3}\det(P'_{kk})^{-1/2}>0\) (\(\Lambda\) the matrix of the splitting, \(P'_{kk}\) the vertical block of the window form) and no free parameter; the difference is \(O(f)\) at four orbit points (relative \(8\cdot10^{-4}\), \(1.4\cdot10^{-3}\), \(7\cdot10^{-4}\), \(8\cdot10^{-5}\) at \(f=10^{-3}\)). Here, with the base cometric \(\bar g^{\mu\nu}=\eta^{ab}W_a(y^\mu)W_b(y^\nu)\), \(h^{\mu\nu}\) its first-order change \(-\sin\phi\,(u^\mu v^\nu+v^\mu u^\nu)\), \(u^\mu=\eta^{ab}\alpha_aW_b(y^\mu)\), \(v^\mu=\{\phi,y^\mu\}\) (so \(q\cdot v=0\)) and \(a_\mu=\bar g_{\mu\nu}u^\nu\),
\[
A=\tfrac1{24}\Bigl[v^2\bigl((q\cdot a)^2-q^2a^2\bigr)-2q^2(a\cdot v)^2\Bigr],
\]
the sum of the Einstein--Hilbert second variation, with the phase-averaged Fierz--Pauli form \(\tfrac16\,\mathrm{FP}[h]=\tfrac1{24}\bigl[v^2\bigl((q\cdot a)^2-q^2a^2\bigr)+q^2(a\cdot v)^2\bigr]\), and the dilaton-gradient term \(-\tfrac18q^2(a\cdot v)^2\) of the Liouville measure. \(A\) is invariant under \(a\to a+\lambda q\) (checked to \(10^{-16}\)), vanishes only on that direction, and is indefinite in general (definite only for \(q\) time-like and \(v\) space-like, where it is positive). (iv) The base two-form \(\theta^{\mu\nu}=\{y^\mu,y^\nu\}=M^{\mu\nu}\) satisfies \(\theta^{\rho\mu}\eta_{\mu\nu}\theta^{\sigma\nu}=c_\theta\eta^{\rho\sigma}+y^\rho y^\sigma-t^\rho t^\sigma\), \(y^\mu=M^{\mu5}\), \(t^\mu=M^{\mu4}\) (to machine precision at six points), with \(c_\theta\) a scalar function of the orbit point, so \(v^2=c_\theta q^2+(q\cdot y)^2-(q\cdot t)^2\) depends on the time-like vector \(y^\mu\) and on the fibre point. After a Haar average over the fibre at a base point with \(y^i=0\), the \(A\)-form (weighted by the Liouville density and \(\lambda_1\)) remains gauge invariant but is not proportional to \(q^2\eta_{ab}-q_aq_b\) (remainder \(0.8\)--\(1\) of its norm), and its covariant part changes by a factor \(2\) between boosts of rapidity \(0\) and \(0.8\) at fixed \(q\cdot q\). The window and the leaf both single out \(y^\mu\), which this check does not separate.

\emph{What is not shown.} The Pauli--Sakharov completion (the term \(M_{\mathrm{str}}\) multiplying the logarithm of the cutoff is accompanied by \(-\sum_iw_id_im_i\ln m_i\) when the masses are kept non-perturbatively) is expected from the Euclidean heat-kernel structure and is not derived for this hypoelliptic operator. The background flux, which cuts off large \(s\) in the full leaf, is not included, so the divergence in (i) is not resolved.

This is a statement for the Poisson limit, one loop, the term linear in the masses, the order \(\epsilon^2q^4\) with the first jet frozen, the supplied content with no bilinear fermion mass, the Gaussian window of \(\Delta_{3/4}\), and numerical sampling; the limit in (iii) is a limit of the window family and not the window of the class. It identifies no induced form with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}, makes no Wick-rotation claim, and gives no value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). This changes no ledger label; Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class: exact Landau (nilpotent) resummation of the leading symbol in the background flux, and its effect on the mass-supertrace term and on the massless resummed character term (conditional; bounded no-go).}
Keep the setting of the preceding paragraphs: Poisson limit, leaf \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\), \(T^a=M^{a4}\), \(H_X=-\Box_X\), the supplied content with no bilinear fermion mass, \(M_{\mathrm{str}}=4\mu^2+n_{\mathrm{ex}}\mu_i^2\), and the nilpotent (leading) symbol, in which the horizontal momenta \(\omega_a=\xi(W_a)\) obey \([\omega_a,\omega_b]=\mathrm iF_{ab}(k)\) with \(F_{ab}\) the constant flux at the centre variable \(k\) of the kernel plane of \(\sigma(\Box)\); \(\eta F\) has eigenvalues \(\pm f_e\) and \(\pm\mathrm if_m\). At four sampled orbit points the ratio \(f_e/f_m\) on the kernel plane equals \(\ell/|y^4|\) in every direction of \(k\) to five digits, which is the closed form of the kernel-plane flux above. Write \(a=f_e\), \(b=f_m\), \(x=sa\), \(y=sb\).

\emph{Orbital factor.} For \(H_0=\eta^{ab}\omega_a\omega_b\) the Gaussian (KMS) two-point function \(G=-\mathrm iFM^{T}(1-M^{T})^{-1}\), \(M=\mathrm e^{2sF\eta}\), gives, per unit horizontal volume, \(\Theta(s)=\operatorname{Tr}\mathrm e^{-\mathrm isH_0}=-\mathrm i\,ab\,\bigl(16\pi^2\sinh x\,\sin y\bigr)^{-1}\), with \(s\to s-\mathrm i0\) (the poles of \(1/\sin y\) lie above the contour, as for the Landau-level sum \(\tfrac{b}{2\pi}\sum_n\mathrm e^{-\mathrm isb(2n+1)}\)). The identity \(\mathrm d\ln\Theta/\mathrm ds=-\mathrm i\eta^{ab}G_{ab}\) holds to \(10^{-10}\) at complex \(s\), and \(\Theta\to-\mathrm i/(16\pi^2s^2)\) for \(s\to0\). The electric pair gives \(1/\sinh\) and the magnetic pair \(1/\sin\).

\emph{Mass-supertrace term.} The term \(+M_{\mathrm{str}}\operatorname{Tr}[(H_X-\mathrm i0)^{-1}]=M_{\mathrm{str}}\,\mathrm i\!\int_0^\infty\!\mathrm ds\,\operatorname{Tr}\mathrm e^{-\mathrm isH_X}\) becomes, per unit horizontal volume and per centre point, \(M_{\mathrm{str}}(16\pi^2)^{-1}(s_0^{-1}+g)\) with
\[
g(a,b)=\int_0^\infty\!\mathrm ds\Bigl[\frac{ab}{\sinh(as)\,\sin(bs-\mathrm i0)}-\frac1{s^2}\Bigr]=\pi\bigl[\,b\,S(b/a)+\mathrm i\,a\,S(a/b)\bigr],\qquad S(r)=\sum_{n\ge1}\frac{(-1)^n}{\sinh(n\pi r)}<0 .
\]
The integrand is analytic in the open fourth quadrant and bounded by \(\exp[-|s|(a\cos\vartheta+b\sin\vartheta)]\) on the ray \(s=|s|\mathrm e^{-\mathrm i\vartheta}\), so \(g\) is finite for \(a,b>0\) and independent of \(\vartheta\); the closed form follows from the two residue sums (poles of \(1/\sinh\) at \(s=-\mathrm in\pi/a\), poles of \(1/\sin\) at \(s=n\pi/b\)) and was confirmed against the ray integral to \(10^{-24}\) at ten pairs \((a,b)\). \(g\) is homogeneous of degree one, \(\operatorname{Re}g<0\) and \(\operatorname{Im}g<0\) for all \(a,b>0\), \(\operatorname{Re}g\to-a\ln2\) for \(b\ll a\) and \(\operatorname{Im}g\to-b\ln2\) for \(a\ll b\). Thus the linear divergence in \(s\) of the Maxwell tail \(c_0\) of the preceding paragraph is cut off at \(s\sim1/f\), and the flux supplies an exponentially convergent infrared end of the proper-time integral.

\emph{Completeness of the \(M_{\mathrm{str}}\) term.} With the flux kept to all orders, the mass-linear term is not \(M_{\mathrm{str}}\operatorname{Tr}[(H_X-\mathrm i0)^{-1}]\) alone: the vector mass multiplies the Zeeman-dressed resolvent, and the complete term is \(\mathrm i\!\int\!\mathrm ds\,\Theta(s)\bigl[M_{\mathrm{str}}+\mu^2(\chi_V-4)\bigr]\), \(\chi_V=2\cosh2x+2\cos2y\). The second part is the all-order version of the constant \(\sum_iw_im_i\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_i\Sigma_i\) listed above and has the growth of the next paragraph.

\emph{Massless resummed character term.} With \(\chi_{\mathrm{str}}=4(\cos y-\cosh x)^2\) (the species sum \(2\cosh2x+2\cos2y+6-2-8\cosh x\cos y\), identity checked to \(10^{-40}\)) the resummed integrand is \(\Theta\chi_{\mathrm{str}}/s\). On the ray of angle \(\vartheta\) one has \(\bigl|\Theta\chi_{\mathrm{str}}\bigr|\sim\exp\bigl[|s|\,|a\cos\vartheta-b\sin\vartheta|\bigr]\) (measured rates agree to \(4\cdot10^{-3}\) at four angles away from \(\vartheta_*\)), and \(\Theta\chi_{\mathrm{str}}\to(ab/\pi^2)\sinh^2[s(a-\mathrm ib)/2]\) at large \(|s|\) (ratio \(1\) to \(10^{-8}\)). The exponential growth therefore occurs on the real axis (the \(\mathrm i\epsilon\) contour) for every \(a>0\), the same exponent holds for \(\Theta(\chi_V-4)\), and the massless exponent vanishes only on the single ray \(\vartheta_*=\arctan(a/b)\), which depends on the flux; on the leaf \(\vartheta_*=\arctan(\ell/|y^4|)\). The exponent is unchanged by the phases \(\mathrm e^{-\mathrm ism_i}\) of real masses on the real axis; on a ray of angle \(\vartheta\) a mass \(m\) shifts it by \(-m\sin\vartheta\), so for the vector with \(\mu^2=-3\) the rate is \(|a\cos\vartheta-b\sin\vartheta|+3\sin\vartheta>0\) on every ray, whereas a positive mass-squared would open a window of convergent rays about \(\vartheta_*\) for the massless term treated here. On that ray the integrand is bounded, but it tends to the constant \(-ab/(2\pi^2)\) plus oscillations, so \(\int\mathrm ds/s\) grows logarithmically in the real part (numerically \(-0.032\) per doubling of the upper limit at \(a=0.7\), \(b=1.3\); the imaginary part converges). For the massless term no ray of the closed fourth quadrant gives a convergent integral (rays of the first quadrant correspond to the opposite \(\mathrm i\epsilon\) prescription and are neither claimed nor excluded), and with \(\mu^2=-3\) the same holds for the mass-dressed term, so the massless resummed character term has no finite value from the proper-time representation of the class; its non-analytic dependence on \(f_e\) is that of a spectral sum over the complex eigenvalues \(\lambda\pm2\mathrm if_e\) of the vector sector and is not computed.

\emph{Dependence on \(A\).} The leading nilpotent coefficient of the \(M_{\mathrm{str}}\) term depends on \(A\) only through the centre-valued flux, so its second variation is \(\tfrac12D^2g\,[Q,Q]\) with \(Q_{ab}=\zeta_Y(k)(\kappa_a\alpha_b-\kappa_b\alpha_a)\); it contains no second derivative of the fluctuation and no term of Einstein--Hilbert form; this concerns the \(M_{\mathrm{str}}\) term only, since the Zeeman-dressed part has no contour. It rests on the frozen nilpotent frame in which \(Y\) is central, and on the leaf \([W_a,Y]\ne0\) at order \(q\) by the Jacobi identity; the same omission is made in the preceding frozen-frame paragraph. At four orbit points, averaging over the direction of \(k\) with the window of \(\Delta_{3/4}\), this form is gauge invariant (\(\alpha\propto\kappa\) in its kernel) but is not proportional to \((\kappa\cdot\kappa)\eta^{ab}-\kappa^a\kappa^b\) (relative residual \(0.4\)--\(1.0\) in real and imaginary parts at the points with \(0.3\le f_e/f_m\le0.8\)), and its eigenvalues on the quotient by \(\kappa\) have both signs. The infrared-finite replacement of the Maxwell tail of the \(M_{\mathrm{str}}\) term is therefore not of the covariant Maxwell form and is not sign-definite (leaf average, four points).

\emph{Einstein--Hilbert sector in a four-dimensional Landau model.} Let \(\Omega_a=\omega_a+\epsilon\sin\phi\,(m_a{}^c\omega_c+n_a)\), \(\phi=\kappa\cdot x\), \(m_a{}^c\kappa_c=0\), on the algebra above with \(F\) constant; the choice \(m_a{}^c=-\alpha_ay^c\), \(n_a=-\alpha_a c\) reproduces the sector of the preceding paragraph with the commutators \([\omega_a,\omega_b]=\mathrm iF_{ab}\) kept, but without the commutator of \(Y\) with \(W_a\) that the Jacobi identity fixes on the leaf, so it is a model and not the leaf (an omission shared with the frozen-frame paragraph above). The second order in \(\epsilon\) of \(\operatorname{Tr}\mathrm e^{-\mathrm isH}\) was computed exactly by Wick's theorem in the state \(\mathrm e^{-\mathrm isH_0}\). It reproduces the Maxwell coefficient at \(F=0\) (ratio \(1\) to \(10^{-10}\)), the form \(A\) of the preceding paragraph at \(F=0\) (\(c_1/A=\mathrm i\) to \(10^{-8}\) in five random draws, including the dilaton term), gauge invariance in \(n\) at \(F\ne0\) (\(2\cdot10^{-15}\)), and the constant-field limits (Hessian of \(\Theta\) in \(F\), and \(|\det M|^{-1}\Theta(MFM^T)\) for a constant vielbein). At \(F\ne0\) the coefficient \(c=A/16\pi^2\) of \(\mathrm ds/s\) is unchanged, the integrand decays like \(\mathrm e^{-|s|(a\cos\vartheta+b\sin\vartheta)}\), and the finite part \(\mathrm{Fin}=\lim[\int_{s_0}^\infty T_2\,\mathrm ds-c\ln(1/s_0)]\), in units of \(c\), is complex; its real part is about \(1.05\), \(1.58\), \(0.27\) in three Lorentz frames of \(F\) at \((a,b)=(0.7,1.3)\) (data \(\kappa,\alpha,y\) rotated, \(A\) fixed; imaginary parts between \(-1.52\) and \(-1.00\)), and \(0.31\), \(-1.40\), \(0.03\) in three frames at \((a,b)=(1.92,1.0)\) (imaginary parts between \(-0.15\) and \(0.08\)). The flux therefore turns the infrared end of \(\int\mathrm ds/s\) into a finite remainder that depends on the orientation of \(F\) relative to \(\kappa,\alpha,y\): at fixed \((a,b)\) and fixed \(A\) (the meaningful comparison, since \(\mathrm{Fin}/c\) shifts by a constant under a rescaling of \(s\)) the real part varies between frames by an amount of the order of, or larger than, the magnitude of its mean, and the Maxwell part of the same sector changes between frames by about \(30\)--\(45\%\) at \((a,b)=(0.7,1.3)\) and by a factor of order one at \((1.92,1.0)\) (complex values). The coefficient of the logarithm is the flux-independent \(A\) of the preceding paragraph.

This is a statement for the Poisson limit, one loop, the nilpotent (leading) symbol resummed in a constant flux at the centre variable, the Lorentzian \(\mathrm i\epsilon\) proper time, the supplied content, and numerical evaluation at sampled points; the closed form of \(g\) is checked numerically, not proved, and the Landau model for the Einstein--Hilbert sector is not the leaf. It does not include the next-to-leading symbol of the hypoelliptic operator with the commutators of \(Y\), the dependence of the window on the flux, non-perturbative masses, or the spectral definition of the vector sector, and it identifies no induced form with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}, makes no Wick-rotation claim, does not consider the first-quadrant rays, and gives no value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). This changes no ledger label; Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class: the Jacobi commutator of the plane-wave field with the horizontal fields, and the flux-resummed next-to-leading symbol (conditional; bounded no-go; supersession of the preceding Landau model).}
Keep the setting of the preceding paragraphs: Poisson limit, leaf \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\), \(T^a=M^{a4}\), \(W_a\) the Hamiltonian field of \(T^a\), \(Y=q_\mu\{y^\mu,\cdot\}\), \(\phi=q\cdot y\), \(\kappa_a=W_a(\phi)\), \(A^a=\epsilon\alpha^a\cos\phi\), the nilpotent (leading) symbol with centre flux \([\omega_a,\omega_b]=\mathrm iF_{ab}\), \(\omega_a=-\mathrm iW_a\), and the term \(+M_{\mathrm{str}}\operatorname{Tr}[(H_X-\mathrm i0)^{-1}]\). The Lie--Poisson relations give exactly \(\kappa^a=\sigma M^{45}q^a\) and \([W_a,Y]=\sigma q_a\operatorname{Ham}(y^4)\), \(y^4=M^{45}\), with \(\sigma=\pm1\) the sign convention of the bracket (the second is the Jacobi identity; both checked at random orbit points to \(10^{-14}\)). In the frame \((W_a,Z_k)\), \(Z_k\) a basis of the vertical space, \(Y=y^cW_c+y^kZ_k\) and the field \(V=\operatorname{Ham}(y^4)\) has no vertical component (below \(10^{-14}\) at six sampled points), so \([W_a,Y]\) has no component along the centre. The flux term \(y^cF_{ac}\) is not small and is not parallel to \(\kappa_a\) (the residual of the parallel fit is \(0.7\)--\(1.0\) at the sampled points and directions of the centre variable).

\emph{Consequence for the model of the preceding paragraph.} There the plane-wave coupling was \(\zeta_Y=y^c\omega_c+c\) with a constant charge \(c\), for which \([\omega_a,\zeta_Y]=\mathrm iy^cF_{ac}\ne0\); this commutator is inconsistent with the Jacobi identity at the nilpotent order with frozen coefficients. That model is not invariant under \(\alpha\to\alpha+\lambda\kappa\), the leaf form of \(\delta A^a=\{\Lambda,T^a\}\), at \(F\ne0\) and \(y\ne0\) (relative change \(0.07\)--\(0.16\) of the second-order response in a test, \(10^{-14}\) at \(F=0\)), whereas the Fin values quoted there are values of that model. On the leaf \(Y\) commutes with \(\omega_a\) and with \(\phi\) (\(Y(\phi)=0\)); at the nilpotent order it is a right-invariant field of the nilpotent group, and its diagonal value at the base point is \(P=y^c\omega_c+c\), \(c=y^k\zeta_k\), which stands at the seam of the trace, \(\operatorname{Tr}[\text{chain}\cdot P^2]\), the chain being the ordered product of the left-invariant factors. Because \(P^2\) separates the first and the last propagator, the time-translation reduction \(\int_0^1\mathrm du\,(1-u)\langle V(su)V(0)\rangle\) is not available and the second order is the double Duhamel integral \(-s^2\int_{0<u_2<u_1<1}\langle V(su_1)V(su_2)P^2\rangle\).

\emph{Checks.} In a two-dimensional Euclidean Landau problem in Landau gauge, where the magnetic translation \(K=y_2p_2\) is an exact c-number, this prescription reproduces the second-order density obtained by exact diagonalisation (relative difference \(2\cdot10^{-4}\), real); the prescription with \(P^2\) at the end of a merged propagator does not (complex, \(1.5\%\) off in the real part). The two-dimensional test validates the prescription for a central \(K\); the identification of the leaf field \(Y\) with the seam insertion \(P\) is conditional on the nilpotent order and the frozen first jet. In four dimensions the central model is invariant under \(\alpha\to\alpha+\lambda\kappa\) to \(10^{-14}\) at \(F\ne0\), \(y\ne0\), and equals the model of the preceding paragraph at \(F\to0\) (\(10^{-8}\)). On the leaf, with \(F_{ab}(\zeta)=-S_{ab}{}^k\zeta_k\), \(\zeta\) the centre variable, \(f_e/f_m=\ell/|y^4|\) at three sampled directions of \(\zeta\) at each of five orbit points, the quadratic form of the Fierz--Pauli plus dilaton coefficient \(A\) of the next-to-leading-symbol paragraph equals the closed form in \((\alpha,\kappa,y^c)\) used there (ratio \(1\) to six digits, five orbit points).

\emph{Result in the class.} (i) The coefficient of \(\mathrm ds/s\) is unchanged: \(c=A/16\pi^2\), with \(T_2(s)\,s/c\to1.0000\) as \(s\to0\) at the flux of the leaf, in both the central and the preceding model. The Jacobi commutator therefore does not change, cancel or add to the Einstein--Hilbert-form coefficient \(A\) of the logarithm. (ii) At \(F\ne0\) the integrand decays as \(\mathrm e^{-|s|(f_e\cos\vartheta+f_m\sin\vartheta)}\) on \(s=|s|\mathrm e^{-\mathrm i\vartheta}\), and the finite part \(\mathrm{Fin}=\lim[\int_{s_0}^\infty T_2\,\mathrm ds-c\ln(1/s_0)]\) is a finite complex number for each centre variable. Split by the content of \(P\) into \(y^cy^d\) (tetrad), \(c^2\) (charge) and cross terms, the three parts are separately invariant under \(\alpha\to\alpha+\lambda\kappa\) (\(10^{-7}\) or better). (iii) The tetrad part of Fin, as a quadratic form in \(\alpha\), is not proportional to the form \(A\): the residual of the best proportional fit, in real and imaginary parts, is \(0.63\) and \(0.82\) (centre direction \(0.8\)), \(0.49\) and \(0.99\) (direction \(2.0\)) at one orbit point, and \(0.011\) and \(0.94\) at a second point where \(f_e/f_m\simeq42\); the quotient by \(\kappa\) has eigenvalues of both signs in every case (as has \(A\)). (iv) The form \(A\) is not Lorentz covariant, as shown for the preceding paragraph (it depends on \(y^\mu\) and on the fibre point), and the flux does not alter the coefficient that carries this dependence. In the sampled per-centre-variable forms the tetrad part of the finite remainder is neither proportional to the form \(A\) nor sign-definite; the sign and covariance of the full finite remainder (tetrad, charge and cross parts) and of its window average over the centre variable were not tested, so no finite, covariant, sign-definite Einstein--Hilbert coefficient is obtained here.

\emph{Size of the dropped terms.} The horizontal part of \([W_a,Y]\) (the weight-one term \(-q^av^cW_c\)), the horizontal parts of \([W_a,W_b]\), the gradients of the flux and of \(y^c,y^k\), and the window are not included. The ratio of the norm of the horizontal part of \([W_a,Y]\) to that of \(y^cS_{ac}{}^k\) is \(0.01\)--\(0.5\) at six orbit points (per unit centre variable), so these terms are small only for \(|\zeta|\) large compared with that ratio and for small \(\omega\); this is the regime of the nilpotent approximation, not a bound on the leaf.

This is a statement for the Poisson limit, one loop, the nilpotent (leading) symbol resummed in the flux with the Jacobi-consistent central plane-wave field, the Lorentzian \(\mathrm i\epsilon\) proper time, the \(M_{\mathrm{str}}\) term, and numerical evaluation at sampled orbit points and centre directions. It does not include the vector-mass term with the Zeeman-dressed resolvent at order \(\epsilon^2\) (whose leading growth was found above and is not shown to be altered), the weight-one terms, the window average over the centre variable, the sign and covariance of the full finite remainder, the spectral definition of the vector sector, or non-perturbative masses, and it identifies no induced form with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}, makes no Wick-rotation claim, and gives no value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). This changes no ledger label; Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class: the Zeeman-dressed vector sector at order \(\epsilon^2\), its logarithmic coefficient, the spin blocks and the continuation (conditional; bounded no-go; correction of the sign of a logarithmic term in the finite-part values of two preceding paragraphs).}
Keep the setting of the preceding paragraphs: Poisson limit, leaf \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\), the nilpotent (leading) symbol with centre flux \([\omega_a,\omega_b]=\mathrm iF_{ab}\), the central plane-wave field with \(P=y^c\omega_c+c\) at the seam, \(A^a=\epsilon\alpha^a\cos\phi\), \(\kappa_a=W_a(\phi)\), and the species data of the paragraph on the supertrace at an arbitrary matrix fluctuation. With \(\Omega_a=\omega_a-\epsilon\alpha_aP\sin\phi\) one has \([\Omega_a,\Omega_b]=\mathrm iF_{ab}+\mathrm i\epsilon P\cos\phi\,K_{ab}\), \(K_{ab}=\kappa_a\alpha_b-\kappa_b\alpha_a\), and the vector operator is \(\eta^{ab}\Omega_a\Omega_b\otimes1+2[\Omega,\Omega]\eta+\mu^2\) (the coefficient \(-2\mathcal Z_{ab}\) is the one for which the longitudinal modes \(D_a\lambda\) are mapped to \(D_a\) of the scalar operator, with \(\mathcal Z_{ab}=-[\Omega_a,\Omega_b]\)). At \(\epsilon=0\) the spin factor \(\mathrm e^{-\mathrm is Z}=\mathrm e^{2sF\eta}\) has the four blocks \(\mathrm e^{\pm2sf_e}\) and \(\mathrm e^{\pm2\mathrm isf_m}\) (trace \(\chi_V\) of the preceding paragraphs). The order \(\epsilon^2\) of \(\operatorname{Tr}\mathrm e^{-\mathrm is(H+Z)}\) was computed by the double Duhamel integral of the preceding paragraph with three kinds of insertion: the kinetic one, the Zeeman one \(\epsilon P\cos\phi\,N\), \(N=2\mathrm iK\eta\) for the vector and \(\tfrac{\mathrm i}{4}[\Gamma^a,\Gamma^b]K_{ab}\) for the Dirac block, and their products, with the spin factors \(\operatorname{tr}[\mathrm e^{2sF\eta}N]\) and \(\operatorname{tr}[\mathrm e^{(s-\tau)K_F}N\mathrm e^{\tau K_F}N]\), \(K_F=2F\eta\). For a scalar species the result equals the preceding engine (\(10^{-13}\)); the response of every species is invariant under \(\alpha\to\alpha+\lambda\kappa\) (\(10^{-14}\) at \(s=0.4\), \(10^{-8}\) at complex \(s\), limited by quadrature).

\emph{Logarithmic coefficient.} Write \(16\pi^2c=\lim_{s\to0}16\pi^2\,s\,T_2(s)\), \(F_1=y^2\bigl((\kappa\cdot\alpha)^2-\kappa^2\alpha^2\bigr)\), \(F_2=\kappa^2(\alpha\cdot y)^2\), so that the form \(A\) of the next-to-leading-symbol paragraph is \(A=(F_1-2F_2)/24\). Fitting the ten independent components of the quadratic form at one orbit point gives, for the four-dimensional vector with the Zeeman dressing, \(16\pi^2c_V=4A-F_1\) (coefficients \(g_1=-0.8337\), \(g_2=-0.3333\) against \(-5/6\), \(-1/3\)), and for one \(4\times4\) Dirac block \(16\pi^2c_D=4A-\tfrac12F_1\) (\(-0.33346\), \(-0.33333\) against \(-1/3\), \(-1/3\)); the scalar value is \(A\). The extra term \(-F_1\) (\(-\tfrac12F_1\)) is the term quadratic in \(N\), proportional to \(\operatorname{tr}\Sigma\Sigma\), with \(\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_D\Sigma_D=\tfrac12\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_V\Sigma_V\). The weighted sum over the supertrace content (\(\sum_iw_ic_i\) with the weights of the preceding paragraph) vanishes to \(10^{-5}\) at the three sampled points, so the massless resummed character term has no ultraviolet logarithm at this order. The mass-linear term has the logarithmic form \(M_{\mathrm{str}}A-\mu^2F_1\): for \(\mu^2\ne0\) it is not proportional to \(A\), and since both \(F_1\) and \(F_2\) contain \(y^\mu\) through \(y^2\) and \(\alpha\cdot y\), the dependence on \(y^\mu\) shown in the next-to-leading-symbol paragraph is not removed by the Zeeman term.

\emph{Spin blocks and the continuation.} Decomposing the vector response in the eigenbasis \(E_\pm\), \(B_\pm\) of \(K_F\) (diagonal blocks and pair blocks with the interpolating phase \(\mathrm e^{(s-\tau)\mu_k+\tau\mu_l}\)), at one orbit point the diagonal and pair blocks containing \(E_+\) grow along the rays \(s=|s|\mathrm e^{-\mathrm i\vartheta}\) with \(\vartheta<\vartheta_*=\arctan(f_e/f_m)\) and those containing \(B_+\) grow for \(\vartheta>\vartheta_*\); at \(\vartheta_*\) every block grows polynomially. No ray of the closed fourth quadrant gives a convergent proper-time integral of the Zeeman-dressed term. A continuation is therefore a hypothesis. Two prescriptions were compared on the leading-symbol integral \(\int\mathrm ds\,f_ef_m[\sinh(f_es)\sin(f_ms)]^{-1}(\chi_V-4)\) at \((f_e,f_m)=(1.6,0.9)\): the groups \(\{E_\pm\}\) and \(\{B_\pm\}\) integrated on their own rays (\(-4.007-9.981\mathrm i\) and \(-4.015-9.973\mathrm i\) for two pairs of rays), and the integral on \(\vartheta_*\) with the factor \(\mathrm e^{-\mathrm ism^2}\), \(m^2>0\), extrapolated to \(m^2\to0^+\) (\(-4.007\) to \(-4.013\), \(-9.984\) to \(-9.992\mathrm i\)); they agree to the stated accuracy. The imaginary part is the unstable branch (a Nielsen--Olesen-type mode) and is not removed.

\emph{Finite part at one orbit point.} For the tetrad part (\(c=0\)) at one orbit point and one centre direction (flux rescaled to \(f_e=1.004\), \(f_m=0.396\)), the finite part \(\mathrm{Fin}=\lim[\int_{s_0}^\infty T_2\,\mathrm ds-c\ln(1/s_0)]\) of the vector term, the scalar term and the massless supertrace were evaluated on \(\vartheta_*\) with the factor \(\mathrm e^{-\mathrm ism^2}\) for \(m^2=0.4,\dots,1.3\) in units of \(\max(f_e,f_m)\) (quadrature checked against the number of nodes; tail truncation \(<10^{-4}\)); the extrapolation \(m^2\to0\) is not stable (entries change by order one between orders), so no value at \(m^2=0\) is claimed. In this regulated family: (i) all three forms are gauge invariant (\(|Q\kappa|/|Q||\kappa|\le6\cdot10^{-6}\)); (ii) the vector form is not proportional to its logarithmic form \(4A-F_1\) (relative defect \(0.91\)--\(0.98\)) nor to \(A\) (\(0.79\)--\(0.82\)), and the massless supertrace form is not proportional to \(A\) (\(0.87\)--\(1.0\)), a statement independent of the logarithm scheme for the supertrace, which has no logarithm; (iii) for the real and imaginary parts of the forms \(\mathrm{Fin}\) of \(T_2\), on the quotient by \(\kappa\), the real part of the vector form is positive definite for every \(m^2\) tried, its imaginary part is positive definite for \(m^2\le0.6\) and indefinite for \(m^2\ge0.8\), the scalar form is indefinite in the real part, and the real part of \(x\,Q_S-3Q_V\), \(x=n_{\mathrm{ex}}\mu_i^2\), is definite on the quotient for every \(x\) in an interval containing \([0,21]\) at every \(m^2\) tried; (iv) the sign statements for the vector and the massless forms change with \(m^2\) and with the real or imaginary part, and a sign statement for a quotient spectrum is not invariant under the finite shift \(c\ln\lambda\) of the scheme for forms that carry a logarithm. The result is for one orbit point.

\emph{Correction to the finite-part values of two preceding paragraphs.} The routine that evaluated \(\mathrm{Fin}\) in the Landau-model and the Jacobi-commutator paragraphs added the term \(-c\ln s_1\), \(s_1=0.05\,\mathrm e^{-0.6\mathrm i}/\max(a,b)\) the end of the small-\(s\) interval, whereas the definition of \(\mathrm{Fin}\) gives \(+c\ln s_1\) (a test function \(c\,\mathrm e^{-s}/s\) returns \(5.41+1.20\mathrm i\) instead of \(-\gamma\) for \(c=1\)). The values differ by \(2c\ln s_1\): in the Landau model \(\mathrm{Fin}/c\) is shifted by the constant \(2\ln s_1\) at fixed \((a,b)\), and in the leaf the tetrad form is shifted by a multiple of the form \(A\); the values quoted in those paragraphs are the corrected ones. Proportionality to \(A\) is unaffected by a multiple of \(A\), and the defect \(\|Q-\lambda A\|/\|A\|\), with \(\lambda\) the best fit, is shift invariant: \(0.19\) and \(0.075\) (real, imaginary parts of the tetrad form; first sample), \(4.5\cdot10^{-3}\) and \(4.4\cdot10^{-3}\) (second sample) and \(2.8\cdot10^{-4}\) and \(8\cdot10^{-8}\) (strongly electric sample, \(f_e/f_m\simeq42\), where the corrected imaginary part vanishes to the accuracy of the quadrature). A normalised residual \(\|Q-\lambda A\|/\|Q\|\), and the signs of a quotient spectrum, are not shift invariant for a form that carries a logarithm, because \(A\) is indefinite; the quotient spectra of the real and imaginary parts of the tetrad form remain of both signs.

This is a statement for the Poisson limit, one loop, the nilpotent (leading) symbol resummed in the flux with the central seam field, the Lorentzian \(\mathrm i\epsilon\) proper time, the tetrad part at one orbit point and one centre direction, a regulator \(\mathrm e^{-\mathrm ism^2}\) not removed, the signs of the Zeeman couplings fixed by the longitudinal-mode identity above, and numerical evaluation. It does not include the charge and cross parts, the window average over the centre variable, the weight-one terms, the continuation \(m^2\to0\) of the four-dimensional integral, the rays of the first quadrant, the sign convention of the factor \(\mathrm i\) of the mass-linear term, or a derivation of the continuation prescription, and it identifies no induced form with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}, makes no Wick-rotation claim, and gives no value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). This changes no ledger label; Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class: the Casimir form of the Zeeman-dressed vector operator and the base-point dependence (conditional; bounded no-go).}
Keep the Poisson limit, the leaf \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\) of \(\mathfrak{so}(4,2)\), \(T^a=M^{a4}\), \(D^a=\{T^a,\cdot\}\), \(J^{AB}=\{M^{AB},\cdot\}\), \(F_{ab}=\{T_a,T_b\}\), \(\Box=\eta_{ab}D^aD^b\) and the vector operator \(H_V^{0}\varepsilon_a=-\Box\varepsilon_a-2\{F_{ab},\varepsilon^b\}\) without the mass term, \(H_V=H_V^{0}+\mu^2\) being the operator of the paragraph on the \(\Theta\)-built heat kernel. Let \(C_{41}=\tfrac12J^{AB}J_{AB}\) and \(C_{31}=\tfrac12J^{ab}J_{ab}\) (indices \(\le4\) and \(\le3\)) act on functions, let \(R^{ab}\) be the generators of the vector representation of \(\mathfrak{so}(3,1)\) with the sign for which \(J^{ab}+R^{ab}\) commutes with \(H_V^{0}\), and \(C_{31}^{\mathrm{tot}}\) the Casimir of \(J^{ab}+R^{ab}\). The exact relation of the paragraph on the Casimir of \(\mathfrak{so}(4,2)\), \(H_V=C_G-C_{41}-C_\Sigma+\mu^2\), with \(C_G=C_{31}^{\mathrm{tot}}\) and \(C_\Sigma=C_V=-3\), reads \(H_V^{0}=-C_{41}+C_{31}^{\mathrm{tot}}+3\) (re-checked on random polynomial fields; the other sign of \(R^{ab}\) and the opposite sign of the Zeeman term give no such identity). In this class the vector operator is therefore a Casimir operator of the total Lorentz spin: a bundle connection covariant under \(J^{ab}+R^{ab}\) is present, and the dependence of \(A\), \(F_1\) and \(F_2\) of the paragraph on the Zeeman-dressed vector sector on \(y^\mu\) is not the absence of a connection, but the dependence of the induced form on the base point. From the displayed definitions, \(F_1=y^2\bigl((\kappa\cdot\alpha)^2-\kappa^2\alpha^2\bigr)\) depends on the base point only through the scalar \(y^\mu y_\mu=-\ell^2-(y^4)^2\), and \(F_2=\kappa^2(\alpha\cdot y)^2\) is the term that contains \(y^\mu\) as a vector. Since \(y\cdot y=-\ell^2\) in the five-dimensional contraction, \((y^0)^2=\ell^2+y^iy^i+(y^4)^2\ge\ell^2\): no point of the leaf is fixed by the matrix Lorentz group, so a local density cannot be evaluated at a Lorentz-symmetric base point. For the compact form \(\mathfrak{so}(6)\) with \(\delta\) the points \(y^\mu=0\), \(\lvert y^4\rvert=\ell\) exist, and they are the points where the preceding paragraphs find \(f_1=f_2\) in that case.

This is a statement for the Poisson limit, the Lie--Poisson algebra \(\mathfrak{so}(4,2)\), the operator without the mass term and with the background gauge function, and the algebraic identity on polynomials; the sign of \(R^{ab}\) is fixed by invariance, not by an independent convention. It does not compute a finite coefficient, does not treat fermions, the window or the Zeeman-dressed resolvent at order \(\epsilon^2\), and gives no value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). This changes no ledger label; Problem~F remains open.

\paragraph{Product backgrounds \(M\times K\) at the maximally supersymmetric content: factorisation of the one-loop trace and the index part of the induced Einstein coefficient (conditional; not closure).}
Work in the Poisson limit with the content of~\cite{IKKT1997}, supplied as an input since the displayed action is bosonic: the ten-component vector with weight \(1\), the ghosts with weight \(-2\) and one sixteen-component Weyl spinor with weight \(-\tfrac12\), with operators \(H_0+\Sigma^{AB}\mathbb F_{AB}\), where \(H_0\) is the common scalar operator, \(\Sigma^{AB}\) are the generators of the vector or spinor representation and \(\mathbb F_{AB}\) is the action of the flux \(\{X_A,X_B\}\). Take a product background: the indices split into a block \(M\) of dimension \(d\) and a block \(K\) of dimension \(10-d\), \(\{X^a,X^i\}=0\) for \(a\) in \(M\) and \(i\) in \(K\), the fluctuations are functions on \(M\) times matrices of a fuzzy space \(K\), and the operators of the two factors commute.

\emph{Factorisation.} On one factor write \(s_0\) for the trace of \(e^{-sH_0}\), \(v\) for the trace over the vector representation of the block minus its dimension times \(s_0\) (the index part), and, for \(d=4\), \(\sigma_\pm\) and \(\tau_\pm\) for the index parts of the chiral spinors of \(\mathfrak{so}(4)\) and \(\mathfrak{so}(6)\), \(\sigma=\sigma_++\sigma_-\), \(\tau=\tau_++\tau_-\). Then, exactly,
\[
  \operatorname{str}e^{-sH}=s_0^K\bigl(v^M-2\sigma^M\bigr)+s_0^M\bigl(v^K-\tau^K\bigr)-\tfrac12\bigl(\sigma_+^M\tau_+^K+\sigma_-^M\tau_-^K\bigr),
\]
and for \(d=7\), with the spinor blocks \(8\) of \(\mathfrak{so}(7)\) and \(2\) of \(\mathfrak{so}(3)\), \(s_0^K(v^M-\sigma_8^M)+s_0^M(v^K-4\tau_2^K)-\tfrac12\sigma_8^M\tau_2^K\) (checked with random non-commuting finite-dimensional operators, relative residual below \(10^{-12}\)). The index parts of the vector and of the spinors agree at second order in the flux, so the first two terms start at fourth order in the flux of their block, and the mixed term starts at second order in each, where it equals \(-\tfrac18v_2^Mv_2^K\) with \(v_2\) the second-order index part of the vector (Fourier extraction of the coefficients in both splits). The vector representation does not enter the mixed term, since \(\Sigma^{ab}\Sigma^{ij}=0\) on it. For \(d=4\) the mixed term is \(-\tfrac14\sigma^M\tau^K-\tfrac14(\sigma_+^M-\sigma_-^M)(\tau_+^K-\tau_-^K)\); the second part is odd under the orientation of each block, starts at third order in the flux of \(K\) and is not treated, and \(\tau^K=v_2^K\) at second order. The mixed term corresponds to the term \(2F^{\mu\nu}F_{\mu\nu}F^{ij}F_{ij}\) of Section~6.2 of~\cite{Steinacker2023}. This covers, for product backgrounds, transverse matrices with flux, which the paragraph on the supertrace at an arbitrary matrix fluctuation does not treat.

\emph{Two-derivative part for four coordinates on \(M\).} Let \(M\) be the four-coordinate class of the Presentation~A paragraphs above, \(d=4\), Euclidean section. There \(s_0^M\) has the density \(\tfrac16\rho R[g_\rho]\), and the second-order index parts of the vector and of a Dirac field have the densities \(\rho^2X\) and \(\tfrac12\rho^2X\) (paragraph on fermionic content in Section~\ref{sec:status-B}). The first term has no two-derivative part, and the part of the induced action with two derivatives on \(M\) that depends on \(K\) is, apart from the orientation-odd remainder,
\[
  -\frac1{32\pi^2}\int\Bigl(\tfrac16W_R\,\rho R[g_\rho]+W_X\,\rho^2X\Bigr),\qquad
  W_R=\int_0^\infty\frac{\mathrm ds}{s^2}\bigl(v^K-\tau^K\bigr)(s),\qquad
  W_X=-\frac18\int_0^\infty\frac{\mathrm ds}{s^2}\,\tau^K(s),
\]
both finite without a cutoff and set by the scale of \(K\) when the vector operator of \(K\) has no negative mode (the spinor operator is a square); at second order in the flux of \(K\), \(\tau^K=v_2^K\). The spinor generators are traceless, so \(\tau^K\ge0\) by the Peierls--Bogoliubov inequality (checked at random data), and \(W_X<0\) for a nonzero flux of \(K\); \(W_R\) has no fixed sign. On the two modes with \(\delta\rho=0\) about constant \(\bar\Theta\) one has \(V_\mu=0\), \(\mathcal T=X/4\) and \(\tfrac32S_{\lambda\mu\nu}S^{\lambda\mu\nu}=X/2\) identically in the polarisation (exact rational arithmetic, four backgrounds), so \(\rho^2X\) acts as \(-4\rho R[g_\rho]\), of which the term \(-2\rho R[g_\rho]\) of the presentation \(r=-2\) of the paragraph on local frame rotations is one half and the axial torsion the other; the axial part of the torsion is also nonzero as a tensor on the on-shell null mode with \(\delta\rho=0\) of the Lorentzian section, although its quadratic invariants vanish on a single null wave. In the units \(c\) of the paragraph on the order \(\Lambda^2\) in Section~\ref{sec:status-B}, with \(\Lambda_{\mathrm{UV}}^2\) replaced by these integrals (\(1/G=c/2\pi\)), the tensor coefficient is \(c_T=\tfrac16W_R-4W_X\); the mixed part \(-4W_X>0\) has the sign of a scalar component. In the presentation \(r=-2\) the coefficient of \(\rho R[g_\rho]\) is \(\tfrac16W_R-2W_X\). The coefficient that~\cite{Steinacker2023} identifies with the inverse Newton constant corresponds structurally to its part \(-2W_X\) (the same mixed term; the normalisations and the sign convention are not compared, and there the axial torsion is the axion); the contribution of \(K\) alone is evaluated in Section~6.1 there as an effective potential, and its curvature term \(\tfrac16W_R\), of the same order in the scale of \(K\), is not given there.

\emph{Area coefficient.} The cone on \(M\) leaves \(K\) a spectator, so the factorisation holds on the replica cover. With the cone values of Section~\ref{sec:status-B} (Dirac index part \(-1\) per Dirac field for the antiperiodic gluing, \(0\) for the periodic one, and \(-\tfrac12\) times the coefficient of \(\rho R[g_\rho]\), with \(X\) kept as the torsion invariant, for the smooth replica), the fixed-state replica gives \(c_A=\tfrac16W_R-2W_X\) plus \(2\) per matrix mode of \(K\) at order \(\LUV^2\) (the value \(c_A=2\) at \(N_s=6\), \(n_D=2\), carried by the first term), the periodic replica \(c_A=\tfrac16W_R\) and the smooth replica \(c_A=-\tfrac1{12}W_R\). Hence \(c_A-c_T\) is \(4W_X\) for the periodic and \(-\tfrac14W_R+4W_X\) for the smooth replica, and for the fixed-state replica cutoff-dependent terms from the first term (the order \(\LUV^2\) term and a logarithmic term proportional to the trace of \(H_0^K\), whose finite remainders are not computed) plus the part \(2W_X\) from the mixed term. For \(W_X\neq0\) the periodic gap does not vanish, and the smooth gap vanishes only if \(W_R=16W_X\), a condition on \(K\). The vector index part, which with the weight \(\kappa_X\) carries the gap \(4\kappa_X\) of the paragraph on the normalisation, cancels against the Dirac fields in the first term and is absent from the mixed term. The part \(2W_X\) of the fixed-state gap that comes from the mixed term is the axial-torsion part of the Dirac index density on the tensor modes; the Dirac cone value \(-1\) equals the coefficient of \(R\) in \(\tfrac12\rho^2X\) in the presentation \(r=-2\) (an equality of the two values, not derived), so, with the same index density and cone value, the mixed-term part of the gap would vanish for tensor modes that source neither the axial nor the vector torsion at first order. This is the case for the dressed density of the paragraph on local frame rotations (an input of Assumption~\ref{ass:local-lorentz}, not derived), which sets \(S=0\) and gives \(c_T^{\mathrm{ext}}=\tfrac16W_R-2W_X\), equal to the mixed-term part of the fixed-state \(c_A\).

\emph{Classes with six polarisations.} For a product the linearised Einstein rank is that of \(M\) (paragraph on leaf dimension above four), so \(M\) must carry the six polarisations. For \(D\) matrix coordinates on a four-dimensional leaf with constant \(\theta\) in the chart \(y\), the scalar operator is \(-\lambda\Delta_{\hat g}\) with \(\hat g=\lambda G\), \(\lambda=\sqrt{\lvert g\rvert}/\rho\) and \(\lvert\det\hat g\rvert=\lvert\det g\rvert\), and its density is \(\tfrac16\rho R[\hat g]\) pointwise (exact algebra in both signatures; the density checked symbolically at a random Euclidean jet); \(\rho\) is constant, so the scalar part of the two-derivative density in this class is \(\rho\) times the Einstein--Hilbert functional of \(\hat g\). At the frozen data of the paragraph on more than four matrix coordinates the linearised Einstein operator of \(\hat g\) has rank \(\min(6,D-1)\) on the first-order image (one exact-rational draw for each \(4\le D\le11\)), and the Hamiltonian gauge direction is a linearised diffeomorphism of \(\hat g\). With \(d=7\) (four tangent and three normal directions) and \(K\) in the remaining three, the index density of the mixed term is \(X_7=g^{\rho\lambda}\partial_\rho\Theta^{IJ}\partial_\lambda\Theta_{IJ}\), which on a transverse wave \(\delta X^i=\phi_i\cos(p\cdot y)\) about \(x^i=\tfrac12\mathrm{II}^i_{\mu\nu}y^\mu y^\nu\) contains the term \((p\cdot g^{-1}p)(p\cdot\mathsf Kp)\lvert\phi\rvert^2\) of fourth order in \(p\) at \(y=0\) (exact check at one background), whereas the Einstein--Hilbert form of \(\hat g\) on those modes is of second order in \(p\) by derivative counting, since \(\delta\hat g\) is algebraic in \(\phi\) at first order. The fourth-order term is the product of the symbol \(p\cdot g^{-1}p\) of the induced d'Alembertian and the tree-level kinetic symbol \(p\cdot\mathsf K p\) of \(\phi\), with \(\mathsf K=\theta^{T}g\theta\) the kinetic tensor; at this order it can be moved by a local redefinition of \(\phi\) and vanishes on the tree-level mass shell. The part of second order in \(p\), which is nonzero in the same check, is not compared with the Einstein--Hilbert form of \(\hat g\), so a common \(G_T\) on the transverse polarisations is neither established nor excluded. The conjugate-coordinate class with \(K\) in the transverse directions has the structure \(S^2_n\,\bar\times\,M^{3,1}\times K\) of Section~7 of~\cite{Steinacker2023}, with frame \(\{t^\alpha,x^\mu\}\), where \(t^\alpha\) and \(x^\mu\) are the semiclassical forms of the generators \(T^a=R^{-1}M^{a4}\) and \(X^a=rM^{a5}\) of~\cite{SperlingSteinacker2019}; the bundle structure and the generator definitions agree with those of the conjugate-coordinate class here up to normalisation, the assignment of the two families to the matrices and the base functions following Section~7 of~\cite{Steinacker2023}. In that construction the mixed term is the spinor index part of the Dirac operator of the background \(T^a\) on \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\). Its two-derivative density, its values on the six level-one amplitudes and on the cone, and whether those amplitudes source the axial torsion, are not computed; in the Poisson limit its heat trace is the hypoelliptic one of the paragraph on the \(\Theta\)-built heat kernel, whereas the cited construction uses a finite fibre.

These statements are for the Poisson limit on \(M\), one loop, the content stated, a fuzzy space \(K\) that is not required to solve the equations of motion, product backgrounds without mixed flux, the orientation-even part of the mixed term, the replicas of Section~\ref{sec:status-B} with the hypotheses stated there, and, for the transverse-mode check, frozen coefficients at one background point. They do not construct \(K\) or fix the sign of \(W_R\), so no value or sign of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\) follows; they do not derive the replica selected by the matrix state, identify \(G\) or \(\hat g\) with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}, or treat Moyal corrections, non-product backgrounds or a finite fibre. Problems~B and~F remain open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class times a fuzzy factor: torsion of the level-one amplitudes and the induced Einstein coefficient (conditional; bounded no-go and conditional route).}
This paragraph supplements the preceding one, in which the two-derivative density of the mixed term on the six level-one amplitudes, its cone values and the torsion those amplitudes source were not computed. Take for \(M\) the conjugate-coordinate class (Poisson limit, leaf \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\), \(X^a=T^a+A^a\), \(T^a=M^{a4}\), base functions \(y^\mu=M^{\mu5}\), \(m=M^{45}\), units \(R=1\)), the split \(d=4\) and \(K\) of the preceding paragraph, the level-one amplitudes \(A^a=c_i^a(y)\,n^i\) with \(M^{\mu\rho}=\sum_in^iS_i^{\mu\rho}\) on the fibre, \(n\) a unit vector of the fibre two-sphere, a plane wave \(e^{\mathrm iq\cdot y}\) with frozen coefficients at a base point, \(|q|\ell\gg1\), \(s_i^\mu=S_i^{\mu\nu}q_\nu\), \(G_{ij}=s_i\cdot s_j\), and the leading symbol in which the fibre momenta and the fibre components of the Hamiltonian fields are dropped and the trace over the fibre harmonics of a multiplication operator is its fibre average. This is the hybrid evaluation of Section~7 of~\cite{Steinacker2023}, for a finite fibre and a scale of \(K\) above the fibre fluxes.

\emph{Frame and torsion.} With \(E^{a\mu}=\{X^a,y^\mu\}\), \(F^{ab}=\{X^a,X^b\}\) and \(T^{ab\mu}=\{F^{ab},y^\mu\}\), the background has \(E^{a\mu}=\pm m\,\eta^{a\mu}\) and \(T^{ab\mu}=\eta^{a\mu}y^b-\eta^{b\mu}y^a\), independent of the fibre point (exact in the Lie--Poisson algebra). At the leading symbol, for an amplitude \(\alpha^a\) with any dependence on the fibre point, \(\delta E^{a\mu}=\alpha^a v^\mu\) up to a constant phase, with \(v=\sum_jn^js_j\), so the frame perturbation at a fibre point has rank one, and \(\delta T^{ab\mu}=-m\,v^\mu(q^a\alpha^b-q^b\alpha^a)\); for the level-one amplitudes \(\alpha=\sum_in^ic_i\) (verified by a Lie--Poisson computation at points of an explicit realisation of the orbit, relative residual \(3\cdot10^{-14}\)). Write \(c_i=D_{ij}s_j+\gamma_iq\). The \(\gamma_i\) are gauge directions, the fibre-averaged metric depends only on the symmetric part of \(D\), which gives the six polarisations, and the antisymmetric part gives the three inert amplitudes. The torsion of the fibre-averaged frame has no axial part on the six polarisations and a vector part proportional to \(q^a\operatorname{tr}(DG)\), that is to the trace of the averaged metric perturbation; the inert amplitudes carry axial torsion. At a fibre point the six polarisations do source axial torsion, proportional to \([D,nn^{T}]\) and so in the second fibre harmonic; it averages to zero and vanishes identically only for \(D\propto\mathbb I\) (exact rational arithmetic in seven sampled cases: four base points, five wave vectors including two time-like ones, both orientations of the fibre basis).

\emph{Two-derivative forms.} In the normalisation of the preceding paragraph, for a real wave averaged over its phase, and taking the index density to be the torsion invariant of the four-coordinate class evaluated on the frame at the fibre point (an identification carried over from that class and not recomputed for the leaf \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\)), the index density at a fibre point is \(X=m^{-4}(v\cdot v)\,(q^2\alpha\cdot\alpha-(q\cdot\alpha)^2)\), and the scalar density is \(\tfrac16\sqrt{|G|}R[G]-\sqrt{|G|}\,G^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\phi\,\partial_\nu\phi\), with \(G\) the inverse of \(\eta_{ab}E^aE^b\) and \(\phi=-\tfrac14\ln|\det G|\). With these densities the two modes with \(\delta\rho=0\) of four coordinates have the index form \(-4\) and the scalar form \(\tfrac16\) times the Einstein--Hilbert form, as in the preceding paragraph. For the fibre-averaged frame, exactly at every sampled point, the index form is \(-2\) times the Einstein--Hilbert form \(\mathrm{EH}(\bar h)\) of the averaged metric plus a multiple of \(q^2(\operatorname{tr}\bar h)^2\), and the scalar form is \(\tfrac16\,\mathrm{EH}(\bar h)\) plus such a multiple. The inert amplitudes do not couple to \(\bar h\) and enter only through the axial term. Hence, in this averaged-frame reading, which is not the fibre trace treated below, and in the units \(c\) of the preceding paragraph, \(c_T=\tfrac16W_R-2W_X\) on the five traceless polarisations and \(-\tfrac5{24}W_R+W_X\) on the trace polarisation, their complement in the six. With the cone values of the preceding paragraph and the cone in the base at every fibre point, the fixed-state replica has \(c_A=\tfrac16W_R-2W_X\), equal to \(c_T\) on the five traceless polarisations for the finite parts, without the dressed density of the paragraph on local frame rotations; this inherits the equality of the Dirac cone value with the coefficient of the Einstein term in the index density, which the preceding paragraph does not derive. The periodic and smooth gaps there are \(2W_X\) and \(-\tfrac14W_R+2W_X\). On the trace polarisation the fixed-state, periodic and smooth gaps are \(\tfrac38W_R-3W_X\), \(\tfrac38W_R-W_X\) and \(\tfrac18W_R-W_X\).

\emph{Fibre trace.} The fibre averages of the pointwise densities differ from these by the second fibre harmonic. On the six polarisations, with the inert amplitudes set to zero, neither form lies in the span of \(q^2\bar h_{\mu\nu}\bar h^{\mu\nu}\) and \(q^2(\operatorname{tr}\bar h)^2\) (relative residual of the best fit between \(0.03\) and \(0.36\)), both couple the inert amplitudes to the six, and their generalised eigenvalues relative to \(\mathrm{EH}(\bar h)\) on the five traceless polarisations are distinct and of both signs (for the index form \(-4.36\), \(-0.73\), each twice, and \(0.47\) at one point). No ratio \(W_R:W_X\) makes their sum proportional to \(\mathrm{EH}(\bar h)\) or covariant under the Lorentz group of the frozen point (a form that also depends on the background vector \(y^\mu/\ell\) is not tested; exact linear algebra at the seven sampled points; a Monte Carlo fibre average on the explicit realisation agrees). For this trace, with the inert amplitudes set to zero rather than eliminated, no single \(G_T\) covers the six polarisations or the five traceless ones, and \(-2\) is not a generalised eigenvalue of the index form at any sampled point, so the mixed-term part of the fixed-state gap vanishes on no generalised eigendirection of the traceless polarisations. The trace with equal weight on all fibre harmonics applies when the scale of \(K\) exceeds the fibre gaps, which is the regime of the cited construction. The averaged-frame forms are the trace over the fibre-constant loop modes with projected coefficients. The full trace approaches them when the fibre gaps exceed the scale of \(K\), since intermediate states in the second harmonic are then suppressed in the proper-time integral; this is an argument and is not computed. In that regime the first term of the factorisation has a part with two base derivatives from the fibre flux, the mixed \(S^2\)--\(M\) term of Section~7.1.2 of~\cite{Steinacker2023}, which that work states is not Lorentz invariant a priori and finds subdominant for a scale of \(K\) above the fibre scale, a condition that fails in that regime; this part is not computed here. In the cited construction the mixed term is written with the torsion of a frame on \(M^{3,1}\). Replacing the frame of the level-one amplitudes by its fibre average gives the averaged-frame forms above, and for a fibre-independent torsion, such as that of the background, the two evaluations agree.

These statements are for the Poisson limit, one loop, the content and product backgrounds of the preceding paragraph, the orientation-even mixed term, the level-one amplitudes, the leading symbol at a frozen base point with \(y^i=0\) (other points follow by Lorentz covariance), a formal evaluation with the Lorentzian \(\eta\), and the replicas of Section~\ref{sec:status-B} with the cone in the base. They do not compute the first-term contributions of order \(\LUV^2\), the logarithmic and fibre-flux contributions, the subleading symbol, the interpolation between the two regimes, the elimination of the inert amplitudes or the fibre spectrum. They do not construct \(K\) or fix the sign of \(W_R\), derive the replica or the regime from the matrix state, identify \(\bar h\) with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}, or bear on Assumption~\ref{ass:lorentzian}. Problems~B and~F remain open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class times a fuzzy factor: fibre spectrum, proper-time interpolation between the two readings, and the background torsion (conditional; bounded no-go; correction of a regime argument of the preceding paragraph).}
This paragraph supplements the preceding one, in which the interpolation between the fibre trace and the averaged-frame reading, the fibre spectrum and the fibre-flux part were not computed. Keep its setting (Poisson limit on \(M\), leaf \(\mathbb{CP}^{1,2}\), \(T^a=M^{a4}\), \(y^\mu=M^{\mu5}\), \(m=M^{45}\), units \(R=1\), the split \(d=4\), the level-one amplitudes, the leading symbol at a frozen base point, the orientation-even mixed term, a formal evaluation with the Lorentzian \(\eta\), inert amplitudes set to zero), write \(t^\mu=T^\mu\), \(\coth\tau=y^0/y^4\) at \(y^i=0\), and let the loop modes be \(\phi(y)Y_{Jm}(n)\) with fibre harmonics of order \(J\le n\). The scale of \(K\) is measured by the eigenvalues \(M\) of the \(K\)-part of the loop operator, in the units of \(\Box\).

\emph{Fibre spectrum.} In the polynomial Lie--Poisson algebra, for both signs of the bracket, \(\Box t^\mu=-3t^\mu\) and \(\Box(t^\mu t^\nu)=-6t^\mu t^\nu+2\eta_{ab}M^{a\mu}M^{b\nu}\), and on the orbit \(\eta_{ab}M^{a\mu}M^{b\nu}=c_\theta\eta^{\mu\nu}+y^\mu y^\nu-t^\mu t^\nu\) (\(40\) random points, residual below \(10^{-15}\)). On the fibre over a base point \(t\cdot y=0\) and \(t\cdot t\) is constant, and for \(C\) symmetric, transverse to \(y\) and traceless on \(y^\perp\), \(\Box(C\cdot t^J)=-J(J+2)\,C\cdot t^J\) (\(J=1,2,3\), two base points, one with \(y^i\neq0\), relative residual below \(10^{-15}\)). The fibre levels of the loop operator \(-\Box\) at zero base momentum are therefore \(\mu_J=J(J+2)\); they agree with the value \(3J\) of Equation~(7.5) of~\cite{Steinacker2023} at \(J=1\) and exceed it for \(J\ge2\), and the two are not reconciled here. The cross term \(2\eta_{ab}\{T^a,\phi\}\{T^b,Y\}\) for a plane wave \(\phi\) of momentum \(q\) maps each level \(J\) into itself, with singular values \((2m/\lvert y^0\rvert)\bigl((y^4q_0J)^2+(\ell\lvert\mathbf q\rvert m_L)^2\bigr)^{1/2}\), \(m_L=-J,\dots,J\), at \(y^i=0\) (Monte Carlo agreement within four per cent for \(J=1,2\)); it is comparable with \(m^2q^2\) and \(\mu_J\) when \(m\lvert q\rvert\sim J\) and is dropped below, as in the preceding paragraph.

\emph{Proper-time weights.} A vertex that multiplies by a fibre function \(f=f_0+f_2\) (harmonic orders \(0\) and \(2\)) connects \(J\) to \(J\) and \(J\pm2\), with \(\sum_{m,m'}\langle Jm|f|J'm'\rangle\langle J'm'|g|Jm\rangle=(2J+1)\bigl[\delta_{JJ'}f_0g_0+c(J,J')\langle f_2g_2\rangle\bigr]\), \(c(J,J')=\langle J0\,20|J'0\rangle^2\). At second order the intermediate level enters through \(e^{-s\Delta a}\), \(\Delta=\mu_{J'}-\mu_J\), \(0\le a\le1\). The torsion vertex is already of order \(q^2\), so its weight is \(\int_0^1e^{-s\Delta a}\,\mathrm da\). For the scalar operator \(-\partial\gamma\partial\) with cometric wave \(h\), \(G=\bar\gamma^{-1}\), the part of order \(q^2\) is proportional to \(\int_0^1e^{-s\Delta a}[a(1-a)F_1+(1-2a)^2F_2]\,\mathrm da\) with \(F_1=-\tfrac14(q\bar\gamma q)\bigl((\operatorname{tr}hG)^2+2\operatorname{tr}(hGhG)\bigr)-(qhq)\operatorname{tr}(hG)\) and \(F_2=\tfrac12\,qhGhq\) (exact Gaussian moments, checked independently in both signatures); at \(\Delta=0\) this gives the scalar density of the preceding paragraph, which equals \(\tfrac14(\tfrac16F_1+\tfrac13F_2)/\sqrt{\lvert\det\bar\gamma\rvert}\) exactly. Hence, with level weights \(D(s)=\sum_J(2J+1)e^{-s\mu_J}\) on the averaged parts and the corresponding sums \(B_i(s)\) on the second-harmonic parts, the index form is \(W_X^{(0)}\,\mathrm{IX}_1+W_X^{(2)}(\mathrm{IX}_2-\mathrm{IX}_1)\) and the scalar form is \(W_R^{(0)}\,\mathrm{SC}_1+W_R^{(1)}L_1+W_R^{(2)}L_2\), where \(\mathrm{IX}_{1,2}\), \(\mathrm{SC}_{1,2}\) are the averaged-frame and fibre-trace forms of the preceding paragraph, \(L_1+L_2=\mathrm{SC}_2-\mathrm{SC}_1\) (exact at the seven sampled points), \(L_2\) vanishes on the five traceless polarisations, and each \(W\) is the integral of \(s^{-2}\tau^K(s)\) or \(s^{-2}(v^K-\tau^K)(s)\) against the corresponding level weight. The cone carries only the weight \(D\), so the mixed-term part of the fixed-state gap on the generalised eigendirections of the five traceless polarisations is \(-W_X^{(2)}(\lambda_i+2)\), with \(\lambda_i\) the eigenvalues of \(\mathrm{IX}_2\) relative to \(\mathrm{EH}(\bar h)\). The form therefore interpolates exactly between the two readings, and a single coefficient on the five holds only where \(W_X^{(2)}=W_R^{(1)}=0\).

\emph{Which regime.} For \(\mu_J\gg M\) a level contributes \((2J+1)\int\mathrm ds\,s^{-2}\tau^K(s)e^{-s\mu_J}\simeq(2J+1)\tau_2/\mu_J\), with \(\tau^K\simeq\tau_2s^2\) at small \(s\): the levels are suppressed by a power, not exponentially, and the transitions \(J\to J\) of the second harmonic have \(\Delta=0\). The ratio \(\beta_X=W_X^{(2)}/W_X^{(0)}\) is small only if \(M\sum_{J\ge1}(2J+1)/\mu_J\ll1\) and \(M\ll\mu_2\), and tends to one as \(n\to\infty\) at fixed \(M\), the sum growing as \(2\ln n\). For \(\tau^K\propto s^2e^{-sM}\) and \(n=30\), \(\beta_X=0.065\), \(0.40\), \(0.86\), \(0.96\) at \(M=0.01\), \(0.1\), \(1\), \(8\) (\(0.19\), \(0.69\), \(0.94\), \(0.97\) for the levels \(3J\)), and at the base point \((y^0,y^4)=(5,3)\) with \(q=(\tfrac37,\tfrac12,-\tfrac23,\tfrac15)\) the half-spread of the five eigenvalues of the index form relative to \(\mathrm{EH}(\bar h)\) is \(2.42\,\beta_X\) about the averaged-frame value \(-2\) (numerical). The argument of the preceding paragraph that the full trace approaches the averaged-frame forms when the fibre gaps exceed the scale of \(K\) is therefore incomplete: the condition is \(M\sum_{J\ge1}(2J+1)/\mu_J\ll1\), which, at fixed \(M\), fails as \(n\to\infty\), since the sum grows as \(2\ln n\).

\emph{Background torsion.} The flux operator of a fibre-constant loop mode is \(\mathrm ik_\mu T_{ab}{}^\mu\) with \(T=\bar T+\delta T_0\), \(\bar T^{ab\mu}=\eta^{a\mu}y^b-\eta^{b\mu}y^a\) and \(\delta T_0\) the fibre average of the level-one torsion, and at frozen coefficients the index part of a species is the Gaussian average over \(k\) of \(\operatorname{tr}e^{-sZ(k)}-\dim\), \(Z=-\mathrm i\Sigma^{ab}T_{ab}{}^\mu k_\mu\), exactly. Its expansion \(sQ_1+s^2Q_2+\dots\) gives for the Dirac representation \(Q_1=m^4\,\mathrm{IX}_1\) to machine precision at ten sampled points, so on these modes the index density is the torsion invariant of the averaged frame, and the vector representation gives twice it. The part of \(Q_2\) quadratic in \(\delta T_0\) is the first background-torsion correction of the mixed term (fluxes of order four on \(M\) and two on \(K\)); the same part of \(Q_2^V-2Q_2^D\) is the first term \(s_0^K(v^M-2\sigma^M)\) at order four, with the weight \(\int\mathrm ds\,s^{-1}s_0^K(s)\), logarithmic at both ends and not suppressed by the flux of \(K\). On the five traceless polarisations the forms \(\mathrm{IX}_2-\mathrm{IX}_1\), \(Q_2^D/m^4\) and \((Q_2^V-2Q_2^D)/m^4\) lie in the span of \(\mathrm{EH}(\bar h)\) and one further form, with \(Q_2^V-2Q_2^D=-6Q_2^D\) modulo \(\mathrm{EH}(\bar h)\) and \(\mathrm{IX}_2-\mathrm{IX}_1=\lambda Q_2^D/m^4\) modulo \(\mathrm{EH}(\bar h)\), \(-3.31\le\lambda\le-0.32\) (residual below \(10^{-11}\) at seven sampled points); the eigenvalues of \(Q_2^D/m^4\) relative to \(\mathrm{EH}(\bar h)\) there are one simple and two double values, one of which is \(\tfrac32\coth^2\tau\) at every sampled point. The scalar second-harmonic form \(L_1\) is not in that span. The relative size of the correction is \(s\coth^2\tau\), with \(\coth^2\tau\ge1\) on the leaf, weighted by the scale of \(K\) through \(\langle s\rangle\simeq1/M\).

\emph{Consequence.} Where the second-harmonic weight is small, \(M\sum_{J\ge1}(2J+1)/\mu_J\ll1\), the torsion parameter is \(\coth^2\tau/M\gg\coth^2\tau\sum_{J\ge1}(2J+1)/\mu_J\ge1\), so the forms of order two in the flux of \(M\), on which the averaged-frame coefficient \(c_T=\tfrac16W_R-2W_X\) and the equality \(c_A=c_T\) on the five traceless polarisations rest, are not the leading result there; where the torsion expansion holds, \(M\gg\coth^2\tau\), the weight \(\beta_X\) is of order one (it tends to one as \(n\) grows) and the fibre trace of the preceding paragraph is the leading reading. At first order in the torsion, and with the first-term part weighted by the ratio \(L/c\) of its proper-time logarithm to the flux normalisation \(\tau_2=cM^2\) of \(K\) (a parameter, not computed), the coefficient of the non-Einstein form of the index sector changes sign at one value \(M^*\); at the base point \((5,3)\) and the wave vector above, for \(2\le n\le300\), it lies at \(0.21\le M^*\le0.68\) with \(\coth^2\tau/M^*\ge4.1\) for \(L/c=0\), and at \(1.1\le M^*\le31\) with \(\beta_X\) between \(0.51\) and \(0.98\) for \(L/c=1\) and \(10\), where the cancellation is between the fibre-trace part and the first-term torsion part. The logarithm \(L\) comes from fibre-constant modes without \(K\) flux, for which the torsion expansion is not controlled at large proper time. A single coefficient there would further require the scalar coefficient of the independent form \(L_1\) to vanish and the cone at the same order to match, which is not computed. The part of the first term with two fibre fluxes, the mixed \(S^2\)--\(M\) term of Section~7.1.2 of~\cite{Steinacker2023}, has per level a weight of order \(J^2/\mu_J\) by power counting and is not computed. Within these computations no open range of the scale of \(K\) gives a single Einstein coefficient on the five traceless polarisations with \(c_A=c_T\); the range \(M\sim\coth^2\tau\), where the first-order torsion parameter is of order one, is not computed, and isolated values of the data of \(K\) at which two independent forms vanish and the cone matches are not excluded, and would lie where the fibre trace, not the averaged frame, dominates.

These statements are for the hypotheses of the preceding paragraph, fibre-constant loop modes for the torsion correction, the base-fibre cross term dropped, commutative Clebsch--Gordan coefficients with truncation at \(n\), and the single-scale model of \(\tau^K\) for the numbers quoted; the spectrum identity is exact, the forms are exact or numerical as stated. They do not compute the fibre-flux part, the higher orders in the background torsion, the cone corrections at those orders, the elimination of the inert amplitudes or the trace polarisation, do not construct \(K\), and do not identify \(\bar h\) with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence} or bear on Assumption~\ref{ass:lorentzian}; the replicas are those of Section~\ref{sec:status-B}. Problems~B and~F remain open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class times a fuzzy factor: closed forms of the level-one forms, Lichnerowicz reduction of the mixed term, the range of the background torsion and the coefficient on the null cone (conditional; bounded no-go; refinement of three statements of the preceding paragraph).}
This paragraph supplements the preceding one, in which the forms were sampled numerically at points, the range \(M\sim\coth^2\tau\) was not computed and isolated data of \(K\) were not excluded. Keep its setting and notation and write \(\vartheta=\coth^2\tau\).

\emph{Closed forms.} At \(y^i=0\) the Gram matrix of the \(s_i\) is \(G_{ij}=a\,\delta_{ij}-(a-b)\hat q_i\hat q_j\), with \(a=\ell^2q^2+(q\cdot y)^2\), \(b=-m^2q^2\) and \(\hat q\) the unit spatial direction of \(q\). Put \(x=a/b\): then \(x\le-\ell^2/m^2\) for space-like \(q\), \(x\ge1\) for time-like \(q\), \(|x|\to\infty\) on the null cone at fixed \(q\cdot y\neq0\), and \(y_\perp=y-(q\cdot y)q/q^2\) has \(y_\perp^2=m^2(x-1)\). With \(\bar h^{\mu\nu}=\tfrac{2m}3D_{ij}s_i^\mu s_j^\nu\) the fibre-averaged level-one torsion is \(-\tfrac12(q^a\bar h^{b\mu}-q^b\bar h^{a\mu})\) and \(\mathrm{EH}(\bar h)=-q^2\bar h\cdot\bar h/8m^6\). On the five traceless polarisations count the helicity about \(y_\perp\) in the three-space orthogonal to \(q\), and put \(\varphi=(\bar hy)^2/(\bar h\cdot\bar h\,y_\perp^2)\), so that \(\varphi=0,\tfrac12,\tfrac23\) for helicity \(\pm2,\pm1,0\). The fourth moment of the fibre vector gives exactly, for the eigenvalues relative to \(\mathrm{EH}(\bar h)\),
\[
\mathrm{IX}_2:\ -\tfrac65\bigl[2+(2+\tfrac1x)(1+(x-1)\varphi)\bigr],\qquad
\mathrm{SC}_2:\ \tfrac1{20}\bigl[4+2r+(2+\tfrac1x)(1+(x-1)\varphi)\bigr],
\]
with \(r=\tfrac12(x+\tfrac1x)\) on helicity \(\pm1\) and \(r=1\) otherwise, and the Gaussian moments give, as rational identities in \(q,y,m\),
\[
Q_2^D/m^4:\ \tfrac32\vartheta+(x-1)\varphi,\qquad
