(Q_2^V-2Q_2^D)/m^4:\ 9\vartheta+(x-1)(8-6\varphi).
\]
The exact forms agree with these at eighteen sampled points. Hence the further form of the preceding paragraph is \(q^2(\bar hy)\cdot(\bar hy)\), the Maxwell form of \(a_b=\bar h_{b\nu}y^\nu\), that is the square of the contraction of the level-one torsion with \(y\); the double eigenvalue \(\tfrac32\vartheta\) belongs to helicity \(\pm2\); and \(\lambda=-\tfrac65(2+\tfrac1x)\), a function of \(x\) that changes sign at \(x=-\tfrac12\), reached only where \(m^2\ge2\ell^2\). The term \(8(x-1)\) is not a multiple of the Einstein--Hilbert form, since \((x-1)q^2\bar h\cdot\bar h=[y^2q^2-(q\cdot y)^2]\bar h\cdot\bar h/m^2\).

\emph{Dependence on the wave vector.} A coefficient on the five for every \(q\) at a base point must not depend on the helicity or on \(x\). At first order in the torsion on fibre-constant modes the \(K\)-weights of \(Q_2^D\) and \(Q_2^V-2Q_2^D\) do not depend on \(x\). Then helicity \(\pm1\), through the term \((x-1)^2/20x\) of \(\mathrm{SC}_2\), requires \(W_R^{(1)}=0\), and helicities \(0\) and \(\pm2\), through \((x-1)(2+\tfrac1x)\), then require \(W_X^{(2)}=0\). Since \(W_X^{(2)}=-\tfrac18\int\mathrm ds\,s^{-2}\tau^KB_0\) with \(\tau^K\ge0\) and \(B_0>0\), \(B_0\) being the sum \(B_i\) of the preceding paragraph with the weight \(\int_0^1e^{-s\Delta a}\,\mathrm da\) of the torsion vertex, this fails for every non-zero flux of \(K\). At this order and at the frozen symbol the isolated data of \(K\) left open in the preceding paragraph are excluded, and the value \(M^*\) quoted there belongs to one class of wave vectors: at \((5,3)\), \(n=30\), \(L/c=0\) in the single-scale model the zero of the first-order coefficient is \(M^*\simeq0.292\) at \(x\simeq-2.70\) and \(M^*\simeq0.223\) at \(x\simeq1.33\).

\emph{Lichnerowicz reduction of the mixed term.} For fibre-constant loop modes with projected coefficients (the transitions to \(J'=2\) enter through the weights \(B_i\) of the preceding paragraph, not here) and at the order considered, the operators are built from the averaged frame fields \(\bar E^a\), which are divergence free for \(\rho=1/m\); the second-harmonic product \(\langle\delta E\delta E\rangle\) carries fewer powers of \(q\). Then \(T^{ab\mu}=[\bar E^a,\bar E^b]^\mu\) and \(\partial_\mu(\rho T^{ab\mu})=0\) identically, so with \(g^{\mu\nu}=\eta_{ab}\bar E^{a\mu}\bar E^{b\nu}\), \(\phi=\tfrac12\ln(\rho/\sqrt{|g|})\) and \(B^\mu=-\Sigma_{ab}T^{ab\mu}\) every species has connection \(\partial\phi-\tfrac12B\) and endomorphism \(E_0-\tfrac14B_\mu B^\mu\), \(E_0=-\Box_g\phi-|\partial\phi|^2\), without a term linear in \(T\). The Dirac operator \(\Gamma_a\bar E^a\) is similar through \((\rho/\sqrt{|g|})^{1/2}\) to the Levi-Civita Dirac operator of \(g\) up to the axial torsion, and for any such frame, with \(Z(k)=Z^\mu k_\mu\), the index density satisfies
\[
\tfrac14g_{\mu\nu}\operatorname{tr}_DZ^\mu Z^\nu=-R[g]+4\Box_g\phi+4|\partial\phi|^2\mp\tfrac18A_aA^a,
\]
with \(A_d=\epsilon_{abcd}T^{abc}\) the axial torsion and the upper sign for \(\eta\), the lower for \(\delta\) (exact rational arithmetic, ten random two-jets each). On the background \(T^{ab\mu}=\bar T^{ab\mu}\) is the Levi-Civita spin connection of \(G=\eta/m^2\), \(R[G]=18\vartheta-24\) and \(R/6+E_0=2-\tfrac34\vartheta\) (with \(\vartheta=-y\cdot y/m^2\) off \(y^i=0\)), and \(\bar{\mathcal Z}_{ab}\) is \(\pm J_{ab}\), which commutes with \(\Box\), so that, without mass terms, \(e^{-sH_i}=e^{s\Box}e^{\pm sJ_{ab}\Sigma_i^{ab}}\) and \(H_i=C_{31}^{\mathrm{tot},i}-C_{41}-C_{\Sigma_i}\) for the vector and for the Dirac index (polynomial Poisson algebra, both signs of the bracket). The background torsion is therefore not an independent expansion field of the mixed term. Where the axial torsion and \(\delta\phi\) vanish at first order, as on the five for the fibre average at the leading symbol, the mixed term is a functional of \(g\) and \(\phi\) alone. Its order \(s\) is \(-\sqrt{|g|}R[g]\) at order \(q^2\), that is \(\tfrac12\mathrm{IX}_1=-\mathrm{EH}(\bar h)\) for a wave averaged over its phase, in agreement with the density \(\tfrac12\rho^2X\) of a Dirac field; the forms \(Q_i\) and the Dirac trace of the preceding paragraph and of this one are taken at unit amplitude of \(\delta T_0\) and are twice their phase averages, so that \(Q_1=m^4\mathrm{IX}_1\). Its order \(s^2\), up to total derivatives, is \(\tfrac1{72}R^2-\tfrac1{24}R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}R^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}-2(\tfrac16R+E_0)^2\), a density quadratic in the curvature and \(E_0\), whose second variation meets the Einstein--Hilbert form only through background values of order \(\vartheta\); the form \(Q_2^D\) is only its part of fourth order in \(Z\) (at the background \(\tfrac{3125}{648}\) against the complete \(\tfrac{1783}{648}\) at \((5,3)\)), so the first background-torsion correction of the preceding paragraph is that of the frozen symbol and not the complete term of order \(s^2\). Thus the equality of the Dirac cone value with the coefficient of the Einstein term in the index density, recorded above as an equality of values and not derived there, is the Lichnerowicz identity with the antiperiodic gluing of Section~\ref{sec:status-B}, as for the Levi-Civita Dirac field of~\cite{Kabat1995}. The cross term of the same density with the conical deltas of Section~\ref{sec:status-B}, with \(\rho\) regular, gives the surface density \((1-n)\pi[\tfrac19R-\tfrac13R_{ijij}-\tfrac83(\tfrac16R+E_0)]\), which matches the Dirac weights stated there in the relative normalisation of \(\sigma_{\log}\) (the spin term there has a heat-trace prefactor \(4\pi\) times that of the terms in \(c_B\)); to this order the cone of the mixed term is not an independent quantity. The vector index does not reduce in this way, but it occurs only in the first term, whose weighted coefficient of order \(s^2\) is the analogue of \(\tfrac12\mathcal S_4\) of Section~\ref{sec:status-B}, with no term linear or quadratic in the curvature, so that the first-term form of the preceding paragraph is complete at that order. At a fibre point the frame has axial torsion and \(\hat X=m^{-4}q^2(v\cdot v)(\alpha\cdot\alpha)=-4\,\mathrm{EH}[h_n]+m^{-4}q^2(\alpha\cdot v)^2\) for \(h_n=m(\alpha\otimes v+v\otimes\alpha)\) (exact at six fibre points), half of the coefficient \(-4\) being axial. The identity above has the form of Equation~(3.31) of~\cite{MantaSteinackerTran2024} (the normalisation of the dual is not compared). As a comparison of structure only, the fibre trace of the preceding paragraphs corresponds to the higher-spin trace of the torsion term there, in the regime assumed there of a scale of \(K\) above the higher-spin masses, which that work replaces by one positive scale. The sections compared make no statement of one coefficient on the five polarisations: that is a criterion of this ledger, which is neither established in that work nor contradicted by it.

\emph{The range \(M\sim\vartheta\).} The frozen integral can be done: with \(\xi=s\vartheta/2\), \(\langle\operatorname{tr}_De^{-sZ}\rangle=4(1-\xi)e^{-\xi/2}\), \(\langle\operatorname{tr}_Ve^{-sZ}\rangle=2+2(1-4\xi)e^{-2\xi}\), and the second variation of the Dirac trace is \(-2s\,e^{-\xi/2}[1-(1+\nu\varphi)\xi+(\tfrac2{15}+\tfrac15\nu\varphi)\xi^2]\) times \(m^4\mathrm{EH}(\bar h)\), with \(\nu=(x-1)/\vartheta\) (exact; a direct evaluation agrees). For \(\tau^K\propto s^2e^{-sM}\) the weight \(M^{-2}\) of the preceding paragraph is replaced by \(F(M)=\tilde M^{-2}-\tfrac15\vartheta\tilde M^{-3}>0\), \(\tilde M=M+\tfrac14\vartheta\), which is bounded at \(M=0\); at \((5,3)\) the zeros at \(L/c=0\) are then absent for the sampled \(2\le n\le300\) and values of \(x\), and those at \(L/c=1,10\) for \(n=30,300\) and two values of \(x\) move by less than \(2\%\). This is a statement about the frozen integral only. The Levi-Civita form above shows that the terms dropped by freezing are of the same order in \(s\vartheta\): the curvature of \(G\) is of order \(\vartheta\). A frozen plane wave of two-derivative order needs the squared components of \(mq\) in the rest frame of \(y\) above that curvature and below the scale of \(K\), which implies \(M\gg\vartheta\) (a scale argument; the invariant \(m^2q^2\) vanishes on the null cone and is not the controlling quantity). In the range \(M\lesssim\vartheta\) no local two-derivative coefficient is defined by these forms, and the averaged-frame reading of the preceding paragraph lies there.

\emph{Null cone.} As \(q^2\to0\) at fixed \(q\cdot y\neq0\), \(\hat y_\perp\) tends to the direction of \(q\), helicity \(\pm2\) are the two transverse traceless polarisations with \(\bar h_{\mu\nu}y^\nu=0\), and their eigenvalues stay finite and equal: \(\mathrm{IX}_2\to-\tfrac{24}5\) and \(\mathrm{SC}_2\to\tfrac25\) (the exact forms approach these at nearly null \(q\)), so at the leading symbol the pole stays on the null cone of \(G\). At zeroth order in the background torsion, which by the preceding part requires \(M\gg\vartheta\), and for every \(K\) and every \(n\), these two polarisations therefore have one coefficient, the same for every null \(q\) (at fixed \(q\) the equality of the two values is the rotation about \(\hat q\))
\[
c_T^{\mathrm{TT}}=\tfrac16W_R^{(0)}+\tfrac7{30}W_R^{(1)}-2W_X^{(0)}-\tfrac{14}5W_X^{(2)},
\]
outside the hypothesis of five polarisations of the preceding paragraph; the other three keep distinct \(x\)-dependent values, so the tensor equation of the first law is not obtained. Eliminating the inert amplitudes through the induced forms alone, without the tree-level form, changes only helicity \(\pm1\) at three sampled cases, so it leaves this coefficient unchanged there. The first term shifts the cone: the helicity-two value \(8(x-1)\) contributes \((q\cdot y)^2\bar h\cdot\bar h\), and in the single-scale model the shift is of relative order \((L/c)\vartheta/(M^2W)\) with \(W=\sum_J(2J+1)/(M+\mu_J)\).

\emph{Sign and replicas.} The generators are traceless, so \(v^K\ge0\) as well as \(\tau^K\ge0\) by the Peierls--Bogoliubov inequality for Hermitian couplings (random non-commuting data), and \(B_0\ge B_1>0\) pointwise, \(B_1\) being the sum \(B_i\) of the preceding paragraph with the weight \(6\int_0^1a(1-a)e^{-s\Delta a}\,\mathrm da\) of the term \(F_1\) there. With \(V_i,T_i\) the integrals of \(s^{-2}v^K\), \(s^{-2}\tau^K\) against \(D\) for \(i=0\), \(B_1\) for \(i=1\), \(B_0\) for \(i=2\) (\(V_i,T_i\ge0\), assumed finite, and \(T_2\ge T_1\)),
\[
\tfrac16W_R-2W_X=\tfrac16V_0+\tfrac1{12}T_0>0,\qquad
c_T^{\mathrm{TT}}=\tfrac16V_0+\tfrac1{12}T_0+\tfrac7{30}(V_1-T_1)+\tfrac7{20}T_2>0
\]
for a non-zero flux: the sign of \(W_R\) is not fixed, but that of these coefficients at that order is, and no sign is claimed for \(M\lesssim\vartheta\). For commuting fluxes with \(f_1=f_2+f_3\) on every mode, \(v^K=\tau^K\), so that all \(W_R\) vanish. Against \(c_T^{\mathrm{TT}}\) the periodic and smooth replicas give \(c_A-c_T^{\mathrm{TT}}<0\) for a non-zero flux: \(-\tfrac14T_0-\tfrac7{30}(V_1-T_1)-\tfrac7{20}T_2\) and \(-\tfrac14V_0-\tfrac7{30}(V_1-T_1)-\tfrac7{20}T_2\). The mixed part of the fixed-state gap is \(-\tfrac7{30}(V_1-T_1)-\tfrac7{20}T_2<0\), so equality on these two polarisations needs the cutoff-dependent first-term cone contribution of the fixed-state replica, or contact and edge terms, to supply it.

These statements are for the hypotheses of the preceding paragraph, with the fibre-constant reduction for the Lichnerowicz form and first order in the torsion on fibre-constant modes for the exclusion of isolated data; the cross term is dropped, which on the null cone requires the squared components of \(mq\) to lie below the scale of \(K\). They do not compute the second variation of the order \(s^2\) density on the leaf, the torsion corrections on modes with \(J\ge1\), the fibre-flux part, the elimination of the inert amplitudes with the tree-level form, the trace polarisation or a Newtonian limit; they do not construct \(K\), do not make the Lorentzian evaluation more than formal, and do not identify \(\bar h\) with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence} or bear on Assumption~\ref{ass:lorentzian}, so no value of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\) and no sign of it for the emergent metric follows. Problems~B and~F remain open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class times a fuzzy factor: fibre-point forms, the level-one two-point function with the tree form, the equal-weight elimination of the fibre harmonics and the static response (conditional; bounded obstruction).}
This paragraph supplements the preceding one, in which the elimination with the tree-level form, the trace polarisation and a Newtonian limit were not computed. Keep its setting and notation, and take \(q=(q_0,0,0,k)\) at \(y^i=0\), so that \(q^2=k^2-q_0^2\), \(a=m^2q_0^2+\ell^2k^2\), \(b=m^2(q_0^2-k^2)\), \(\sinh^2\tau=m^2/\ell^2\) and \(\vartheta=1+\ell^2/m^2\). Below \(\alpha\) is any function of the fibre point with values in the frame directions, modulo the gauge directions \(\gamma(n)q\), so that \(q\cdot\alpha=0\) can be taken. All forms are for a real wave averaged over its phase.

\emph{Fibre-point forms.} At a fibre point, exactly, \(X_n=m^{-4}q^2(v\cdot v)(\alpha\cdot\alpha)\) and the scalar density is \(\mathrm{SC}_n=-\tfrac1{24}m^{-4}q^2[(v\cdot v)(\alpha\cdot\alpha)+2(\alpha\cdot v)^2]\). With \(h_n=m(\alpha\otimes v+v\otimes\alpha)\) and \(t_n=\eta_{\mu\nu}h_n^{\mu\nu}\) these are \(\tfrac12m^{-6}q^2(h_n\cdot h_n-\tfrac12t_n^2)\) and \(-\tfrac1{48}m^{-6}q^2(h_n\cdot h_n+\tfrac12t_n^2)\). Since \(q\cdot\alpha=q\cdot v=0\), the form \(L_2\) of the preceding paragraphs vanishes on all nine amplitudes \(D_{ij}\) with \(\gamma_i=0\). For \(\alpha=n^ic_i\), \(c_i=D_{ij}s_j\), the fibre average gives, for every \(D\), \(\mathrm{IX}_2=\tfrac1{15}m^{-4}q^2\sum_{ij}(\operatorname{tr}G\,\delta_{ij}+2G_{ij})\,c_i\cdot c_j\) and \(\mathrm{SC}_2=-\tfrac1{360}m^{-4}q^2[\operatorname{tr}G\operatorname{tr}P+2\operatorname{tr}GP+2(\operatorname{tr}DG)^2+2\operatorname{tr}DG^2D^{T}+2\operatorname{tr}DGDG]\), with \(P=DGD^{T}\) (exact rational arithmetic at nine cases, two with \(q\cdot y=0\)). These forms are polynomial in the amplitude \(c\). The term \(1/x\) of the preceding paragraph comes from dividing by \(\bar h\cdot\bar h\), which on helicity \(\pm2\) is \(\tfrac49m^2a\,c_i\cdot c_i\), so it needs no eliminated tower to explain it. Exactly, \(\operatorname{tr}G\,\delta_{ij}+2G_{ij}=5m^2[\delta_{ij}(q_0^2-\tfrac15k^2)-\tfrac25k_ik_j]+\ell^2k^2(4\delta_{ij}-2\hat q_i\hat q_j)\), so \(\mathrm{IX}_2\) has, up to the terms suppressed by \(1/\sinh^2\tau\), the bracket of Equation~(4.41) of~\cite{Steinacker2026}, including its transversal factor \(q_0^2-\tfrac15k^2\) and longitudinal factor \(q_0^2-\tfrac35k^2\); that work has the torsion term only, so the comparison is of \(\mathrm{IX}_2\) and of the structure, and the overall normalisations are not compared. With \(n\cdot\hat q=\cos\theta\) one has \(v\cdot v=a\sin^2\theta+b\cos^2\theta\), and the numbers \(\tfrac13\), \(\tfrac15\), \(\tfrac35\) are the averages of \(\cos^2\theta\) with the weights \(1\), \(n_\perp^2\) and \(n_\parallel^2\): they are moments of one fibre function, up to normalisation the symbol of the operator \(\tilde\Box\) of that work, taken on different fibre profiles.

\emph{Helicity \(\pm2\) at level one with the tree form.} Take \(S_E=\tfrac1{4g^2}\operatorname{Tr}F_{ab}F^{ab}\) with \(\operatorname{Tr}f=\int\mathrm d^4y\,\rho_M\langle f\rangle\), \(\langle\cdot\rangle\) the fibre average, so that the leading-symbol tree form is \(\tfrac{\rho_M}{4g^2}m^2(q^2\langle\alpha\cdot\alpha\rangle-\langle(q\cdot\alpha)^2\rangle)\); the coupling and \(\rho_M\) are supplied. With the weights of the preceding paragraph the induced coefficient relative to \(\mathrm{EH}(\bar h)\) on helicity \(\pm2\) is \(c_T^{\mathrm{TT}}+\kappa/x\), \(\kappa=\tfrac1{20}(V_1-T_1)+\tfrac3{20}T_2\ge\tfrac1{20}(V_1+2T_1)\). In the formal continuation \(S_L=-S_E\) with the Lorentzian \(\eta\), for \(c\cdot c=\sum_ic_i\cdot c_i\),
\[
S_L=c\cdot c\;Z\,(q_0^2-k^2)(q_0^2-w^2),\quad
Z=\frac{c_T^{\mathrm{TT}}+\kappa}{576\pi^2m^2},\quad
w^2=v^2k^2-\mu^2,\quad
v^2=\frac{\kappa-c_T^{\mathrm{TT}}\ell^2/m^2}{c_T^{\mathrm{TT}}+\kappa},\quad
\mu^2=\frac{48\pi^2\rho_Mm^4}{g^2(c_T^{\mathrm{TT}}+\kappa)},
\]
exactly for these forms, and \(1-v^2=c_T^{\mathrm{TT}}\vartheta/(c_T^{\mathrm{TT}}+\kappa)>0\). For \(V_i=T_i=T\), \(v^2=(m^2-4\ell^2)/5m^2\); the value \(\tfrac15\) at large \(\tau\) holds at these weights and, among equal weights \(V_i=V\), \(T_i=T\), only there, and in general the limit is \(\kappa/(c_T^{\mathrm{TT}}+\kappa)\). The factorisation and the sign of the tree term agree with Equation~(4.44) of~\cite{Steinacker2026}, whose extra factor has the mass term of Equation~(4.45), not compared in size with \(\mu^2\). At fixed \(k\) the coefficient of \(q_0^2-k^2\) at the massless root is \(Z(k^2-w^2)>0\) and that of \(q_0^2-w^2\) at the extra root is \(-Z(k^2-w^2)<0\), with \(k^2-w^2=(1-v^2)k^2+\mu^2\). The propagator of \(\bar h\) has the extra factor \(\tfrac49m^2a\), with \(a=m^2\vartheta k^2\) at the massless root, which gives the residue \(256\pi^2m^6/c_T^{\mathrm{TT}}\) for \(k^2\gg\mu^2\), independent of \(\kappa\), and \(a=m^2(w^2+k^2/\sinh^2\tau)>0\) at the extra root for \(w^2\ge0\). So within these hypotheses the massless root has the healthy sign and the extra root has the opposite sign in both variables. It has real frequency only for \(v^2k^2\ge\mu^2\) and is tachyonic otherwise. The one-loop term is \(-1\) times the tree term at the extra root for every \(k\), and \((1-v^2)k^2/\mu^2\) times it on the null cone, so the root is not inside an expansion in which the one-loop term is small compared with the tree term, while the Einstein regime on the null cone needs \((1-v^2)k^2\gg\mu^2\). Whether it is a propagating state therefore depends on the control of higher loops, for example by a large number of modes of \(K\), which is not established here. Adding \(-Z(V_{00}+V_{01}q_0^2)\), with the signs of Equation~(4.46) of~\cite{Steinacker2026}, does not change the opposite signs while the roots are real and distinct, since they are the signs of the derivative of a quadratic in \(q_0^2\) at its two roots; a complex pair needs \((k^2-w^2)^2+2V_{01}(k^2+w^2)+V_{01}^2+4V_{00}<0\), and to first order the root near \(k^2\) moves by \((V_{00}+V_{01}k^2)/(k^2-w^2)\). These terms are not computed here. That work states that the extra modes raise no issue of ghost-like signatures because they arise only after quantization; this is neither derived nor contradicted here.

\emph{Equal weights and all fibre harmonics.} Suppose all fibre transitions have the same proper-time weight, the equal-weight trace of the preceding paragraphs, which holds when \(M\) exceeds the fibre levels that enter; then \(V_i=V\), \(T_i=T\), with \(T>0\) for a non-zero flux and \(V>0\) and finiteness assumed. The induced form is then a multiplication operator on the fibre, so the elimination of the harmonics, which the preceding paragraphs left open, is pointwise. With \(C=q^2A/32\pi^2m^4\), \(A=\tfrac1{24}(V+2T)\), \(A+B=\tfrac18V\), the form at \(n\) is \(C[(v\cdot v)(\alpha\cdot\alpha)+\tfrac BA(\alpha\cdot v)^2]\) plus the tree term \(\tfrac{\rho_M}{4g^2}m^2q^2\alpha\cdot\alpha\). The component of \(\alpha\) orthogonal to \(v\) has one coefficient at every fibre point, \(4A=\tfrac16W_R-4W_X\) with \(W_R=V-T\), \(W_X=-T/8\), in the sense that its form is \(-\tfrac1{32\pi^2}\cdot4A\,\mathrm{EH}[h_n]\); for \(V=T\) the whole form at \(n\) is \(-\tfrac1{32\pi^2}\cdot\tfrac T2\) times the form \(-\tfrac18m^{-6}q^2(h_n\cdot h_n-\tfrac12t_n^2)\), which is the Einstein--Hilbert form of \(\hat h_n=h_n-\tfrac12\eta t_n\). For \(V=T\) the level-one extra root is a Rayleigh quotient of a continuum of roots \(q_0^2=k^2(\cos^2\theta-\sin^2\theta/\sinh^2\tau)-\varpi/m^2\), \(\varpi=8\pi^2\rho_Mm^6/g^2A\), over all \(\theta\). On polarisations with \(\alpha\cdot\alpha>0\) their sign is the one found above, and on \(\alpha\cdot\alpha<0\) the opposite. So the obstruction to a two-point function without a negative residue persists at equal weights, for the same reasons and with the same caveat.

Let a symmetric \(X\) couple through \(X_{\mu\nu}\bar h^{\mu\nu}=\langle2m\,\alpha\cdot Xv\rangle\). Eliminating \(\alpha\) gives \(-32\pi^2m^6I[X]/q^2A\) with, for \(\varrho=A/(A+B)=(V+2T)/3V\),
\[
I[X]=\Bigl\langle\frac{Xv\cdot Xv}{v\cdot v}\Bigr\rangle+(\varrho-1)\Bigl\langle\frac{(v\cdot Xv)^2}{(v\cdot v)^2}\Bigr\rangle,
\]
a principal or finite-part value where \(v\cdot v\) vanishes; this is the real part of the prescription \(q_0^2\to q_0^2+\mathrm i\epsilon\), whose imaginary part is not treated. The form \(c\,\mathrm{EH}\) with one coefficient \(c\), on the variable \(\hat h=\bar h-\tfrac12\eta\operatorname{tr}\bar h\), would give \(-64\pi^2m^6(X_3\cdot X_3-(\operatorname{tr}X_3)^2)/cq^2\), with \(X_3\) the projection of \(X\) on the three-space orthogonal to \(q\). On the null cone with \(X\) transverse and traceless, \(I=\tfrac14(1+\varrho)X\cdot X\) exactly, so the coefficient is \(c^{\mathrm{TT}}=8A/(1+\varrho)=V(V+2T)/2(2V+T)\), which is \(T/2\) at \(V=T\) against the level-one value \((2V+T)/5\), which is larger for all \(V,T>0\). A truncation to fibre orders \(J\le N\) reproduces the level-one value at \(N=1\) and tends to this one (numerical, up to \(N=25\)).

\emph{Static response.} Matter coupling is not derived in this ledger, so two couplings are recorded for dust \(T^{\mathrm m}_{\mu\nu}=\varrho_0u_\mu u_\nu\), \(u\cdot u=-1\), \(q\cdot u=0\). (S1) The source couples to \(\hat h\) as \(T^{\mathrm m}_{\mu\nu}\hat h^{\mu\nu}\), the form of the matter variation in Equation~(3.9) of~\cite{Steinacker2026}, so that \(X=T^{\mathrm m}-\tfrac12\eta\operatorname{tr}T^{\mathrm m}\); it then satisfies \(X\eta^{-1}X=\tfrac14\varrho_0^2\eta\), hence \(Xv\cdot Xv=\tfrac14\varrho_0^2v\cdot v\). (S2) The source couples to \(\bar h\) with \(X=T^{\mathrm m}\), the Poisson-limit source of a mode with action \(\int\rho_M(\gamma^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\phi\partial_\nu\phi+M^2\phi^2)\) and a rigid mass from \(K\). Put \(c_0=1/\cosh\tau\) and \(g(\tau)=\vartheta^{-1}(1-c_0\operatorname{arctanh}c_0)\), the principal value of \(\langle(v^0)^2/(-v\cdot v)\rangle\) in the rest frame of \(y\) at \(q\cdot y=0\); it tends to \(1\) with \(1-g\simeq2/\sinh^2\tau\).

At level one, for \(q\cdot y=0\) and \(D=\mathrm{diag}(d_\perp,d_\perp,d_q)\), the block with helicity \(0\) and the trace polarisation, the response with the weights of the preceding paragraph has, as \(\ell/m\to0\) at fixed weights, a ratio to the one-coefficient value \(-64\pi^2m^6(T^{\mathrm m}\cdot T^{\mathrm m}-\tfrac12(\operatorname{tr}T^{\mathrm m})^2)/cq^2\) at \(c=c_T^{\mathrm{TT}}\) of \(10c_T^{\mathrm{TT}}/(5V_0+4(V_1-T_1)+4T_2)>0\) for (S1) and four times this for (S2). The helicity-\(0\) eigenvalue relative to \(\mathrm{EH}(\bar h)\) does tend to \(-\sinh^2\tau[\tfrac1{60}(V_1-T_1)+\tfrac1{20}T_2]<0\), but the source does not act on that polarisation alone: tracelessness forces \(d_\perp=m^2d_q/2\ell^2\), whereas at large \(\tau\) the source acts on \(d_q\), whose diagonal entry in the block is positive at large \(\tau\). So at late times the sign of the static response is that of \(c_T^{\mathrm{TT}}\). It is not so at every \(\tau\): for \(V_i=T_i=T\) and (S1) the ratio is \(2(\sinh^4\tau-10\sinh^2\tau+4)/(\sinh^2\tau-4)(3\sinh^2\tau-2)\), with poles at \(\sinh^2\tau=\tfrac23,4\), zeros at \(\sinh^2\tau=5\pm\sqrt{21}\), negative for \(5-\sqrt{21}<\sinh^2\tau<\tfrac23\) and \(4<\sinh^2\tau<5+\sqrt{21}\), tending to \(\tfrac23\); and for (S2) it is \(8\sinh^2\tau/(3\sinh^2\tau-2)\), tending to \(\tfrac83\).

With all harmonics and equal weights the level-one defect is removed for (S1) at \(V=T\): then \(\varrho=1\) and \(I=\tfrac14\varrho_0^2\) for every \(q\) with \(q\cdot u=0\), every base point and every \(\tau\), without a principal value, which is the value of the one-coefficient form at \(c=T/2=c^{\mathrm{TT}}\) since \(X_3\cdot X_3-(\operatorname{tr}X_3)^2=T^{\mathrm m}\cdot T^{\mathrm m}-\tfrac12(\operatorname{tr}T^{\mathrm m})^2=\tfrac12\varrho_0^2\). So at \(V=T\), and with that coupling, the static dust response and the transverse-traceless pole have one coefficient, with the attractive sign. Solving for \(\alpha\) at rest gives \(\hat h^{00}\) independent of \(\tau\) and \(\hat h^{11}/\hat h^{00}=g(\tau)\), which is the ratio of the two potentials, read in \(\hat h\), the variable to which this source couples, once the part along \(\hat q\hat q\) is removed as a gauge term, and it tends to \(1\) as \(\tau\to\infty\). For \(V\neq T\) and (S1), \(I=\tfrac14\varrho_0^2[1+(\varrho-1)K(\tau)]\) with \(K\) a closed function tending to \(1\), so at large \(\tau\) the coefficient is \(V/2\) and the response is \((V+2T)/(2V+T)\) times the one-coefficient value at \(c=c^{\mathrm{TT}}\); that ratio of potentials still tends to \(1\) (numerical at two values of \(\varrho\)). For (S2) at \(V=T\), \(I=\varrho_0^2g(\tau)\): the response is \(4g(\tau)\) times the one-coefficient value at \(c=c^{\mathrm{TT}}\), and \(\bar h^{ab}=0\) on the two directions orthogonal to \(q\) and \(u\), so, read in \(\bar h\), the variable to which this source couples, the second potential vanishes and the ratio is \(0\) (in \(\hat h\) the same solution has ratio \(1\); the sum of the two potentials is the same in both readings). So within these hypotheses the value \(1\) of the ratio so read depends on the coupling of massive modes to the scale of \(K\): (S1) is, on the mass shell, the source of the same action with \(M^2\propto\rho/\sqrt{|g|}\), \(\rho\) the density of the preceding paragraph, which is not derived. Beyond dust the agreement fails: for \(T^{\mathrm m}=\varrho_0uu+p(e_1e_1+e_2e_2)\) in (S1) at \(V=T\), \(I=\tfrac14\varrho_0^2+g(\tau)(\varrho_0p+p^2)\), against \(\tfrac14\varrho_0^2+\varrho_0p\), and on helicity \(\pm2\) at \(V=T\), away from the null cone, \(I\) carries the factor \(\tfrac{x}{x-1}\bigl[1-\operatorname{arctanh}\sqrt{(x-1)/x}\,/\sqrt{x(x-1)}\bigr]\) for \(x>1\), and \(1-g\) at \(q\cdot y=0\), so a single coefficient for all \(q\) and all sources is not obtained. With the tree term at \(V=T\), (S1) and \(q\cdot y=0\), the factor is \(\langle v\cdot v/(v\cdot v+\varpi)\rangle\) as a principal value, which has the structure of \(\tilde\Box/(\tilde\Box-\varpi/m^2)\) resolved on the fibre, the mass in the inverse of the factor in Equation~(3.25) of~\cite{Steinacker2026} being read as \(\varpi/m^2\), a normalisation not compared; for \(\varpi\ll m^2k^2\) at large \(\tau\) it is \(1-\varpi/m^2k^2\) to first order, against \(1+3\varpi/m^2k^2\) for the fibre average of \(\tilde\Box\) with which that work reads its static halo, and it tends to zero as \(k\to0\) as there. This difference of sign is conditional on these hypotheses, on the principal value and on dropping the fibre Laplacian, which by a scale estimate requires \(\varpi\gg1\); it may be an artefact of the frozen symbol or of the equal-weight trace, and that work itself states that the higher-spin parts were ignored in that derivation, so no conclusion on that work is drawn.

These statements are for the hypotheses of the preceding paragraph, zeroth order in the background torsion, the leading symbol for the loop and for the tree form, which drops non-derivative terms and the fibre Laplacian and on the null cone holds up to a shift of relative order \(\vartheta/m^2k^2\), the formal continuation with the Lorentzian \(\eta\), and, for the elimination of all harmonics, a weight independent of the fibre orders of the external amplitudes, which is an assumption outside \(M\) above the levels that enter. The forms are exact as stated; the eliminations are exact at these hypotheses apart from the principal values and the numerical checks stated. They do not compute the fibre Laplacian, the cross term, higher loops or \(V_{00},V_{01}\), do not derive a matter coupling, the scale of \(K\) or the condition \(V=T\), and do not construct \(K\). They do not compare the modified Einstein equations of~\cite{KumarSteinacker2023,Steinacker2026} beyond the equations named, do not identify \(\bar h\), \(\hat h\) or \(h_n\) with the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}, and do not bear on Assumption~\ref{ass:lorentzian}, so no value of \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\), no bound of Cassini type and no statement on the presence or absence of a propagating ghost for the emergent metric follow. Problems~C, F and~N remain open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\paragraph{Conjugate-coordinate class times a fuzzy factor: response of the scale of \(K\) to the frame, the exponent of the \(K\) masses and the scalar channel of the static response (conditional; bounded obstruction).}
This paragraph supplements the preceding one, in which the coupling of massive modes to the scale of \(K\) was recorded as the two alternatives (S1), (S2) and not derived. Keep its setting and notation, with \(V=T\), equal weights and \(q\cdot y=0\) where they are used, and write \(X^i=\chi\Lambda^i\) with fixed Hermitian \(\Lambda^i\), \(\bar a=-\tfrac14\operatorname{tr}[\Lambda^i,\Lambda^j]^2\ge0\), \(\bar n=\operatorname{tr}\Lambda^i\Lambda_i\), \([\Lambda^i,[\Lambda_i,\Lambda_A]]=\mu_A^2\Lambda_A\), \(\gamma^{\mu\nu}=\eta_{ab}E^{a\mu}E^{b\nu}\), \(g=\gamma^{-1}\) and \(\phi=\tfrac12\ln(\rho_M/\sqrt{|g|})\), with \(\rho_M\) taken proportional to the density \(\rho\) of the preceding paragraphs, so that \(\phi\) is the field there up to a constant. A mode \(\psi\Lambda_A\) has the mass \(M^2=\chi^2\mu_A^2\) in the operator \(\gamma^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\partial_\nu\) of the preceding paragraph. Define the exponent \(\beta_K\) by \(\delta\ln M^2=2\beta_K\delta\phi=\beta_Kt/2m^2\). On the mass shell the source of a free massive mode is then \(X_\beta=T^{\mathrm m}-\tfrac12\beta_K\eta\operatorname{tr}T^{\mathrm m}\), which couples to \(h_\beta=\bar h-\tfrac12\beta_K\eta t\), the perturbation of \(e^{-2\beta_K\phi}\gamma\); (S1) is \(\beta_K=1\) and (S2) is \(\beta_K=0\).

\emph{Tree level.} For a product the operator on \(\psi\Lambda_A\) is the sum of the operator on \(M\) and \(\chi^2\mu_A^2\), and, with \(g^2\) in \(\rho_M/g^2\) the coupling of the tree form of the preceding paragraph, the tree form is exactly \(\tfrac{\rho_M}{2g^2}\operatorname{tr}(\Lambda_A^2)(\gamma^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\psi\partial_\nu\psi+\chi^2\mu_A^2\psi^2)\). So at fixed \(\Lambda^i\) and \(\chi\) the mass is rigid in \(\gamma\), which is (S2), and in the frame \(e^{-2\phi}\gamma\), the effective metric of~\cite{Steinacker2026}, it is \(e^{-2\phi}\chi^2\mu_A^2\), the factor stated in~\cite{MantaSteinacker2025b}. At fixed \(\chi\) a loop carries by power counting one factor \(g^2\sqrt{|g|}/\rho_M=g^2e^{-2\phi}\), a trace over \(M\) against the weight \(\rho_M/g^2\), so corrections to \(M^2\) at \(\ell\) loops have the exponent \(-\ell\). So, while the loop expansion is controlled, a value \(\beta_K\) of order one can come only from the response of \(\chi\); a loop correction of negative sign contributes a small positive part.

\emph{Scaling.} Under \(X^a\to\sqrt\lambda X^a\), the operators of the content of the preceding paragraphs are exactly \(\lambda H_M+\chi^2H_K\). Since the supertrace of the identity vanishes, the one-loop functional, where its proper-time form converges, depends on \(\lambda,\chi\) only through \(\chi^2/\lambda\) (random product matrices, agreement to twelve digits; the invariance under a common rescaling is used in~\cite{MantaSteinacker2025b}). At one loop alone \(\delta\ln\chi=\tfrac12\delta\ln\lambda\) is a flat direction, and for this homogeneous mode \(\phi=\ln\lambda\) up to a constant, so its exponent is \(\tfrac12\).

\emph{Potential of \(\chi\).} From the factorisation of the preceding paragraphs, with equal weights, \(F=(n+1)^2\) and hats for the unit scale \(\chi=1\), the terms of the action density without derivatives of the wave that depend on \(\chi\), through first order in the background torsion, are
\[
\frac{\rho_M}{g^2}V_{\mathrm t}(\chi)+U_4+U_2,\qquad
U_4=-\frac{F}{32\pi^2}\sqrt{|g|}\,\hat U\chi^4,\qquad
U_2=-\frac{F}{32\pi^2}\sqrt{|g|}\Bigl[2(\hat V-\hat T)-\tfrac34\vartheta(\hat V-2\hat T)\Bigr]\chi^2,
\]
with \(\hat U=\int_0^\infty\mathrm ds\,s^{-3}(\hat v^K-\hat\tau^K)(s)\), and \(V=F\hat V\chi^2\), \(T=F\hat T\chi^2\) at equal weights. Here \(V_{\mathrm t}\) contains \(\bar a\chi^4\) and any supplied stabiliser, and \(U_2\) uses \(R/6+E_0=2-\tfrac34\vartheta\) and the index density \(-6\vartheta\) of the background. This has the structure of Equation~(7.48) of~\cite{Steinacker2023}, including the negative sign of the torsion term in the Lorentzian section. At the next order the first term \(s_0^K(v^M-2\sigma^M)\) of the factorisation enters: with the fibre flux dropped, at the frozen symbol \(v^M-2\sigma^M\) has the value \(\tfrac{15}{4}\vartheta^2F\sqrt{|g|}/16\pi^2\) at order \(s^0\) on the background, an order that has no term linear or quadratic in the curvature, as recorded in the preceding paragraphs. So the derivative of the density with respect to \(\ln\chi\) gains \(Y=\tfrac{15F}{64\pi^2}\vartheta^2\sqrt{|g|}N_K\ge0\), with \(N_K\) the number of modes of \(K\) with \(\chi^2\mu_A^2\gg\vartheta\). It is not suppressed by the flux of \(K\) or at large \(N\); on \(S^2_N\) at large \(N\), \(Y/2|U_2|=\tfrac54\vartheta/\chi^2\). The fibre flux in \(Y\) is treated below. The background Feynman gauge is used (Hessian checked by finite differences); its dependence at the stationary point is of higher order by the usual argument, not computed. The value \(U_2\) above is the order \(\vartheta\) of the mixed term, and on \(S^2_N\) the mixed term with all orders of the frozen symbol agrees with it (ratio above \(0.9999\) at \(N\ge256\) for \(\chi^2\ge0.1\vartheta\)). At \(q\cdot y=q\cdot v=0\) the product \(\bar T\cdot\delta T\) vanishes, so with \(\bar h^{22}=\bar h^{11}\) one has \(\delta\ln(\sqrt{|g|}X)=\Omega/m^2\), \(\Omega=\tfrac12\bar h^{00}-\tfrac53\bar h^{11}\), and \(\Omega=-\tfrac12t\) where \(\bar h^{11}\) is negligible, which holds at large \(\tau\) (below). Write \(U\) for this density with the first term and \(U_{\chi\chi}=\partial^2U/\partial(\ln\chi)^2\) at a stationary point. Since \(\bar T^{ab\mu}y_\mu=0\), a perturbation along \(y^\mu y^\nu\) leaves the Gaussian average over the loop momentum unchanged, so where \(\bar h^{11}\) is negligible \(U_4\), \(U_2\) and the fibre-constant part of \(Y\) carry \(\sqrt{|g|}\) alone. For \(q^2\) below the mass of \(\chi\) defined below and \(V=T\), the first-order response gives in this static channel at large \(\tau\)
\[
\beta_K=\frac{2(4U_4+2U_2+Y)}{U_{\chi\chi}}=-\frac{2\,\partial_{\ln\chi}(\rho_MV_{\mathrm t}/g^2)}{U_{\chi\chi}},
\]
and for the homogeneous mode, with \(\partial Y/\partial\ln\chi\) neglected, \(\beta_K=(8U_4+2U_2)/U_{\chi\chi}\), which is \(\tfrac12\) for \(V_{\mathrm t}=0\). Hence at a local minimum, \(U_{\chi\chi}>0\), \(\beta_K\) has the sign opposite to the slope of the tree term; for \(U_4\le0\) and \(U_2<0\) one has \(\beta_K<0\) for every \(V_{\mathrm t}\) exactly where \(Y<4|U_4|+2|U_2|\), in particular for \(\chi^2\hat T\gg\vartheta N_K\). With \(\tilde U=\rho_M\bar a\chi^4/g^2\) and \(\varepsilon=U_4/\tilde U\): for \(V_{\mathrm t}=\bar a\chi^4\), \(\beta_K=-1/(1+\varepsilon-Y/4\tilde U)\); adding a tree cubic term, of the form of the cubic term of Equation~\eqref{eq:Smatrix} read on \(K\) and supplied there, gives \(\beta_K=2(4U_4+2U_2+Y)/(4\tilde U+4U_4-2U_2-3Y)\); adding a tree mass term gives \(\beta_K=(4U_4+2U_2+Y)/(4\tilde U+4U_4-Y)\), with \(\partial Y/\partial\ln\chi\) neglected in these three forms. Where the tree stabiliser dominates, \(\beta_K\to0\), from below where the slope of the tree term is positive and from above where it is negative. For \(Y\) negligible, \(\beta_K>0\) needs \(U_4>0\), so \(\hat U<0\); then \(\beta_K=1\) is attained in the cubic case at \(U_4=\tilde U-\tfrac32U_2\) and approached from below in the mass case as \(U_4\) comes to dominate \(\tilde U\) and \(U_2\), each with a tree term of negative slope. In the rotating class of~\cite{MantaSteinacker2025b}, Equation~(3.37) there gives \(\chi\propto\lambda^{-1/6}\) at fixed charge and fixed \(\tau\), the exponent \(-\tfrac16\) for the homogeneous mode; the fluctuation operator about a rotating \(K\) is not treated here.

\emph{Fibre flux in the first term.} On the background \(\mathbb F_{ab}\) is the action of the generators \(J_{ab}\) of \(\mathfrak{so}(3,1)\), which commute with \(\Box\). Write \(\mathfrak{so}(3,1)\) over \(\mathbb C\) as \(\mathfrak{su}(2)\oplus\mathfrak{su}(2)\), so that the vector is \((\tfrac12,\tfrac12)\) and the Dirac spinor is \((\tfrac12,0)\oplus(0,\tfrac12)\). Write \(H_i=H_0+Z\) for the vector and for the Dirac index, so that \(Z=C_{31}^{\mathrm{tot},i}-C_{31}-C_{\Sigma_i}\) in the form of the preceding paragraphs, with \(C_{31}=\tfrac12J_{ab}J^{ab}\) for real generators; the sign is fixed by \(H_VT_a=3H_0T_a\), that is \(Z=6\) on the singlet of the orbital \(T_a\) with the vector index. On an irreducible orbital component with labels \(j_\pm\), \(\Xi_\pm=2j_\pm+1\), and per state, with \(P(\Xi)=e^{-s}[2\cosh s\Xi+2\Xi^{-1}\sinh s\Xi]\), exactly,
\[
\operatorname{tr}_De^{-sZ}=P(\Xi_+)+P(\Xi_-),\qquad
\operatorname{tr}_Ve^{-sZ}=P(\Xi_+)P(\Xi_-),\qquad
\operatorname{tr}_Ve^{-sZ}-2\operatorname{tr}_De^{-sZ}+4=\bigl(P(\Xi_+)-2\bigr)\bigl(P(\Xi_-)-2\bigr)
\]
(on each chiral block \((Z-1)^2=\Xi^2\) and the trace of \(Z\) over the block vanishes, in any representation; explicit vector and gamma matrices at finite-dimensional labels). On a unitary principal-series component \(\Xi_\pm=\pm m_L+\mathrm i\varsigma\), with \(m_L\) an integer and \(\varsigma\) real, so the two factors are complex conjugates and the integrand of the first term is \(|P(\Xi_+)-2|^2\ge0\) on each component, for every \(s\) and with every fibre flux included. At fibre order \(J\), at a frozen base point, the rotations about \(y\) act as spin \(J\), \(|m_L|\le J\), the density of \((m_L,\varsigma)\) is the Plancherel density \(\varsigma^2+m_L^2\), the squared boost is \(\varsigma^2+1-m_L^2+c_J\) with \(c_J=J(J+1)\), and \(H_0\) is the squared boost divided by \(\vartheta\) plus \(\mu_J\), with the cross term dropped and the direction along \(y\) a spectator. With \(M_A^2=\chi^2\mu_A^2\), the proper time is small where \(M_A^2+\mu_J\gg\vartheta\), and there the average of the integrand over these labels is \(s^2[\tfrac{15}4\vartheta^2-s\vartheta c_J+s^2c_J^2]\), positive, with corrections of relative order \(s\) and \(s\vartheta\) that do not depend on \(J\) at this order (the integral over \(m_L\) is exact in the limit of large \(J\) at fixed \(sJ^2\); the exact average agrees). Hence
\[
Y=\frac{\sqrt{|g|}}{32\pi^2}\sum_A\sum_{J\le n}(2J+1)\,2M_A^2\Bigl[\frac{15\vartheta^2}{4\mathcal D}-\frac{\vartheta c_J}{\mathcal D^2}+\frac{2c_J^2}{\mathcal D^3}\Bigr],\qquad\mathcal D=M_A^2+\mu_J,
\]
the sum running over the modes with \(M_A\ne0\) and \(\mathcal D\gg\vartheta\), every summand positive (the bracket times \(\mathcal D^3\) is a positive definite quadratic form in \(\vartheta\mathcal D\) and \(c_J\)), which reduces to the value above for \(M_A^2\gg\mu_n\). On \(S^2_N\) its relative correction is of order \((n/\chi N)^2\) with a logarithm, the condition for equal weights below (the ratio to the value above is \(0.998\) at \(N=1024\), \(n=10\), \(\vartheta=1\), \(\chi^2=0.3\)), and the fibre-flux part of the mixed term contributes \(4(2J+1)c_J\) at level \(J\) to \(\partial U/\partial\ln\chi\) in the same units for \(\mu_J\gg\chi^2\), and less for smaller \(\mu_J\) (numerical), of relative order at most \((n/N)^2\). The cross term commutes with \(J_{ab}\), so it leaves the non-negativity of the integrand unchanged, but it changes the weights at relative order \(sJ^2\), the order of the \(J\)-dependent terms of this form: those terms hold only with it dropped, and what carries over is their order. So at equal weights the fibre flux does not change the leading value of \(Y\) or its sign on the background. The response of the fibre levels to \(\bar h\) and the part of the first term with two fibre fluxes and two base derivatives of the wave are not computed.

\emph{Explicit \(K\).} For the fuzzy sphere \(S^2_N\), \(\Lambda^i=J^i\) in three of the six directions, the spectra are closed: the vector \(l(l-1)\), \(l(l+1)+2\), \((l+1)(l+2)\) and the spinor \(l^2\), \((l+1)^2\), without negative mode; \(\bar a=N(N^2-1)/8\), \(\bar n=N(N^2-1)/4\), and \(\chi\) is the only singlet of its symmetry group, so the masses share one exponent at this order. Numerically \(\hat U>0\) with \(\hat U/N^2\to1\), \(\hat T/N^2\to4\) and \(\hat V-\hat T\simeq4\ln N+1.016\) (exact spectra, \(N\le512\)), so \(U_4<0\), \(U_2<0\) and, at large \(N\) with \(x=\chi^2/\vartheta\) in this part, not the ratio \(a/b\) of the preceding paragraphs, and \(N^2\chi^2\gg\vartheta\), \(4U_4+2U_2+Y\) is proportional to \(-4x^2-6x+\tfrac{15}2\), positive for \(x<(\sqrt{39}-3)/4\simeq0.81\). For \(V_{\mathrm t}=\bar a\chi^4\) a stationary point then exists only for \(|\varepsilon|\ge\tfrac{10}{13}\) and a local minimum only for \(\tfrac{10}{13}<|\varepsilon|<1\), with \(x>\tfrac52\) and \(\beta_K<-1\), so there is none at weak coupling. With a tree cubic term \(\beta_K=1\) is attained at a local minimum with \(\hat U>0\): in units \(F\sqrt{|g|}N^2\vartheta^2/32\pi^2\), at \(x=\tfrac3{10}\), a tree quartic \(7.935\) and a tree cubic \(-12.36\) give \(U_{\chi\chi}=10.68\) and \(|\varepsilon|\simeq0.011\). These tree values are supplied, and the tree quartic there is of the order of \(Y\), so the one-loop term is not small against the tree term, and higher loops are not computed. The sign \(\hat U>0\) holds also for the sampled \(S^2_N\times S^2_{N'}\) and the squashed \(\mathrm{su}(3)\) orbits (a finite list, no general statement). For modes between stacks with constant relative fluxes \(f_1\ge f_2,f_3\ge0\), exactly, \(v-\tau=16s_0\prod_\pm\sinh\tfrac12(f_1\pm f_2\pm f_3)s\), a product over the four sign choices, so for \(f_1<f_2+f_3\), the range without a negative mode, the integrands of \(\hat U\) and \(\hat V-\hat T\) are negative for every \(s\), and for \(f_1=f_2+f_3\), \(v\equiv\tau\). These integrals diverge at small \(s\) for such non-compact stacks, so \(\hat U<0\) and \(\hat V<\hat T\) are signs with a lower limit on \(s\); the scaling \(U_4\propto\chi^4\) then needs a limit that scales as \(\chi^{-2}\), such as a fixed truncation of the modes, for which the sign is not established. On this evidence the sign of \(\hat U\) depends on \(K\).

\emph{Scale dependence.} With the tree kinetic coefficient \(Z_\chi=\rho_M\chi^2\bar nm^2/g^2\) of \(\ln\chi\) (the loop part is not computed), \(\ln\chi\) has the mass \(m_\chi^2=U_{\chi\chi}/Z_\chi\) in \(q^2\), and integrating it out replaces \(\beta_K\) by \(\beta_Km_\chi^2/(q^2+m_\chi^2)\), with a contact term \(-2\varrho_0^2m^4/Z_\chi(q^2+m_\chi^2)\). So \(M^2\) is rigid in \(\gamma\) above \(m_\chi\), for every \(K\). For \(V_{\mathrm t}=\bar a\chi^4\), \(m^2m_\chi^2=8(1+\varepsilon-Y/4\tilde U)\bar a\chi^2/\bar n\), which is \(4(1+\varepsilon-Y/4\tilde U)\chi^2\) on \(S^2_N\). So (S2) holds above \(m_\chi\) and approximately where a tree stabiliser dominates, and below \(m_\chi\) the value (S1) is a condition on the tree stabiliser and on \(\chi^2/\vartheta\), not an output of this class.

\emph{Scalar channel of the static response.} For dust and \(X_\beta\), with \(g_2=\vartheta^{-2}[1-\tfrac32c_0\operatorname{arctanh}c_0-\tfrac12(\vartheta-1)]\) the finite part of \(\langle(v^0)^4/(v\cdot v)^2\rangle\), in addition to the function \(g=g(\tau)\) of the preceding paragraph (in the next display \(g^2\) is its square, not the coupling),
\[
\frac{I[X_\beta]}{\varrho_0^2}=\varrho\bigl(\tfrac12\beta_K-g\bigr)^2+g-g_2+\varrho\,(g_2-g^2),
\]
with \(g-g_2\simeq2/\sinh^2\tau\) and \(g_2-g^2\simeq-\tfrac43\sinh^{-4}\tau\), which tends to \(\varrho(1-\tfrac12\beta_K)^2\) as \(\tau\to\infty\) (regulated quadrature agrees). So the response relative to the one-coefficient value is \(4g(1-\beta_K)+\beta_K^2\) at \(\varrho=1\), which reproduces \(1\) and \(4g\) for (S1) and (S2). At \(\varrho=1\), \(\bar h^{00}\), \(\bar h^{11}\) and \(t\) are proportional to \((2-\beta_K)g\), \(\tfrac12\beta_K(1-g)\) and \(\beta_K-2g\), and the ratio of the potentials read in the variable to which the source couples is
\[
\frac{h_\beta^{11}}{h_\beta^{00}}=\frac{\beta_K(1+g-\beta_K)}{4g(1-\beta_K)+\beta_K^2}\ \longrightarrow\ \frac{\beta_K}{2-\beta_K},
\]
while \(\bar h^{00}+\bar h^{11}\) does not depend on the reading. At large \(\tau\) the source therefore acts through \(t\) alone: with \(L=32\pi^2m^6/A\) the reduced form is \(S_E=(q^2/4L)t^2-(1-\tfrac12\beta_K)\varrho_0t\), the phase average of the density \((A/4\pi^2)\sqrt{|g|}g^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\phi\partial_\nu\phi\) in this static channel, with the positive sign in \(S_E\); \(t\) is gauge invariant here, since \(q\cdot v=0\), and the matter charge is \(1-\tfrac12\beta_K\). With a term \(\tfrac12\mathcal Mt^2\), \(q_*^2=2\mathcal ML\), at \(\varrho=1\) the response is \(-L\varrho_0^2[(\tfrac12\beta_K-g)^2/(q^2+q_*^2)+g(1-g)/q^2]\). A heavy \(t\) therefore leaves only the part with \(g(1-g)\simeq2/\sinh^2\tau\) and ratio \(\tfrac12\) for every \(\beta_K\). A light \(t\) gives \(\beta_K/(2-\beta_K)\), so the value \(1\) at large \(\tau\) needs \(\beta_K=1\), and \(1-\beta_K/(2-\beta_K)=2(1-\beta_K)/(2-\beta_K)\).

\emph{Vacuum term.} At a fibre point the frame perturbation has rank one, so \(\sqrt{|g_n|}=m^{-4}(1+x_n)^{-1}\) with \(x_n\) proportional to \(\alpha\cdot v/m\) exactly, and for a real wave the second-order part of \(U_4\) is \(\tfrac12V_0m^{-6}(\alpha\cdot v)^2\), \(V_0=-F\hat U\chi^4/32\pi^2\), not the weight \(V_0\) of the preceding paragraphs. This is the expansion of Equation~(4.27) of~\cite{Steinacker2026}, with its moments, and it enters the form at \(n\) of the preceding paragraph as a shift \(B/A\to B/A-q_b^2/q^2\), \(q_b^2=12\hat U\chi^2/m^2(\hat V+2\hat T)\), which is \(\chi^2/m^2\) on \(S^2_N\) at large \(N\). No term \((v\cdot v)(\alpha\cdot\alpha)\) arises, so on the component of \(\alpha\) orthogonal to \(v\) this term gives no mass. Then \(\varrho\to q^2\varrho/(q^2-\varrho q_b^2)\), and at \(V=T\)
\[
-L\varrho_0^2\Bigl[\frac{(\tfrac12\beta_K-g)^2}{q^2-q_b^2+q_*^2}+\frac{g-g_2}{q^2}+\frac{g_2-g^2}{q^2-q_b^2}\Bigr].
\]
So the forms of the preceding paragraph hold for \(q^2\gg|q_b^2|\). Below that scale, for \(\hat U>0\) the static scalar pole has the tachyonic sign unless \(q_*^2\) exceeds \(q_b^2\), and for \(\hat U<0\) and \(\mathcal M\ge0\) it is a Yukawa pole. That work uses \(V_{01}>0\) from \(S_{\mathrm{vac}}=-\int\rho_M\rho^{-2}V_0\) in Equation~(4.46), and in the passages read does not state the sign of \(V_0\); with the same convention the sampled \(K\) with \(\hat U>0\) have \(V_0<0\), normalisations not compared, so no conclusion on that work is drawn. The tree non-derivative terms, the terms that cancel the tadpole of \(U_4\) at \(q=0\), and any term with the same fibre dependence are not computed, and a long-range form needs them or \(\hat U\to0\); where \(\chi^2\lesssim\vartheta\) the second-order non-derivative terms of \(U_2\) and of the first term are of the size of this one and are among those not computed.

\emph{The inputs \(V=T\) and equal weights.} \(V\) and \(T\) both scale as \(\chi^2\), so the response of \(\chi\) does not move the condition \(V=T\). On \(S^2_N\), \((V-T)/T\simeq(4\ln N+1.016)/4N^2\to0\) (numerical), so \(V=T\) is approached at large \(N\) for this \(K\) and does not need a tuned \(K\); it holds exactly at \(f_1=f_2+f_3\). At \(V=T\), \(U_2=-(3F/128\pi^2)\vartheta\hat T\sqrt{|g|}\chi^2<0\), so for \(\hat U\ge0\) and \(Y\le4|U_4|+2|U_2|\), (S1) and \(V=T\) are incompatible at a local minimum: there the sign of the torsion term that supplies the minimum makes \(\beta_K\) non-positive. For \(\hat U\ge0\) and \(Y>4|U_4|+2|U_2|\), \(\beta_K>0\), and its value is set by the tree term of negative slope. On \(S^2_N\) the weights of \(D\) reach \(F\hat V\chi^2\), \(F\hat T\chi^2\) up to a relative correction of order \((n/\chi N)^2\) with a logarithm, with ratios \(0.9994\) at \(N=1024\), \(n=10\), \(\chi=1\), and those of \(B_0,B_1\) carry a common factor from the truncation \(J'\le n\), about \(1-3/2n\) (\(0.8634\) there), the same for \(V_1,T_1,T_2\). So equal weights among the loop levels hold for \(\chi N\gg n\gg1\) on this \(K\). The hypothesis on the external harmonics above \(J=1\) is not tested.

\emph{Consequence.} Within these hypotheses a static, late-time response with ratio \(1\), read in the variable to which the source couples, requires a light \(t\) and \(\beta_K=1\), and the computed exponent is negative for \(\hat U\ge0\) where \(Y<4|U_4|+2|U_2|\). With a light \(t\) and \(\beta_K<0\) the deviation of this ratio from \(1\) exceeds \(1\), and lies in \((1,\tfrac43]\) for \(\beta_K\in[-1,0)\), to be set against the value \((2.1\pm2.3)\times10^{-5}\) of \(\gamma-1\) in~\cite{Bertotti2003}. This is not a Cassini bound on the emergent metric, since solar-system scales lie where \(q^2\ll|q_b^2|\) and the non-derivative terms are not computed. The value \(\beta_K=1\) needs a tree term of negative slope at the minimum and, for \(\hat U\ge0\), \(Y>4|U_4|+2|U_2|\), which at large \(N\) on \(S^2_N\) is \(\chi^2<(\sqrt{39}-3)\vartheta/4\); for \(\hat U<0\), where \(q_b^2<0\), it is available at negligible \(Y\). In each case the relation among the tree data that sets it is not derived, and the vacuum term must be cancelled at the minimum without cancelling its derivative in \(\chi\).

These statements are for the hypotheses of the preceding paragraph, the product background without mixed flux, one loop, the formal continuation and the frozen symbol, for scales of \(q\) as stated; the first-order response in the static channel at \(q\cdot y=0\), with \(V=T\) where the index part alone enters \(U_2\) and with \(W_R\propto\hat V-\hat T\) neglected; the fibre-constant background for \(U_2\) and for the response of \(Y\), the fibre flux in \(Y\) at the background only and with the base-fibre cross term dropped, and higher orders in \(\vartheta/\chi^2\mu_A^2\) not treated; and a mass of the matter modes that comes from \(\chi\) alone, so that masses from gauge dynamics, which see \(g^2e^{-2\phi}\), and the non-singlet moduli of \(K\) are not treated. The sign statement and the exponents are exact for these forms, the first-term identity and its non-negativity are exact on the background, the level form of \(Y\) is the leading form in \(s\) stated there, and the values on explicit \(K\) are exact spectra with numerical fits. They do not construct the \(K\) selected by the theory, determine \(\mathcal M\) or the background value of \(\phi\), compute the loop kinetic term of \(\chi\), the rotating class, higher loops or the fibre Laplacian, identify \(\bar h\), \(h_\beta\) or \(t\) with quantities of the emergent metric of Chapter~\ref{ch:vierbein-emergence}, discharge Assumption~\ref{ass:gap-psi}, or bear on Assumption~\ref{ass:lorentzian}. Problems~C, F and~N remain open and PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

\section{Problem H: modular origin of the interpolating function}
\label{sec:status-H}

\paragraph{Ledger label.}
Status: OPEN. Priority: OPTIONAL within the phenomenological scope.
Completion: PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL. The modular derivation of the
interpolating function is not established.

Appendix~\ref{app:empirical} tests the function
\[
\nu_{\sqrt{}}(g_{\mathrm N};g_s)
=
\frac{1}{1-\exp\!\left(-\sqrt{g_{\mathrm N}/g_s}\right)}
\]
against SPARC with \(g_s\) fixed to the empirical value
\(g_\dagger\). This is a coefficient-level consistency check, not
a microscopic derivation. Appendix~\ref{app:matrix_entropy} gives
the standard heat-kernel area contribution but does not compute a
source-dependent modular spectrum. Appendix~\ref{app:gaia} applies
an external-field prescription to wide binaries; it does not derive
that prescription from the modular data.

\paragraph{Conditional lemma: occupation of one modular ladder.}
Suppose the relevant reduced state contains one bosonic ladder with
normalized probabilities
\[
p_n=(1-e^{-\Delta k})e^{-n\Delta k},
\qquad n=0,1,2,\ldots,\qquad \Delta k>0.
\]
Then its mean occupation is
\[
\langle n\rangle
=
\sum_{n=0}^{\infty} n p_n
=
\frac{1}{e^{\Delta k}-1},
\]
and therefore
\[
1+\langle n\rangle
=
\frac{1}{1-e^{-\Delta k}}.
\]
Thus, if the extra-stress response is exactly \(1+\langle n\rangle\)
for this single mode, the target interpolator follows if and only if
\[
\Delta k(g_{\mathrm N})
=
\sqrt{\frac{g_{\mathrm N}}{g_s}}.
\]
The quantity to calculate is the modular excitation gap
\(\Delta k\), not the absolute lowest eigenvalue of \(H_D\), which
depends on an additive normalization constant.

For a multimode response, the corresponding quantity is instead a
weighted spectral sum, schematically
\[
\mathcal R(g_{\mathrm N})
=
\int_{(0,\infty)}
\frac{d\mu_{g_{\mathrm N}}(\Delta k)}
     {e^{\Delta k}-1},
\]
where \(d\mu_{g_{\mathrm N}}\) includes the mode weights coupling
to the extra stress. In that case a square-root gap for one mode
does not by itself establish the target function; the measure and
its source dependence must also be calculated.

\paragraph{Missing step.}
Construct the regulated modular operator, or an equivalent
well-defined spectral response, for a weak-field causal diamond
containing a baryonic source. Derive the relevant excitation gap
\(\Delta k(g_{\mathrm N},\kappa_{\mathrm{IR}})\) and its spectral
weight from the microscopic theory. Fix the normalization relating
\(g_s\) to \(\kappa_{\mathrm{IR}}\) independently of galaxy data.
In particular, reconcile the convention
\(g_s\sim\kappa_{\mathrm{IR}}/(2\pi)\) in
Appendix~\ref{app:rar-bridge} with the undetermined factor
\(g_s=c_\star\kappa_{\mathrm{IR}}\) in Chapter~17 before claiming
that the Bose occupation yields the target.

Until this calculation is supplied, Appendices~\ref{app:empirical},
\ref{app:matrix_entropy}, and~\ref{app:gaia} provide an empirical fit
check, a standard area-term calculation, and an external-field
consistency test, respectively; together they do not derive or
uniquely select \(\nu_{\sqrt{}}\). Problem~H remains open.

\section{Problem I: dynamical origin of the drag}
\label{sec:status-I}

\paragraph{Ledger label.}
Status: OPEN. Priority: OPTIONAL falsifiable phenomenological
extension; prerequisite only if drag is claimed as derived or predicted.
Completion: PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

Chapter~\ref{ch:dissipation} adopts a vector drag proportional to
\(-\mathbf v/d^4\) as a working assumption. The microscopic target
is to derive the low-frequency force-response tensor, including its
separation and finite-size dependence, from specified dynamics and
a specified stationary reference state.

\paragraph{Conditional Kubo formulation.}
Let \(\mathbf r\) denote the relative separation and
\(H(\mathbf r)\) a microscopic Hamiltonian. At fixed \(\mathbf r\),
choose a stationary reference state and define the connected
generalized-force operator
\[
\delta\hat F_i
=
-\frac{\partial H}{\partial r_i}
-\left\langle-\frac{\partial H}{\partial r_i}\right\rangle_{\mathbf r}.
\]
For a perturbation
\(\delta H(t)=-\delta r_j(t)\delta\hat F_j\), define
\[
\chi^R_{ij}(t;\mathbf r,\ell_A,\ell_B)
=
\frac{i}{\hbar}\theta(t)
\left\langle[\delta\hat F_i(t),\delta\hat F_j(0)]\right\rangle_{\mathbf r},
\qquad
\chi^R_{ij}(\omega)
=
\int_{-\infty}^{\infty} e^{i\omega t}
\chi^R_{ij}(t)\,\mathrm dt .
\]
For a reciprocal passive reference state, if the low-frequency
limit exists, define
\[
\Gamma_{ij}(\mathbf r;\ell_A,\ell_B)
=
\lim_{\omega\downarrow0}
\frac{\operatorname{Im}\chi^R_{(ij)}
(\omega;\mathbf r,\ell_A,\ell_B)}{\omega},
\qquad
\chi^R_{(ij)}=\frac{\chi^R_{ij}+\chi^R_{ji}}{2}.
\]
Passivity requires this dissipative tensor to be positive
semidefinite. The corresponding low-frequency force contribution is
\[
\delta F_i^{\mathrm{diss}}
=
-\Gamma_{ij}(\mathbf r;\ell_A,\ell_B)\dot r_j.
\]
This is a conditional response prescription, not a calculation of
the NAMCG force correlator, its reference state, or its passivity.

If the reference state and environment are rotationally invariant,
the response at nonzero separation can have distinct longitudinal
and transverse components,
\[
\Gamma_{ij}
=
\Gamma_L(d;\ell_A,\ell_B)\hat r_i\hat r_j
+\Gamma_T(d;\ell_A,\ell_B)
 \bigl(\delta_{ij}-\hat r_i\hat r_j\bigr).
\]
A radial response calculation determines only \(\Gamma_L\).
The isotropic scalar drag used in the laboratory requires
\(\Gamma_L=\Gamma_T\), or an explicit restriction to radial motion.

For example, the causal response family
\[
\chi^R_{ij,\tau}(\omega)
=
\frac{\chi_{0,ij}}{1-i\omega\tau},
\qquad \tau>0,
\]
with real symmetric positive-semidefinite \(\chi_{0,ij}\), has a
pole at \(\omega=-i/\tau\) and satisfies
\[
\chi^R_{ij,\tau}(0)=\chi_{0,ij},
\qquad
\lim_{\omega\downarrow0}
\frac{\operatorname{Im}\chi^R_{ij,\tau}(\omega)}{\omega}
=\tau\chi_{0,ij}.
\]
Thus a fixed static susceptibility does not determine the
low-frequency dissipative coefficient. This example is not a
NAMCG derivation.

Positivity of mutual information establishes \(I(A:B)\geq0\) for
each state. It does not establish an ordering between adiabatic
and non-adiabatic mutual informations, or the sign or separation
dependence of a dissipative response.

\paragraph{Static tail, distinct from the response.}
Chapters~\ref{ch:entanglement-equilibrium} and \ref{ch:dissipation}
and Appendix~\ref{app:rar-bridge} use the same large-separation
scaling,
\[
I(A:B)\sim c_{p_*}
\left(\frac{\ell_A\ell_B}{d^2}\right)^{p_*},
\qquad d\gg\ell_A,\ell_B,
\]
which is dimensionless. It is established in the literature for
R\'enyi mutual information of regions in a conformal vacuum, with the
von Neumann limit shown for free-scalar spheres
\cite{Cardy2013MI}; its leading power for NAMCG cores is an
assumption. Neither this static tail nor a coincidence of its power
with the assumed drag exponent determines the retarded force
response, its tensor structure, or its coefficient.

\paragraph{Missing step.}
Specify the microscopic dynamics, stationary reference state, and
force operators for the finite cores. Compute
\(\chi^R_{ij}(\omega;\mathbf r,\ell_A,\ell_B)\), justify the
passivity and low-frequency assumptions, and derive the
longitudinal and transverse responses. Determine whether they
reduce to the isotropic \(d^{-4}\) laboratory law and calculate its
coefficient before estimating \(\eta\) at tidal or LIGO-band scales.
Problem~I remains open.

\paragraph{Status.}
Optional, not part of the matrix-derived completion criterion unless
drag is claimed to be derived or predicted. Its absence does not kill
the framework within a scope that treats the drag law as
phenomenological.

\section{Problem J: spherical ECG solutions}
\label{sec:status-J}

\paragraph{Ledger label.}
Status: OPEN. Priority: OPTIONAL within the stated ECG model.
Completion: PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL. The reduced results do not derive
singularity resolution from the matrix theory.

For the explicit invariant \(\mathcal P\) in~\eqref{eq:ECG}, consider
the reduced action per unit time and angular volume
\[
L[N,f]=N r^2\bigl(R-2\Lambda_{\mathrm{eff}}+\kappa\mathcal P\bigr),
\qquad
\kappa=16\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}}\beta_{\mathrm{ECG}}.
\]
This reduction assumes that the static, spherically symmetric
variations capture the corresponding field equations; an appropriate
symmetric-criticality argument is not established here. The reduced
Euler--Lagrange operators, including all higher-derivative terms, are
\[
\mathcal E_q
=\frac{\partial L}{\partial q}
-\frac{\mathrm d}{\mathrm dr}\frac{\partial L}{\partial q'}
+\frac{\mathrm d^2}{\mathrm dr^2}\frac{\partial L}{\partial q''}
-\cdots,\qquad q=N,f.
\]
Variations are taken with the fields and the derivatives required by
the order of \(L\) fixed at the endpoints (or with compact support);
otherwise the associated boundary terms must be retained. For the
specified \(\mathcal P\), these reduced equations have been checked
symbolically:
\[
\left.\mathcal E_f\right|_{N=1}\equiv0,\qquad
\left.\mathcal E_N\right|_{N=1}
=\frac{\mathrm d}{\mathrm dr}
\left[2r(1-f)+3\kappa\mathcal B[f]
-\frac23\Lambda_{\mathrm{eff}}r^3\right].
\]
Thus \(N=1\) is a consistent vacuum branch of the reduced equations,
and, with \(\alpha=-\kappa\), the lapse equation has first integral
\[
\mathcal F[f]-\frac23\Lambda_{\mathrm{eff}}r^3=4M,\qquad
\mathcal F[f]=2r(1-f)-3\alpha\,\mathcal B[f],
\]
where \(\alpha\) is the reduced ECG coupling and
\[
\mathcal B[f]
=
\frac43(f')^3
-\frac8r\,ff''
 \left(\frac{rf'}2+1-f\right)
+\frac4r(f')^2
-\frac8{r^2}ff'(f-1).
\]
For the rational profile with \(a=r_0^3\) and
\(\Lambda_{\mathrm{eff}}=0\),
\[
f_{\mathrm{rat}}(r)
=
1-\frac{2Mr^2}{r^3+a}
=
1-\frac{2M}{r}+\frac{2Ma}{r^4}+O(r^{-7}),
\]
substitution gives
\[
\mathcal F[f_{\mathrm{rat}}]-4M
=-\frac{4Ma}{r^3}-\frac{432\alpha M^2}{r^5}+O(r^{-6}).
\]
The \(r^{-3}\) term cannot be cancelled by the cubic contribution,
which begins at \(r^{-5}\). Hence, for the displayed equation, this
profile is not an exact vacuum solution when \(Mr_0\ne0\).

Moreover, a smooth regular center has \(f(r)=1+O(r^2)\), which
implies \(\mathcal B[f]\to0\) and
\(\mathcal F(0)-\tfrac23\Lambda_{\mathrm{eff}}0^3=0\).
Consequently the integration constant vanishes: this displayed
equation has no smooth regular-centre vacuum solution with a nonzero
integration constant, for any \(\Lambda_{\mathrm{eff}}\). For
\(\Lambda_{\mathrm{eff}}=0\), the asymptotic falloff
\(f=1-2M/r+o(r^{-1})\) identifies that constant as \(4M\) and \(M\)
as the ADM mass. For nonzero \(\Lambda_{\mathrm{eff}}\), its
interpretation is not automatically the ADM mass.

\paragraph{Maximally symmetric branch.}
For \(f=1-kr^2\), the first integral gives
\[
\frac{\mathcal F[f]}{r^3}=2k-16\alpha k^3,\qquad
k-8\alpha k^3=\frac{\Lambda_{\mathrm{eff}}}{3}.
\]
This is a conditional background-curvature relation, not a stability
claim.

\paragraph{Large-radius tail.}
For the displayed equation, write \(f=1-2M/r+\delta(r)\). Then
\(2r(1-f)=4M-2r\delta\) and
\(\mathcal B[1-2M/r]=144M^2/r^5+O(r^{-6})\). The leading
correction and the next asymptotic order give
\[
f(r)=1-\frac{2M}{r}-\frac{216\,\alpha M^2}{r^6}
+\frac{368\,\alpha M^3}{r^7}+\cdots .
\]
The rational profile instead has a \(+2Ma/r^4\) term. The tail
expansion is an asymptotic check of the displayed first integral; it
does not determine the model parameter \(r_0\) or establish a match to
an independently published expansion. In the stated action
normalization \(\alpha=-16\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}}\beta_{\mathrm{ECG}}\).
If \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}>0\), then \(\alpha>0\) iff
\(\beta_{\mathrm{ECG}}<0\), which fixes the sign of the leading
correction. For \(M>0\), its magnitude defines the characteristic
scale \(r_*\sim|\alpha M|^{1/5}\); it is not a core scale.

\paragraph{Status.}
Conditional on the stated symmetric reduction, the reduced-action
identities, first integral, regular-centre constraint, maximally
symmetric curvature relation, and asymptotic tail above have been
checked for the specified \(\mathcal P\). The cited Einstein-like
linearized spectrum on maximally symmetric backgrounds is a separate
result; no nonlinear, sourced, or non-maximally-symmetric stability
claim follows. Problem~J
remains open because the reduction's relation to the full field
equations and the cubic truncation and its matching to the matrix
theory are not established. A regular massive core still requires a
specified source, different boundary conditions, or modified
field equations; the entanglement stress for such a source is not specified.

\section{Problem N: control of the effective expansion}
\label{sec:status-N}

\paragraph{Ledger label.}
Status: OPEN. Priority: CRITICAL PATH for the matrix-derived label.
Completion: PROGRAMME-CONDITIONAL.

Assumption~\ref{ass:EFT-a2} treats the four-derivative terms as
expansion vertices at order \((p/\LUV)^2\) and not as propagating
modes. It is stated in Chapter~\ref{ch:ultraviolet} and justified
there only by the composite character of the metric. The ghost mass
of Stelle gravity is of order \(1/\sqrt{\alpha G}\), and its relation
to \(\LUV\) depends on the physical \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\) and on
\(\alpha\), which are part of Problem~F.

\paragraph{Missing step.}
Derive from the matrix path integral that the graviton two-point
function has no propagating Ostrogradsky ghost for \(p<\LUV\), for
the values of \(\alpha\) and \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\) computed in Problem~F (here \(\alpha\) is the Weyl-squared coupling
of~\eqref{eq:stelle}, whose \(C_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}C^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\)
coefficient is \(-\alpha/2\); with the continuation convention stated
below, \(c_W=-\alpha/2\) in~\eqref{eq:W4}; it is not any other coupling
denoted \(\alpha\) in this book), with the region of those values in which it holds stated.
This is the requirement of item~6 of Chapter~\ref{ch:geometrogenesis}
and no stronger.

\paragraph{Induced Weyl term and the extra spin-two pole (conditional; not closure).}
Work in the truncated identity of Theorem~\ref{thm:Geff}, expanded about
a flat background with the vacuum term cancelled or neglected so that
flat space is a solution, with every included sector assigned the common
limits \(\LUV,\mu\), \(\mu\ll\LUV\), with \(S_E=-iS_L\) as in
Appendix~\ref{app:seeley-dewitt}, and with curvature invariants
continued without change of sign (an added convention). Rewrite the
traced \(a_{2,s}\) of~\eqref{eq:appA-a2} in the basis
\(\{C^2,R^2,E_{\mathrm{GB}}\}\), and let
\(\mathcal W_C:=\sum_sw_sc_{C,s}\) and \(\mathcal W_R:=\sum_sw_sc_{R,s}\),
where \(c_{C,s}\) and \(c_{R,s}\) are the coefficients of
\(C_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}C^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\) and \(R^2\). For a real
scalar sector with \(E=E_0+QR\), \(c_{C,s}=\operatorname{tr}\mathbb I_s/120\)
and \(c_{R,s}=\tfrac12\operatorname{tr}(\tfrac16\mathbb I_s-Q)^2\); for
other sectors \(c_{C,s}\) also depends on \(E\) and on the bundle
curvature and is not computed here. The logarithmic term
of~\eqref{eq:W-expand} gives, in~\eqref{eq:W4},
\[
  c_W=\frac{\mathcal W_C L}{32\pi^2},\qquad
  c_R=\frac{\mathcal W_R L}{32\pi^2},\qquad
  L:=\ln\frac{\LUV^2}{\mu^2},
\]
that is, \(\alpha=-2c_W\) and \(\beta=2c_R\) in~\eqref{eq:stelle}; the
Gauss--Bonnet term does not contribute at quadratic order on a flat
background. With \(g_{\mu\nu}=\eta_{\mu\nu}+h_{\mu\nu}\) and
\(k^2=\eta^{\mu\nu}k_\mu k_\nu\), the spin-two part of the quadratic form
of \(R/(16\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}})+c_WC^2+c_RR^2\) is proportional to
\(k^2(1-32\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}}c_Wk^2)\) and its spin-zero part to
\(k^2(1+96\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}}c_Rk^2)\) (a symbolic check, not a proof).
There is therefore a spin-two pole at \(k^2=-m_2^2\) with residue
opposite in sign to that of the massless graviton, and a spin-zero pole
at \(k^2=-m_0^2\) with residue of the graviton's sign, where
\[
  m_2^2=-\frac{1}{32\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}}c_W}
       =-\frac{\Upsilon\LUV^2}{L},
  \qquad
  m_0^2=\frac{1}{96\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}}c_R},
  \qquad
  \Upsilon:=\frac{\pi}{G_{\mathrm{eff}}\mathcal W_C\LUV^2}.
\]
If \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\mathcal W_C>0\), the extra spin-two pole of this
fixed-\(L\) form is a tachyonic ghost, with \(|m_2|<\LUV\) exactly when
\(L>\Upsilon\). If \(1/G_{\mathrm{eff}}\) is taken from the
universal \(a_1\) term alone, \(1/G_{\mathrm{eff}}
=\mathcal A_1(\LUV^2-\mu^2)/(2\pi)\) with
\(\mathcal A_1:=\sum_sw_s\mathcal A_{1,R,s}\), and
\(\Upsilon=\mathcal A_1/(2\mathcal W_C)\) up to \(O(\mu^2/\LUV^2)\),
independent of \(\LUV\). For sectors of minimally coupled real scalars,
\(\mathcal A_1=\mathcal N_0/6\) and \(\mathcal W_C=\mathcal N_0/120\), so
\(\Upsilon=10\) for every \(\mathcal N_0>0\),
\(|m_2|<\LUV\) in this fixed-\(L\) form once \(\LUV/\mu>e^{5}\), and
\(m_0^2=2\LUV^2/L\). Since \(|m_2|^2/\mu^2=\Upsilon e^{L}/L\), which is
much larger than one for \(\mu\ll\LUV\) unless \(\Upsilon\) is
exponentially small, this pole lies at momenta where the fixed-\(L\)
form does not apply (see below); it is a property of that form, not a
one-loop prediction. For \(|k|\lesssim\mu\), where the fixed-\(L\) form
applies, the Weyl correction relative to the Einstein term is
\(32\pi G_{\mathrm{eff}}c_Wk^2=(L/\Upsilon)\,k^2/\LUV^2\).

The fixed-\(L\) form is not the one-loop result at momenta
\(\mu\ll|k|\ll\LUV\). At second order in curvature the one-loop action
is nonlocal, with momentum-dependent form factors~\cite{BarvinskyVilkovisky1990}; assuming that
the Weyl form factor decays when \(sk^2\gg1\), its proper-time integral
is cut off at \(s\sim k^{-2}\) rather than at \(\mu^{-2}\), and for
massless sectors \(L\) is replaced by \(\ln(\LUV^2/k^2)+\delta_W\), with
a constant \(\delta_W\) fixed by the non-logarithmic part of the form
factor and by the finite Weyl counterterm. For the analogous
second-order form factor \(\int_0^1\mathrm d\tau\,
e^{-\tau(1-\tau)sk^2}\) of a potential insertion, a direct
numerical evaluation gives \(\ln(\LUV^2/k^2)+2-\gamma_{\mathrm E}\),
with \(\gamma_{\mathrm E}\) Euler's constant,
independent of \(\mu\) for \(k\gg\mu\); this illustrates the mechanism
and does not compute \(\delta_W\). With \(x:=k^2/\LUV^2\) and the
principal logarithm, the spin-two factor becomes
\[
  F(x)=1-\frac{x}{\Upsilon}\Bigl(\ln\frac1x+\delta_W\Bigr),
\]
whose zeros on the principal sheet are
\(x=-\Upsilon/W_j(-\Upsilon e^{-\delta_W})\), \(j\in\{0,-1\}\), with
\(W_j\) the branches of the Lambert function; \(\mu\) no longer enters.
At \(\delta_W=0\) the zeros cross \(|x|=1\) at \(x=\pm i\) when
\(\Upsilon=\pi/2\). A numerical scan with an argument-principle count
(a check, not a proof) finds that \(F\) has no zero in the disc
\(|k^2|<\LUV^2\) exactly when \(\Upsilon>\Upsilon_*(\delta_W)\), with
\(\Upsilon_*(0)=\pi/2\) and \(\Upsilon_*(\delta_W)=\delta_W\) for
\(\delta_W\ge1\), where the crossing is at \(x=1\). For \(\Upsilon=10\)
the condition is \(-9.58<\delta_W<10\); at \(\delta_W=0\) the zeros
are a complex-conjugate pair with \(|k^2|\approx3.9\LUV^2\).

These statements are conditional on the truncated identity, the
continuation and common limits stated, massless sectors (massive
sectors require the threshold functions of Problem~F), and the use of
the leading-logarithmic form factor up to \(|k^2|\sim\LUV^2\), where
the derivative expansion is not controlled. They do not compute
\(\mathcal W_C\), \(\mathcal A_1\), \(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\) or \(\delta_W\)
for the locked block, do not derive the induced coefficients from the
matrix path integral, and do not decide whether complex-conjugate zeros
beyond \(\LUV\) are propagating ghosts. Within the leading-logarithmic
model \(F\), absence of a zero in \(|k^2|<\LUV^2\) is a numerical
condition on \(\Upsilon\) and \(\delta_W\), which are outputs of
Problem~F, and is not implied by the metric being composite.
Problems~F and~N remain open.

\paragraph{Status.}
Remains Assumption~\ref{ass:EFT-a2}.

\section{What this appendix does not do}
\label{sec:status-not}

It does not produce an unsuppressed laboratory coupling between a
controllable external field and \(T_{\mu\nu}^{\mathrm{ent}}\) or
\(G_{\mathrm{eff}}\). The observational window therefore remains empty.
It does not alter the chain of Chapter~\ref{ch:falsifiers} or the agenda of
Chapter~\ref{ch:agenda}.
